# Backlog run log

Notes from the scheduled backlog runs. Newest at the bottom.

## 25 November 2026: v0.159, task 1 of 7 (mixer settings, master extras, fader designs)

Note: a manual run (2:54) and the scheduled 3:05 run overlapped; the 3:05 run was cancelled by the user and pushed nothing (checked: main was still at v0.158, no extra branches or tags).

### Built
- **Mixer settings button** `#mxSetB` in the mixer tab, right of `#mxCmp`. Opens the drop-down `#mxsPop` (`mxsPop()`).
- **Settings › Layout › Mixer** (`#mxsSet`, placed above Level meters). Same controls plus advanced ones: channel number, master strip width, fader height (left end = automatic), highest-peak read-out per channel, group colour band.
- Both places are built by `mxsHtml(adv)` and edit the same values, so they stay in sync (`mxsSync()`): `UI.mxs` (defaults in `MXSD`, read with `mxsO()`), `UI.mxCompact` (same value as the compact button) and `UI.mtr` (meter speed, reading, peak hold, colours, the same as Level meters). `mtrUI` and `syncSettings` are wrapped so changes made in one place show in the other.
- What strips show: effect slots, sends, picture, pan, mute/solo/record, dB scale, volume number. These are window classes on `#mixWin` (`mxsApply()`): `mxnofx`, `mxnosnd`, `mxnov`, `mxnopan`, `mxnobtn`, `mxnosc`, `mxnoval`, `mxnonum`, `mxshowpk`, `mxgb`. Strip widths are set through the style tag `#mxsCss`.
- **Master strip extras** (`.mxme`, built in `mxsMount()`, updated by `mxsMaster()` and `mxsTick()`):
  - True peak (`LM.tpMax`, turns amber/red against the target's peak limit; click to reset).
  - Loudness target (distance in LU from `lmTarget()`, with a bar and a green ±1 LU band; click to pick the target, which stays in sync with the loudness pop-up `#lmSel`).
  - Stereo correlation -1..+1 (computed from `anL`/`anR`, smoothed; red below 0).
  - Each one can be hidden in the mixer settings (`mLu`, `mTp`, `mTgt`, `mCorr`).
- **15 fader and meter designs** (`FDS`, CSS generated by `fdCssUp()` into `#fdCss`, scope class `fdN` on `#mixWin`). The designs are: 1 Classic (the old look, default), 2 Console, 3 LED ladder, 4 Dots, 5 Hairline, 6 Glass, 7 Flat bars, 8 Zones, 9 Level colour, 10 Neon, 11 Vintage, 12 In the groove, 13 Big blocks, 14 Rails, 15 Line and dot.
  - Each design is shown in both settings places as a small preview with its name.
  - The meters stay the existing `clip-path` bars with `<s>` peak lines, so instant levels, hold and the meter colour setting work in all 15.
  - Zones uses stepped colours from `lvStops()`. Level colour paints the whole bar in the colour of the current level (`mxsTick`).
- Fixes found while building:
  - The master's side meters never moved. `drawMixer` looks for `.fm` in the canvas's parent, and the master canvas sits in `.mxvw`. `mxsTick` now drives them.
  - With the picture hidden in the meter view, the side meters are driven too.
  - Channel names no longer shrink away in small windows (`.mx>.mxn{flex:none}`).
  - `mxsFit()` (wraps `mxFit`) applies the fixed fader height and makes faders shorter when the master would not fit. In a very small window it hides the master extras (`.mxtight`).

### Tested
- Headless Playwright with the demo projects (10 and 30 tracks), playing. No page errors on load or during the tests.
- All 15 designs switched live while playing and screenshotted on channels and the master.
- Checked:
  - every toggle and slider in the drop-down
  - sync with Settings and with the compact button
  - meter colour (Heat, Ice) carrying into the designs
  - peak hold from Settings
  - the target menu
  - saving across reload
  - fit at 470 px and 360 px window heights, compared with v0.158

### Not verified
- How it looks and how fast it runs on the user's Mac. CSS masks (designs 2, 3, 4, 11, 13, 15) on 30 strips at 120 Hz could cost frames.
- The true-peak read-out shows the maximum since the last reset (from the loudness worklet), not a live value.

### Left
- Tasks 2 to 7 of the scheduled backlog. Ideas: a reference A/B in the master strip (A/B already exists), and per-design fader-cap hover states.

## 25 November 2026: v0.160, task 2 of 7 (mixer view colours, spectrogram, fun views)

### Built
- **Bigger view drop-down** (`#ctxm.mxvm`): wider, larger text, bigger previews, descriptions wrap. New last entry **Colours and options** opens the colour and option menu of the chosen view (`mxMenuItems`, now reassigned to handle every view). Right-clicking a picture still opens the same menu.
- **Colour schemes for every view** (`MXMODES`, `MXPAL`, tables from `mxLut()`): temperature heat, three bands (bass red, mids green, treble blue, like Serato/Traktor), bass heat (hot when the bass is loud), fire, ice, purple spectral, mono white, track colour; meters keep green/yellow/red zones, spectrum keeps rainbow. Saved per view in `UI.mxCol` as before.
  - Band levels per channel: `mxBands()` (5 bands in `H0.b5`, 3-band bytes in `H0.b3`; waveform rows keep their band colour in `H0.bc`). Only computed when a band scheme or the Fun view is used.
  - Colour helpers: `mxC()`, `mxTriRGB()` (cached), `mxPosRGB()` (colour by position along the frequency axis).
- **Stereo field size**: Small, Medium, Large (`mxOpt('stereo','size')`, default Large = old look).
- **Spectrogram view** (`UI.mxView='sgram'`, `mxSgram()`): 64 x 160 ring-buffer canvas per channel (`H0.sg`), bass left, treble right, newest at the top, scaled into the strip. Default colours purple spectral; all schemes available.
- **Fun view** (`UI.mxView='fun'`, `mxFun()`, choice in `mxOpt('fun','anim')`, list `MXFUN`): fireflies (drift with stereo position, more of them light up and glow brighter with level), liquid surface (spring-driven slosh from the balance, rises with level, ripples on hits, dB ticks at the side), kaleidoscope (waveform mirrored 8 times, bigger when louder, turns with the balance), bouncing balls (5 bands, bass left, launch speed from band level, peak marks), particle fountain (gravity, wall and floor bounces, particle-to-particle collisions, max 70 per strip, hits throw bursts, dashed line at recent height). Default colours "AudioLab blue and teal" (the logo's ball colours, cached ball/glow sprites in `mxSpr()`).
- Channels scrolled out of view are still skipped in `drawMixer()`.

### Tested
- Headless Playwright, demo project (30 tracks) playing at bar 48: no page errors on load or while switching every view and several schemes; screenshots of each view, the master strip and both menus checked.
- Time per `drawMixer()` with 17 visible strips (headless, software drawing): 3 to 5 ms for the new views and schemes, same range as the old waveform view (stereo field is still the slowest at about 9 ms).
- Choosing a view and an animation from the menus saves to `audiolab-ui`.

### Not verified
- Look and frame rate on the user's Mac. In headless runs the spectrogram fills slowly because the test browser draws few frames; at 60 updates a second it covers about 2.7 s.
- Track analysers use 256-point FFTs, so the bass part of track spectrograms and the lowest ball are coarse (the master uses 2048).

### Left
- Tasks 3 to 7. Optional dancing character for the Fun view was not built.

## 25 November 2026: v0.161, task 3 of 7 (spectrum overlay fixes, dockable analyser with modules)

### Research (how analyser suites lay this out)
- iZotope Insight 2: one window made of modules (loudness, levels, sound field, spectrogram, spectrum, history) that are switched on and off with buttons in the title bar and share the space; the sound field puts the correlation bar beside or under the goniometer.
- Cubase: the right zone / meter bridge is a narrow strip with a Loudness tab that lists momentary, short-term, integrated, range and true peak as numbers beside a bar with the target marked.
- Followed here: module buttons in the title bar, numbers beside loudness bars with a green target band, goniometer with L and R on the diagonals and mono upright, correlation -1 to +1 with red below zero, spectrogram with bass at the bottom and newest on the right.

### Built (all new code sits before `askChoice`, names start with `anz` / `ANZ`; the old `spo*` functions are reassigned)
- **Steady spectrum scale** (`ANZD.spec`): the top of the picture is fixed at -24 dB (`ANZTOP`, measured so a very loud master sits about 12 dB under the top). Height can be 60, 72 (default) or 96 dB, or "Slow automatic", which only moves after the song has been louder or quieter for several seconds, in 6 dB steps. The old code rescaled on every frame.
- **Rising line when stopped: fixed.** Cause: silent bins (minus infinity) were turned into -120 dB, the treble tilt of 4.5 dB per octave was added, and the auto scale then zoomed in on that tilted line. Silent bins now stay on the floor and nothing is drawn without sound.
- **Glass border**: the window is now `.spov.anz` with a separate glass layer `.spgl` (class `lg` for the edge light, `rfAttach` for refraction) behind it. The canvases live in `.anzb`, a layer of their own above the glass, never inside it. Docked strips are solid (no glass at all).
- **Docking** (`anzSetDock`, `anzDock`, `anzSide`; button in the title bar or right-click): window, or a strip at the bottom (`#botOut`), top (`#topOut`), left or right (fixed, `#stage` gets a margin through `body.anz-l` / `body.anz-r` and `--anzl` / `--anzr`). Strips are resized by dragging their inner edge (`.anzrs`; `ov.dh`, `ov.dw`). One strip per side; `applyLayout` is wrapped so strips stay next to the main window. Docked analysers are saved and come back (`spoSave`, `UI.spov` now also holds `dock, mods, col, rng, dh, dw`).
- **Modules** (`ANZM`, drawn by `ANZD`, built by `anzBuild`): spectrum, spectrogram, stereo field, scrolling waveform, loudness (momentary, short-term and integrated bars, true peak max, range, target band), correlation (with a mark at the lowest recent value). Switch them with the buttons in the title bar, drag those buttons or the module labels to reorder (`anzWire`), or right-click for a menu (`anzMenu`). In a row the stereo field is square, loudness and correlation are narrow; in a column (left, right, or a tall window) they stack. The same modules work in the floating window. The first time an analyser is docked all modules are switched on.
- **Colours** (`anzCols` reads the schemes from `MXMODES`, so schemes added to the mixer views later appear here too): source colour, heat, three bands, bass heat, rainbow, fire, ice, purple spectral, AudioLab blue and teal, mono white. Uses `mxLut`, `mxC`, `mxPosRGB`, `mxBands`.
- **Idle**: after about 2 seconds without sound the pictures are only checked 3 times a second (`anzFrame`).
- Comparing several sources still works in the spectrum; the other modules show the first source. Loudness is always the master.

### Tested
- Headless Playwright, demo project (30 tracks) playing at bar 48: no page errors on load or during the tests.
- Screenshots checked: floating window with one and with six modules, tall window (column), docked bottom, top, left and right with different colour schemes, the right-click menu, and the picture 0.6 s and 4 s after stop (curve falls away, no rising line).
- Stage size checked for each dock (for example left: strip 250 px, stage starts at 250). Reorder by dragging a title bar button, toggle by click, two sources, save and reload (strip came back docked with the same modules, order and colours), close (body classes and `UI.spov` cleared).
- One frame with all six modules took about 1.7 ms (headless, software drawing).

### Not verified
- Look, refraction edge and frame rate on the user's Mac.
- Dragging a strip's inner edge to resize it and dragging module labels (only the title bar buttons were dragged in the test).
- The research came from general knowledge of these products; the web search only returned page links, not their text.
- Track sources use the track's 256-sample analysers for stereo field, waveform and correlation, so those are coarser than for the master.
- The older "Visualizer" panel (Settings) still exists next to this; both can be docked left or right together but that combination was not tested.

### Left
- Tasks 4 to 7. Ideas: level meters as a seventh module, loudness history graph, retire the old Visualizer panel in favour of this one.

## 25 November 2026: v0.162, task 4 of 7 (cables in the mixer, FL Studio style)

### How routings are stored (nothing here was changed)
- Sends: `t.sends[returnId] = level` (0 to about 2, same value as the Sends bars, `SPEC_SEND`); applied by `busRoute()` and the offline render.
- Group output: `t.grp` = id of the group track (`grpOf`, `grpAnc`, `addToGroup`); `busRoute()` connects the track to the group input instead of the master.
- Sidechain: `f.p.src` (track id) on a Sidechain effect (`side`), and `m.p.src` on a Sidechain follower modulator (`t.mods`, type `side`, routes to parameters such as `t:<id>:vol`). Note: the Sidechain effect pumps in time with the song; its builder does not read `f.p.src` (only `scSrc` and `frzCut` do). The follower is the one that actually listens to another track (live, through `modFollow`).

### Built (all new code sits before `askChoice`, names start with `cab` / `CAB`)
- **Cable layer** (`cabDraw`, `cabList`, `cabGeo`): an SVG inside `.mxs` (so it scrolls with the strips) draws every existing send (solid line), group output (dashed) and sidechain (dotted) as a curve in the source track's colour, in a 46 px lane under the strips (`CABLANE`, class `mxcab` on `#mixWin`). Send cables get fainter at low levels; a switched-off follower is drawn faint. Several cables into one channel fan out. Legend at the bottom right (`cabLegend`).
- **Sockets**: a dot under every channel. Drag from it to another channel (or to the master): a dashed cable follows the pointer, the target outline turns green when something valid is possible, red when not, and the mixer scrolls when the pointer nears its edge (`cabTarget`, `cabMark`, `cabEnd`).
- **Drop menu** (`cabOpts`), only valid choices: "Send to" (target is a return, sends at 0.5), "Output to" (target is a group, uses `addToGroup`, no loops), "Output to Master" (dropped on the master while the channel is in a group), and "Sidechain into": switch an existing follower or Sidechain effect on the target to this source, add a new ducking follower (route to the target's volume at 50%), or add a Sidechain effect with this source.
- **Hover** a cable: both channels get an outline in the cable colour, other cables fade (`cabHi`, `cabUnhi`). **Click**: a small panel (`cabPop`) with the send level (the same control id as the strip's Sends bar, so both move together), a plain description, "Show <track>" and a remove button (`cabRemove`: stop sending, take out of the group (to the parent group or the master), remove the sidechain source, or remove the follower and put its targets back to their own values).
- **Toggle**: cable button in the mixer tab (`#mxCab`) and a "Cables" switch under Routing in the mixer settings drop-down and in Settings > Layout > Mixer (`UI.mxs.cab`, default on, `MXSD.cab`). All three stay in step through `mxsSet` / `mxsSync`.
- **Keeping it linked**: `changed()` is wrapped (`cabChanged`): if the tracks are different objects (undo, redo, track added, moved or removed) the mixer is rebuilt, otherwise the cables are redrawn when the routing signature (`cabSig`) changed. Track menu routing (`routeItems`), the Sends bars and the modulator source menu all go through `changed()`, so cables follow them. Every cable action calls `pushUndo()` first.
- `mxFit` is wrapped so the faders are sized for the room above the lane (it measures with the mixer temporarily that much shorter, plus 12 px spare because the volume numbers under the faders are often still empty when it runs; without that the pan row got squeezed onto the buttons).

### Tested (headless Playwright, 30-track project with groups and a reverb return, plus the Psytrance demo)
- No page errors on load, while opening the mixer, dragging, using the menus, the panel, compact mode, scrolling, toggling, settings.
- 33 existing cables drawn (25 group outputs, 8 sends). Hover outlines both channels. Click opens the panel; dragging its send bar changed the value from 0.1 to about 1.6 and moved the strip's Sends bar too; one undo step.
- Drag Kick to Plate reverb: menu offered Send plus sidechain choices; Send created `sends={..:0.5}` and a cable, one undo step; undo removed both. Pad to Drums: Output to Drums set the group and moved the track. Kick to Lead: new follower with a volume route. Kick to Bass: offered the existing Sidechain effect in slot 2 and set its source. Lead to Master: "Output to Master" only. Snare to Kick: only sidechain choices. Removing the follower cable removed the modulator. Track menu "Send to" from `routeItems` made a cable appear.
- Compact mode: cables follow the narrow strips. Horizontal scroll: sockets move with the strips; grab-scrolling in the lane still works. Toggle off: lane and SVG gone, padding back to 8 px, both settings switches unticked; switching on in the drop-down brought them back.
- Sound unchanged: renders through `AudioLab.render` (`renderMix`) of the Psytrance demo and the Shrine Legion demo, beats 0 to 96, three or more runs each before and after. The renders are not bit-identical between runs even on the old version (hats, acid lead, pad and pluck use random parts); mix RMS old -16.64 to -16.69 dB vs new -16.59 to -16.66 dB (Psytrance), old -11.116 to -11.120 dB vs new -11.116 to -11.118 dB (Shrine). Per track, beats 64 to 128: Kick -20.59628 dB and Rolling bass -36.12767 dB identical before and after in every run; the random tracks vary by the same amounts in the old and new version. The cable code does not run during a render (it returns at once when the mixer is closed).

### Not verified
- Look and feel on the user's Mac, and dragging with a real mouse or trackpad (only Playwright's mouse).
- Very long cables to a far-away channel all sit near the bottom of the lane and overlap; with many sends to one return the lane gets busy.
- The ducking follower works during playback through the modulator system; whether exports include its movement is the same as for any modulator (not checked here).
- Dropping onto a channel while the mixer auto-scrolls far (tested only with both channels visible).

### Left
- Tasks 5 to 7. Ideas: an "only show cables of the selected channel" option, curved cables over the strips like FL Studio when the lane is hidden, and a send level knob on hover.

## 25 November 2026: v0.163, task 5 of 7 (zoomed-out overview, Ableton style)

### Built (all new code sits before `askChoice`, names start with `ovw` / `OVW`)
- **Overview picture** (`ovwPaint`, `ovwGeo`): every track is a thin row (9 to 20 px, so 30 tracks fit; more than about 60 scroll) of plain blocks in the track colour, one per clip. No notes, waveforms or names. Muted tracks and muted clips are faint. A group shows a thin line over the span of its tracks. Bar numbers and the section labels stay on top, section starts run down as faint lines, the loop is a band on the ruler. Track headers become a 128 px column drawn on the canvas: a colour tab and the name (groups bold, members indented, selected track white). The background is darker than the normal view. The rows stop above the device panel and transport (`OVW.bot`, measured from `#fx` and `#tr`).
- **Getting in**: `zoomAround` is wrapped. When a zoom-out step is cut short by the view's limit, the cut-off part counts as "pushing against the end" (`OVW.acc`). After the end has been reached (180 ms earlier or more), a push of 0.25 (about half a wheel notch, a short pinch) or one more zoom key press opens the overview (`ovwEnter`). Also: Shift+O (new key command "Song overview on / off" in the shortcuts list, group View), right-click on the empty arrangement, the logo menu, and "Song Overview" in the desktop app's View menu (`desktop/main.js`).
- **Animation** (`ovwAnim`, `ovwFrame`, 170 ms, ease-out): the same picture is drawn with every position blended between the normal view and the overview, so rows compress and the background darkens. The last normal frame (`OVW.snap`) fades out over the first part, the DOM headers fade with CSS (`body.ovw #hdrcol`). Leaving plays it backwards onto a freshly drawn normal frame.
- **Inside** (events go to a see-through layer `#ovwL` over the canvas, so none of the normal clip handlers run and clips cannot be moved): pinch or Cmd/Ctrl-scroll zooms 1x to 2.2x around the pointer (`ovwZoom`), scroll or drag moves (`ovwPan`), a click puts the playhead on the nearest bar and selects the track, a click on a name only selects, Space plays. Zoom keys and map zoom act on the overview while it is open.
- **Getting out** (`ovwLeave`): zooming in past the top of the range (same push rule) goes back to the normal view with the beat and track under the pointer staying under the pointer. Double-click does the same. Esc, Shift+O, the "Overview" chip (`#ovwChip`, top left) and right-click > "Leave the overview" go back to exactly where you were. `V.ppb` and `V.th` are never touched while the overview is open and are written back on leaving.
- **Cheap drawing**: the picture is cached (`ARRC`); a playhead-only frame copies it and draws one line. While playing, full redraws are held to 10 per second (follow mode marks the view dirty every frame).
- `drawArr` is wrapped once more on top of the glass-copy wrapper; `ovwBase` is the wrapped original. `#hglass` is hidden in the overview so the half-resolution glass copy does not replace the left column.

### Tested (headless Playwright, Fractals demo, 30 tracks, 17 sections)
- No page errors on load or in any test. (The only console message is the service worker file missing in the test server, as before.)
- Screenshots checked: normal view, overview at 1600x1000 and 1200x800 (all 30 rows visible), frozen frames at 25% and 60% of the transition, a live frame during the animation, overview zoomed in while playing, tracks on the right, a folded group, the chip tooltip, the normal view after leaving.
- Ctrl-scroll out from a mid zoom: 3 notches reach the end and open the overview. Further zoom-out does nothing. Zoom in, sideways scroll, drag: move as expected. Click: playhead to bar 187 and track selected; click on a name selects only. Space plays and the playhead moves.
- Zoom in past the top: left after 2 notches; beat 840.52 and track 14 under the pointer before, the same after. Zoom and track height identical before and after (3.4 / 34 and 6 / 60 in two runs).
- Shift+O in and Esc out: view identical. Esc with a menu open closes the menu first. Zoom keys: opens one press after the end is reached; 5 zoom-in presses leave again. Double-click, chip, both menus, seven fast toggles in a row, window resize, an empty project: all fine.
- Cost (headless, software drawing): full overview frame about 4.4 ms, playhead-only frame 0.01 ms.
- New test script: `tools/ui-tests/overview.py`.

### Not verified
- Look, feel and frame rate on the user's Mac; a real trackpad pinch (only Ctrl-scroll and keys were simulated). The push needed to get in or out (`OVW.PUSH` = 0.25, 180 ms rule) may need tuning by feel.
- The "Song Overview" item in the desktop View menu (the file passes a syntax check, the desktop app was not run).
- Safari gesture events on the overview layer.
- With horizontal-only zoom keys the normal view has a soft far end (each step gets shorter and the view creeps back on redraw). This was already so; in that zone the zoom is not restored to the digit.
- The map still shows the box of the normal view while the overview is open.
- Dropping files in the overview only shows a hint.

### Left
- Tasks 6 and 7. Ideas: hover highlight of a row, follow the playhead when zoomed in inside the overview, drag on the ruler to scrub, show the overview range in the map.

### Release
- Commit 2cdca93 on main, desktop build workflow (desktop.yml) dispatched, artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 191 (v0.163).

## 25 November 2026: v0.164, task 6 of 7 (track header layout editor)

### Built (all new code sits before `askChoice`, names start with `hcz` / `HCZ`; CSS classes `hcz`, `zm`, `zr1`, `zw`, `zb`, `ze*`)
- **Setting**: `UI.hcz = {order:[ids], icon:'normal'|'big'|'hidden', pan:'slider'|'half'|'round', ms:'normal'|'large', meter:1|0}` (`hczGet`, `hczSet`, default `HCZD`). When the layout equals the default, nothing is stored and the headers are drawn exactly as before (old code path, no class). Any other layout sets `#hdrin.hcz`.
- **Drawing** (`hczBuild`, runs after `renderHeaders` through one more wrapper): the existing parts of each header (volume, pan, M, S, arm, e, R, write, lanes) are moved into one wrapping row `.zw` under the name in the chosen order, so all existing click handlers still work. Rows that do not fully fit the track height are hidden (CSS `round()`), so put the important controls first. Below 46 px the controls sit beside the name.
- **Controls list** (`HCZI`): Volume, Mute, Solo, Record arm, Edit channel, Read automation, Pan, Write automation, Automation lanes (the default set, each removable), plus new optional ones: **Freeze** (`freezeAsk`), **Effects off** (`t.fxByp`, stays in step with the button in the device strip through a `renderFx` wrapper, `hczSyncByp`), **Output** (master or a group, `hczOutMenu`, uses `addToGroup` / `groupTracks`), **Sends** (`hczSendMenu`, same actions as the track menu), **Track colour**. The icon and the level meter are switched in the options.
- **Icon**: normal, big (as tall as the track, grows with the track height, at most 80 px wide) or hidden. **Pan**: slider, half-circle dial (`hp` control) or round dial (`kn` control); all three use the same control id `hp:<track id>`, so header, mixer and device strip stay one value. **Mute and Solo**: normal or large. **Level meter**: shown or hidden.
- **Editor** (`hczOpen`, panel `#hczEd`, not modal, so the tracks change live behind it): three ready-made layouts (Minimal, Standard, Everything; `HCZP`), the shown list (drag a row, or the up / down buttons, ✕ to remove), "Not shown: click to add", the four options, "Reset to default", Done. Drag the title to move it, Esc closes it.
- **Ways in**: a "Controls" button with a tooltip next to the TRACKS heading (`#bHcz`), right-click on a track header or under the tracks ("Customise track controls…" at the top of the menu; `openMenu` is wrapped and looks at a time stamp set by a `contextmenu` listener on `#hdrin`), and a card in Settings > Layout > Track controls (`#hczCard`, with Reset).
- **Linking fix found on the way** (`hczLink`): moving volume or pan in the header or the device strip did not repaint the open mixer strip (the other directions already worked). Now it does, in every layout.

### Tested (headless Playwright, Fractals demo, 30 tracks)
- No page errors on load (default layout and with a stored custom layout) or in any test. Only console message: the service worker file missing in the test server, as before.
- Screenshots checked: default (unchanged), Everything at 140 / 96 / 64 / 44 px and collapsed, big icon + half dial + large M/S at 140 / 64 / 44 px and collapsed, round dial without meter, Minimal, tracks on the right, the editor, the right-click menu, the Settings card.
- Editor: add five optional controls, remove one, move with the buttons, drag a row to the end and to the third place (tracks follow during the drag), all options, presets (the matching one lights up), reset (old header DOM comes back), title drag, Esc (closes a menu first, then the panel).
- Pan: half dial drag gave R71 in the header and in the device strip; round dial drag, double-click to centre; set from the device strip and from the mixer, the header dial follows; set from the header, the mixer follows.
- Optional buttons: Effects off both ways with the device strip, Output menu lists Master and the groups, Sends menu switches a send on and the button lights up, colour changes, Freeze opens the freeze dialog.
- Layout survives undo and a page reload. Playback with the meter hidden: no errors.
- New test script: `tools/ui-tests/hdrlayout.py`.

### Not verified
- Look and feel on the user's Mac and with a real mouse; Safari (the row fitting uses CSS `round()`, Safari 15.4+ / Chrome 125+; older engines would show a cut-off row instead of hiding it).
- Monitor and lock buttons were not added: the app has no monitor or lock function yet (`t.mon` is only read, never set).
- With the big icon the room for controls is small (the header column is 216 px), so fewer controls fit.
- In custom layouts the controls that do not fit are simply hidden; the old layout's special small-size arrangements only apply to the default layout.
- MIDI-learn marks on the dial pan styles were not checked.

### Left
- Task 7. Ideas: a resizable header column, per-track-kind layouts, a track number toggle, monitor and lock once they exist.

### Release
- Commit 16e42c4 on main (v0.164), desktop build workflow (desktop.yml) dispatched, artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 192.

## 25 November 2026: v0.165, mixer cables: resizable lane and a tidy layout (asked for by the user during run 7)

### Built (names start with `cab` / `CAB`; `cabDraw` and `cabLegend` were rewritten in place)
- **Tidy layout** (`cabPlan`, default; `UI.mxs.cabSty` = `tidy` or `curve`): all cables of one kind into the same channel share one straight line in the colour of the channel they go into, with an arrow into that channel and a label ("Output to Drums", "Send to Plate reverb") when there is room. Each source drops onto that line in its own colour. Lines that would run over each other get their own level (shortest spans on top). The old hanging curves are still there under the "Tidy / Curved" button in the legend.
- **Lane height** (`cabH`): by itself the lane is as tall as the levels need (52 px + 12 px per extra level, at most 150 px, and never so tall that the channels are squeezed under 378 px). A grip on the top edge of the lane (`cabGrip`, `.cabrz`) drags it taller or lower (46 to 400 px, stored in `UI.mxs.cabH`); the mixer window grows downwards with it so the channels keep their size, and only when the screen is used up do the channels give way. Double-click the grip: back to automatic.
- **Legend is now clickable**: Send, Output to group and Sidechain each hide or show that kind (`UI.mxs.cabHide`); "Selected only" shows just the cables that start or end at the selected channel (`UI.mxs.cabSel`, follows `selectTrack` through a wrapper).
- Hover lights up the whole route of one cable (`.cfu`) and fades the rest; click, drag from a socket, the pop-up and remove work as before.
- Fader sizing fixes found on the way: with cables on, the pan row can no longer be squeezed and the volume read-out keeps its height when empty (the fader fit measured both, so the result changed from call to call and the pan row could end up on the buttons).

### Tested (headless Playwright, Fractals demo, 30 tracks, 33 cables)
- No page errors. Screenshots checked: automatic lane (2 levels, 64 px), scrolled to the right end, lane dragged to 177 px (window grew from 470 to 583 px, channels unchanged), Selected only, outputs hidden, Curved, hover, compact mixer, a third level after adding two sidechain followers (76 px, channels still fit), undo back to 64 px.
- Click on a cable opens the pop-up; dragging from a socket to another channel shows the menu; double-click on the grip resets; the height survives a reload; cables off removes lane, grip and legend.
- Test script: `tools/ui-tests/cables2.py`.

### Not verified
- Look and feel on the user's Mac, real mouse or trackpad. Sound is untouched (drawing code only), so no renders were compared.
- Projects with many returns and nested groups (more than about 8 levels) in a small mixer window: the levels get as close as 3 px.

## 25 November 2026: v0.166, run 7: particle devices, part 1 (Bouncy Notes)

### Change of plan (the user answered during the run)
- The user asked for a list of Ableton's particle devices and then chose to copy all of them: Bouncy Notes, Emit, Vector FM, Vector Grain, Vector Delay, Vector Map (the "Inspired by Nature" pack). Wishes: engines and controls as close to Ableton as possible, in our look, little grey, the black picture from the top to the bottom of the device with the controls floating inside it. The room-shape synth and slice-bounce effect of the original task 7 are replaced by this list.
- Done in this version: Bouncy Notes. The other five are set up as scheduled tasks (one device per run).

### Built (new code before `askChoice`; one call `pbxInit();` sits right after `const MFXRUN={};` because the device registry `ND` only exists from there)
- **Bouncy Notes** (`bnotes`, MIDI effect, group MIDI): every note of a clip drops a ball onto a keyboard; each bounce plays a note. Worked out from the notes alone in `bnBalls` (drop height, gravity 20 units/s² x Gravity, each bounce keeps `1 - Friction / sqrt(Mass)` of its speed, ball ends when bounces are closer than 30 ms or after Lifetime) and turned into notes by `bnRun` = `MFXRUN.bnotes`. Same notes in playback, freeze and export.
- Controls (Ableton's names): Gravity, Mass, Friction, Speed, Voices (oldest ball is removed), Lifetime, Vel > Height, Vel fade, Length, Rnd > Len, Rnd > Dir (the ball drifts across the keys and turns round at the edge of Range), Range, Seed, Scale, Tonic, Quantize, Auto + Interval (a held note drops a new ball every interval), Thru (also plays the original note). 12 presets in three groups (`FXPRE.bnotes`).
- Picture (`bnDraw`): balls with short trails over a keyboard; keys outside the scale are dark, the tonic has a blue mark, a key lights up when a ball lands on it. While the song plays it shows the balls of the clip under the playhead; when stopped it drops one test ball again and again so the knobs can be judged by eye.
- **Look for the particle devices** (CSS class `pbx`, added by an `fxDevHtml` wrapper for every type in the set `PBX`): black picture over the whole device, header, selects, knobs and sliders float on its right side without a grey panel. The draw function leaves `PBXW[type]` px free on the right while the parameters are shown.

### Tested
- Headless load: no page errors. Screenshots checked: stopped (test ball), playing the Arp track of Fractals with the Scatter preset, parameters hidden.
- Offline render (`tools/engine-tests/bouncy_notes.py`): pluck track with two notes: 28 notes out, not silent (RMS 0.0134, peak 0.125), no NaN, two renders bit-identical, first sound at 0.308 s (the fall). Same seed gives the same notes, another seed gives other notes, Scatter stays in the scale.
- Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo), identical in v0.164 and now.

### Not verified / not built
- Not compared against Ableton itself (no access): the feel of Gravity, Mass and Friction ranges is a best guess from descriptions.
- Missing compared with Ableton: walls drawn into the picture that deflect balls, Freeze, the note Filter. Live keyboard notes do not go through MIDI effects in this app (same as the Arpeggiator), so balls only come from clip notes.
- The picture uses the clip's own notes; a MIDI effect placed before Bouncy Notes is not shown in the picture (the sound is right).

### Left
- Vector Delay, Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM, Vector Map (scheduled). Track controls editor redesign asked by the user (see next entry or the schedule).

### Scheduled after run 7 (25 November 2026)
- Backlog 17 (04:56 UTC): track controls editor made universal and visual (mini track with drag and drop, layouts as pictures in a 2 by 2 square, a fourth layout, pan styles as pictures, icon as Normal / Big / Hidden).
- Backlog 12 (09:30 UTC) Vector Delay, 13 (11:00) Emit with the rotating centre emitter, 14 (14:30) Vector Grain, 15 (16:00) Vector FM, 16 (17:30) Vector Map plus the walls for Bouncy Notes.

## 25 November 2026: v0.167, run 8 (mixer that grows from the right, Settings tab "Interface", the app is now called AudioLab Pro)

### Built (new code before `askChoice`; names start with `mxr` / `MXR`, `st` / `STI`, `fdPop`, `hf` / `HFD`)
- **Mixer anchored to the right** (`mxAutoW` was replaced, `mxrNeed`, `mxrBar`, `mxrSoon`): the window is exactly as wide as its channels plus the master. Adding or removing a track, the strip width, compact channels and the master width all move the LEFT edge; the right edge and the tab above it stay where they are. When the channels do not fit to the left of the right edge, the window stops at the screen edge and the channels scroll.
  - The master strip is now `position:sticky` at the right, so it stays in view while the channels scroll under it (class `mxover` adds a shadow on its left). The cables are redrawn on scroll so the master's socket stays under it.
  - Dragging an edge of the mixer narrower than the channels need is remembered as the widest it may get (`UI.mxCapW`); dragging it wider, or to the left screen edge, clears that. After moving the window it grows or shrinks to the new room.
  - The tab above the mixer is never wider than the window: first the words on the view and Spectrum buttons go (`mxb1`), then the Spectrum, compact and cables buttons and the DAC volume (`mxb2`). Compact and cables were already in the mixer settings menu; a "Spectrum overlay" switch was added there (section Window). The cable legend is cut to the window width.
- **Settings tab renamed to "Interface"** (it holds the scale, the layout, the track and transport controls, the mixer look and the meters; the key is still `layout`). `stfBuild()` rearranges it once at start:
  1. Interface scale at the very top.
  2. One Layout box: the picture with Presets, then three tight rows: Panels (Map, Visualizer and where it sits), Transport bar (Edit, Reset, Shrink on small screens), Track controls (Customise, Reset). The old separate Panels, Transport bar and Track controls boxes are gone; the same buttons and checkboxes were moved, so their ids and handlers are unchanged.
  3. Colours like another DAW, Mixer and Level meters follow (not redone).
- **Mixer settings with pictures** (`STI`, `stIc`, `stIcV`, through a wrapper of `mxsHtml`, in Settings and in the mixer's own menu): every switch and slider has a small icon that lights up when on. The four meter settings show the chosen value: speed is a tiny meter that falls at that speed while the pointer is on the row, reading shows a peak, RMS, VU or loudness shape, peak hold shows the line with 1, 3 or ∞, colours shows the real colours.
- **Fader and meter design as a drop-down** (`.fdsel` button opens `#fdPop`, `fdPopOpen`, `fdPopClose`): the button shows the current design with its picture; the list shows all 15 with picture and name. Esc or a click outside closes it.
- **The design also applies to the track headers** (`HFD`, `hfdCss`, class `hfd` on `#hdrin`, style tag `#hfdCss`): the volume bar gets the design's fader cap and groove, the level bars get its segments, dots, thin lines or stepped colours. The bars are still drawn as before (one height per bar, a line for the peak); segments are a striped cover drawn by CSS, so nothing extra happens per frame and nothing was added to `#hglass`. "Level colour" sets a bar's colour only when the level crosses into another colour step (wrapper of `trackMeters`).
- **Reset all settings** moved from General to the footer of the Settings window (left, red outline, divider), in view on every tab. It still asks first.
- **Name**: AudioLab Studio is now AudioLab Pro in the app (title, About, file type, saved file header), the web manifest, the READMEs and the desktop app (product name, menus, dialogs, window titles, installer names). The desktop app keeps its data folder with the old name (`app.setPath('userData', …'AudioLab Studio')` in `desktop/main.js`), so settings and downloaded updates stay. `appId` and the package name were left as they were on purpose. The word under the logo reads "Pro" for now; 15 logo options were made for the user to choose from.

### Tested (headless Playwright, Fractals demo, 30 tracks)
- No page errors on load, after a reload with a stored design, or in any test.
- Mixer: 30 tracks (stops at the left screen edge, scrolls, master stays), 3 tracks, 3 compact, 1 compact, 0 tracks, strip width 64, undo back to 30: the right edge stayed at the same pixel every time and the content never scrolled by a few pixels. Edge drag narrower and back, window move, compact button. Screenshots of each looked at.
- Settings: the whole Interface tab, the design drop-down in Settings and in the mixer menu (the mixer menu stays open while choosing), the reset button with its question, General without the old reset row.
- Track headers: all 15 designs screenshotted while playing. Frame rate while playing was the same for designs 1, 3 and 9 in the headless browser (25 to 28 fps there, software drawing).
- Test script: `tools/ui-tests/mixright.py`.

### Not verified
- Look and speed on the user's Mac, real mouse. The header level bars are only about 3 px wide each, so the designs differ less there than in the mixer.
- The desktop build with the new product name (the workflow was started; the installed app's name on disk changes only when a new installer is used). Windows: the installer should replace the old install because `appId` is the same, not checked.
- Desktop DAC volume control in the narrow tab (only exists in the desktop app).
- No analyser row was added to the Panels row: the dockable analyser keeps its own docking menu.

## 25 November 2026: v0.169, Growl instrument and the AudioLab Pro logo (both asked by the user during run 8)

### Research (what makes a good growl)
Read: MusicRadar (FM growl in FM8, neurofunk bass and tearing reese in Serum), Sound On Sound "Dubstep secrets", ADSR (Skrillex growl in Serum), EDM.com "Beefy growls", Native Instruments (reese bass), Splice (talking wobble bass), two smaller blogs. What they agree on:
- **Source**: rich in harmonics. FM with ratio 1 or 2 and the amount moved by an LFO; a saw-like wave; for drum and bass two saws detuned by 15 to 30 cents (reese). Mono, with a clean sine sub underneath that is not distorted.
- **Mouth**: band-pass or vowel resonances that move in step with the beat (1/8 for classic wobble, 1/16 for rolling parts, 1/4 to 2 bars for slow neuro sweeps). Talking sounds jump between vowels far apart; neuro sounds use notch or narrow band sweeps over the whole note.
- **Dirt**: distortion before and after the filter, then strong levelling (OTT), then saturation or a clipper. Bit or sample-rate reduction for an edge.
- **Squeals and scrapes**: no page describes the "moving chair" itself. Built from the physics instead: a chair leg sticks and slips many times a second (rough bursts at about 20 to 60 per second, slightly irregular) while a narrow whistle rises in pitch.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`, names start with `grw` / `GW`; `grwInit();` sits right before the `/*VN-BEGIN*/` marker because the instrument registries only exist from there)
- **Growl** (`PRESETS.growl`, group Bass and acid in the instrument browser). Every note is worked out once as audio by `grwRender` and played as a buffer (`grwBuf`, `grwPlay`, cache `GWC`), so playback, freeze and export give the same sound and a repeated note costs nothing.
- Signal path: FM pair with a saw-like shape and an optional second detuned voice (Reese) → stick-slip bursts (Scrape) → drive → the mouth (three vowel resonances that move from Vowel A to Vowel B: OO, OH, AH, ER, EH, EE) → fold → crush → levelling (Squash) → the squeal (a whistle through a narrow band that glides from "Squeal from" to "Squeal to" over the note and jumps with every slip) → low cut, tone → clean mono sub added at the end.
- Movement: Speed in note lengths (1/1 to 1/32, with triplets), eight shapes (sine, triangle, ramps, square, Yoi, once over the note, random steps), starts again with every note. Stereo gives left and right slightly different mouths; the sub stays in the middle.
- 27 knobs in five groups (Source, Mouth, Movement, Scrape and squeal, Dirt and level), each with a plain-language tooltip (`GWTIP`). The picture shows the mouth as resonance peaks for vowel A and B and the moving one while a note plays.
- **24 presets** (`GWPRE`): dubstep (Yoi talker, Wub 1/8, Riddim grind, Screech, Robot vowels, Tearout machine gun, Deep growl, Metal gargle), EDM (Electro house growl, Complextro stab, Midtempo snarl, Bass house bark, Talk box lead), drum and bass (Neuro reese growl, **Moving chair**, Chair drag long and low, Chair screech short, Squeaky door, Foghorn, Neuro stab, Jump up squelch, Tearing reese 1/8, Rubber squeak), and Plain growl.
- **Logo 1** (chosen by the user): "PRO" in an outlined pill at the top right of the name (`.wmP`), in Settings and on the start screen. The small moving wave next to the old word is gone with it. The 15 options are kept in `docs/logo/audiolab-pro-logo-options.html`.

### Tested
- Headless load: no page errors. Growl on a track: the device, the preset list (24), the picture, playback, the instrument browser.
- `tools/engine-tests/growl.py`: all 24 presets rendered through the app at 174 BPM: no NaN, levels between 0.08 and 0.14 RMS, the sub share and brightness differ per preset as intended, two renders are bit-identical.
- Moving chair measured: the strongest band between 350 and 3000 Hz rises from about 600 Hz to 1350 Hz over the note and the loudness chatters at about 38 times a second.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it.** The presets were set from the research and from measurements, not by ear. They will need tuning on real speakers, the chair presets most of all.
- Working out a new note takes 25 to 50 ms per second of sound in the headless test machine (once per different note; the user's Mac should be several times faster). Very long notes with many different lengths could cause a short hitch the first time they play.
- Live keyboard playing, MIDI learn and automation of the Growl knobs were not tried. The movement always restarts with the note (no free-running mode yet).

### Left
- Tune the presets by ear with the user. Ideas: free-running movement, a second movement source, a formant picture you can drag, notch sweep for neuro basses, render in a worker.
- App icons still show the old picture without text; nothing to change there unless the user wants "Pro" on the icon.

## 25 November 2026: v0.170, run 9 (effect levels, blur from a ready-made picture, refraction as a switch, live edge light, new "Glass and light" settings)

### What the app had before
- "Light mode" (`UI.lite`, General tab): 30 fps cap, still logo, leaner voices. It did not touch the glass. It is unchanged, except that when it switches itself on for a very weak computer the effects now also go to Zero (wrapper of `liteAuto`; "Turn off" in the notice brings them back; not done once a level was chosen by hand, `UI.fxUser`).
- "Make it smooth" (button in the frame rate hint): set blur 8, refraction 0. The button is now "Optimise for this computer" and opens Glass and light and runs the test.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`, new CSS before `.dacv`)
- **Effect levels** (`UI.fx = {refr, blur:'off'|'copy'|'live', edge, anim}`; `fxPre`, `fxGet`, `fxLevel`, `fxSet`; `applyTrans` and `glassPlay` replaced). Zero effects = all off, solid panels (body class `fxzero` removes every backdrop-filter, `fxstill` stops CSS animations and transitions, the logo and bubbles stand still). Optimised = no refraction, blur from a picture, edge light and animations on (or what the test chose, `UI.fxOpt`). Full effects = everything, live blur. Panels are solid whenever blur and refraction are both off. Old settings: refraction slider at 0 becomes Optimised, anything else Full.
- **Blur from a ready-made picture** (`fxBlurPic`, `abDraw`, canvas `#arrBlur`, state `ABL`; `gbCopy`, `gbTick` replaced, `gbOn` and `gbLayout` patched). The transport bar, track headers and device panel only have the arrangement behind them. A quarter-size copy of the arrangement canvas is blurred once (canvas `filter: blur()`, edges stretched outward first so the blur does not fade at the border) each time the arrangement is redrawn, and shown under those panels. They then have no backdrop-filter (body class `fxcopy`). The device panel (`#fxglass`) uses the picture in every level (body class `fxpic`), also in Full effects. Windows, menus and dialogs keep one plain live `blur()` because anything can be behind them. With "Picture" a blur below about 4 screen pixels is raised to 4.2 (the browser's most expensive band).
- **Refraction is a switch** (`fxGet().refr`, `RFK = 60`). The old slider only set the `scale` number of the displacement step; map size, filter area and the per-frame copy of the map were the same at 1% and at 100%. So strength costs nothing and it is fixed at 75% of the old maximum. What does cost is the map: it is now made at 1/6 of the panel size on large panels (was 1/4; the divisor follows the width of the bent band, 2 to 6), about 2.25 times fewer bytes copied per frame per panel. `rfStyle` leaves the blur out of the filter list when the picture is behind the panel.
- **Live edge light** (`EL`, `elSoon`, `elTick`, `elGrad`, `elSampler`, CSS property `--elb`, registered as not inherited). The 1 px rim (half a pixel on high-density screens) is a conic gradient with 8 to 30 stops. Each stop takes brightness and 38% of its colour from three points next to that edge in a 64 x 36 copy of the blurred picture: bright next to bright clips, dim over dark areas. Recalculated at most 11 times a second, only after the backdrop picture changed or while the pointer is in use, and written only when the text changed. The old glow (`filter: drop-shadow` on every rim, a full extra layer per panel) and the wide inner glows are gone; the base rim is about half as bright. Panels that are not over the arrangement keep a fixed rim. Strength slider kept; off in Zero effects.
- **Optimise for this computer** (`fxOptimise`, `fxFps`, `fxUndo`, `FXO`). Measures the screen rate with all effects off (or uses the desktop app's real rate), reads core count and memory, then tries Full, glass without refraction, the same with a fixed rim, solid with animations, Zero, 0.9 s each, and keeps the first that holds 95% of the screen rate. Computers with 2 cores or 2 GB skip Full. The result is written out in plain words. Undo returns to the settings from before (also after picking a level by hand).
- **Settings, Glass and light** (`fxBuild`, `fxSync`, `gpDraw`, `setLoop`): the three levels as the main choice with Optimise and Undo; a preview that is a real glass panel over moving clips and a wandering light, using `rfAttach`, `fxBlurPic` and `elGrad` like the app, with the live frame rate; cards for transparency, blur, edge light (slider) and refraction (switch); a box "What each effect costs" with a picture, plain words and a toggle per option (blur Off / Picture / Live, edge light follows the background, animations, softer blur while playing, half-size picture behind live glass, note cache, sleeping tracks). The old tick boxes are the same inputs moved into toggles, so their handlers are unchanged.
- **Logo in Settings at a steady 120 a second** (`setLoop`): it used to be painted on the visualiser clock, which drops to 2 a second after the mouse rests for 3 s. It now has its own loop while Settings is open: every frame up to about 160 Hz, every second frame at 240 Hz.

### Research on blur (part B)
- Live `backdrop-filter: blur()`: Chromium keeps the filtered result only while nothing under, inside or over the panel changes; any change re-filters the whole panel. Large radii are cheap per pixel because Skia blurs a scaled-down copy; the expensive band is just under 4 device pixels. Sources: grida.co/docs/wg/research/chromium/effect-optimizations, the project doc `claude/audiolab-liquid-glass-research.md` (Skia and Viz source links).
- Pre-blurred copies instead of blurring per frame: developer.chrome.com/blog/animated-blur (a blur that is not animated is rasterised once and reused).
- Dual Kawase / downsample-upsample: blog.frost.kiwi/dual-kawase (5 reads per pixel going down, 8 going up, cost grows slowly with radius). Needs WebGL or many small canvases; small 2D canvases may not be GPU canvases in Chromium, and drawing a GPU canvas into them forces a read-back. Not used.
- Chosen: a static pre-blurred quarter-size picture refreshed only when the arrangement changes. The blur itself is done by the canvas `blur()` filter (Skia, the same scaled-down method) on 1/16 of the pixels. Zero filter passes while only meters, the time display or device pictures move. The arrangement you see directly keeps full resolution and frame rate.

### Other things made lighter (part C)
1. Device panel: no backdrop-filter in any level (picture instead).
2. Rim glow: `filter: drop-shadow` removed from all glass panels.
3. Refraction map 1/6 size on large panels.
4. `#bLogo` glow: `steps(12)` timing, 6 changes a second instead of one on every frame; it sits in the transport glass, which was re-filtered for it all the time, also when idle.
5. `gbLayout` (reads sizes and styles of four elements) ran on every backdrop copy, up to 120 times a second while scrolling. Now at most 4 times a second on that path (`gbCopy(true)`); resize and the 0.7 s timer still run it directly.
6. With the picture, the half-size sharp copy `#arrBack` is not drawn (kept 2 x 2, it only passes clicks on).
7. Bubbles and loading pictures (`hxTick`) are painted once a second instead of 30 times when animations are off.
8. With the picture or Zero: the small blurs on device labels, the EQ overlay, the add-track buttons and tooltips are off.
Looked at and left: level meters at 120 a second in the headers (free with the picture, still re-filter the header glass in Full effects); `fxGlassSync` 0.5 s timer; visualisers and Fun views (they show sound).

### Tested (headless Playwright, Fractals demo; script `tools/ui-tests/fxlevels.py`)
- No page errors on load, after reload, in every level, while playing, with Settings open.
- Full: lens only on `#hglass`, `#tr`, the open sheet and the preview. Optimised and Zero: no element in the page has a `url()` backdrop filter (checked over all elements, also with Settings and a menu open).
- Level buttons, Undo, Optimise (report text, Undo after it), every toggle, the refraction switch, the blur selector. Screenshots of each level, the settings section top and bottom, the preview and a menu at 1x and 2x were looked at.
- Logo: with the pointer resting it is painted on every frame the headless browser delivers (about 13 a second there), not 2 a second.

### Not verified (the container has no GPU)
- All frame rates on the user's Mac. Expected from how Chromium works: Optimised has no filter pass for the three docked panels, so idle and playback should sit at the screen rate; Full effects loses the device panel's blur pass and the per-frame logo glow.
- The read-back of the 64 x 36 edge light sample (through a quarter-size canvas, at most 11 times a second): if the Performance popup shows dips while the arrangement scrolls, switch "Edge light follows the background" off to compare.
- Whether the 1/6 refraction map shows steps at the rim on a 2x screen (looked fine in software rendering).
- 120 pictures a second for the logo on a real 120 or 240 Hz screen. If macOS itself throttles the window (low power mode), the app cannot override that.
- Refraction fixed at 75% of the old maximum is stronger than the old default of 40%. One number to change: `RFK`.

### Note
- This run was first committed as v0.168 on top of v0.167; main had moved to v0.169 (cable fix, Growl) in the meantime, so the same changes were applied again on top of v0.169 and tested again.

### Left
- Windows and dialogs in Optimised still use one live blur. A picture is not possible there without capturing the page.
- Light mode (General) and Zero effects overlap in purpose; they could become one switch.
- Release of run 9: v0.170 (commit 709cb9a, artifact version 198); desktop build workflow dispatched. Project doc `claude/audiolab-backlog-run-9.md`.

## 25 November 2026: v0.171, run 10 (themes with the look of another DAW, with matching faders and meters)

### What the app had before
Six "Colours like another DAW" buttons (`DAWLOOK`). Each one only set the hue, tint amount and brightness of the same dark tint, the grid strength, the clip opacity and a track palette. Panels, accent, grid, playhead and clip shape were the same in all of them, which is why they looked alike.

### Research (colours and general feel only; no logos, icons, fonts or artwork were used)
- **Ableton Live**: real values from the stock theme files (Live 10.1 "Mid Light" and "Dark"): desktop #6e6e6e, surface #878787, highlight #a5a5a5, dark-theme surface #414141 / #555555, accent #ffb532, play #00ff81, record #ff363c, selection #c7edff, black grid lines, black waveform on bright clips; clip palette from Live 10+. Sources: github.com/phixion/ableton-themes (00Light.ask, 01Mid Light.ask, 02Mid Dark.ask, 03Dark.ask), github.com/danhemerlein/ableton-colors. Live 11/12 files were not found; believed near-identical.
- **FL Studio**: playlist background RGB 52,68,78 = #34444e (Image-Line knowledge base, support.image-line.com/action/knowledgebase?ans=632). Theme elements listed in the manual (image-line.com, envsettings_themes.htm) without default values. Everything else (slate panels, orange accent, grey caps) is an estimate.
- **Reaper**: real values from the stock Default 7 theme: chrome #333333, arrange #454545 / #424242, black grid, teal #1abc98, play cursor amber #efc852, meters #00bfbf to #00daad to #00fe95 with clip #bb2500, grey items #818989 with #1a1a1a peaks. Source: github.com/Conceptual-Machines/reaper-theme-builder (Default_7.0_unpacked.ReaperTheme).
- **Bitwig**: track palette from the DrivenByMoss `DAWColor` constants (github.com/git-moss/DrivenByMoss). Panel greys and the orange accent are estimates.
- **Cubase, Logic Pro, Pro Tools, Studio One**: no published colour values were found (Steinberg and Avid help pages only describe what can be coloured and how the meters behave: steinberg.help colours page, avid.com color-coding-tracks-clips, Pro Tools First help on meters). Their colours here are **estimates** of the well-known look: blue-grey with blue meters (Cubase), neutral charcoal with rounded blue and green regions and a white playhead (Logic), light grey edit area with plain blocks and green lights (Pro Tools).
- **Seventh theme: Reaper**, not Studio One. Studio One (dark blue-grey, blue accent, blue meters) sits close to Cubase and FL; Reaper's teal, mint meters, grey items and amber playhead match none of the others.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`, new CSS before `.dacv`)
- **Eight themes** in `THEMES`: AudioLab, Live style, FL style, Cubase style, Logic style, Bitwig style, Pro Tools style (new), Reaper style (new). The old ids (`ableton`, `fl`, `cubase`, `logic`, `bitwig`) are kept, so a saved choice gets the new look by itself.
- Each theme now sets: panel colours (`c`: `--gtr`, `--gtr2`, `--bg`), accent (`a`: `--blue`, plus the new `--bl3`, `--blf`, `--blu1`, `--blu2`), the arrangement (`t`: lanes, ruler, ruler text, section row `secBg`, grid colour `gr` and strength `grk`, hint text), the clip look (`k`: dark or light waveform `wave`, corner radius `cr`, title bar shade `hdk` or no title bar `hd:'none'`, playhead `ph`, a display-only colour blend `cmix` that never changes the project), sharp corners (`sharp`, body class `thsharp`), knob line width (`knw`).
- `thPaint()` writes all of that after `applyTheme()` (wrapper). `drawArr`, `arrPH` and `drawClip` read the new `TH` fields; without a theme they draw exactly as before.
- **Accent as a variable**: inside the main style block, 53 fixed `#4c8dff` and 75 `rgba(76,141,255,…)` became `var(--blue)` / `rgba(var(--bl3),…)`; filled backgrounds use `--blf`, a deeper shade per theme so white text on it stays readable. The logo keeps its own colours.
- **Light arrangements** (Live style mid grey, Pro Tools style light grey): the app's text is light everywhere, so panels stay dark. `thUnder()` paints a dark layer on the arrangement under the transport bar, track headers and device panel, and `thGlass()` (wrapper of `applyTrans`) keeps glass at least 90% solid in these two themes.
- **Controls follow the theme** (`thPick`): fader and meter design (`UI.mxs.fd`, used in the mixer and on the track headers) and meter colours (`UI.mtr.col`). Only a starting point: both can be changed afterwards as before and stay changed (also after a reload).
  - Six new fader and meter designs (now 21 in `FDS` and `HFD`): 16 Slate (FL style), 17 Wide cap (Cubase style), 18 Round cap (Logic style), 19 Slim bar (Bitwig style), 20 Ridged (Pro Tools style), 21 Pill (Reaper style). Live style uses 7 Flat bars.
  - Three new meter colour choices (`MCOLS`, `MXSM`, buttons added by `thMtrBtns`): Lime (Live style), Blue to red (Cubase style), Mint (Reaper style). They show in the mixer settings and in Settings, Level meters like the others.
- **Preview cards** in Settings, Interface (`dawLookRender` replaced, `thCard`): each theme is a small picture with a bit of arrangement (lanes, grid, clips in that theme's clip look, playhead) and one mixer channel (panel colour, fader cap shape, meters in that theme's meter colours, accent button), with the name and one line of description.
- Moving the Background, Hue or Tint sliders while a theme is on takes over from its fixed colours (`UI.dawTint`); accent, clip look and controls stay. Clicking the theme again brings its colours back.
- "Also recolour the tracks in this project" is kept (one undo step).

### Tested (headless Playwright, Fractals demo; script `tools/ui-tests/themes.py`)
- No page errors on load, after reload, or in any of the 24 combinations (8 themes x Zero, Optimised, Full effects) with the mixer open and music playing. Every screenshot was looked at: text readable in each, also the glass mixer over the light Pro Tools style arrangement.
- All six new designs in the mixer and on the track headers; the three new meter colours while playing.
- Settings: the eight cards, a click on a card (theme, design 17 and Blue to red set, the drop-downs follow), changing design and meter colours afterwards and reloading (they stay), slider take-over, recolour and undo.

### Not verified
- How it looks on the user's Mac and at 2x; the screenshots were made in software rendering.
- Colours for Cubase, Logic, Pro Tools and most of FL and Bitwig are estimates, see Research.
- The older tests (`fxlevels.py`, `mixright.py`) were not run again.

### Left
- Panels and all text stay dark with light text in every theme. A truly light interface (Live's default mid grey with black text, classic Pro Tools) needs the several hundred fixed text and panel colours turned into variables first.
- Only the arrangement follows the theme. The piano roll, the map, device panels and the pictures in the mixer keep their dark look. Selection frames and loop lines keep their colours.
- **Knobs**: the app has no knob style setting, so a theme only changes the line width of knob rings (`--knw`). Real knob styles (ring, disc, flat) would be a task of its own.
- The area before the song start is shown dark, which is heavy next to a light arrangement.
- In the default theme the active Settings tab and the hovered menu row are now solid accent blue instead of 85% blue.

## 25 November 2026: v0.172, run 11 (updates, Audio devices window, "Interface and DAC")

### Why "Check for updates" stopped working
Tested in the real desktop app under xvfb with a local update server (`tools/engine-tests/updater/rig_check.js`).
- **Cause:** `updFetch` in `desktop/main.js` had no time limit. One request that never answers (the laptop woke from sleep, a network that drops packets; the app checks every hour) left `updBusy = true` for good, and `checkUpdates` then returned at once without a word. From then on the button did nothing until the app was restarted. Reproduced: with a server that never answers, the first click gives no dialog and every later click nothing.
- Second cause: a click while the automatic check or download was running was also dropped silently.
- The repo is public and both GitHub addresses answer, so it was not an access problem.

### Built: desktop side (`desktop/main.js`, `preload.js`, `native/alvol.swift`). **Only reaches users who install a new app build.**
- `updFetch(file, ms)`: every request has a time limit (15 s for the version file, 120 s for the program). `updDo` / `updCheck(download)`: one check that always answers with a plain result and shows no dialog: `{status, installed, app, latest, notes, size, at, reason, error}`; status `current`, `available`, `ready`, `needs-app`, `reverted`, `publishing`, `error` (reason `offline`, `timeout`, `http`, `bad`, `unreachable`, `disk`). Checks queue up (`updChain`). `manualCheck` and `updBusy` are gone.
- `checkUpdates(manual)` (menu, hourly timer, old program versions): opens the page's own window when the page has `audiolabHost.updOpen`, otherwise the old dialogs, now with a reason.
- New IPC and preload functions: `updateCheck({download})`, `updateStatus()`, `updateApp()`, `dac.info()`, `dac.rate(uid, hz)`.
- `alvol info`: every device, inputs too (kind of connection, channel counts, sample rate, rates on offer, bit depth, which process holds it exclusively, volume). `alvol rate <uid> <hz>` sets the device's own sample rate. **Not compiled in the container (no Swift): see the Mac job of the build.**
- `MIN_SHELL` stays 0.147.0: the page feature-detects everything and works in older app builds.
- New file `desktop/update-notes.txt`: one or two plain sentences per release. `sync-app.js` already read it into the update file, but it never existed, so the notes were always empty. **Write it at every release from now on.**

### Built: page (new code before `askChoice`, new CSS before `.dacv`)
**Updates** (`UPD`, `UPDU`, `updOpen`, `updRun`, `updLook`, `updPaint`, `updAct`, `updApply`, `updNotes`, `updSafe`, `updAutoArm`, `updNote`)
- "Check for updates" (Settings, General and About) opens an own window: installed version, newest version, last checked, size, what is new (first line of each release's commit message, or the notes in the update file), Update now / Later; progress while checking and downloading; on failure the reason (not connected, GitHub did not answer in time, GitHub not reachable).
- New app build: asks the app (`updateCheck`). Older app build: the page reads the version file from GitHub itself; Update now uses the already downloaded version (`applyUpdate`) or the old updater, and says honestly what to do if that old updater is stuck (quit and reopen). Browser: says the web version is always current, offers Reload when a newer one is online. The button is now shown in the browser too.
- "Install updates automatically" (`UI.updAuto`, off by default, Settings, General, desktop app only): a downloaded update is installed when nothing is playing, recording or exporting, no question is open and there was no input for 20 s (or the window is hidden). It goes through the app's `applyContent`, which runs `beforeReload` (stop, save projects and settings). A card says it is waiting (Install now / Not now); after the restart a card says which version is running. Works in older app builds too.
**Audio devices window** (`adScan`, `ADV`, `ADK`, `ADIC`, `adIc`, `adChips`, `devKind`, `audDevOpen`, `audDevRender`, `audDevClick`, `adUseOut`; the three old functions were replaced)
- Two summary cards on top (playing through, recording from), headings Outputs and Inputs with icons and counts, every device with an icon and colour for its kind (built-in speakers, headphones, Bluetooth, USB DAC, audio interface, display audio, microphone, USB audio, virtual), chips for the recognised model, sample rate, bit depth, channels, volume, system default.
- Test is a small dashed pill with a play icon; Use is the solid blue main button; the device in use shows a green "In use" badge and a green edge instead of Use. Inputs have Use too (new `UI.inDev`) and Test shows the input level for five seconds. The system's "Default" entry is folded into the device it points to.
- Rates and channels: read from the system in a new Mac app build (`dac.info`); otherwise the engine rate and channel count of the device in use, what the browser reports for inputs, or "up to" from the device profile.
**Settings tab "Interface and DAC"** (was "DAC & listening"; `ifdBuild`, `ifdPaint`, `ifdSync`, `ifdMode`, `IFD`, `DEVP`, `devProf`). The existing inputs (`bpChk`, `lockSr`, `ceilSel`, `balR`, `monoChk`, `swapChk`, `dcChk`, `hqChk`) were moved into the new layout, so their handlers are unchanged.
- Head: current output with icon and chips, a button to the devices window, and the mode switch Automatic / DAC mode / Interface mode (`UI.ifMode`). Automatic picks Interface mode for recognised interfaces and USB devices that also have inputs, DAC mode otherwise, and says why. "Apply the recommended settings" sets the buffer for the mode (nothing is changed without pressing it).
- Device profile card: recognised model, limits, tips, whether the engine rate is one the device supports, and a drop-down to choose the profile by hand (`UI.dac`).
- DAC mode: volume link (same `DAC` object as the Output slider in the mixer, both follow each other); bit-perfect switch with a live list of what is true right now (mode on, master at 0.0 dB, device rate equals engine rate, device volume 100% or fixed, note about other programs) and a verdict; engine sample rate; "Switch the DAC to the engine's sample rate automatically" (`UI.rateFollow`, `rateFollow`, `rateSet`; only shown when the app can do it) and a button to switch once; exclusive use explained as not possible; DC blocker, safety ceiling, keep the engine awake; the Lossless listening box.
- Interface mode: buffer size and sample rate (mirrors of the Control Panel selects, linked both ways), delay reported by the system, measured round trip (`rtMeasure`: a small worklet plays two clicks and listens; both must come back with the same delay), recording latency offset (mirror), input device, input channels (stereo, input 1, input 2: `UI.inCh`), input monitoring (`UI.inMon`), output device, output pair (`UI.outPair`, only shown when the device has more than two channels).
- Engine hooks, all inactive at the default settings: `inCons()` replaces the two fixed `getUserMedia` settings (adds the chosen input device); `inWire` (wrapper of `armTrack`) picks one channel and/or sends the input to the master for monitoring, with Stereo and monitoring off the stream is used untouched as before; `outRoute` (wrapper of `ensureCtx`) copies the stereo mix to another pair of outputs, with pair 1+2 nothing is touched. The way sound is calculated is unchanged.
- Device profiles (`DEVP`): iFi xDSD Gryphon (also matches the old "griffin" spelling and the generic iFi USB name), Chord (Mojo, Mojo 2, Hugo 2, Qutest), Topping, FiiO, RME ADI-2; Focusrite Scarlett (channels read from the name, for example 18i20), Zoom AMS-22/24/44, M-Audio, Universal Audio Volt, MOTU M2/M4/M6, Audient, RME, PreSonus, Behringer. Unknown devices get a general DAC or interface profile.

Screenshots of every new window were looked at.
