### Research ("unconfirmed" = not found on a page that was read)
DACs, all 2 channels out
- iFi xDSD Gryphon: 44.1 to 768 kHz, 32 bit, DSD512. Filters Bit-Perfect, Standard, GTO. USB name unconfirmed (probably "iFi (by AMR) HD USB Audio").
- Chord Mojo 2, Hugo 2, Qutest: up to 768 kHz, 32 bit. Hugo 2 driverless on Mac (stated). Qutest has no volume (fixed 1, 2 or 3 V). Mojo (first): 768 kHz unconfirmed.
- Topping: D10s up to 384 kHz, E30 II and D90 up to 768 kHz; DX3 Pro+ and E50 unconfirmed.
- FiiO: K5 Pro ESS up to 768 kHz, K9 Pro ESS up to 384 kHz; K7, KA3, BTR5 unconfirmed.
- RME ADI-2 DAC FS and ADI-2 Pro FS R: up to 768 kHz, class compliant, 2 in 2 out in stereo mode.
Interfaces, 24 bit unless noted
- Focusrite Scarlett 3rd and 4th gen: 44.1 to 192 kHz; channels follow the name (2i2 = 2 in 2 out, 18i20 and so on); 3rd gen plug and play on Mac.
- M-Audio M-Track Solo and Duo: 44.1 and 48 kHz only, 16 bit, 2x2. AIR 192|4: up to 192 kHz, 2x2.
- Zoom AMS-22 / 24 / 44: 44.1 to 96 kHz, 2x2 / 2x4 / 4x4. Use Music mode for DAW work (Streaming mode sums to stereo). Direct monitor and loopback are switches on the unit.
- Universal Audio Volt: 44.1 to 192 kHz, class compliant; 276 is 2x2 (others unconfirmed).
- MOTU M2 / M4 / M6: 44.1 to 192 kHz, 2x2 / 4x4 / 6x4, plug and play on Mac; loopback needs MOTU's driver.
- Audient iD4 MkII, iD14 MkII, EVO 4, EVO 8: up to 96 kHz; 2x2, 10x6, 2x2, 4x4.
- RME Babyface Pro FS (12x12) and Fireface UCX II: 44.1 to 192 kHz.
- PreSonus Studio 24c: up to 192 kHz, 2x2. AudioBox USB 96: 96 kHz unconfirmed.
- Behringer UMC202HD / 204HD / 404HD: up to 192 kHz (204HD confirmed, 2x4), no driver on Mac. UMC22 unconfirmed.
Platform facts
- macOS: any app may set a device's sample rate (Apple QA1196). Exclusive "hog" mode belongs to one process; a Chromium or Electron app cannot get it or WASAPI exclusive mode through Web Audio.
- Bit-perfect in audiophile players means: exclusive access, device rate switched to the material's rate, device bit depth at least the material's, no software volume or processing, nothing else mixed in.
- Buffer: as small as the computer manages without clicks for recording, larger for mixing (Sound On Sound, Focusrite); the usual 64 to 256 and 512 to 2048 samples are common practice, not from a page that was read.
Sources: ifi-audio.com/collections/all/products/xdsd-gryphon · richersounds.com/content/product_files/Qutest_User_manual_2_1.pdf · audioadvisor.com (Chord Hugo 2, Mojo 2) · techpowerup.com/review/topping-e30-ii-dac-l30-ii-amplifier-desktop-stack/4.html · apos.audio/blogs/news/topping-l30-vs-d30-pro-vs-d90-comparison-chart · fiio.com/k5proess_parameters · fiio.com/k9proess_parameters · rme-audio.de/downloads/adi2dacr_e.pdf · rme-audio.de/adi-2-pro-fs.html · rme-audio.de/babyface-pro-fs.html · rme-audio.de/fireface-ucx-ii.html · us.focusrite.com/scarlett-in-depth · userguides.focusrite.com (Scarlett 8i6 3rd Gen) · soundonsound.com/reviews/m-audio-m-track-solo-duo · m-audio.com/audio-midi-interfaces/air-192-4.html · zoomcorp.com (AMS series) · soundonsound.com/reviews/zoom-ams-22-24-44 · uaudio.com/products/volt-276 · motu.com/de/news/introducing-the-m2-and-m4 · soundonsound.com/reviews/audient-id4-id14-mkii · soundonsound.com/reviews/audient-evo-4-evo-8 · fender.com/studio-24c/2777700403.html · behringer.com/en/products/0805-aas · developer.apple.com/library/content/qa/qa1196/_index.html · audirvana.com/exclusive-playback-technology · learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/CoreAudio/exclusive-mode-streams · soundonsound.com/sound-advice/q-what-buffer-size-should-use

## 25 November 2026: v0.173, run 18 (three small things left from run 8)

None of the three was done before this run (checked in the log and the commits; latest was 86810d5, v0.172).

### 1. Mixer: the volume numbers under the faders were empty after a redraw
- **Cause:** nothing in `renderMixer` wrote them. The only place that filled `#mixWin output[data-o]` was the meter loop `drawMixer`, on every 8th of its turns (about every 16th frame), so the numbers appeared a moment later, and later still on a slow computer.
- **Fix:** new `mxNums(w)` writes all of them (and only touches a number whose text changed). It runs at the start of every `mxFit` (new outermost wrapper, so before anything is measured) and at the end of `renderMixer` (new outermost wrapper). `drawMixer` now calls the same function instead of its own loop, so it no longer rewrites 31 unchanged texts several times a second.
- **Second cause found while checking the fader height (since v0.170):** with animations switched off (`body.fxstill`, which is also what Light mode gives) every element gets `transition-duration:.01ms`. Even that short a transition keeps the old size until the next frame, so `mxFit` and `mxsFit` set a new fader height and then measured the old one in the same instant. Result with the cable lane on and 30 tracks: faders 136 px right after a redraw, 121 px after the next fit (a 15 px jump on any resize), and the master's extra read-outs dropped as "too tight" although they fit. New CSS: `body.fxstill #mixWin .mxs` and everything in it has `transition:none`. With that the mixer gets the same sizes as with full effects.
- **Fader height and cable lane, before and after** (Fractals demo, 1600 x 1000, `tools/ui-tests/mixnums.py`): with full effects nothing moved by a pixel: faders 94 px (82 px with 31 tracks), lane 64 px (76 px), strip 396 px (384 px), and 156 px / 452 px with cables off. In cable mode the empty number was already 15 px high (`min-height`), so filling it does not change what is measured; the 12 px spare in the cable wrapper of `mxFit` was left as it is. With cables off the empty number was 4 px instead of 15 px while measured; at this window size the faders are at their 156 px limit either way.

### 2. Settings search finds "Reset all settings"
- In the search block (`build`): the button `#genReset` gets its own entry (`tab:''`, `self:1`, shown as "Reset all settings…" with "Bottom of Settings" under it; extra words: restore, defaults, factory, start over, clear, and its tooltip). The dead `#genReset` in the per-page selector was removed.
- `go`: an entry without a tab does not switch tabs, and with `self` the button itself gets `sflash` (not a parent row).
- Also: the small second line of the picked result was grey on the solid blue row and hard to read; it is now white (`#setQR button.on small`).

### 3. Edit button on the Transport block of the layout picture
- `miniEditBtn()` puts a small button (pencil and "Edit", class `mbedit`) on `#mini .mb.k-tr`, between the block's label and its meters. `renderMini` has a wrapper that calls it, so it comes back whenever the block is made again. `pointerdown` on the button is stopped so the block is not dragged; a click calls `trEditOpen()`. The "Edit…" button in the Transport bar row below is unchanged.

### Tested (headless, no page errors in any run)
- `tools/ui-tests/mixnums.py` (new): opening the mixer, `renderMixer`, selecting a track and the master, adding and removing a track, undo twice, compact on and off, changed fader values; with cables on and off; with animations off (the headless default) and with full effects. 100 readings taken in the same instant as the action and again later: no empty and no wrong number, and a second `mxFit` never changed a size. The same script on v0.172 shows the empty numbers and the 136 to 121 px jump.
- `tools/ui-tests/setfind.py` (new): "reset", "reset all", "all settings", "factory" find the button; choosing it from the Audio and the Interface tab keeps the tab and flashes the button (flash gone after 2 s); Enter on the result does the same and does not press the button; another result ("buffer") still switches and flashes its row. Edit button: one button, 43 x 16 px inside the 20 px block, overlaps no other part of the block; still one after three `renderMini` calls and after dragging the block to the top and back; pressing on it and pulling up starts no drag; a click opens the transport editor, Escape closes it; the row button still opens it too. Screenshots of the picture (normal, while dragging, hover), the search list and the flashing button were looked at.
- `cables2.py`: same output as v0.172. `mixright.py`: same output, except that the line read 0.4 s after moving the window sometimes already shows the mixer regrown to the left edge (l 8, w 1402), which v0.172 shows one line later; the end state is the same.

## 25 November 2026: v0.174, run 13: particle devices, part 2 (Emit, a granular instrument)

### Research (what Ableton's Emit does)
Read: Top Music Arts "Sound Design with Inspired by Nature: Emit" (topmusicarts.com/blogs/news/sound-design-with-inspired-by-nature-emit), Attack Magazine "Getting freaky with Live 11's new Inspired by Nature devices", Ableton's pack page (ableton.com/en/packs/inspired-nature). The Live manual does not cover the pack; the pack's own lesson text was not reachable.
- Confirmed by those pages: the picture is a spectrogram (time left to right, pitch bottom to top). An emitter is a box you move and resize; particles are born at a random spot inside it and play the part of the sample they pass over. Speed does not change the pitch (Ableton uses a phase vocoder). Height drives a band filter, or the pan (Filter / Pan switch). Edges bounce or wrap. Angle, Width (range of angles), Bipolar (both directions; a particle moving left plays backwards), Rate, Lifetime, Burst (particles per shot), Spread (how far apart the burst is spaced over the interval), Grains (most particles), Force (start speed), Friction, Pitch in semitones, Blur (spectral smear), Filter width and Q, two emitters, two LFOs and envelopes routed to parameters, an LFO on Angle makes the emitter rotate, notes can set the pitch or only the rhythm.
- **Guesses** (no page gives ranges, units or defaults): every range and default; that Force 0 leaves particles standing; what Glide does (here: how slowly the sound follows its particle); that "Filter width and Q" is a high cut and a low cut that far apart with Q as their resonance; the frequency scale (40 Hz to 16 kHz, logarithmic); the small random pan per particle in Filter mode; that Grains is counted per emitter and per note and that a full emitter simply stops making new ones; LFO shapes and that they restart with every note. Not built from Ableton: walls, the "F" fine tune button, running without notes while the song plays.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`, names start with `em` / `EM`; `emInit();` sits right after `grwInit();`)
- **Emit** (`PRESETS.emit`, in the instrument browser next to the Sampler). One worklet engine per track, `al-emit` (module string `EWL`, added to the two module lists in `wlLoad`).
- `emCore()` is the physics (emission, bursts, movement sources, friction, bounce or wrap), seeded with its own generator. Its source text is put into the worklet (`EWL = ... emCore.toString() ...`), and the same function runs on the main thread for the preview, so there is only one copy of the physics. It steps every 64 samples counted from the start of the note, so the result does not depend on where a note falls in an audio block. Seed = Seed knob and the key: the same key always gives the same particles.
- Sound of a particle: a stream of overlapping short pieces of the sample read at the particle's place (60 ms, longer with Blur, each start slightly randomised so a standing particle does not buzz), so speed never changes pitch; moving left reads backwards. Then a high cut and a low cut around its height (Filter) or pan by height (Pan). 12 ms fade in, fade out at the end of its life. A particle that wraps round an edge fades out, jumps and fades in (without this the filter jumped from top to bottom with a loud click: peak 1.0 before, 0.23 after). Limits: 12 notes, 192 particles per track.
- Notes: `emPlay` (clip notes, export, freeze), a `voiceOn` wrapper (held keys), a `synth0` wrapper, `pvAny` now also covers Emit. Particles alive at note end live out their time; `I.r` is kept at a third of the longest Lifetime so exports leave enough tail (`emTail`). Knob and picture changes reach sounding notes at once (`emPush`).
- Controls (Ableton's names): per emitter Angle, Width, Force, Friction, Rate, Burst, Spread, Grains, Lifetime, Pitch, Glide, Blur, Flt width, Q, Level, On, Bipolar, Height sets Filter / Pan. Tab Movement: two sources (sine, triangle, ramp up, ramp down, square, random steps, fall once, rise once) with Rate, Amount, target (angle, width, force, friction, rate, lifetime, pitch, filter width, left and right, up and down) and emitter 1, 2 or both. Tab Sound: sample (the Sampler's built-in ones, loaded audio, Load a file, or drop a file on the picture), Sides and Top and bottom as Bounce / Wrap, Keys change pitch, Seed, Level. Tooltips in plain language (`EMTIP`).
- Picture (`emDraw`, `emSpec`): spectrogram worked out once per sample (320 by 96, cached), emitters as boxes with a fan for angle and width; drag a box to move it, its corner to resize it, the white dot to aim it; a click on empty space plays a note. While sound plays the worklet posts its particles 30 times a second; otherwise a preview of the same physics runs. The device uses the particle look (`pbx`): picture over the whole device, controls floating on the right; with parameters hidden the picture is full width.
- **10 presets** (`EMPRE`): Sprinkler in the centre (the rotating emitter the user remembered: ramp on Angle), Two sprinklers, Slow scan, Still cloud, Falling rain, Pinball, Fireworks, Two ways, Side to side, Breathing out.

### Tested
- Headless load: no page errors. `tools/engine-tests/emit.py`: all 10 presets, three notes: no NaN, RMS between -28 and -19 dB, peaks under 0.7 except Pinball (up to 1.15 in one run, its Level was then lowered from 60% to 25%: RMS -24 dB, peak 0.48), every preset bit-identical in two renders; seed 2 differs from seed 1; an octave higher key doubles the spectral centre (1587 to 3038 Hz), with Keys change pitch off it stays (1547 Hz). Heaviest case (two emitters, 48 grains each, three notes): 4 s of sound in 1.2 s including the app's own overhead.
- `tools/ui-tests/emit.py`: no control outside the device; moving, resizing and aiming the emitter with the mouse change the right values, the Angle knob follows, three undos bring everything back; while the song plays the picture shows the engine's own particles and the rotating angle; a held key makes particles and they are gone after release; all tabs, buttons and selects; adding Emit from the instrument browser. Screenshots looked at: stopped, playing, parameters hidden, each tab, the browser card, a noise sample.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo); Bouncy Notes test gives the same numbers; the Sampler's grain mode still plays.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it**, and it was not compared with Ableton's device (no access). The presets were set by measurement.
- Blur is a smear made by longer, more scattered pieces, not Ableton's spectral blur. The grain method is not a phase vocoder: a standing particle sounds grainier than Ableton's.
- The built-in samples (choir, glass, rain) are made with the app's shared random generator, so their fine detail depends on what else was made before them in a session (as in the Sampler). Levels of the same preset therefore differ a little between sessions (Pinball: 0.108 and 0.157 RMS at the same settings in two test runs). Within a session renders are bit-identical.
- CPU on the user's Mac, real mouse or trackpad, dropping a real file on the picture (the function is the Sampler's with another message), MIDI learn and automation of the knobs, clips with pitch bend (they take a slower path with one engine per note).
- The preview uses key C3; live and export are the same for the same seed and key, but a note played by hand never starts at exactly the same moment relative to another, so chords differ in their overlap, not in each note.
- Found on the way, not changed for other devices: with animations off, `dfFit` measured stale sizes and made the side column of this device 800 px wide. Emit now pins its column width; Bouncy Notes looked right in its test but other devices may be affected.

### Left
- Vector Delay (task 12, not in the log yet), Vector Grain, Vector FM, Vector Map and the walls for Bouncy Notes.
- For Emit: tune presets by ear, tempo-synced Rate and movement, fine tune, an option to run without notes.

## 25 November 2026: v0.175, run 14: particle devices, part 3 (Vector Grain, an instrument, and the shared particle field)

### Research (what Ableton's Vector Grain does)
Read: Top Music Arts "Sound Design with Inspired by Nature: Vector Grain & Vector Delay" (topmusicarts.com/blogs/news/sound-design-with-inspired-by-nature-vector-grain), Ableton's pack page (ableton.com/en/packs/inspired-nature), Ableton's blog "World of Sound: Explore Live 11's Inspired by Nature Devices" (does not cover Vector Grain). The Live manual does not cover the pack.
- Confirmed by those pages: a granular looper; every particle of the shared field is one copy of the sample looping. Left: Scale (scales the positions, so everything they drive swings further), Speed (speed of the physics), Gain (output). Right: an ADSR. Top: Grains (most particles; in MIDI mode one per note, up to 16) and Lock (nothing plays unless the transport runs). Grain tab: Start, Size (milliseconds or synced note values), Fade, Fade Center (left: the loop fades out, right: it fades in), Pan, Gain, Reverse, and five knobs for particle values (horizontal position, vertical position, distance from centre, speed, mass), each with a dropdown for what it drives. Particle tab: Mass and Rnd > Mass. Particles move by gravity and magnetism, or through a flow field.
- **Guesses** (no page gives them): every range, unit and default; the physics formulas and the knobs Gravity, Magnet, Center, Drag, Flow, Detail, Drift and Start speed (the pages only name gravity, magnetism and flow field); what the X and Y bars look like (here: two bars with two ends each); the list of things a particle value can drive (start, size, pan, pitch, gain, fade, reverse) and how far (start: the whole sample, size: 3 octaves, pitch: 24 semitones, gain: to zero or double); that start, size, fade and reverse are taken over once per pass through the loop while pitch, pan and gain follow all the time; that a loop running past the end of the sample continues at its beginning; a smallest fade of 2 ms at both ends so a loop never clicks; that Lock only stops keys played by hand (clip notes only exist while the song plays anyway). Not built from Ableton: live recording into the sample, the Emit mode the article mentions in passing, walls.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`; names start with `vf` for the shared field and `vg` / `VG` for Vector Grain; `vgInit();` sits right after `emInit();`)
- Vector Delay (task 12) is still not in the repo, so there was no field code to reuse. **`vfCore()` is now the shared particle field for all Vector devices**: 16 slots, a square from -1 to 1, `field(seed)`, `step(F,P,dt)`, `attr(F,P,slot,out)` (the five values: left-right, up-down, distance, speed, mass). Two kinds of motion: Forces (pull between particles by mass, magnets with every second particle the other pole, pull to the middle of the range, drag) and Flow (a stream made of three slow sine waves, seeded). The range `x0 x1 y0 y1` is a box with bouncing walls. `P.pn` chooses between "the first n slots" and "one slot per held note" (`F.ref`). The next Vector devices should put `vfCore.toString()` into their worklet the same way and can copy the field part of `vgDraw`.
- **Vector Grain** (`PRESETS.vgrain`, in the instrument browser after Emit). One worklet per track, `al-vgrain` (module string `VGWL`, added to both module lists in `wlLoad`; `pvAny` covers it). All notes of a track share one field. The field starts again from its seed when a note begins while nothing sounds, and steps every 64 samples counted from that note, so the result does not depend on where a note falls in an audio block.
- Sound: every note plays all particles (Grains, 1 to 16) or, with One per key, gets one particle of its own (the oldest note gives way after 16). A grain loops its piece with a smooth fade in and out; ADSR per note; level divided by the square root of the number of grains. Limits: 24 notes.
- Main side: `VGP` (knobs), `vgPar`, `vgSendP`, `vgPlay` (clip notes, export, freeze), wrappers on `synth0`, `voiceOn` (held keys) and `startRendering`, `vgPush` (changes reach sounding notes at once), `vgTail` (sets `I.r` from Release for export tails), `vgFile` (load or drop a file). Sample handling is Emit's and the Sampler's (`sampleBuf`, `emMono`).
- Controls: always visible Scale, Speed, Gain, Grains, Attack, Decay, Sustain, Release. Tab Grain: One per key, Reverse, Sync (with a note length), Start, Size, Fade, Fade center, Pan, Gain, Pitch. Tab Vectors: X, Y, Distance, Speed, Mass, each with a choice under it. Tab Particles: Motion Forces / Flow, Mass, Rnd > Mass, Gravity, Magnet, Center, Drag (or Flow, Detail, Drift, Drag), Start speed, Seed. Tab Sample: sample, Keys change pitch, Lock. Tooltips in plain language (`VGTIP`).
- Picture (`vgDraw`, `vgGeo`, `vgWave`): black field over the whole device with rings for the distance, the range box, particles with trails (size shows mass, one colour per particle), the X bar under the field and the Y bar on its left (drag an end, or the middle to move the range), and a strip with the outline of the sample where every particle's looped piece is marked in its colour. Live it shows the worklet's own particles (30 times a second), otherwise a preview of the same physics.
- **9 presets** (`VGPRE`): Orbits, Swarm, Magnets, River (flow), One particle per key, Glass stutter (synced), Backwards cloud, Frozen (speed 0), Drifting pitch.

### Tested
- Headless load: no page errors. `tools/engine-tests/vector_grain.py`: all 9 presets, four notes: no NaN, RMS -37 to -23 dB over 12 s (half of it silence), peaks under 0.6, every preset bit-identical in two renders, tails end; seed 2 differs from seed 1; a note moved by 293 samples gives exactly the same sound (difference 0); an octave higher key doubles the spectral centre (1400 to 2744 Hz), with Keys change pitch off it stays; X on Pan gives a left-right difference, X at 0 gives none; Reverse changes the sound; 20 keys at once with One per key works; 16 grains and six notes: 4 s of sound in 0.35 s.
- `tools/ui-tests/vector_grain.py`: no control outside the device in any tab; dragging both bar ends and the middle changes the right values, three undos bring them back; the song playing shows 6 live particles; a held key shows particles and none after release; Lock stops a held key while the song is stopped; three held keys with One per key give three particles; it is in the instrument browser. Screenshots looked at: stopped, playing, parameters hidden, Vectors tab, Flow.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo); the Emit test still passes (all presets equal in two renders).

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it**, and it was not compared with Ableton's device (no access). Presets were set by measurement and by looking at the picture. River is quiet (-37 dB, the rain sample is quiet). 20 keys at once in the One per key preset peaked at 1.17.
- Because all notes share one field, a note's sound depends on the notes before it in the same phrase: live from the middle of a song can differ from an export from the start. Export twice is identical.
- Sync uses the song tempo at the moment a note is sent; tempo changes inside a held loop are not followed. Pitch driven by a particle glides freely, it is not snapped to a scale.
- CPU on the user's Mac, real mouse or trackpad, dropping a real file, MIDI learn and automation of the knobs, clips with pitch bend, the small picture in the instrument browser (code path written, not looked at).

### Left
- Vector Delay (task 12, still missing), Vector FM, Vector Map, walls for Bouncy Notes. They can now build on `vfCore`.
- For Vector Grain: tune presets by ear, live recording into the sample, a scale for particle-driven pitch.

## 25 November 2026: v0.176, run 15: particle devices, part 4 (Vector FM, an instrument)

### Research (what Ableton's Vector FM does)
Read: Dillon Bastan's own Vector FM manual (dillonbastan.com/inspiredbynature_manuals/Vector%20FM%20User%20Manual.pdf), Top Music Arts "Sound Design with Inspired by Nature, part 2" (topmusicarts.com/blogs/news/sound-design-with-inspired-by-nature-part-2), Attack Magazine "Getting freaky with Live 11's new Inspired by Nature devices", Ableton's pack page (ableton.com/en/packs/inspired-nature). The pages were read through summaries, not word for word.
- Confirmed by those pages: a "granular FM instrument"; a 2D particle system drives the FM. Voices = number of particles (0 = MIDI mode: notes make and tune particles, and an ADSR appears). Lock (only runs with the transport), Scale, Speed, Gain. A menu sets operators per chain and the algorithm: at 1 every particle is a modulator and carrier pair, above 1 every particle is one operator and the algorithm joins them (12 voices with 3 operators = 4 chains). Carrier: wave, tuning in Hz or as a note, scale quantising when modulated, keys can tune it. Modulator: wave, ratio free or whole numbers ("N"), index free or "x10". Pan, gain and a filter per voice (several types). Five particle values (X, Y, distance from centre, speed, mass) each with an amount knob and a target menu; the examples name pan and filter cutoff. White rings mark carriers and white lines show the signal flow. Particles: Mass, Rnd > Mass, attractor, magnetism, flow field.
- **Guesses** (no page gives them): every range, unit and default; the full list of targets (here: Ratio, Index, Pan, Gain, Detune, Cutoff, Pitch) and how far each goes at 100% (ratio 2 octaves, index 6, detune 100 cents, cutoff 4 octaves, pitch 24 semitones, gain to zero or double); the names and wiring of the algorithms (Stack, Fan in, Fan out); that "x10" means ten times stronger; the four waves; that a modulator's Gain target scales how hard it modulates; "Env > Index" (ours, not in Ableton: the envelope also shapes the index, needed for bells and electric pianos). The physics knobs are Vector Grain's (see v0.175).
- Not built from Ableton: Voices 0 as the switch for MIDI mode (here the button "One per key", as in Vector Grain), carrier or modulator tuned in Hz, Scala files, the ladder filters and the LP-HP type, charge and the charge filters, Edge modes Clip and Wrap, the position offset sliders, drawing the flow field by mouse, Spawn Area, Save state, the Emitter section.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`; names start with `vfm` / `VFM`; `vfmInit();` sits right after `vgInit();`)
- **Vector FM** (`PRESETS.vfm`, in the instrument browser in Synths after the wavetable synth). One worklet per track, `al-vfm` (module string `VFMWL`, added to both module lists in `wlLoad`; `pvAny` covers it). It uses the shared field `vfCore()` unchanged (its source text goes into the worklet, as in Vector Grain). All notes of a track share one field; it starts again from its seed when a note begins while nothing sounds and steps every 64 samples counted from that note.
- `vfmCore()`: `calc(P,values,out)` turns the five values of a particle into pitch factor, ratio, index, pan, gain and cutoff; `car(k,n,L,alg)` says whether operator k is heard or only bends another. The worklet and the picture both use it, so there is one copy.
- Sound: wave tables (sine, triangle, saw, square; three versions with 48, 16 and 5 overtones, chosen by the frequency of the oscillator). Operators 1: every particle is a pair (modulator bends carrier). Operators 2 to 4: particles are joined into chains; Stack (each bends the next, the first is heard), Fan in (all bend the first), Fan out (the last bends all the others, which are heard). Every carrier has its own filter (none, low pass, band pass, high pass, notch) and pan. ADSR per note; level divided by the square root of the number of carriers; start phases come from a fixed hash per particle so equal oscillators do not add up to one loud tone. Index, pan and gain glide between steps. Limits: 24 notes, 16 particles; with One per key every note gets one particle (the oldest note gives way after 16).
- Main side: `VFMP` (knobs), `vfmPar`, `vfmSendP`, `vfmPlay` (clip notes, export, freeze), wrappers on `synth0`, `voiceOn` (held keys) and `startRendering`, `vfmPush` (changes reach sounding notes at once), `vfmTail` (sets `I.r` from Release for export tails).
- Controls: always visible Scale, Speed, Gain, Voices, Attack, Decay, Sustain, Release. Tab Operators: One per key, Operators 1 to 4, Algorithm, Pitch (with the carrier wave under it), Fine, Ratio (N under it), Index (x10 under it), Env > Index (modulator wave under it). Tab Vectors: X, Y, Distance, Speed, Mass, each with a choice under it. Tab Particles: as in Vector Grain. Tab Output: Keys change pitch, Lock, Pitch steps (free, semitones or a scale that starts at the played key), Cutoff (filter type under it), Q, Pan, Gain. Tooltips in plain language (`VFMTIP`).
- Picture (`vfmDraw`, `vfmGeo`): the field of Vector Grain in violet with the X and Y range bars; carriers have a white ring, white lines join a modulator to what it bends, a pair shows a ring that grows with the index. The strip at the bottom shows every carrier with its overtones on a frequency scale from 30 Hz to 16 kHz (`vfmBes`). Live it shows the worklet's own particles (30 times a second), otherwise a preview of the same physics as if C3 were held.
- **10 presets** (`VFMPRE`): Glass bells, Tower bell, Moving pad, Overtone drift (flow, whole-number ratios driven by height), One particle per key (electric piano), Stacked organ (3 operators, stack), Metal swarm (4 operators, fan in, magnets), Slow drone (2 operators, fan out, band pass, flow), Wobble bass, Scale chimes (pitch driven by height, major pentatonic).

### Tested
- Headless load at 0.176: no page errors. `tools/engine-tests/vector_fm.py`: all 10 presets, four notes: no NaN, RMS -28 to -21 dB while notes sound, peaks 0.26 to 0.40, every preset bit-identical in two renders, tails end; seed 2 differs from seed 1; a note moved by 293 samples gives exactly the same sound (difference 0). Tuning: with Index 0 a plain sine at 440.0 Hz for A3, 220.0 for A2, 261.6 with Keys change pitch off, everything else 127 dB lower. Ratio 2 gives parts at 220, 660, 1100, 1540 Hz, ratio 3 at 220, 440, 880, 1100, and 2.6 with N equals 3 exactly. X on Pan gives a left-right difference, X at 0 none; each of the six other targets changes the sound without NaN; pitch with a pentatonic scale lands on a scale note (-5 semitones). All operator counts and algorithms, all four waves at a high and a low key, all four filters (the right part of the spectrum goes), extreme settings (x10, ratio 16, Q 12, 16 voices), 20 keys with One per key (peak 0.87). Heaviest case (16 pairs with filters, six notes): 4 s of sound in 0.9 s including the app's own overhead.
- `tools/ui-tests/vector_fm.py`: no control outside the device in any tab, with 3 operators, or with One per key; the algorithm choice appears above 1 operator; dragging both bar ends and the middle changes the right values and three undos bring them back; the song playing shows 5 live particles; a held key shows particles and none after release; Lock stops a held key while the song is stopped; three held keys with One per key give three particles; it is in the instrument browser. Screenshots looked at: stopped, playing, parameters hidden, chains with Fan in and Fan out, Output tab.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo); the Vector Grain test still gives bit-identical renders for all presets.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it**, and it was not compared with Ableton's device (no access). Presets were set by measurement and by looking at the picture.
- Saw and square tables are limited to a fixed number of overtones, but strong FM (high Index, x10, high keys) still folds back above the top of the hearing range as in any simple FM synth; no oversampling.
- As in Vector Grain, all notes share one field, so a note's sound depends on the notes before it in the same phrase: live from the middle of a song can differ from an export from the start. Export twice is identical.
- A held key with Sustain at 0 keeps its oscillators running silently until the key is let go (costs CPU, no sound).
- CPU on the user's Mac, real mouse or trackpad, MIDI learn and automation of the knobs, clips with pitch bend, the small picture in the instrument browser (code path written, not looked at).

### Left
- Vector Delay (task 12, still missing), Vector Map, walls for Bouncy Notes.
- For Vector FM: tune presets by ear, the parts of Ableton's device listed under "Not built" (charge, edge modes, position offsets, drawn flow field, emitter, Hz tuning), feedback on an operator.

## 25 November 2026: v0.177, run 16: particle devices, part 5 (Vector Map, a modulator; walls for Bouncy Notes)

### Research (what Ableton's Vector Map does)
Read: Ableton's pack page (ableton.com/en/packs/inspired-nature). Not reachable in this run: Top Music Arts "Sound Design with Inspired by Nature, part 2" and Dillon Bastan's manuals (both refused by the fetch tool), the Live manual does not cover the pack. Ableton's blog "Dillon Bastan: Water Ripples and Sound Waves" was read and does not mention Vector Map.
- Confirmed by the pack page: a modulation source with no sound of its own; each particle's motion is mapped to any automatable parameter; up to 8 destinations; the attributes it names are size, direction and velocity. The particles move by gravity and magnetism or through a flow field (the field of the other Vector devices).
- **Guesses** (no page gives them): every range and default; that an output is one value of one chosen particle; the list of values (here X, Y, Distance, Speed, Direction; "size" is left out because the mass of a particle never changes, so it would not move anything); how the depth works (here the depth chip of the app's other modulators, -100% to +100%, instead of a minimum and maximum per mapping); that the movement follows the song time. The physics knobs are Vector Grain's (see v0.175).

### How the app's modulators run (found out for this task)
- Modulators (`t.mods`, LFO, Envelope, Sidechain, Steps) are worked out on every screen frame in `modTick` and written into the stored value of the control (`PAREG` entry, `e.set`), on top of a base value kept on the route (`r.b`). The sound engine then picks the value up like a knob turned by hand.
- **Before this version no modulator reached an export, freeze or bounce**: `renderMix` reads every setting once when the render starts, so a control stayed wherever the modulator had left it at that moment. The same is true for parameter automation lanes (`S.pauto`): they are applied by `paApply` on screen frames only.
- Vector Map is the first modulator that is rendered (see below). LFO, Envelope, Sidechain and Steps still are not; a synced LFO and Steps could use the same hook (`vmOffPlan`) later, the random shapes and the free-running LFO would first need a clock that follows the song.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`; names start with `vm` / `VM`; `vmInit();` sits right after `vfmInit();`)
- **Vector Map** (`MODT.vmap`, in the "Add a modulator" menu). Up to 8 outputs; an output is one value of one particle and goes to a control with the usual target picker ("＋ Add an output", then click a knob). Per output: the particle (1 to 16, numbers are drawn in the picture), the value (X, Y, Distance, Speed, Direction), the depth chip (drag, double-click flips, ✕ removes) and a small live meter. Stored on the route as `r.pt` and `r.at` next to `pid` and `d`.
- The field is the shared `vfCore()`, unchanged. While the song plays, `vmAt(m,T,P)` gives the field at song time T (seconds, from `secOf(beat)`): always stepped from the start of the song in steps of 1/60 s, with a check point every 5 s so jumping back is cheap. Nothing but the time, the settings and the seed goes in, so playing, looping, jumping and exporting agree. While the song is stopped the field runs freely with the clock (`vmFree`) so it can be watched and set up. Changing a knob while the song plays works the field out again from the start with the new settings (10 minutes of song with 16 particles take about 140 ms once).
- `modTick` was replaced by a copy in which a Vector Map route has a value of its own (`vmOut`); `modNew`, `modCard`, `modColHtml`, `modPaint` and `modGrid` have wrappers. The other modulator types run as before.
- Controls: presets (6: Orbits, Swarm, Magnets, River, Slow drift, Wide circles; all keep moving for at least 10 minutes), tab Outputs, tab Particles (Motion Forces / Flow, Particles, Speed, Scale, Mass, Rnd > Mass, Gravity, Magnet, Center, Drag or Flow, Detail, Drift, Drag, Start speed, Seed), on / off, remove. The two range bars under and left of the field can be dragged. Tooltips in plain language (`VMTIP`, `VMATT`).
- Look: the card is one black picture with the field on the left and the controls floating on the right (`vmCard`, `vmPaint`, CSS block "Vector Map"). The modulators column gets 548 px wide while a Vector Map is in it; next to other modulators the card takes two columns and both rows of their grid. **`vfPaint()` is a shared painter for the field** (rings, range box, the two bars, particles with trails, numbers), written after the picture of Vector Grain; Vector Grain and Vector FM still use their own copies of the same drawing.
- **Export, freeze and bounce** (`vmOffPlan`, called from a `startRendering` wrapper; `quietMeters`, `mkFxNode` and `synthB` have wrappers, and `renderMix` got one added call, `vmOffTrk`, that hands over the volume and pan nodes of a track): track volume and pan get a value curve (one point every 1024 samples); effect settings are set again every 1024 samples while the render is paused for a moment (works for built-in nodes and for worklet effects); instrument settings are set for the moment each note starts and put back afterwards.
- **Bouncy Notes: walls.** Drag in the picture to draw a wall (up to 8), click a wall to remove it. A ball that crosses a wall is pushed sideways towards the lower end of the wall (an arrow shows the side; a flat wall pushes away from its middle). The up and down movement is not changed, so the times and loudness of the bounces stay and only the keys change (`bnSeg` adds a sideways path `b.sg` to every ball, `bnX` follows it, `bnBalls` has a wrapper, `bnWallDraw`, `bnWallHit`; walls are stored in `f.p.walls` as [key, height, key, height] and are kept when a preset is chosen).
- **Fixes to the earlier particle devices**: (1) Bouncy Notes had lost its look with animations off or Light mode on: the automatic fit made its controls 780 px wide so they covered the picture (the problem noted in the v0.174 entry). Particle effects now keep their controls at 444 px. (2) The small pictures of Vector Grain and Vector FM in the instrument browser were empty for an instrument that is not selected (a picture drawn only once never made its particles); they now show the particles.
- Same look checked for all four devices (Bouncy Notes, Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM): class `pbx`, background #05060a, picture as large as the device.

### Tested (headless, no page errors in any run)
- `tools/engine-tests/vector_map.py` (saw track, 16 s): without a modulator left and right are equal and the level is steady. Pan from X: the left-right balance moves between -4.7 and +2.7 dB over the render; volume from Distance: level moves between -31 and -43 dB; cutoff of the Low-pass filter from Y: spectral centre moves between 520 and 2174 Hz (1220 Hz without); bit crusher (a worklet) from X and Y: centre moves between 2711 and 5406 Hz (2900 without); cutoff of the instrument from X: 1251 to 3340 Hz (2180 without). Every case: no NaN, peaks under 0.31, two renders bit-identical (three for the two effect cases), seed 2 differs from seed 1. The second half rendered alone has the same balance as the second half of the whole render. The instrument's settings are back after the export. The field at 7.3 s is the same after jumping to 60 s and 0.1 s and after throwing the check points away.
- `tools/ui-tests/vector_map.py`: no control outside the card; adding outputs with the target picker until 8 (then the button goes); particle and value selects; tabs; Forces / Flow; preset keeps the outputs; a knob of the card; both range bars and undo; depth chip; with the song playing in a loop the pan was sampled 129 times over 3 loop passes and was every time exactly the value the export would use at that song time (difference 0); on / off; remove. LFO, Steps and Vector Map together on one track still move their targets. The modulator and its outputs are in the project data (JSON).
- `tools/engine-tests/bouncy_walls.py`: walls change the keys but not the times or the loudness of the notes; a wall leaning right sends the ball up the keyboard, leaning left down; a wall above the drop height changes nothing; the Scatter preset with three walls stays in its scale and gives the same notes twice; render not silent, no NaN, bit-identical twice. `tools/ui-tests/particles_look.py`: two walls drawn with the mouse, a click removes one, undo brings it back; screenshots of all four devices and of the instrument browser.
- Screenshots looked at: Vector Map empty, with outputs, Particles tab, Flow, playing, 8 outputs, next to an LFO and Steps; Bouncy Notes stopped, playing and parameters hidden with walls; Vector FM device; the small pictures of Vector Grain and Vector FM in the instrument browser.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo); the Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM and Bouncy Notes tests give the same results as before (all presets bit-identical in two renders); the UI tests of Emit, Vector Grain and Vector FM pass.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it or tried it with a real mouse**, and it was not compared with Ableton's device (no access, and only the pack page could be read this run).
- Export of effect settings pauses the render every 1024 samples; a 16 s render took 0.7 s here, but a long song with many modulated effects was not timed. If the progress display pauses the render at the very same sample, that one update is skipped (expected from the code, not tested).
- Instruments follow only once per note in an export; live, a held note of an instrument that reacts to knob turns keeps moving. Macros as targets ("x:" controls) and the master volume are not rendered. Other modulators on the same control are not rendered, only Vector Map's part.
- Live, a control follows on screen frames (about 60 times a second, less in Light mode); in an export every 1024 samples. Same values, different step sizes.
- Automation of Vector Map's own knobs is not possible (they are not registered as automation targets). Turning one while the song plays makes the particles jump to where they would have been with that setting from the start.
- Walls: the push strength (2 to 9 keys per second, by how steep the wall is) is a guess. A ball passes through a wall, it is not stopped by it. Ableton's own wall behaviour could not be read anywhere.
- Projects saved with a Vector Map and opened in an older version: the older version has no card for the unknown type and will probably fail to draw the modulators column (expected from the code, not tested).
