## 25 November 2026: v0.178, task 17 (track controls: one layout for all tracks, a mini track you rearrange by dragging)

### What "not universal" was (the cause)
- Checked on the Fractals demo (30 tracks: 4 groups, 25 instruments, 1 return), every setting one by one. The setting itself was always stored once (`UI.hcz`) and every header was redrawn, but **the tracks did not end up the same**: `hczBuild` left out controls that have no use on a kind of track (Record arm and Freeze on group and return tracks, Output and Sends on return tracks) instead of keeping their place. So adding or removing one of those changed only some tracks, and on group and return tracks every control after the gap sat one place further left, sometimes on another row, so a row that was cut off on one track was visible on the next. In the Everything layout a group track showed "M S e / R pan" where an instrument track showed "M S arm / e R pan".
- Second, smaller cause: the Automation lanes button grew wider on a track with open lanes (it shows a count), which could push the next control to another row on that track only.
- Fix: a greyed, switched-off button keeps the place (`hczNa`, class `zna`, with a tooltip that says why), and the lanes button has one fixed width (26 px). The test now compares the position and width of every control on every track: identical on all tracks for all four ready-made layouts, each icon, pan, Mute and Solo and meter choice, track heights 140, 96, 64, 44, 26 and 20, folded and unfolded groups, tracks added afterwards (audio and return), undo, redo, scrolling away and back, lanes open on one track, each of the 14 controls removed and added again (0 of 32 tracks, then 32 of 32), page reload, reset.
- Nothing was found where a setting reached only one track. Things that are per track by nature stay so: the state of Mute, Solo, Effects off, the chosen output, sends and colour.

### Built (names `hcz` / `HCZ`, new code replaced the v0.164 list editor in place)
- **Copies of a track that look exactly like the tracks**: `hczMini(c,o)` builds a track header in layout `c` with the same classes and the same control code (`ctl`) as the real headers. `hczCss()` copies every CSS rule written for `#hdrin` so it also applies to `.hczM` (selector `:is(#hczNone,.hczM)`, which keeps the same weight), once per style sheet, about 18 ms the first time. So themes and fader styles show in the copies too. The copies are pictures: `data-c` is removed and nothing is registered in `CT`. `hczThumb` is a scaled copy, `hczCtlHtml` one control.
- **Editor** (`hczOpen`, `hczEdSync`, panel `#hczEd`, 340 px): "Your track" is a mini track (class `zmain`, 1.35 times as large). Grab a control and drop it somewhere else; a blue bar shows where it lands (`hczDropAt`, classes `zmkL` / `zmkR`), the control follows the pointer, and the real tracks change during the drag. Drag it out of the track or onto the tray to remove it. Esc during a drag puts everything back (`hczDragEnd`). A red mark appears on a control under the pointer and removes it with a click (`.zqx`).
- **Tray "Not shown"**: every control that is not on the track, drawn as it really looks with its name under it. Drag it into the track or click it (added at the end).
- **Keyboard**: every control in the mini track can be focused; arrow keys move it earlier or later, Delete or Backspace removes it; Enter or Space on a tray item adds it; the picture buttons work with Enter and Space. Focus stays on the control that was moved.
- **Ready-made layouts**: four picture buttons in a 2 by 2 square, each a scaled real drawing of a 96 px track in that layout with the name under it; the one in use is outlined. New fourth layout **Mixing** (`HCZP.mix`): volume, pan as a round dial, large Mute and Solo, sends, output, level meter, no automation buttons.
- **Pan control, Mute and Solo, Level meter**: picture buttons with the real controls (slider, half dial, round dial; normal and large M S; the edge of a track with and without the level bars). **Track icon**: three plain text choices, Normal, Big, Hidden.
- Kept: Reset to default, the Controls button, the right-click entry, the row in Settings > Layout (it now has a small real drawing of the current layout, `.hczprev`, refreshed by `hczCardSync` when Settings opens and on every change; Customise opens the same editor). `UI.hcz` has the same format as before; stored layouts load unchanged.

### Tested (headless Playwright, Fractals demo, no page errors on load or in any run; the only console message is the missing service worker file of the test server)
- `tools/ui-tests/hdrlayout.py` (rewritten): part 1 is the list above (29 checks, all tracks identical). Part 2 with the mouse: a control dragged to the front and to the end inside the mini track, from the tray into the track, out of the track onto the tray; order, marker and the real tracks checked in the middle of each drag; Esc during a drag; a plain click changes nothing; arrow keys, Delete, Enter, Space; the Settings row and its drawing; Customise from Settings.
- Screenshots looked at: the editor in Standard and Mixing, the bottom half with all picture buttons, three moments in the middle of a drag, the tracks in Minimal, Standard, Mixing and Everything, Everything with big icon, the Settings row, a 1100 by 700 window.
- Speed: opening the editor 26 ms, redrawing it 14 ms, one change of order with 30 tracks 54 ms (the redraw of the headers, as before; during a drag it runs only when the landing place changes). With the song playing and the editor open nothing inside the editor changes (0 DOM changes in 2.5 s). Pan as a round dial is still one value with the track (set to R40).

### Not verified
- Not tried with a real mouse or on the Mac; touch dragging not tried. Safari and Firefox not tried (the mini track uses CSS `zoom` and `:is()`).
- **The Standard picture is not exactly the real Standard tracks.** While the layout equals the default, the tracks are still drawn by the old code (Write and Lanes at the bottom right, special arrangements at small heights), and the mini track and the Standard picture show the same controls in the wrapping-row arrangement that every other layout uses. As soon as anything is changed the tracks look exactly like the mini track. Not changed here because it would change how the default tracks look for everybody; say so if the default should be drawn the new way too. In the default layout a group track also still has no Record arm button (old code).
- On a low screen the editor is taller than the window and scrolls.
- Other themes and Light mode were not looked at in the editor (expected to follow, since the copies use the tracks' own CSS).

### Left
- Decide about the default layout (see above). Ideas: show in the mini track which rows a track of the current height cuts off, a wider header column, monitor and lock buttons once those functions exist.

## 25 November 2026: v0.179, run 12 finished by hand: particle devices, part 6 (Vector Delay, an audio effect)

Run 12 had stopped at its usage limit before it pushed anything. This entry is from the session that picked it up afterwards (the user asked for it), which also published v0.174 to v0.178 to the app artifact (those runs had pushed to GitHub but were waiting for an approval to publish) and wrote their project docs.

### Research (what Ableton's Vector Delay does)
Read: Dillon Bastan's Vector Delay manual (dillonbastan.com/inspiredbynature_manuals/Vector%20Delay%20User%20Manual.pdf) and Top Music Arts "Sound Design with Inspired by Nature: Vector Grain & Vector Delay" (topmusicarts.com/blogs/news/sound-design-with-inspired-by-nature-vector-grain). Both were read through summaries, not word for word.
- Confirmed by those pages: every particle is one independent delay line. Voices (number of particles, also the most the emitter makes), Scale, Speed, Mix. Three pages: delay, forces, particles and emitter. Delay: Time in milliseconds or synced, Glide (0 = no glide with crossfading delay lines; above 0 the pitch bends while the time slides), Feedback, D (Damp: a little high and low cut in the feedback), Pitch in steps, F (fine), **Recycle = the pitch shift moves into the feedback so every repeat is shifted again** (the task text had guessed "re-launch a particle"; the manual and the article agree on this meaning), a scale for pitch that is driven by a particle, Pan, Gain, Reverse, filter types. Five knobs (X, Y, distance, speed, mass) each with a target menu under it; mass never changes after a particle is made. Forces: attractor, flow field, magnetism by charge. Particles: Charge (chance that a new particle is positive), Edge (bounce, clip, wrap), Mass, Rnd > Mass. Emitter (off by default): particles are made and die on synced intervals; Burst, Rate, Life, Skip, Rnd > Rate, Rnd > Life, Fade and Fade shape (left ramp down, middle triangle, right ramp up).
- **Guesses** (no page gives them): every range, unit and default; the list of targets (Time, Pitch, Pan, Gain, Filter) and how far each goes at 100% (time 2 octaves, pitch 24 semitones, cutoff 4 octaves, gain to zero or double); the 50 ms window of the pitch shifter; reverse in pieces as long as the delay time; how strong Damp is (cuts below 140 Hz and above 6.5 kHz, 6 dB per octave); emitter direction, spread and start speed. The physics knobs are Vector Grain's (see v0.175).
- Not built from Ableton: the X and Y offset sliders (here the two range bars of the other Vector devices), the Velocity limit, attractor position and "Attracts" charge choice (the pull goes to the middle of the range), drawing the flow field by mouse and its grid size, Edge modes Clip and Wrap, Save, Spawn area, On click (random, freeze), Rnd > Rate and Rnd > Life, the ladder filters, Res and LP-HP, Scala files.

### Built (new code before `askChoice`; names start with `vdl` / `VDL`; `vdlInit();` sits right after `vmInit();`)
- **Vector Delay** (`FXDEF.vdelay`, in the effect browser under Delay & space; registered with `ND` inside `vdlInit`, in the set `PBX`, `PBXW.vdelay`). Worklet `al-vdelay` (module string `VDLWL`, added to both module lists in `wlLoad`), made with `wk`; `x.sp = p => vdlSp(p, c)` builds the settings.
- `vdlCore(C)` sits on top of the shared field `vfCore()` (unchanged) and is used by the worklet and by the preview picture, so there is one copy: `sim(seed)`, `step(S,P)` (one step of 1/100 s: emitter on a beat grid, lifetime, fade, charge of new particles, then `C.step`), `snap` / `load` (save points; the field's random generator was replaced by one whose state can be saved), `calc(P,values,out)` (what the five values of a particle do: time factor, semitones, pan, gain, cutoff factor; pitch steps free, semitones or a scale).
- **Song time**: while the song runs the field is always the one that belongs to the song time: stepped from the start of the song, with a save point every 4 s. When the time jumps back (loop, new start) the engine goes back to a save point or to the start; a jump forward is caught up at 75 steps per 64 samples; an export does all of it at once. While the song is stopped the field runs freely. Turning a knob that moves the particles while the song plays drops the save points and carries on from where the particles are; the next start of playback works them out again from the start, as an export does.
- **Sound**: every particle has a delay line of its own (5.5 s of stereo memory, made when the particle first appears) with its own feedback, so the feedback of a line is never more than the knob says (the first version shared one memory and fed the sum of all lines back; with many lines close together that howled, the Metal combs preset sat at -6 dB for ever). Two reading heads per line fade into each other all the time (50 ms, or one delay time when reversed). A head takes over a new delay time and pitch only while it is silent: a jump in delay time is a short crossfade, a pitch shift is a sliding delay, reverse is a head running backwards. With Glide above 0 the delay time slides instead. Recycle off: the feedback is read a second time without the shift (four readings per line instead of two). A filter per line (off, low pass, band pass, high pass, notch). In the feedback: a DC blocker, Damp, and a soft limit. The level of the lines is divided by their number when they all have the same delay time and by its square root when they are spread in time. Mix: up to 50% the sound going in stays at full level.
- The engine works in pieces of 64 samples counted from the start, so the result does not depend on the block size of the audio system (128 live, 512 in an export).
- Controls, on five pages (buttons Delay, Vectors, Forces, Particles, Emitter; Voices, Scale, Speed and Mix are on every page; the page is stored as `p._td` and so on; a wrapper of `fxDevHtml` hands only the open page to the usual device code): Delay: Time (milliseconds or a note length), Glide, Feedback, Pitch, Fine, Pan, Gain, Filter, Q, filter type, Damp, Recycle, Reverse. Vectors: X, Y, Dist, Speed, Mass, each with a choice of what it drives, and Steps (free, semitones, a scale). Forces: Motion Forces / Flow, Gravity, Magnet, Center, Drag or Flow, Detail, Drift, Drag. Particles: Mass, Rnd > Mass, Charge, Start speed, Seed. Emitter: on, Every (note length), Burst, Life in beats, Skip, Fade, Fade shape, Angle, Spread. Tooltips in plain language on every knob and choice (set after `devLayout`).
- Picture (`vdlDraw`, `vdlGeo`, `vdlHnd`): the shared field painter `vfPaint` in orange, a faint line from the middle to every particle, size shows how loud a line is, colour its place between left (blue) and right (yellow). Handles: the ends of the two range bars, the emitter (drag to move) and a white dot to aim it. Live the picture shows the engine's own particles (30 times a second), otherwise a preview of the same steps.
- **14 presets**: Echo cloud (the default), Three in orbit, Wide slap, Still taps, River (flow, tape glide), Rising shimmer, Falling cascade, Fifths above (once), Scale chimes, Backwards swarm, Bursts every beat, Sprinkler, Metal combs, Thick chorus.
- **Fix found on the way (affects every effect that follows the song time in an export, also Tape stop and the modulated effects):** `originT` took the start beat of an offline render from the global `SIDE_B0`, which the next render overwrites. The loudness map renders pieces of the song in the background after every change, so an export that started while the map was at work could get the wrong song time (seen here as two renders of the same thing that differed, about once in 15). `originT` now takes the start beat from the render itself (`VMOF`, filled at the first line of `renderMix`).

### Tested (headless, no page errors in any run)
- `tools/engine-tests/vector_delay.py` (a short pluck every two beats): all 14 presets: no NaN, peaks 0.13 to 0.18 (the dry sound peaks at 0.125), every preset bit-identical in two renders (run four times in a row after the `originT` fix), tails end. Seed 2 differs from seed 1. Mix 0 is exactly the dry sound. One line at 500 ms with mix 100 is an exact copy of the dry sound 500.0 ms later; 1/4 at 120 bpm gives the same. Pitch +12 doubles the spectral centre of the repeat (645 to 1278 Hz), -12 halves it (331 Hz). Pitch +7 with feedback 70: with Recycle off the repeats stay (948, 903, 877 Hz), with Recycle on they climb (948, 1265, 1608, 2105, 2795 Hz). Reverse changes the sound. Feedback 100 with Q 12 and 16 equal lines for 16 s: peak 0.013, ends. Each of the five targets, Glide 200 ms, emitter on and off: no NaN. Song time: with X on pan, the left-right balance heard in a render from the start and in a render of the second half alone both follow the X of particle 1 worked out on the main thread (difference at most 0.0035 where X moves between -0.29 and 0.29).
- Cost: the presets take 0.5 to 1.2 s for 12 s of sound including the app's own 0.5 s. Heaviest case (16 lines, pitch shifted without Recycle, filter on): 20 s of sound in 3.5 s here.
- `tools/ui-tests/vector_delay.py`: nothing outside the device on any of the five pages; Flow shows the other knobs; the three bar ends dragged with the mouse change the right values and three undos bring them back; the emitter is switched on, moved and aimed with the mouse; a preset keeps the open page; while the song plays the picture gets the engine's particles and the engine's step equals the song time (377 of 378); parameters hidden. Screenshots looked at: all five pages, Flow, emitter, playing, parameters hidden, the whole window.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo); the Vector Grain test (all presets bit-identical) and the Vector Map test give the same results as before.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it**, and it was not compared with Ableton's device (no access). Presets were set by measurement and by looking at the picture.
- Live sound on the user's Mac, CPU there, a real mouse or trackpad, MIDI learn and automation of the knobs.
- Memory: every line that has been used keeps about 2 MB at 48 kHz, so a Vector Delay with 16 voices holds about 34 MB. Making a line's memory happens in the audio thread when its particle first appears; not measured whether that can click on a slow computer.
- A particle launched by the emitter starts with empty memory: it only repeats what comes in after it was launched.
- The pitch shifter is the simple two-head kind: on sustained low notes a faint 20 Hz flutter is to be expected at large shifts. Not measured.
- After a jump in song time while playing live, the lines keep their old sound in memory (as any delay does); only the particles jump.
- The Time slider keeps showing its millisecond value while a note length is chosen; dragging it switches to milliseconds (the app's usual behaviour for Time).
- An export of more than 32 beats is rendered in pieces by the app; a delay tail that crosses a piece border depends on how the app overlaps the pieces (not looked at for this device).

### Left
- All six particle devices of the series now exist. For Vector Delay: listen and tune the presets; the parts of Ableton's device listed under "Not built".
- Still open from earlier entries: LFO, Steps and parameter automation lanes are not in exports; Vector Grain and Vector FM could use `vfPaint`; the default track layout question from v0.178.
- New requests from the user during this session, not started in this version: an orbit synth (a centre mass with balls in orbit that make the sound), and a redesign of all older devices in the look of Vector Delay (picture on the left, parameters arrow top left, power then preset then Change at the top, gain bottom right, no empty space, pictures for simple devices such as Pluck, one slider design), with instruments and effects that change live while browsing and a double-click to choose.

### Release
- v0.179, commit 9edbb62 on main, artifact version 202 (this publish also brought v0.174 to v0.178 to the app artifact), desktop build run 45648296373 dispatched. Project docs `claude/audiolab-backlog-run-12.md` to `-17.md` written. The scheduled tasks 12 to 17 were deleted at the user's request.
- The requests the user sent during this session are written down in `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md` (tasks A to E), one scheduled run each.

## 25 November 2026: v0.180, Orbit, an instrument (asked for by the user during the session that finished run 12; task A of `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md`)

### The user's request
"A center mass that you can change the size of the mass, and then you can drop in small little balls that orbit around the center mass. You can change their mass, make them orbit faster or slower, add more or less. And as they orbit around the center mass, it creates sound on the synthesizer. And then on the right, I want to have some extra parameters."

### Built (names `orb` / `ORB`, code before the Vector Map block; `orbInit();` sits right after `vdlInit();`)
- **Orbit** (`PRESETS.orb`, in the instrument browser under Synths), worklet `al-orb` (`ORBWL`, added to both module lists in `wlLoad`; `pvAny` covers it).
- **The sound engine, the pages and the picture code are a renamed copy of Vector FM** (made by a script from the v0.176 block): every ball is an oscillator of the held note, or a modulator and carrier pair, or part of a chain; the five values of a ball (left-right, up-down, distance from the centre, speed, mass) drive Ratio, Index, Pan, Gain, Detune, Cutoff or Pitch; ADSR, filter per carrier, pitch steps in a scale, One per key, Lock. So Orbit has everything Vector FM has, and a fix to one of the two does not reach the other.
- **New: the movement, `orbPhys()`** (same interface as `vfCore`, so the engine is unchanged). Real gravity towards the centre: pull = 4 x Centre x Gravity / distance squared, made soft very close to the centre (as if the centre had a radius of 0.06) so nothing is thrown out to infinity. Stepped with velocity Verlet every 64 samples. A new ball gets the speed of a circle orbit for its distance; Orbit shape scales that start speed (50% = circle, lower = it dives in and swings out, an ellipse; higher = it swings out to the rim). The rim of the picture is a wall. Ball pull lets the balls attract each other by mass. Drag slows them (heavier balls less), so they spiral in. Clockwise turns the direction round.
- **Dropping balls**: a click in the picture adds a ball at that place (`I.balls`, up to 16; the Balls knob goes up by one); balls without a dropped place start at seeded places. "Clear dropped balls" forgets the places. Dragging the centre up or down changes Centre (how heavy it is; the drawn size follows). A click also plays a test note.
- Pages: Operators, Vectors, Orbits (Centre, Gravity, Orbit shape, Ball mass, Rnd > Mass, Ball pull, Drag, Seed, Clockwise, Clear dropped balls), Output. Always visible: Scale, Orbit speed, Gain, Balls, Attack, Decay, Sustain, Release. Tooltips rewritten for the orbit knobs.
- Picture: a square field so circles are circles, the centre as a glowing mass, balls with trails, the strip with the overtones of every carrier below. Orange accent (`.obd`).
- **9 presets**: Solar system, Comets, Orbit bells, Slow planets (pad), Double star (bass), Ring of moons (organ), Asteroid swarm, One moon per key, Slingshot.

### Tested (headless, no page errors)
- Physics (`tools/engine-tests/orbit.py`, last part): one ball dropped at distance 0.6 with a circle start, 60 s in steps of 64 samples: the distance stays between 0.6000 and 0.6000, the first turn takes 2.943 s (2.942 s from the formula), the energy drifts by 6e-12. Orbit shape 25% gives an ellipse between 0.174 and 0.600. Four times the pull gives twice the turns (13 in 20 s against 6.8).
- Sound (same file, the Vector FM test with the names changed): all 9 presets no NaN, peaks 0.22 to 0.50, bit-identical in two renders, tails end; seed 2 differs; a note moved by 293 samples sounds exactly the same; tuning exact (440.0 Hz for A3 with Index 0); ratios give the expected parts; all targets, operator counts, waves and filters; 20 keys with One per key (peak 0.74); a dropped ball changes the sound and renders the same twice. Heaviest case (16 pairs, six notes, filters): 4 s of sound in 1.0 s.
- `tools/ui-tests/orbit.py`: nothing outside the device on any page; two clicks drop two balls (Balls 6 to 8, places stored); dragging the centre up 30 px makes it 74%; three undos bring everything back; Clear dropped balls; while the song plays the picture gets the engine's balls; it is in the browser. Screenshots looked at: the four pages, dropped balls, playing, the browser tile.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it.** Presets were set by measurement and by looking at the picture.
- Real mouse or trackpad, the Mac, MIDI learn and automation of the knobs.
- As in Vector FM: all notes of a track share one set of balls, which starts again when a note begins while nothing sounds; a note's sound depends on the notes before it in the phrase. Strong FM on high keys folds back (no oversampling).
- A ball can only be dropped at rest relative to a circle (no throwing by dragging), and a single dropped ball cannot be removed on its own (lower Balls, or clear all).
- The mass of a ball does not change its orbit round the centre (as in nature); it counts for Ball pull, Drag and for whatever "Mass" drives on the Vectors page.

### Left
- Listen and tune the presets; throw a ball by dragging; remove one ball with a right-click; per-ball mass by scrolling over a ball; Vector FM and Orbit could share one copy of the engine again.
- Tasks B to E of `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md` (bouncing ball devices tied to the rhythm, one design language for all older devices, Change button with live browsing, light devices and background freeze).

## 25 November 2026: v0.181, bounces tied to the rhythm, melody and harmony for Bouncy Notes, and the Bounce Delay effect (task B of `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md`, most of it)

### Research (the user asked for it)
- Bouncing ball delays in other plug-ins: Sinevibes Dispersion ("a single round of such delay bounces is always summing up to a precise tempo fraction", up to 32 delay lines, pan spread so the ball moves across the stereo field; pluginboutique.com, sinevibes.com/dispersion, kvraudio.com), Boscomac Bouncing Ball for Reaktor (number of bounces, time between bounces that can follow the tempo, gravity, decay; audiofanzine.com, rekkerd.org), G-Delay for Max for Live (a fixed number of taps instead of feedback, Gravity and Antigravity modes, the interval sets the largest gap; isotonikstudios.com), the Audacity Bouncing Ball Delay (decay per bounce, delay time, number of bounces, a version with pan; plugins.audacityteam.org), a "Panning gravity delay" MIDI patch that retriggers notes as if bouncing (patchstorage.com). Ableton's own is Bouncy Notes in Inspired by Nature (copied in v0.166). Search results and page summaries were read, not the manuals.
- How games do ball physics: a fixed time step with an accumulator so the result is the same on every machine, semi-implicit Euler or velocity Verlet as the step (Glenn Fiedler, "Fix Your Timestep!", gafferongames.com; summaries of it on gamedev.net and others).
- What was taken from it: (1) taps instead of feedback for the delay, so every repeat lands exactly where the formula puts it; (2) both ways of tying to the tempo: the first gap is the note length, or the whole round is the note length (Dispersion's way); (3) no time stepping at all for a single ball on a flat floor: the landing times are a closed formula, which is exact and costs nothing while playing. Time stepping is only used where forces change all the time (Orbit, the Vector devices), there with a fixed step of 64 samples.

### How the rhythm is real physics
A ball thrown up with speed v is in the air for 2v/g and comes back with speed v; a bounce multiplies the speed by the bounciness e. For a chosen note length T the gravity is solved so that a ball that rises to its height h comes down after exactly T: g = 8h/T². Then the gaps are T, Te, Te² and so on. Steady is e = 1 (it never loses height: a bounce on every step for as long as Lifetime lasts), Halving is e = 1/2 (1/4, 1/8, 1/16), Two thirds is e = 2/3, By friction takes e from the Friction and Mass knobs, Whole round makes the first gap T(1 - e) so that all gaps together are exactly T. In a rhythm the ball starts on the keys at the note (the note itself is the first landing), and the Gravity and Speed knobs no longer change the timing, only Free mode uses them. The picture shows the same flight.

### Built
- **Bouncy Notes** (`bnBalls`, `bnRun` changed in place; new `bnDeg`, `BNWALK`, `BNHARM`; `bn2Init();` after `orbInit();`):
  - Rhythm (`p.sync`: Free, 1/16 to 2 bars) and Bounce (`p.bmode`: By friction, Steady, Halving, Two thirds, Whole round).
  - Melody (`p.walk`): each bounce moves along the chosen scale: up, down, up and down, skipping up. One held key becomes a melody.
  - Harmony (`p.harm`): third, fifth, octave, fifth and octave, chord of 3 notes, chord of 4 notes, all in the scale (major is used when the scale is chromatic).
  - With Thru on, the first landing is not played twice.
  - The device now has two pages, Balls and Notes (same way as Vector Delay's pages), so everything fits.
  - 9 new presets in two groups: On the beat (Every beat for ever, Ratchet, Falling by two thirds, One round fills a bar, Steady 1/8 walk up the scale) and Melody and chords (Ratchet melody, Chords on every beat, Falling sevenths, Skipping fifths). The old presets are unchanged (Rhythm free).
- **Bounce Delay** (`FXDEF.bdelay`, Delay & space, particle look; worklet `al-bdelay` in `BDWL`; `bdTaps` works out the landings, `bdSp`, `bdDraw`): 2 to 24 repeats at the landing times of a ball. First gap in milliseconds or as a note length; Length is the first gap or the whole round; Bounces get faster (falling ball), get slower (the ball in reverse, a wind-up) or stay even; Bounciness, Fade per bounce, Pan sweep (the ball crosses from one side to the other), Feedback (the last repeat starts the round again, with a soft limit), Tone, Mix. Changing a setting fades the old taps out and the new ones in over 30 ms. The picture shows the flight, a dot per landing (size = level, colour = side) and a ball running along. 7 presets. Tooltips on everything.

### Tested (headless, no page errors)
- `tools/engine-tests/bounce_rhythm.py`: 1/4 steady gives notes at beats 0, 1, 2, 3 ...; halving gives gaps 1, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125; two thirds 1, 0.6667, 0.4444; a whole round in a bar ends at beat 4 (last landing 3.84 of 10); 1/8 triplet stays on the grid with other Speed and Gravity; at 90 bpm the notes are still on the beats. Melody up the major scale from C: 60 62 64 65 67 69 71 72; up and down in minor pentatonic; chords (60 64 67 on every landing); four-note chords walking down in minor (60 63 67 70, then 58 62 65 68); Thru does not double. Free mode gives the same starts as before (0.6164, 1.6027, 2.3918 ...). A render is bit-identical twice.
- Bounce Delay, same file: for all 7 presets the output equals the dry sound repeated at the formula's times with the formula's levels and pans, to 2e-8; bit-identical twice; one round in a bar at 120 bpm is 2.0000 s; feedback 80% for 16 s peaks at 0.15 and ends; mix 0 is exactly the dry sound; 24 bounces: 16 s of sound in 0.6 s.
- `tools/ui-tests/bounce.py`: nothing outside the devices; the two pages of Bouncy Notes; choosing 1/4 and Halving in the page gives 0, 1, 1.5, 1.75; tooltips; screenshots of both pages, playing, Bounce Delay with two presets, parameters hidden.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it.** Real mouse, the Mac.
- Bouncy Notes with walls (v0.177) together with a rhythm: the walls only move the keys, so the times should hold; not tested together.
- Bounce Delay follows a tempo change only when its settings are sent again (the app does that on every change); tempo automation inside a held round was not tested.

### Left from task B
- **A bouncing-ball instrument of its own** was not built: Bouncy Notes in front of any instrument does what was asked (every bounce plays the instrument), and a separate sound engine would only repeat that. Say so if a dedicated one is still wanted (for example a struck resonator whose loudness follows the impact).
- A chord progression per bar for the Harmony choice; more than one rhythm per ball; throwing balls onto pads.
- Tasks C, D and E of the device backlog are not started: one design language for all older devices, the Change button with live browsing, light devices and background freeze.

### Release
- v0.180: commit cfa6d2d, artifact version 203. v0.181: commit e69fe26, artifact version 204. Project docs `claude/audiolab-device-backlog-run-A.md` and `-B.md`.
- **Desktop builds**: the builds for v0.179, v0.180 and v0.181 failed or could not be started because GitHub answered with server errors (HTTP 500 and 502 on the releases and dispatch addresses, between 15:43 and 16:20 UTC; the page deploy failed once too). Nothing is wrong in the code. The program update inside the installed app comes from the repo and is not affected; only new installers are missing until the build is started again.

## 25 November 2026: v0.182, one design language for every device (task C of `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md`)

### What changed for the user
- **Title row, the same on every device**, across the whole device: arrow, on/off (effects) or the logo (instruments), name, preset menu with previous and next, Change, then the device's own buttons (play, Editor, Pitch and Amp), and remove at the far right.
- **The arrow** (top left) slides the parameters shut and open. It replaces the Params button at the top right. Shut, a device is its title (arrow, on/off, name, remove) and its picture. A device without a picture has no arrow (only Grid, whose whole face is the picture).
- **Change on effects** (new): opens the effect browser, titled "replace X on track"; the effect picked there takes the old one's place in the chain and keeps its on/off state. One undo step. Cancel changes nothing. This is the plain replace; live browsing is task D.
- **Picture on the left, controls on the right, as narrow as the controls allow.** Knobs fill the height in up to three rows, so a device with three knobs is one column wide (Pluck was 444 px of mostly grey; its controls now take 380 px next to a picture). Choices that are not presets (Wave, Sync, Ping-pong, Mode and so on) sit in a row above the controls, as in Vector Delay.
- **The device gain** is a bar at the right end of every device, the last control, drawn like Vector Delay's Gain bar. A Size or Length control stands as a bar next to it (Blur Size, Convolution Size, Kick Length, FX Length, Clap Room).
- **One look per kind of control**: every knob 30 px in a 54 px column, every bar 44 px wide with a 16 px track, every slider 20 px high. Envelope bars have plain labels (Attack, Decay, Sustain, Release) in the same type as the knob labels; the small "ENVELOPE" heading and the cramped capitals are gone.
- **Presets everywhere**: the nine basic instruments (Saw lead, Square lead, Soft pad, Strings, Pluck, Soft keys, Sub bass, Square bass, Reese bass) each have Default and four or five more; Drum kit has Default kit and five more (Tight and dry, Deep and long, Bright and snappy, Dark and low, Trap kit; the "Default kit" button became the first entry). Effects got previous and next buttons beside their menu. Chord's shape menu and the EQ's menu are their preset menus.
- **A picture for the basic instruments**: the filter curve on top (dashed: where it starts when a note begins) and the envelope below; both follow the knobs, and a white dot runs along the envelope while a note sounds.
- **Faster**: rebuilding the device strip took 106 ms for a Fractals track and 115 ms for Drum kit with three effects in the headless test browser; now 28 ms and 44 ms. The old fit of the title row (`dfFit`) measured the page over and over; the title has its own row now, so that step is switched off.
- Fixed on the way: the gain of an effect always showed 0.0 dB in its control (it read the wrong field; the sound was right). The strip no longer jumps sideways when a device is rebuilt while scrolled to the right. The arrows between devices are drawn again once widths have settled. In the Devices window every device has the panel's height.

### How it is built
- New block before `askChoice` (search "one design language"): `duLayout()` runs as the last wrapper of `renderFx`, after `devLayout` and `devFill`. `duDev(D,t)` moves `.devh` to the top of the device, rebuilds its order, moves choices into `.ndtb`, makes the arrow (`.duar`, it keeps the old `data-kt` / `data-ikt` attribute), the Change button (`.duchg`, `data-duchg` on effects) and the gain (`.dug`, a `vb` control with the same id `fx:og:...` as before). `duFit(D)` sets knob rows and columns and widens the side when a row of choices would squeeze the knobs. Classes: `.du` on every laid-out device, `.dushut` when the parameters are shut.
- `duInit()`: `DUBI` (presets of the basic instruments, registered in `IPRE`), `DUKIT` and `duKit()` (drum kits made from the default pads with the pad knobs and another kick), `PL2.du_basic = duBasicDraw` (the picture; `instDevHtml` is wrapped to add the canvas).
- `chooseIB` is wrapped: with `ib.repl` set the new effect is moved to the old one's place. `duChangeFx(t,f)` opens the browser.
- CSS before `.dacv` (search "one design language"). Everything inside a `.du` device has `transition:none`: sizes are measured right after the layout is built, and with transitions the browser still reports the old sizes (this was behind several wrong fits).
- Particle devices: 44 px wider for the gain bar (868 px, instruments 916 px); the picture ends where the bar begins, so the drawing code and its width constants are unchanged.
- Nothing in the sound code was touched.

### Tested (headless, no page errors)
- `tools/ui-tests/devices_all.py` (new): builds every instrument and every effect of the two browser lists, checks that nothing sticks out, prints the title order, the sizes of knobs, sliders and bars, shuts and opens the parameters, saves a screenshot of each. All 66: nothing outside; title order arrow, on/off or logo, name, preset, Change, (own buttons), remove; 65 shut to zero visible controls and open again with the same controls, Grid has no arrow.
- `tools/ui-tests/design.py` (new): Change replaces in place (comp > delay(off) > reverb becomes comp > phaser(off) > reverb, undo restores, Cancel keeps, plain Add still adds at the end); previous and next preset on an effect; Pluck presets and Default equal to a new Pluck, three undos; drum kits and Default kit equal to `defaultPads()`; the gain bar sets `f.p.og` and `I.og`, drag and double-click; the arrow with animations on and off; shut state is saved with the project; no em dash and no button without a tooltip in the titles; the basic picture changes with the knobs.
- `vector_delay.py`, `orbit.py`, `bounce.py`, `hdrlayout.py`, `vector_fm.py`, `vector_grain.py`, `emit.py`, `vector_map.py`, `particles_look.py`: same results as on v0.181.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).
- Screenshots looked at: all 66 devices open, eight shut, the Devices window, both browsers.

### Every device looked at
Instruments (28): Venom, Plant, Wavetable, Orbit, Vector FM, Saw lead, Square lead, Soft pad, Strings, Pluck, Soft keys, Growl, Sub bass, Square bass, Reese bass, Acid, Drum kit, Kick designer, Snare designer, Clap designer, Hat designer, Tom designer, Percussion, Sampler, Emit, Vector Grain, FX, Grid.
Effects (38): Compressor, OTT, Sidechain, Limiter, Soft Clipper, Gate, Multiband Dynamics, Transient Shaper, De-Esser, Resonance Suppressor, Echo delay, Timeless delay, Space reverb, Blur, Convolution Reverb, Tape Stop, Vector Delay, Bounce Delay, Pro EQ, Low-pass filter, Drive, Auto Filter, Bitcrusher, Pitch and Formant, Pitch Correction, Phaser, Chorus, LFO Shaper, Vocoder, Frequency Shifter, Chord, Bouncy Notes, Arpeggiator, MIDI Toolkit, Spectrum, Loudness, Tuner, Stereo Imager.

### Still deviating
- **Venom**: its green sliders keep their own colour and grouping (same height as the others now). **Sampler**: Filter and Amp keep their own panels with pictures in the controls area. **Growl**: knobs in five named groups, no bars. **Grid**: the patch is the device, only title and gain follow. **Pro EQ**: band editor instead of knobs; its own Scale and Out sliders keep their look.
- **The Kick designer inside a Drum kit pad** (the second device that appears when a pad is a Kick designer) still has the old slider look and a low picture; the old layout steps skip it too.
- **Two bars called Gain** on Vector Delay and Soft Clipper: their own Gain parameter and the device gain stand side by side. One of them needs another name; left for the user to choose.
- **Instruments have no on/off** (there is none in the engine); the logo stands in that place.
- **Devices in the two browsers** (the tiles and the live device) keep the old look with the Params button; task D reworks the browsers.
- Some long labels are cut with dots in a 54 px knob column (Downsample, Max reduction, Pitch (semitones)); the tooltip has the full name.
- Low-pass filter: its title is 15 px wider than picture plus controls.
- The slide of the parameters is a short width and fade animation on ordinary devices; particle devices switch at once.
- Modulators, the track device and macros were not part of this task.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has seen it on a real screen or tried it with a mouse**; the Mac build; touch.
- The new presets of the basic instruments and the drum kits were set by reasoning from the knobs, **nobody has listened to them**.
- Old projects with shut devices were tested only through the saved `kOpen` flag, not with a real old file.
- MIDI learn, modulators and automation on the gain bar (the control id is the same as before, so they should hold).

### Left
- Task D (Change with live browsing; the browsers in the new look), task E (CPU and background freeze).


### Release
- v0.182: commit d12f4ad, artifact version 205, project doc `claude/audiolab-device-backlog-run-C.md`.
- **Desktop build**: run 77 for this commit, all three jobs succeeded (release, Mac, Windows; 18:25 to 18:29 UTC). The installers of v0.182 are on the GitHub release; the builds for v0.179 to v0.181 that failed in the GitHub outage were not started again, since v0.182 contains them.

## 25 November 2026: v0.183, Change with live browsing, for instruments and effects (task D of `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md`)

### What changed for the user
- **Change on an instrument or an effect opens its browser in change mode.** The title says "change on (track)", the button says Keep, the line at the bottom says what the clicks do, and the device the track had is marked "On the track before". The tile that is open is the track's own device with its own settings, moved into view above the piano.
- **One click on an entry puts that device on the track at once**, whether the song plays or not. Another click swaps again. A click on the entry marked "On the track before" brings the old device back with all its settings.
  - While the song plays, the notes of the track carry on with the new sound: every note that is sounding, or that was already handed to the audio engine, is played again with the new instrument, and the old sound fades out in 30 ms (longer when the new sound has a slow attack).
  - While the song is stopped, a click on an instrument plays a short test note through the track, so the track's effects are heard too. A click on an effect plays two short notes of the track's instrument through the chain (on a track without an instrument: the test sound chosen in the piano).
  - An effect keeps its place in the chain and its on/off state.
  - The piano in the browser plays through the track as well.
- **A double-click anywhere on an entry keeps it and closes the browser**: on its picture, its name, its text, anywhere on the tile. **Enter** does the same. In the ordinary browsers (add an instrument, add an effect) a double-click on the picture now works too; before, the second click landed on the device that had just been opened and nothing happened.
- **Escape, Cancel or the close button put the original device back exactly as it was**, with all its settings, also when knobs were turned inside the browser.
- **The arrow keys move to the nearest entry in that direction** and swap live, in both browsers (also when adding). The view follows.
- **The whole visit is one undo step.** Undo steps made inside the browser (a knob, a drag in a picture) are folded into it. Undo and redo are ignored while the change browser is open.
- The tooltips of the two Change buttons say what happens.
- Fixed on the way (was there in v0.182): after any visit to a browser the knobs of the strip behind it answered to the browser's copies until the strip was drawn again, and every Stop threw an error in the console. The strip is now drawn again when a browser closes, and one control that cannot be painted no longer stops the others.

### What the old "live mode" was, and what it is now
- Before: a click in the instrument browser made a temporary track (`ib.t`) with a fresh copy of the device, drew it as a real, editable device inside the tile (`ibLiveRender`, `IBCTX` makes `curT()` return that temporary track), and played a phrase through the preview bus. The track itself changed only on Use (`chooseIB`). The effect browser did the same with a temporary effect chain (`ibFxChain`).
- Now, in change mode (`ib.chg`, state in `CHG`): the temporary track holds **the very object that is on the track** (`ib.t.inst === track.inst`, `ib.t.fx[0]` is the effect in the chain). So the tile shows and edits the real device, and what is heard is the track. The preview chain is not built in change mode.

### How it is built (search "Change with live browsing" in index.html, before `askChoice`)
- `chgOpen(mode,t,f)`: called by the wrapped `openBrowser(track)` and by `duChangeFx`. Takes a copy of the device (`CHG.orig`) and one undo snapshot (`CHG.snap`).
- `chgSwap(k)`: called from the wrapped `renderIBTry` when the selection differs from what is on the track. Instrument: the instrument object is refilled in place. Effect: one entry of `t.fx` is replaced and `syncGraph()` builds the one missing node. Nothing else is rebuilt: the device strip behind the browser is not drawn during the visit (the one device in it is emptied once, `chgBlank`).
- `chgRevoice(t)`: the track gets a fresh input node; the old one, with everything connected to it (clip chains, voice engines, note tails), is faded out and dropped. The notes to play again come from a list kept by a wrapper of `synthB` (`CHGN`: every note the scheduler hands over while playing). Notes of instruments built from browser nodes continue as if they had played from their start (`pastShim`, as presets already did); instruments with a voice engine start the held notes again with their own attack.
- `chgEnd(keep)`: keep pushes the one snapshot and calls `changed(true)`; otherwise the copy is put back. A watcher on the browser's `hidden` attribute ends the visit however the browser was closed.
- Double-click: the second press is recognised by time and place (`pointerdown`, 500 ms, 8 px) because the first click redraws the tile and the browser's own double-click may never arrive. On a tile that was already open, a double-click on a control or a picture still belongs to the device.
- `chgNav(key)`, `chgSee(tile)`: arrow keys and bringing a tile into view (above the piano).
- Opening Change a second time reuses the tiles that are already built (wrapper of `renderPlaneInst`).
- Nothing in the sound code was changed. The only addition on the audio side is the note list and the fresh input node at a swap.

### Tested (headless, no page errors)
- `tools/ui-tests/change_live.py` (new, mouse and keyboard): Change on the instrument and on the middle effect of comp > delay > reverb.
  - Instrument, stopped: clicks on Saw lead, Reese bass, Wavetable: the track holds each, level of the test note -18.6, -13.5, -12.8 dB, no undo step.
  - Instrument, playing: Soft pad, Soft keys, Acid, Orbit, Pluck: the track holds each, loudest block -13.6 to -8.8 dB. Back on the original tile: settings equal to before.
  - Double-click on the picture of Plant: browser shut, track holds Plant, one undo step; one undo: equal to before. Double-click on a name while stopped: the same.
  - Escape after two clicks, and the Cancel button: equal to before, no undo step.
  - Arrow keys: each press swaps; Enter keeps; undo: equal to before.
  - Effect, stopped: Phaser, Drive, Timeless delay: chain comp > X > reverb each time, three nodes, level -13.9, -7.5, -13.4 dB. Playing: Bitcrusher, Chorus, Space reverb, Echo delay: the same. Double-click on the picture of Blur keeps it, one undo brings the chain back; Escape after two clicks: equal to before; arrows and Enter; an effect that is switched off stays off; first and last place of the chain.
  - Adding an instrument (Enter) and an effect (double-click on the name) the usual way still work.
- `tools/ui-tests/change_live_gap.py` (new): three long notes held, the instrument swapped twelve times while playing (pad, strings, sub, keys, wavetable, saw, Venom, Orbit, Plant, reese, acid, sampler, pad), the track recorded across each swap. No silent block; the quietest 5 ms block around a swap is at most 8 dB under the level next to it (pad to strings: -30.9 against -23.6 and -25.6 dB). The biggest step from one sample to the next around a swap is in the range of the steps of the new sound itself, except keys to wavetable (0.14 against 0.05 afterwards): that is the start of the wavetable notes, not a break. Escape while playing brings the pad back and it sounds.
- Edge cases by script: Change on a master effect, on an effect of an audio track, from a track that is not selected, a knob changed inside the visit then Escape and then Enter, undo while open, Stop and Play inside a visit.
- Speed in the headless test browser: a click on an entry 12 to 50 ms in all (drawing the device in the tile is most of it); the swap on the track itself 0.1 to 0.4 ms. Opening Change: 137 ms the first time, 51 ms after that (110 to 250 ms before).
- `design.py`, `orbit.py`, `vector_delay.py`, `particles_look.py` (one line adapted: the browser now opens at the device of the track), `hdrlayout.py`: as before.
- Unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).
- Screenshots looked at: both browsers in change mode, after swaps, after arrow keys, the strip after keeping.

### Not done, or different from what one might expect
- **An effect swap is a hard switch**, as adding an effect always was: the tail of the old effect (a delay, a reverb) is cut at once and there is no crossfade. On loud material this can be heard as a small step. Only the instrument swap has the fade.
- **Instruments with a voice engine** (the basic synths, Plant, Wavetable, Acid, Venom, Sampler grains, the particle instruments) start a held note again from its attack after a swap; they do not pick up in the middle of the envelope.
- An effect that is switched off stays off while browsing, so nothing is heard of the swap.
- Modulators, automation and MIDI learn that pointed at the old effect are not carried to the new one (as with the plain replace of v0.182). After Escape they are all still there.
- The track takes the name of the new instrument only if it still had the name of the old one, and only when kept.
- Right arrow on the last tile of a row does nothing; the arrows go by where the tiles are, not by a fixed order.
- **The tiles of the two browsers still have the old look** (left from task C).
- A frozen track was not tried.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has heard it.** "No gap, no click" rests on the recorded numbers above, not on ears.
- **Nobody has tried it with a real mouse on a real screen**; the Mac build; touch; a trackpad double-tap.
- In the headless test, during playback, a tile sometimes could be clicked only about 300 ms after the view had moved to it (the test waits and says so). Positions and sizes were stable in that time, so it looks like the test browser's hit test lagging; on a real machine it was not checked.
- Projects with many tracks: the swap itself does not depend on the number of tracks except for `syncGraph()` (0.04 ms on the small test project) and the undo snapshot taken once when Change opens.

### Left
- Task E (CPU and background freeze). The browsers in the new look. A crossfade for effect swaps.

### Release
- v0.183: commit f4aea01, artifact version 206, project doc `claude/audiolab-device-backlog-run-D.md`.
- **Desktop build**: run 45683210948 for this commit, all three jobs succeeded (release, Mac, Windows; 19:14 to 19:20 UTC).

## 25 November 2026: v0.184, clean preset switch and picking up notes in the middle (task I, from the user's message)

The user's words: "we do have some problems with changing presets on synths. And I suspect we might have the same problem for effects. [...] The previous preset still keeps going under your next preset. [...] when you change presets, you instantly hear one or the other preset or the next without hearing the previous one. [...] Let's say you have like a long pad note and you're in the middle of it. It continues in the middle of the note. [...] Look into how other DAWs do this and what the best algorithms are to keep everything light and snappy".

### What changed for the user
- **A preset change is a clean switch.** When the preset of an instrument changes while the song plays (menu, arrow buttons, undo, a new sample, a whole new instrument through Change), the old sound is gone within 13 to 19 ms: the notes that were already handed to the audio engine ahead of time, held notes, release tails, and the echo or reverb of the old sound in the delays and reverbs of that track. The new preset sounds from that same moment.
- **Notes that are sounding are picked up.** A long pad that is in the middle of its note carries on with the new preset at once. A fast pattern keeps its timing: every note of the look-ahead is handed over again at its own time, none twice, none missing.
- **The same when Play is pressed in the middle of a long note**, after a jump of the playhead (a jump is a stop and a start) and at the start of every loop round: the note sounds. Before, it stayed silent until its next start.
- **Effects**: a preset change on Echo delay, Timeless delay, Reverb, Space reverb, Blur, Vector Delay, Bounce Delay or Resonator clears the memory of the effect; the old tail fades out in 8 ms. Other effects take the new settings as before, without a break. The Change browser's effect swap now fades the old effect out the same way (it was a hard switch in v0.183).
- **Keys held on the keyboard** carry on with the new preset and still end when they are let go.
- **The test note of a preset change is left out while the song plays** and the track's own notes were picked up (it used to play a C over the song).
- **Settings, Audio system, Playback**: "Pick up notes that are already sounding" (on by default). Switched off, a note is only heard when the playhead passes its start; the clean switch stays.
- While the loop is on, a note that began before the loop now ends at the loop end and is picked up again at the loop start (before, it rang on once through the loop and was then gone in every later round).
- Fixed on the way (was there before): pressing Stop and then Play again within 0.4 s (which is what a jump of the playhead does) cleared away the first notes of the new playback 0.4 s later, for instruments made of browser nodes (Sampler, Growl, Grid, SFX, drums).

### Part 1: what happened before (v0.183), measured
Method: the track's own output is recorded on the audio thread together with a marker that jumps at the moment the preset menu gets its change event (the same path as a click on the menu or the arrows). From that moment the test swallows every new note, so all that is heard after the marker was started before the switch. "gone" = the last sample above -60 dB of the level before. Three cases: a note held for 30 s, a switch 100 ms into the release of a note, sixteenth notes.

    instrument                     | switch                 | long note held | in the release | sixteenth notes
    Saw lead (voice engine)        | def > bright           | over 2772 ms   | 581 ms         | 1215 ms
    Soft pad (voice engine)        | def > swell            | 2819 ms        | 2732 ms        | 2817 ms
    Strings (voice engine)         | def > slow             | 2816 ms        | 1481 ms        | 2317 ms
    Plant (voice engine)           | lush > supersaw        | 2819 ms        | 2772 ms        | 2819 ms
    Acid (voice engine)            | psy > rubber2          | 2819 ms        | 0 ms           | 676 ms
    Wavetable (voice engine)       | init > supersaw        | 2819 ms        | 644 ms         | 1339 ms
    Sampler, grains (al-grain)     | choir > cloud          | over 2726 ms   | 919 ms         | 1716 ms
    Sampler, plain                 | choir > cloud          | 0 ms           | 1072 ms        | 1141 ms
    Emit                           | spin > duo             | 2786 ms        | 1517 ms        | 2221 ms
    Vector Grain                   | orbit > swarm          | 2786 ms        | 595 ms         | 1280 ms
    Vector FM                      | glass > church         | 521 ms         | 2089 ms        | 2811 ms

- "0 ms" in the first column for Sampler (plain), Growl, Grid and SFX: these are made of browser nodes and already had a re-voicing of held notes (`liveRevoice`, 12 ms fade). Everything on a voice engine had nothing: the note kept its old sound to its end, up to the whole recording.
- "silent before": a drum or percussion hit is over long before the switch; with the voice engine switched off (not the default) a 30 s note is silent after about a second (the note cache, not looked into).

    all 23 instruments: silent (-180 dB), measured at beat 20 of a note from 0 to 60

### Part 2: how others do it
- **Chasing notes** is a standard option. Logic Pro (Project settings, MIDI, Chase) sends a note-on for a note when playback starts between its start and its end, with extra switches for notes held by the sustain pedal and for drum and sample channels, where a sample started late would be out of step (read from Apple's page through a summary; the page says nothing about where a sample starts). Ableton Live has "Chase MIDI Notes" in the Options menu: starting in the middle of notes whose starts lie before the start point plays them (forum reports, some of them about cases where it failed; no official text read). Cubase has "Chase Events" in the MIDI preferences; its manual page could not be read in this run (the page is a stub that points to a 1.2 MB PDF), so nothing is claimed about it here.
- All of these send a plain note-on, so the instrument starts the note from its attack and a sample from its beginning. AudioLab knows its own instruments, so it can do better where it is cheap: start the envelopes where they would be by now, and samples at the right spot.
- **All notes off against all sound off** (MIDI controllers 123 and 120): as far as the MIDI standard is known to me, 123 lets go of the keys, so releases ring on, and 120 silences at once including releases and effects inside the instrument. This could not be confirmed from the two pages fetched for it (one returned a home page, the other only names 123). A preset switch that must not let the old sound ring needs the second kind, with a short fade so it does not click. That is what was built.
- **Program changes in plug-ins**: no source read; the common ways are a short crossfade between the old and the new state or a fast fade of all voices. The cheapest that is exact to the sample here is a gain ramp on what the track's notes play into.
- Sources: https://support.apple.com/guide/logicpro/lgcp621f927c/mac , https://forum.ableton.com/viewtopic.php?p=1744482 , https://steinberg.help/cubase_ai/v13/en/cubase_nuendo/topics/preferences/preferences_midi_r.html (not readable), https://www.sweetwater.com/sweetcare/articles/what-controller-number-midi-all-notes-off-midi-panic/

### Part 3: how it is built (search "Clean switch and picking up notes" in index.html, before `askChoice`)
- **One rule for all instruments, in one place: `chsTrack(t)`.** It is called from one wrapper of `changed()` when the instrument's signature changed (preset key, user preset, instrument kind, sample; `chsSig`), and from `chgRevoice` (the Change browser, whose own re-voicing code and note list were removed).
  1. `chsWarm`: tables and samples the new sound needs are made first.
  2. `chsCut`: the track gets a fresh input node. The new path is wired before the old one is taken away (a disconnect followed by a connect left one block of silence, measured). The old input goes through a gain that fades to zero in 4 ms, with everything that hangs on it: notes of the look-ahead, held notes, tails, clip chains. Nothing has to be tracked per note.
  3. Voice engines (`al-poly`, `al-grain`, Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM, Orbit): the engine that was playing stays on the old input and fades with it, exact to the sample; then it is emptied and kept as the track's spare, and the spare of last time takes over at once with its samples and tables still loaded. A message to an engine takes up to one output buffer to arrive, so telling the engine to stop was not exact enough (measured: 3 to 15 ms). The spare is made when Play is pressed, once per track (`chsSpare`); an engine with no voices does nothing in its loop. Venom cuts hard when told to stop and has effects inside, so it always gets a fresh engine, which is also kept ready.
  4. Effects with memory on that track are replaced by fresh nodes (`chsFxOut`); the old node fades out in 5 ms into the place it played into, and that fade starts before the fresh node is built (a Reverb takes about 40 ms to build).
  5. `chsResched`: the scheduler runs once more for this one track (`play.only`) from the moment of the cut to where it had got to (`play.sched`), with picking up switched on (`play.chs`). It also handles the 150 ms before a loop end, when the scheduler is already in the next round and the sound is not.
  6. The fade of the old sound is set after the new notes have been handed over. Both are set one output buffer ahead (5 to 12 ms): a fade set at the present moment can already be over when the audio thread gets to it, which would be a hard cut, and a message to an engine needs that long. If handing over took longer than planned, the old sound stays until the new one is there.
- **Picking up is one function: `chsNote`**, called from `schedule()` for every note that began before `from` and has not ended, when `play.chs` is set: after a clean switch, and whenever `schedule` is called with `first` (Play, which is also every jump, and the start of every loop round). Which engine does what:
  - Made of browser nodes (Sampler plain, Growl, Grid, SFX, Perc, drum kit and drum instruments, and the basic synths, Plant and Wavetable when the voice engine is off): the note is built with its real start time inside `pastShim` (it existed for held notes since the preset feature), so envelopes are where they would be and samples continue at the right spot. A 4 ms fade-in.
  - `al-poly` (basic synths, Plant, Acid, Wavetable): the note starts now with the length that is left, and the engine gets the age of the note (`q[8]`): amplitude envelope, filter envelope, FM envelope, the LFOs and the wavetable position start where they would be by now; the level is reached in 4 ms. The oscillators start fresh.
  - `al-grain` (Sampler in grain mode): the same with `q[6]`, and the scan through the sample carries on from where it would be.
  - Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM, Orbit, Venom: the cheap way, the note starts now with the length that is left and its own attack.
- **Effect presets**: `chsFxWatch` (in the same wrapper of `changed()`, before the rest of the change is worked through) compares `f.p.pre` of every effect with what it was; the kinds with memory are in `CHSMEM`. Master effects are covered too.
- **No cost while nothing changes**: there is no timer and no per-note bookkeeping. `schedule()` has two extra comparisons per note. The v0.183 note list (every note pushed into a list while playing) is gone.
- Direct edits outside the new block: `schedule()` (one track only, the chase hook, the loop end), the two worklets (the age of a note), `pvPlay` and `gvPlay` (the age travels with the note), `pastShim` (a source that would have ended already must not throw), `stop()` (the sweep fix), `chgSwap`.

- Venom, 74 ms: the first time a Venom wavetable is used it has to be worked out (70 to 140 ms on this machine). The old sound stays for that time instead of a silence. The second switch to a preset with tables that exist: 16 ms.
- 13 to 19 ms is the look-ahead of one output buffer (12 ms here) plus the 4 ms fade. On a machine with a smaller buffer it is shorter (5 ms plus 4 ms at the least).

In some runs one block dips by 10 to 20 dB on a voice engine (the message with the note arrived after its start time). The Sampler in grain mode has a real dip of about 10 ms when the new preset uses another sample (its grains fade in).
