      plant    before  -26.8 dB | quietest 5 ms  -33.3 dB | after  -25.7 dB
      wtab     before  -24.1 dB | quietest 5 ms  -30.0 dB | after  -35.2 dB
      acid     before  -20.9 dB | quietest 5 ms  -22.7 dB | after  -20.6 dB
      sampler  before  -24.3 dB | quietest 5 ms -110.8 dB | after  -24.2 dB
      venom    before  -16.8 dB | quietest 5 ms  -17.7 dB | after   -4.7 dB
      orb      before  -20.7 dB | quietest 5 ms  -21.4 dB | after  -18.2 dB
      vfm      before  -32.6 dB | quietest 5 ms  -36.1 dB | after  -17.8 dB
      grid     before  -31.2 dB | quietest 5 ms  -31.9 dB | after  -25.4 dB
    

- **Exports unchanged** (`tools/engine-tests/export_same.py`, new, run on v0.183 and v0.184): Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo), and the checksums of the samples are equal for Fractals Kick, Fractals Bass, a Saw, a Pad, an Acid, a Wavetable and a Venom clip. Plant, Sampler and Drum kit renders differ from run to run in v0.183 as well (random detune, grains, noise), so they prove nothing either way. `orbit.py` and `growl.py`: the same output on both versions apart from timings.
- `tools/ui-tests/change_live.py`, `change_live_gap.py`, `design.py`: pass. The gap test of v0.183 now shows no silent block for 11 of 12 swaps; Orbit to Plant has one (see below).
- The demo song, playing at bar 81: a preset change on each of its 24 instrument tracks, one after the other: the cut and the picking up take 1 to 32 ms of the page's time per switch (mostly 1 to 3 ms; the long ones are a first-time drum sample or an effect being built). Play from the same spot: the first scheduling round takes as long as before (7 to 31 ms against 4 to 31 ms).

### Not done, or different from what one might expect
- **Changing to another kind of instrument in the Change browser** can leave a hole of some milliseconds the first time (measured once: Orbit to Plant, one silent 5 ms block): the engine of the new kind has to be built. A second swap to that kind has none.
- **Venom**: 70 to 140 ms of the old sound the first time a wavetable is used (above). Every Venom switch leaves the old engine behind, stopped and disconnected; they are not removed until the audio engine restarts.
- **Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM, Orbit and Venom start a picked up note with their attack**, not in the middle of their envelope. Their particles start fresh as well.
- **The oscillators of a picked up note start fresh** on every engine, so the phase is not where it would have been. With a 4 ms crossfade this is not a click, but a slow beating between detuned oscillators starts over.
- **A shared Space (Plant's reverb send on the master) is not cleared**: the old sound's reverb there rings out. Return tracks are not cleared either. Only the effects on the track itself.
- A knob turn is not a preset change: held notes of a voice engine keep their old settings to their end, as before.
- A drum hit is "picked up" only inside its note length, and then from the right spot of its sample.
- **Each track with a voice engine now has two engines** after its first Play (one spare). Measured load of the demo while playing: the same within noise (27 to 33 per cent before and after on this machine).
- With the voice engine switched off in the settings, notes longer than about a second are silent after that second, before and after this release. Not looked into.
- The Cubase manual and the MIDI controller texts could not be read (part 2).

### Not verified
- **Nobody has heard it.** "Gone within 20 ms", "no click" and "no hole" rest on recorded numbers from a headless browser with a 10 ms output buffer, not on ears and not on a real sound card. On Windows with a large buffer (2048 samples) the times will be longer by about the buffer.
- The Mac build, a real MIDI keyboard for the held keys, and projects with tempo changes inside a picked up note were not tried.
- The switch in the 150 ms before a loop end was hit only once or twice in the test runs (no note missing, none twice).

### Left
- Envelope fast-forward for the particle instruments and Venom. A phase that carries on for the voice engine. Clearing the shared Space. Removing old Venom engines. Task E (CPU and background freeze). The browsers in the new look.

### Release
- v0.184: commit 7849602, artifact version 207, project doc `claude/audiolab-device-backlog-run-I.md`.
- **Desktop build**: run 45699105826 for this commit succeeded (3 min 30 s, started 20:52 UTC).

## 25 November 2026, v0.185: pinned devices (task M)
> "Let's have a system for every single device, also effects, and it's going to be a pin button next to the X. [...] that's gonna create a window where you can have a single device just pinned over your arrangement or anywhere on the screen. It doesn't need to be resizable, but I want it to be mostly borderless. Just have like a grabbing handle in the same way that we have with the mixer. [...] It also needs to be visible that a device in the devices panel is pinned. [...] I also want that for the visual devices that show like volume and spectrogram and whatever."

### What it does
- **A pin button** in the title row of every instrument, effect, MIDI effect and analyser (also the particle devices and the master chain), just left of the remove ✕ (instruments have no ✕: at the right end). Tooltip: "Pin: show this device in its own window over the arrangement, so you can watch and change it while you work on other tracks". It stays visible when the device is slid shut.
- **The window**: the device itself, on a solid dark body (no glass, so a moving picture never makes glass recalculate), a thin border and a shadow, not resizable. On top a slim tab in the mixer's style: dotted grip, device name, a coloured dot with the track name, and a lit pin that unpins. Drag the tab to move it; it stays inside the screen (also when the window is made smaller). A click anywhere in a window brings it to the front. Several windows at once, from one track or from many.
- **Where a new window goes**: above the device, over the arrangement (not over the device panel when there is room), moving down and right until it covers no more than a quarter of a window that is already there; on a full screen the place with the least overlap.
- **In the device panel** a pinned device leaves a slim placeholder (132 px, dashed, striped): its name with a lit pin (click: unpin), and "Pinned. Shown in its own window. Click to bring it to the front." Decision: a placeholder rather than the full device, so every control exists in exactly one place and nothing is drawn twice.
- **It is the same device**: every knob, picture, preset menu, Change, on/off, the arrow and the remove ✕ work in the window. A value changed somewhere else (automation, a macro, a modulator, the bridge) moves the knob in the window too (checked every 120 ms; only changed controls are painted).
- **Pictures keep running** while another track is selected: a spectrum or meter of the drums can be watched while working on the lead.
- **Kept with the project** (`S.pins`: track, device, place, height), so they come back after a reload and with the project file. Not in the undo history: undo and redo keep the open windows. A pinned device whose track or device is removed closes its window; undo of that removal brings the window back. When Change replaces a pinned effect in its place, the window follows the new effect.

### How it works (for the next person)
- The look of the panel is written for `#fxin` (345 rules). At start `pinCss()` rewrites those selectors to `:is(#fxin,.pinb)`, which keeps their weight, so a window body (`.pinb`) gets the exact panel look.
- A pinned device is rendered by the panel itself. `renderFx` is wrapped: for every track with a pinned device whose window needs a new copy (no copy yet, the device object changed, or an event in its window), the panel is rendered once for that track with `selT` swapped (`PIN.inner`), the pinned devices are taken out (`pinHarvest`) and moved into their windows, then the panel is rendered for the selected track as usual and its pinned devices are replaced by placeholders. Their controls get their own ids (`pn:<track>/<device>:fx:...`) so the next panel render does not delete them. Selecting a track renders only the panel (measured: median 31 ms with three pins on other tracks against 32 to 36 ms without pins; pin or unpin 33 to 39 ms).
- The panel's event handlers are recorded when they are added to `#fxin` (`FXH`, a line in the existing `fxin.addEventListener` wrapper) and also listen on every window (`pinMirror`; the panel's drag-to-scroll is left out). An early capture listener on the window (`PINCAP`, registered before all others) makes an event inside a window run with that window's track as the selected track and its pictures as the picture lists; it is put back after the event (the last window listener, a timer, and the start of every frame). `changed` and `renderFx` step out of the swap while they run.
- Pictures: `drawIV` is wrapped; once per picture frame (`FRN`) the windows are drawn with their own track and lists (`pinDraw`). The panel's lists no longer hold the canvases of its pinned devices, so nothing is drawn twice.
- Pointing at a picture in a window wakes the pictures like a picture in the panel does.

### Measured (headless Chromium, 1600 × 1000, demo song, `tools/ui-tests/pin.py` and a rate script)
Three pinned windows (Orbit instrument from a new track; Pro EQ and Spectrum from Kick), a third track (Bass) selected:

| state | picture passes per second | window redraws per second | canvases drawn per second | app frames per second |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| playing | 23 | 69 | 161 | 23 |
| stopped, just clicked | 39.5 | 118.5 | 276 | 39.5 |
| stopped, calm (no input for 10 s) | 2 | 6 | 14 | 53 |
| playing, light mode (this machine's default) | 16 | 48 | 48 | 23 |

One pass per app frame at most, three windows per pass (no double drawing). The app's work per frame while playing went from 6.8 ms (no pins) to 9.9 ms with these three windows, most of it the five pictures of the Plant (Lead) or Orbit's particles drawn in software by the headless browser; stopped and calm 0.6 ms.

### Tested (`python3 tools/ui-tests/pin.py index.html <out dir>`, with Playwright's mouse)
- Pin the Orbit instrument (new track), Pro EQ and a Spectrum (Kick) with the pin buttons; select Bass: three windows, all with their device; the Orbit picture and the Spectrum picture change while the song plays.
- A knob in the Orbit window (Scale) dragged up: 100 % to 150 %, one undo step; the same parameter set from outside: the window shows 30 %; after unpinning, the panel shows 30 %.
- Drag by the tab: moved by 300, 120; dragged far off: right edge 1600, bottom 1000 of a 1600 × 1000 screen; dragged to the top left: 0, 26 (the tab stays on screen).
- The ✕ in the pinned EQ: window closes; undo: device and window back; redo: closed; undo: back.
- Reload: the three windows come back at their places.
- Unpin from the window's tab: the window closes and Orbit is back in the panel. Unpin from the placeholder's pin: same. A click on the placeholder brings the window to the front.
- Also: on/off and the arrow (slide shut) in a pinned window; a preset menu in a pinned Acid window (the track's preset changes, the selected track stays Bass); Change in a pinned window opens the browser and Escape puts it back; removing the whole track of a pinned instrument closes its window, undo brings it back; a pinned master chain device; pinning with the device panel slid down (hidden) and with the panel in its own floating window.
- No page errors (the only console line is the service worker's fetch message, also in v0.184).
- `design.py` and `change_live.py` give the same results as v0.184 apart from live levels and timings (noise), plus the pin in the title row.
- **Exports unchanged** (`tools/engine-tests/export_same.py`): Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo), equal checksums for Kick, Bass, Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom (Plant, Sampler and Drum kit differ from run to run in v0.184 as well).
- Screenshots looked at: the panel with a pinned device (placeholder), three windows over the arrangement while playing, a pinned Spectrum close up (the kick's spectrum while the Kick & Bass group is selected), the windows with the panel hidden and with the panel floating.

### Not done, or different from what one might expect
- **Modulators** (LFO, envelope, Vector Map and the others in the left column) and macro devices have no pin; the drum pads inside a kit and the Kick designer inside a pad are pinned together with their kit, not alone.
- The panel shows a placeholder, not the full device, while it is pinned (see above). MIDI learn and the "show modulation on the knob" marks address the panel's knob ids, so a knob in a pinned window does not answer to a MIDI controller mapped before; the value itself still follows.
- A window can cover another window's tab; click the window in front or the placeholder in the panel to bring the other one up. The floating device panel (when it is popped out) can sit above pinned windows until one is clicked.
- Keyboard keys pressed while a button in a window has the focus act on the selected track, not on the window's track (only text fields and menus in a window type into its device).
- Venom's own big window and the browsers are unchanged.

### Not verified
- The real desktop app on the user's Mac and Windows (frame rate with real glass, a trackpad), a real MIDI controller, very small screens.

### Left
- A pin for modulators. Task E (CPU and background freeze). The browsers in the new look.

### Release
- v0.185: commit 2f380ec, artifact version 208, project doc `claude/audiolab-device-backlog-run-M.md`.
- **Desktop build**: run 45705272218 for this commit succeeded (3 min 51 s, started 21:32 UTC).

## 25 November 2026: v0.186, Sub, a sub bass instrument (from the user's message: "create a device with the same design language. For subbass ... do some deep research online")

### The user's request
"Let's also create a device with the same design language. For subbass ... do some deep research online. How a sub bass should sound, what the best algorithms are, what the best presets are. And this is one of the most important devices because Audiolab is for electronic dance music mostly. So sub bass is very important ... decide yourself what research you should do to have the best outcome."

### Research (two research passes on the web, about 60 pages read; what is well supported, what is one source, what is a guess is said per line)

**How a sub should sound**
- Range: the sub band is 20 to 60 or 80 Hz; synth bass notes sit at 40 to 80 Hz (MusicRadar "ultimate guide to sub bass", Native Instruments blog, EDMProd; well supported). Best keys F to A, 43.7 to 55 Hz (EDMProd, one source); "centre on low G (49 Hz), do not go below E flat (39 Hz)" (Computer Music, one source). Both agree. The Key level control and the default picture are built around 49 Hz for that reason.
- Club systems: ordinary subs are usable to the mid 40s Hz, 40 Hz is common, 30 Hz is rare (ProSoundWeb "25 Hz is the new 50 Hz", one live engineer's measurements). No source gives a standard cutoff. The picture marks 30, 40 and 60 Hz for that reason.
- Per genre (thin evidence, said so by the sources): trance and techno most energy at 50 to 60 Hz, dubstep mid to high 40s (ProSoundWeb); dubstep sub in the 30 to 60 Hz octave (Sound On Sound "Dubstep basics"); 808 notes 32 to 49 Hz; drum and bass around E; psytrance bass between E and A with less than an octave of usable range (Psynews forum: folklore, but it matches the above). Nothing reliable found for house.
- Wave: sine first; triangle or light saturation for small speakers (MusicRadar, Native Instruments, EDMProd: well supported). Mike Senior (Sound On Sound) prefers triangle over saw or square for a sub layer and warns against detuned subs because the beating moves the level of the fundamental. So the instrument has one voice and no detune.
- Small speakers: they stop at 100 to 200 Hz, phones play little under 100 Hz (Aalto virtual bass paper DAFx 2020; Mastering The Mix). The ear rebuilds a missing low note from its overtones (Schouten's residue pitch; Licklider 1954; Wikipedia "Missing fundamental"). The Waves MaxxBass patent (US 5,930,373) asks for at least three overtones in a row above the speaker's cutoff: for notes of 40 to 60 Hz the 3rd to 5th, for 60 to 120 Hz the 2nd to 4th, anything higher at least 12 dB lower. Even overtones alone are heard an octave too high, so a full row is needed (Aalto paper; NTU thesis by Oo on the choice of nonlinear device). **No source gives a simple "2nd and 3rd at X dB" rule.** The level of the "Phones" knob (100% = the strongest added overtone as loud as the note, falling 9 dB per octave) is my choice, guided by the 3 to 10 dB per octave weighting in the Aalto paper.
- Mono: below about 100 Hz (MusicRadar), 80 to 100 Hz, sometimes 150 or 200 Hz (folklore). Reasons: clubs and small speakers sum to mono, phase cancellation, vinyl (the vinyl rule is disputed by a Masterdisk engineer). The instrument is mono, and its optional Width only touches overtones above 150 Hz.
- Phase: a click is a fast jump in level; a wave that starts at a random point with no fade-in can start at its top (KVR thread quoting a Waldorf FAQ, one source). Free-running oscillators give every note another level and punch (forum). Ableton Operator has a retrigger switch that "restarts the waveform at the same position in its phase" (Ableton manual). So: Same start, on by default, and a Start phase knob.
- Fade times: no reputable source gives milliseconds, only "raise the attack until the click goes". A rule of thumb from the KVR thread: a change over t ms spreads over about 1000/t Hz. Release 50 to 100 ms (Beat Kitchen, one source). The defaults (5 ms in, 60 ms out) are my choice from those.
- 808: pitch drop 1 to 2 semitones in 10 to 30 ms or 2 to 4 in 20 to 50 ms (Audeobox, one source); 44 ms pitch decay with 1 s amp decay (MusicRadar); the real TR-808 "sighs" from about 58 to 48 Hz over 300 ms and has a 6 ms transient at the start (Werner, DAFx 2014; Sound On Sound "Practical bass drum synthesis"). Decay 1 to 5 s by tempo, release 100 to 250 ms, soft clip, low pass at 500 to 800 Hz (Audeobox, EDMProd; single sources). Glide times: nothing found, my choice.
- Loudness against the key (ISO 226:2003, worked out from the standard's parameters as in the University of Surrey toolbox): a 40 Hz tone needs 14 dB more level than an 80 Hz tone to sound as loud at 40 phon, 12 dB at 60, 10 dB at 80, 9 dB at 90. So about 10 dB per octave at club level. "Key level" at 100% is that slope, limited to 12 dB either way.
- Levels and ducking: **no reputable figures** for sub against kick in dB, or for ducking depth per genre. What there is: up to 6 dB of ducking before it is heard as pumping, attack as fast as possible (Attack Magazine); 808 peaks at -6 to -3 dB (one source). The device's level is set by measurement against the Fractals kick instead (see tests).

**Algorithms**
- A sum of exact sine overtones has no aliasing at all as long as every overtone stays under half the sample rate; PolyBLEP and its relatives only reduce it (Välimäki, Pekonen, Nam, JASA 2012: two-point PolyBLEP is clean by ear up to about 2.1 kHz, no absolute figures). For a sub the sum is cheap (a saw at 50 Hz has 441 overtones under Nyquist, but nobody needs more than a few dozen in a sub). Chosen: a sum of up to 96 overtones.
- Drive without aliasing: oversampling and antiderivative anti-aliasing (Parker, Zavalishin, Le Bivic DAFx 2016; Chowdhury's CCRMA notebook) both only reduce it. Because the wave here is periodic and known, the driven wave is worked out for one cycle and turned back into overtone levels (a Fourier series), which is exact. This is the idea behind Chebyshev waveshaping ("additive synthesis with one oscillator", Csound manual; Puckette's book, which notes it is only exact at full level, so the drive sits before the envelope here). That this replaces oversampling is my own conclusion; it is confirmed by the measurements below.
- DC: the usual DC blocker with R = 0.995 cuts at about 35 Hz at 44.1 kHz, inside the sub band (worked out from J. O. Smith's formula). Here there is no DC to remove: the constant part of the Fourier series is left out. Low cut is an ordinary two-pole filter (minimum phase: linear phase rings before the note, Mastering The Mix and Craig Anderton), off by default.
- Known instruments: SubLab (one oscillator, sampler and "X-Sub", a sub held between 30 and 65 Hz plus a row of overtones with odd and even balance, that skips filter and effects; MusicRadar review; Future Audio Workshop's blog describes shifting the phases of the overtones to lower the peaks). Serum's sub oscillator with "direct out" past filter and effects. MaxxBass and Renaissance Bass (split at 60 to 110 Hz, make overtones of what is below, mix with or replace the original; Waves manuals). Submarine, bx_subsynth and dbx 120 make lower octaves instead. From these: the overtones for small speakers are added after the drive and do not touch the sub, and their phases are spread (Schroeder phases) so they raise the peak as little as possible.

**Presets**: sine with short fades for the club; triangle or saturation for small speakers (tube drive 60%, mix 40%: Native Instruments); techno rumble on F with slow attack, low pass and overdrive (Attack Magazine); drum and bass sine sub around E under a reese; dubstep two sines an octave apart (Sound On Sound); 808 as above; psytrance one saw, low pass a third open, no attack, short decay, low sustain, no release, 16th notes that skip the kick (one blog and the Psynews forum; a sine layer under it is disputed among producers).

Sources: musicradar.com/news/ultimate-guide-to-sub-bass · native-instruments.com/blogs/blog/sub-bass · edmprod.com/sub-bass · edmprod.com/808s · soundonsound.com/sound-advice/q-whats-best-way-create-sub-bass-synth-sounds · soundonsound.com/techniques/dubstep-basics · soundonsound.com/techniques/practical-bass-drum-synthesis · soundonsound.com/sound-advice/q-how-do-you-make-only-low-frequencies-mono · attackmagazine.com/technique/synth-secrets/rumbling-techno-bass-ableton-operator · attackmagazine.com/technique/ten-production-tips-for-better-basslines/4 · prosoundweb.com/going-deeper-25-hz-is-the-new-50-hz · pressreader.com (Computer Music, 10 August 2016) · acris.aalto.fi (Moliner et al., "Virtual bass system", DAFx 2020) · patents.google.com/patent/US5930373 · dr.ntu.edu.sg (Oo, nonlinear devices in virtual bass) · aes.org/e-lib/browse.cfm?elib=8288 (Ben-Tzur and Colloms 1999; only the listing was read) · en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missing_fundamental · IoSR-Surrey MatlabToolbox iso226.m · mac.kaist.ac.kr/pubs/ValimakiPeknenNam-jasa2012.pdf · ccrma.stanford.edu/~jatin/Notebooks/adaa.html · dafx.de (Parker et al. 2016) · csound.com/manual/opcodes/chebyshevpoly · msp.ucsd.edu/techniques (Puckette, waveshaping) · ccrma.stanford.edu/~jos/fp/DC_Blocker.html · dafx14.fau.de (Werner, TR-808 bass drum) · musicradar.com/reviews/future-audio-workshop-sublab · futureaudioworkshop.com (sub-bass deep dive) · ableton.com/en/manual/live-instrument-reference · Waves MaxxBass and Renaissance Bass user guides · masteringthemix.com · craiganderton.org · masterdisk.com/post/mastering-for-vinyl · audeobox.com (808s in FL Studio) · beatkitchen.io · kvraudio.com/forum/viewtopic.php?p=8488138 · psynews.org/forums/topic/72935 · synapse11.blogspot.com (psytrance bass tutorial). Not read: the Larsen and Aarts book (only its abstract), the Bilbao 2017 paper, ISO 226:2023.

### Built (names `sbs` / `SBS`, code before `askChoice`; `sbsInit();` sits right after `orbInit();`)
- **Sub** (`PRESETS.sbass`, first in "Bass & acid" in the instrument browser, with its picture; the old basic "Sub bass" stays). Worklet `al-sbs` (`SBSWL`, added to both module lists in `wlLoad`; `pvAny`, the engine list of the clean preset switch `CHSENG` and the list inside `chsCut` cover it).
- **The sound engine**: one voice at a time, a sum of up to 96 exact overtones worked out with one sine and one cosine per sample. `sbsCore()` is shared by the page and the worklet:
  - `stat()`: the fixed part of the wave as overtone levels. Shape (sine towards a triangle, square or saw, each built from its overtones with a soft roll-off at the top), 2nd and 3rd, then Drive (a soft ceiling, tanh; Asymmetry moves the wave off centre before it, which adds even overtones) worked out on one cycle and turned back into overtones, then the Octave up layer. The note itself always keeps the same level and phase, whatever is added. Worked out on the page (1 to 2 ms with drive, kept in a cache) and sent to the engine.
  - `dyn()`: per note and 3000 times a second: adds the overtones for small speakers (those between about 100 Hz and the 10th overtone, strongest just above 125 Hz, falling 9 dB per octave, spread in phase) and fades out any overtone that comes near 47% of the sample rate, also while the pitch drops or glides.
  - `keyLv()`: Key level.
  - No DC by construction. Nothing is oversampled and nothing is clipped; there is no limiter inside.
- **Notes**: Same start (every note starts at Start phase; off: a note carries on where the wave was). A new note fades the one before over the time of its own fade-in, so two notes never add up to more than one. Glide: Off, Legato (a note that starts while another is held slides there, the wave runs on unbroken, the envelope does not start again; when the later note ends first, the pitch slides back to the one still held), Always. Pitch drop (amount and time). Octave shift.
- **Envelope**: fade-in as an S curve, decay and release with no corner at their start, and the whole envelope runs through a 0.4 ms smoother, so no setting can make a step.
- **After the voice**: Tone (two-pole low pass) and Low cut (two-pole high pass), both skipped entirely at the ends of their range; Width (overtones above 150 Hz turned in opposite directions left and right; the note and everything under 150 Hz stay in the middle).
- **Duck to kick**: a button. The app has no sidechain that listens to another track's sound in an export: the Sidechain effect is a pump in time with the song whose `f.p.src` only marks the source track (for the cable and so the source keeps playing), and the Sidechain follower modulator reads a level meter once per screen frame and does nothing in an export. So the button adds the Sidechain effect after the device with the kick track marked as its source (it looks for a Kick designer track, then a track called kick, then a drum kit), and takes it away again. One undo step each.
- **Picture** (`sbsDraw`, `sbsPaint`): one cycle of the wave on top with the start point and the peak level in dB (red when it would clip); below it the note and every overtone as bars against note names (top) and Hz (bottom), with dashed lines at 30, 40 and 60 Hz and a red hatched area "Small speakers cannot play this" up to 120 Hz. The bars include the two filters. While a note plays the picture shows that key and glows. It is painted once into a spare canvas and only copied until a setting, the key or the size changes (0 paints per second at rest, measured). A click plays the last note.
- **Controls**: always shown: Shape, 2nd, 3rd, Phones, Attack, Decay, Sustain, Release. Page Tone: Shape goes to (triangle, square, saw), Duck to kick, Drive, Asymmetry, Octave up, Tone, Low cut, Width. Page Pitch and level: Glide (off, legato, always), Same start, Octave, Pitch drop, Drop time, Glide, Start phase, Velocity, Key level. The device gain is the bar at the right end, as everywhere. Every control has a tooltip in plain words (`SBSTIP`).
- **22 presets in 8 groups** (`SBSPRE`, the preset menu shows the groups): Club (Pure club sub = the default, Warm club sub, Even loudness sub), House (round, Deep house organ, Garage square), Techno (Rumble, Hard techno drive), Drum and bass (sub, Jungle triangle bite), Dubstep (two octaves, heavy), 808 and trap (long, short, with knock, slide), Psytrance (sub layer, short saw bass, rolling sub), Small speakers (Small speaker sub, Phone and laptop sub, Mono sub with wide top).
- The play button in the title row and the test note of the browser play the last key (or G at 49 Hz) for this instrument instead of middle C.
- New value format for degrees (Start phase).

### Tested (headless, no page errors on load; `tools/engine-tests/sub.py`, `tools/ui-tests/sub.py`)
- **Tuning**: keys 24 to 72, 4 s notes, frequency from the phase of the wave: off by at most 0.00012 Hz (the limit of the measurement).
- **Spectrum**, every preset on keys 24, 33, 72 and 96 (33 Hz to 2.1 kHz), envelope held and pitch drop off, 1.5 s window: the strongest thing that is not an overtone is **168.9 dB under the note** (that is the rounding of the 32 bit render). With everything pushed (full saw, drive 100%, asymmetry 60%, Phones 100%, octave 50%) on keys 24, 48 and 84: 173 dB under. Levels of overtones 2 to 6 per preset are printed by the test.
- **DC**: 177 dB or more under the peak on every preset.
- **Clicks**: largest step between two samples, and largest change of that step, against the same numbers of the steady sine (a step of -60 dB would read about 80 in the second number): note start and end 1.0 and 2.4; attack 1 ms with release 5 ms 1.35 and 5.9; notes back to back 1.65 and 3.8; legato glide 1.0 and 1.5; glide of 0 ms (it is given 3 ms) 1.0 and 1.5; 16th notes at 145 BPM 1.6 and 4.4; long release cut by the next note 1.96 and 3.5; Same start off 1.0 and 2.4.
- **Same phase**: nine notes at uneven distances, 20 to 180 ms of each compared with the first: difference exactly 0 with Same start on (1.95 times the peak with it off, as it should be).
- **Left and right**: 21 presets identical sample for sample. Mono sub, wide top: under 150 Hz the difference is 182 dB under the signal, above 150 Hz the side is 8.7 dB under the mid; summed to mono it loses 0.07 dB.
- **Level**: the note is at -14.0 dBFS through a new track, about 13 dB under the peak of the Fractals kick (-1.0 dBFS) and about 8 dB under it in average level, for 16th notes with the kick's beat left out. Presets peak between -15.9 and -8.3 dBFS (Phone and laptop sub is the loudest), the same for short notes as for held ones.
- **Duck to kick**: the sub is 6.9 dB down 0.03 beats after each beat, 1.3 dB down a quarter beat later, back after 0.8 beats; two renders identical.
- **Two renders bit-identical** (six presets, a phrase with legato, glide and velocity). **A note moved by 77, 293 or 1000 samples sounds the same** (difference below 1e-20), so nothing depends on block edges.
- **Cost**: 32 s of sound render in 1.28 s (pure), 1.6 to 1.8 s (808, Phones, wide), 3.1 s with everything at its heaviest, against 1.02 s for the same project with one short note. So 8 to 25 ms per second of sound, 64 ms at the heaviest, on the test machine. Silent, the engine returns at once.
- **Live**: `preset_switch.py` with Sub added (run with `ONLY=Sub`): old preset gone after 25 ms, long note carries on, 16th notes all there once, Play inside a long note sounds. Keys held by hand with legato: no errors.
- **Page**: nothing outside the device on both pages and shut; title order as on Orbit plus the pin; no control without a tooltip; no dash characters in texts; the preset menu's 8 groups; presets and three undos; buttons and undo; Duck to kick on, off, undo; shut and open; the picture while playing; a pinned window; the browser tile. `devices_all.py`: all devices as before, Sub 916 x 253. Screenshots looked at: both pages, 808 with knock, Phone and laptop sub, playing, shut, the browser tile.
- **Unchanged**: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo); `export_same.py` checksums equal for Kick, Bass, Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom (Plant, Sampler and Drum kit differ from run to run on the old version too).

### Not verified, and what is a guess
- **Nobody has listened to it.** Everything was set by measurement and from the research. The presets need the user's ears, most of all the levels of the Phones overtones, the drive amounts and the 808 times.
- The level of the small-speaker overtones, the default fade times, the glide times and the 10 ms class of note-to-note fades are my choices; the sources give no numbers for them.
- Drive sits before the envelope, so it does not get cleaner as a note dies away (an 808 keeps its colour to the end). A real saturator would. This is the price of having no aliasing.
- While the pitch drops fast, the overtone levels are updated 3000 times a second and glide in between. Not measured as a spectrum (a sweep has no steady overtones); the step test shows no jumps.
- Real mouse or trackpad, the Mac build, MIDI learn and automation of the knobs, modulators on the knobs.
- Tone and Low cut jump over a very small step when they are turned to the very end of their range while a note sounds (they switch off there).

### Left
- Listen and tune the presets with the user.
- A duck that follows the kick's own notes (the engine would have to be told the kick's note times); today it follows the beat.
- Tone that follows the key, a second pitch envelope shape, a "sub only" output for layering, drag in the picture (the bars as handles for 2nd and 3rd).
- Task E (CPU and background freeze).

### Release
- v0.186: commit 60cf96e, artifact version 209, project doc `claude/audiolab-sub-bass-research-and-device.md`.
- It was built on top of v0.185 (pinned devices), which another session released while this one was testing; the Sub block was put onto the new version and all its tests were run again there.
- **Desktop build**: run 45707822024 (number 81) for this commit, started 21:49 UTC: succeeded (release, Mac and Windows).

## 25 November 2026, v0.187: the Performance tab (task O)
> "let's rework the light mode [...] we should have a main toggle for light mode [...] what each effect costs [...] The frames per second [...] a little bit more designed [...] a completely separate tab under the settings [...] an automatic button [...] a slider for when you want more effects or more optimized [...] the more optimized go even back [...] for really, really weak computers. So no transparency [...] it should only go in there if you choose it yourself. Not even in automatic mode. [...] some other color [...] snappy, extremely fast, very, very light mode."

### What it does
- **New Settings tab "Performance"** (gauge icon, mint colour, between Interface and Glass & light). Everything about light mode, effect levels, glass cost and frame rate moved here. "Glass & light" keeps only the look (preview, transparency, blur amount, edge light strength) with a link to Performance; General keeps a one-line link where the Light mode card was.
- **Light mode switch** at the top. On: the slider is used. Off: Full effects, the slider stays where it was but dimmed, and the line under it says so. Choosing a stop while it is off turns it on.
- **One slider, four stops**: Lightest (a separate mint pill, behind a dotted divider) | Lighter, Optimised, Full effects. Click, drag along the track, or arrow keys, Home and End. One plain line under it says what the chosen stop does; for Lightest, in mint: "Fastest and lightest: no transparency, no blur, no shadows, no animations." Decision: no confirmation for Lightest (the user leaned that way; it is undone by one click).
  - Full effects: refraction, live blur, edge light, animations.
  - Optimised: the v0.170 Optimised (blur from a ready-made picture), or the user's own mix of effects.
  - Lighter: the old Zero effects plus the old Light mode's lighter pictures (visualisers and spectrum 30 a second, simpler clip drawing, still logo, longer note look-ahead).
  - Lightest: Lighter plus body class `fxmin`: no backdrop-filter anywhere, solid panel colours from the theme (`rgb(var(--gtr))`, `rgb(var(--gtr2))`), no outer shadows on windows, menus, sheets, pinned devices, the mixer's master strip, no text shadows, no CSS filters, no gradients on the big panels, no animations or transitions, the glass rims (`::before` with a mask) not drawn, `#hglass` without its own compositing layer, and the glass layers taken out of the page: `#arrBlur`, `#arrBack` (set to 0 x 0 and removed) and the SVG with the refraction maps. They come back in the same order when another stop is chosen.
- **Automatic**: measures the screen rate (desktop app: the real rate) at Lighter while the visuals run (the arrangement and what shows through the glass are redrawn every frame and the pictures are kept awake), reads cores, memory and the audio engine load, then moves the slider up in front of the user: Optimised with a fixed edge, Optimised, Full effects, about 1.4 s each, and stops at the richest one that keeps 95% of the screen rate. Full effects is skipped with 2 cores or 2 GB, or when the audio engine is above 60%. It never chooses Lightest. It says in a few words where it stopped and why ("Stopped at Optimised. Full effects reached 88 of 120."), shows a small trail of what each step reached, and Undo puts back what was there before. The frame rate hint's button (in the tab) is now "Automatic" too.
- **What each effect costs**: four rows (Refraction, Blur Off / Picture / Live, Edge light, Animations), each with an icon, a name, 1 to 3 dots (filled when on, orange for the expensive one) and a switch; the details are in tooltips. A second list "Savers": softer blur while playing, half-size picture behind glass, note cache, sleeping tracks (the same inputs as before). Switching an effect by hand moves the slider to Optimised with that mix (or Full when all are on).
- **Frame rate**: a big number in green, yellow or red next to "/ 120 Hz", bars for the last 10 seconds with a dashed line at the screen rate, the lowest value of the last 10 seconds and the app's work per frame. Drawn 4 times a second, only while the tab is open (inside the Settings loop). The frame limit (as fast as the screen, 120, 60) moved here.
- **Stored settings**: new `UI.pm = {on, st, user}`; `UI.fx` stays the effective set of effects, so all v0.170 code works unchanged. First start: old Zero effects -> Lighter, old Optimised (also a custom mix) -> Optimised with the same mix, old Full -> Light mode off, old Light mode switched on by hand -> Lighter. Light mode switching itself on for a weak computer (and with three projects open) still happens, as Lighter, unless the stop was chosen by hand; "Turn off" in its notice puts back what was there. The CPU popup button, the View menu item and the command (Mod+Shift+L) switch the same Light mode.

### Changed on purpose (standing rules)
- The old Light mode capped the screen at 30 frames a second and used fewer synth voices while playing live (Plant unison, granular density, the basic synth). Both are gone: the rules say never drop frames on purpose and never lower audio quality. Pictures (visualisers, spectrum) at 30 a second in Lighter and Lightest remain, as the task allows; the playhead and the meters keep the screen rate (the mixer meters no longer drop to every third frame).

### Every place that reads the effect level or light mode (for the next person)
- `UI.fx` through `fxGet()`: `applyTrans`, `fxBlurPx`, `fxPic`, `abDraw`, `gbOn` / `gbLayout` / `gbCopy`, `rfUpdate` / `rfStyle`, `elTick`, `gpDraw`, `glassPlay`; set only by `pmApply` (and `pmToggle`) now. Body classes `fxcopy`, `fxzero`, `fxstill`, `fxpic`, and new `fxmin`.
- `UI.lite` (now: the stop is Lighter or Lightest): `laSec` (look-ahead), clip drawing (`drawClip` alpha path) and the map cache key, `drawMixer` and the mixer view (no longer), `VZ` visualiser 33 ms, `SPO` spectrum overlay 33 ms, the frame loop `vz` 33 ms, the logos (`drawAtom`, `setLoop`), `body.lite` CSS, the project windows (`ECO`, `pwSend lite`), `cpuPopFill`, the View menu item.
- `body.fxstill`: CSS animations and transitions off, `AT.reduce`, `hxTick` once a second. `g30`: the time display and things inside glass at 30 a second (unchanged, all stops). `LINT`: the last real input, keeps pictures awake (Automatic sets it while measuring). `gbCopy`, `rfBuild`: unchanged; `rfSvg`, `GB.cv`, `ABL.cv` are taken out of the page in Lightest (`pmLayers`).
- Code: new block "v0.187: the Performance tab" before `askChoice` (`pmInit`, `pmEff`, `pmFx`, `pmApply`, `pmLayers`, `pmSetOn`, `pmSetStop`, `pmToggle`, `pmAuto`, `pmUndo`, `pmBuild`, `pmSync`, `pmFpsDraw`); `applyLite`, `fxSync`, `gpDraw`, `cpuPopFill`, `liteAuto`, `ecoCheck`, `openSettings` wrapped or replaced; CSS before `.dacv`. Settings search indexes `.pmq` elements and the new tab's words.

### Measured (headless Chromium, software drawing, no GPU; Fractals demo playing, mixer open, two pinned devices from Kick, 1600 x 1000)
| stop | app frames/s | layers | paint ms per frame | layer update ms per frame | elements with backdrop-filter | app work ms/frame |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Full effects | 13.3 | 102 | 9.6 | 8.9 | 19 | 20.1 |
| Optimised | 16.3 | 95 | 7.8 | 8.7 | 13 | 10.0 |
| Lighter | 25.3 | 92 | 6.1 | 6.1 | 0 | 11.1 |
| Lightest | 25.0 | 90 | 6.4 | 6.1 | 0 | 6.9 |

Not verified: the GPU side. In software drawing Lighter and Lightest reach the same rate (the limit there is rasterising the arrangement and the meters). From how Chromium works, Lightest saves on a real weak GPU what this test cannot see: no blending of see-through panels over moving content, no blurred shadows (each is a blur pass when its window moves or repaints), no masked rims (a mask layer per panel), fewer compositing layers and no memory for the blurred picture, the copy behind the glass and the refraction maps.

### Tested
- `tools/ui-tests/fxlevels.py` (rewritten for the tab): each stop's classes, effects, elements with backdrop-filter (Full 8, Optimised 3, Lighter 0, Lightest 0), large see-through panels (none in Lighter and Lightest), outer shadows (0 in Lightest), glass layers in the page (gone only in Lightest); each stop clicked in the tab with its line; the switch off (Full effects) and on; all four effect switches and the four savers; Automatic three times (real, and with the frame rate faked at 60 and 20): it ended on Lighter, Optimised (2 cores, so Full was skipped) and Lighter, never Lightest; Undo restored each time; reload keeps the stop.
- Old settings: zero, Optimised preset, Optimised own mix, Full, Full with Light mode by hand, very old (refraction 0), new user, new user who had refused Light mode: mapped as above (this container has 2 cores, so the new users get Lighter from the weak-computer rule, as before).
- Dragging along the track, arrow keys, Home; the CPU popup button, the menu toggle; the weak-computer notice and its "Turn off".
- Search: "light mode", "lightest", "automatic", "frame rate", "refraction", "note cache", "blur", "sleeping" all find the control in Performance; choosing one switches to the tab and flashes the row. `setfind.py` and `glass.py`: same results as before.
- No page errors on load (the only console line is the service worker's 404, as before). Exports unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo), same checksums for Kick, Bass, Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom (Plant, Sampler and Drum kit differ from run to run, as in earlier versions).
- Screenshots looked at: the tab at each stop, with the switch off, during and after Automatic, Glass & light, General, and the arrangement with the mixer in Lightest and in Full.

### Research (brief)
- What a browser can switch off: web.dev on paint cost (box-shadow with blur and large gradients are among the most expensive paints; https://web.dev/articles/css-paint-times, https://web.dev/articles/simplify-paint-complexity-and-reduce-paint-areas), on layers (each composited layer costs memory and management; https://web.dev/stick-to-compositor-only-properties-and-manage-layer-count/), and Chromium's compositing design (https://www.chromium.org/developers/design-documents/gpu-accelerated-compositing-in-chrome/). Backdrop-filter costs: v0.170 entry.
- How others present it: Ableton puts graphics and CPU advice in help articles rather than one switch (https://help.ableton.com/hc/articles/4405388230674, https://help.ableton.com/hc/en-us/articles/5266527910812); games use one quality preset slider plus an auto-detect that runs a short benchmark and picks a level (Unreal's scalability auto-detect: https://tomlooman.com/auto-detect-graphics-settings-ue4; Red Dead Redemption 2's single quality preset slider: https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/4dhIZ7x25mF7gO4NEhlUO9/graphics-performance-tuning-in-red-dead-redemption-2-on-pc). That is the model used here: one slider, an Automatic that measures and moves it, the details below.

### Not verified
- The user's Mac and Windows with a real GPU, a real 120 Hz or 240 Hz screen, a really weak computer. How Automatic behaves when the screen rate is uneven (it needs 95% of the rate for one second per step).
- Small translucent tints that carry meaning were kept in Lightest (selected track, group colours in the headers, 1 px inset lines); they are painted over solid colour and cost no extra layer.

### Left
- A bias slider separate from the main slider for Automatic was not made: the main slider is the "more effects / more optimised" control, Automatic moves it.

### Release
- v0.187: commit 06b0cf8, artifact version 210, project doc `claude/audiolab-device-backlog-run-O.md`. Desktop build run 45710927753 dispatched.

## 25 November 2026, v0.188: Meter, a measuring device (from the user's message)
> "I want like a device under effects ... It's like a spectrogram or whatever with all the information, levels, wave, like a isotope, so you can measure all kinds of things like LUFS and look online on what are the most important measuring the things that we need for a device like that."

### Research (what the suites show, and the standards)
- **What metering suites put on one screen.** iZotope Insight 2: loudness meters with a loudness history, levels, spectrum and spectrogram, sound field (vectorscope), intelligibility ([Plugin Boutique](https://www.pluginboutique.com/product/2-Effects/25-Spectral-Analysis/4688-Insight-2), [Sound On Sound](https://www.soundonsound.com/node/4903554)). Youlean Loudness Meter 2: a loudness history graph, platform presets, PLR, PSR, LRA and a dynamics view ([manual](https://youlean.co/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/Youlean-Loudness-Meter-2-V2.2.1-MANUAL.pdf), [Bedroom Producers Blog](https://bedroomproducersblog.com/2020/04/30/youlean-loudness-meter-2-review/)). MeterPlugs Dynameter: PSR as the measure of how squashed a master sounds ([Sound On Sound](https://www.soundonsound.com/reviews/meterplugs-dynameter)). Voxengo SPAN: spectrum with a slope, 4.5 dB per octave being the common choice so that a balanced mix reads roughly flat ([user guide](https://www.voxengo.com/files/userguides/VoxengoSPAN_en.pdf/getbyname/Voxengo%20SPAN%20User%20Guide%20en.pdf), [Gearspace thread](https://gearspace.com/threads/your-voxengo-span-settings.798068/)). ADPTR Metric AB: reference comparison of spectrum, stereo width per band, correlation, dynamics and loudness ([Plugin Alliance](https://www.plugin-alliance.com/products/metric-ab), [MusicRadar](https://www.musicradar.com/reviews/adptr-metric-ab)). TC Clarity, Nugen VisLM / MasterCheck, Waves WLM / PAZ, Melda MAnalyzer, Ableton's Spectrum and Bitwig's meters were not read page by page this time; what they show (loudness history, true peak, platform targets, spectrum, vectorscope, correlation) is covered by the list above. What producers and mastering engineers say matters most for club and streaming: integrated loudness against the target, true peak (headroom for encoding), short-term loudness through the loud parts, PSR / PLR (how much punch is left), the low end in mono, and the tonal balance against a reference.
- **ITU-R BS.1770-5** ([PDF](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/bs/R-REC-BS.1770-5-202311-I!!PDF-E.pdf)): K-weighting (two stages; printed coefficients for 48 kHz), 400 ms gating blocks with 75 % overlap, absolute gate -70 LKFS, relative gate -10 LU, constant -0.691, a 0 dBFS 997 Hz sine in one front channel reads -3.01 LKFS. True peak (Annex 2): 4x oversampling with a 48-tap, 4-phase interpolating filter whose coefficients are printed there.
- **EBU Tech 3341** ([PDF](https://tech.ebu.ch/docs/tech/tech3341.pdf)): momentary 400 ms and short-term 3 s windows without gating or extra smoothing, at least 10 updates a second; the test signals 1 to 23 with expected readings (±0.1 LU for loudness, +0.2 / -0.4 dB for true peak). **EBU Tech 3342** ([PDF](https://tech.ebu.ch/docs/tech/tech3342.pdf)): loudness range = 95th minus 10th percentile of the short-term values after a -70 LUFS absolute and a -20 LU relative gate; tests 1 to 4 (10, 5, 20, 15 LU ±1).
- **PLR and PSR** ([MeterPlugs](https://www.meterplugs.com/blog/2017/05/18/crest-factor-psr-and-plr.html)): PSR = true peak minus short-term loudness (a live reading, 3 s); PLR = highest true peak minus integrated loudness (whole song); crest factor = peak minus RMS. Ian Shepherd's advice quoted there: no lower than PSR 8 in the loudest parts.
- **Streaming targets**: Spotify's own page: normalises to -14 LUFS (Loud -11, Quiet -19), keep true peak below -1 dBTP, below -2 dBTP for masters louder than -14 LUFS, album normalisation ([Spotify](https://support.spotify.com/artists/article/loudness-normalization/)). Apple Music -16, YouTube -14, Tidal -14 (album), Amazon about -14, Deezer about -15, SoundCloud -14 with the same -1 / -2 rule, EBU R128 -23 and -1 dBTP ([iZotope, July 2025](https://www.izotope.com/en/learn/mastering-for-streaming-platforms.html), [Fora Soft, 2026](https://www.forasoft.com/learn/audio-for-video/articles-audio/lufs-targets-per-platform-2026)). Beatport and club playback do not normalise; no published club target, so the app's existing club target (-9 LUFS, -0.3 dBTP) stays.
- **The user's own references** (project docs `claude/reference-analysis-fractals.md`, `claude/psytrance-rules-in-numbers.md`): Fractals about -6.3 LUFS integrated; checklist -8 LUFS integrated, -6.5 LUFS short-term in the drop, ceiling -1.0 dBTP; correlation +0.8 to +1.0 below 150 Hz. The Psytrance master target is therefore -7 LUFS ±1 (covers -8 and -6.3) with a -1.0 dBTP limit.

### What was wrong in the existing meter (it feeds the master read-out) and is fixed
- **True peak read low.** The meter worklet (`al-meter`, class `Met`) estimated peaks between samples from 4 neighbouring samples. Against Tech 3341: test 16 (fs/4, 45°) read -7.09 dBTP instead of -6.0, test 19 read +1.91 instead of +3.0, tests 20 to 22 read 0.35 to 1.2 dB low. Now it uses the BS.1770-5 Annex 2 filter itself (`tpx`, `TPH` in the worklet bundle), with a shortcut that skips the filter when the 12 samples around cannot beat the highest value already measured (no phase can exceed 2.0229 times the largest sample). All true-peak tests now pass. The limiter's own true-peak detection (`tpk`) was not changed, so the sound is unchanged.
- Everything else in the meter (K-weighting, gating, momentary, short-term, integrated, LRA, correlation) already passed every test below.

### Built (search "Meter: a measuring device" in index.html, before `askChoice`; `msuInit();` runs after `bn2Init();`)
- **Meter** (`ND('measure')`, Analysis group): one picture with a **Show** choice: Overview, Loudness, Levels, Spectrum, Low end, Spectrogram, Stereo, Waveform, Minimal. The picture changes width per view (`MSUW`; Minimal is 250 px, so with the parameters slid shut a pinned Meter is a 266 × 253 window).
  - Loudness: integrated (big, coloured by the target), short-term, momentary, loudness range, highest true peak, PLR, PSR, highest short-term and momentary, a history of short-term (white) and momentary (blue) since the last Reset, up to 10 minutes, with the target band and the integrated line, and M / S bars.
  - Levels: peak and RMS bars for left and right (-60 to 0 dBFS, held peak for 1.5 s), peak, RMS and highest true peak per channel, highest sample peak with a "Clipped" warning, crest factor (3 s), PSR, PLR, correlation. RMS is plain average power over 300 ms (a sine reads 3 dB under its peak; the AES-17 way would add 3.01 dB, said on screen).
  - Spectrum of (L+R)/2 (8192-point analyser): slope (default 4.5 dB per octave, tilted around 1 kHz), smoothing, peak hold with a fall speed, **Keep spectrum** draws the current curve as an orange reference, **Clear** removes it.
  - Low end: 20 to 200 Hz (16384-point analyser in this view), sub (20 to 60 Hz) and kick and bass (60 to 120 Hz) regions, the loudest pitch with Hz and note name, and the **mono check below 150 Hz**: correlation, what is lost in mono (dB), the side part (%), in words: "Safe in mono", "Too wide for a club", "Cancels in mono"; aim +0.8 to +1.0 from the user's research doc.
  - Spectrogram (bass at the bottom, newest on the right, moving by time, its own heat colours).
  - Stereo: vectorscope (mono upright, dots fade), correlation with the lowest recent value, balance, the bass mono check, **width per band** (low 20 to 150 Hz, low mid 150 Hz to 1 kHz, high mid 1 to 5 kHz, high 5 to 20 kHz) as bars with correlation and a word (Mono, Narrow, Wide, Very wide, Out of phase). Width per band comes from the energy of (L+R)/2 and (L-R)/2 per band: correlation = (M-S)/(M+S), lost in mono = 10·log10(M/(M+S)).
  - Waveform: highest and lowest point of every 10 ms, newest on the right (6.6 s across), smooth scrolling between the 100 ms messages, red where full scale is reached.
  - Overview: the numbers, the history, a spectrum, the vectorscope, correlation and width per band at once.
  - Minimal: integrated with the target in words, short-term, true peak max, PLR, the peak warning and M / S bars.
- **Target** (`MSUT`): Club and DJ (-9, -0.3 dBTP), Psytrance master (-7, -1.0), Spotify, Apple Music, YouTube, Tidal, SoundCloud (-14 / -16, -1.0; Spotify -2.0 when louder than -14), Broadcast EBU R128 (-23, -1.0, ±0.5 LU), Own (the Own target and Own limit knobs). In words (`msuStat`): "On target", "Over by 2.1 LU" with "Spotify turns it down 2.1 dB" for streaming, "Under by 1.7 LU" with "Quieter than Psytrance master (-7)"; colours green, yellow (red when more than 3 LU over), blue. "Peaks over -1.0 dBTP" in red.
- **Presets**: Mastering: Mastering for club, Psytrance master, Streaming check (Loudness view, Spotify). Mixing: Mix balance (Spectrum, 4.5 dB/oct), Low end check, Stereo check, Levels and peaks. Small: Minimal.
- **How it measures** (`msuNode`): the sound goes from input to output through one gain of exactly 1; on the side hang the meter worklet (created with `processorOptions {ext:1}`, which switches on its extended readings: per-100-ms true peak, mean square per channel, and 10 min/max pairs for the waveform), a (L+R)/2 analyser for spectrum and spectrogram, (L+R)/2 and (L-R)/2 analysers for the widths, and L and R analysers for the vectorscope. Everything is kept in typed buffers made once (`hS`, `hM`, `tb`, `pb`, `qb`, `wmn`, `wmx`, ...). Frequency and time data are read at most once per picture (`msuFq`, `msuTD`). Reset (`X.reset`) drops readings already on their way for 300 ms.
- **Fixed on the way**: (1) the Spectrum, Loudness, Limiter and Soft Clipper devices had preset groups written as `...PRS(...)` inside the device description, so they were never in the preset menu (Spectrum showed 7 of 12, Loudness 5 of 13, Limiter 8 of 16, Soft Clipper 5 of 15); they are now joined with `mergePre`. (2) After Stop the app stayed "busy" (all pictures at 60 per second) for up to a minute when a long note had been scheduled, because the end of the last scheduled note (`LHIT`) stayed in the future; Stop now pulls it back (a wrapper on `stop`).

### Tested
- **Accuracy** (`node tools/engine-tests/meter_accuracy.js`; runs the worklet code from index.html in Node and feeds it the signals, made here from their definitions): all 44 checks pass.
  - 997 Hz sine at -18 dBFS in both channels: M, S, I -18.00 LUFS; left only: -21.01; 0 dBFS left only: -3.01 (BS.1770).
  - Tech 3341 #1 at 44.1, 48 and 96 kHz: -22.99, -22.99, -23.01; #2 -32.99; #3 -23.01; #4 -23.01; #5 -22.98; #9 short-term after 3 s -22.99 constant; #12 momentary after 1 s -22.96 constant (all ±0.1).
  - Tech 3342 #1 to #4: LRA 10, 5, 20, 15 LU (±1).
  - True peak, Tech 3341 #15 to #19 at 48 kHz: -6.02, -5.98, -6.31, -6.03, +3.03 dBTP (before: -6.02, **-7.09**, -6.25, -6.09, **+1.91**). #20 to #23 (made here at 4x, low-passed, taken at offsets 0 to 3; true peak of that signal -0.13 dBTP): -0.10, -0.22, -0.10, -0.14 (before: -0.49, -1.36, -0.49, -0.14).
  - Correlation: +1.00 identical, -1.00 inverted, 0.00 at 90°. The worklet's output equals its input bit for bit.
- **On the Fractals demo** (`python3 tools/engine-tests/meter_fractals.py`): Kick beats 64 to 96 with a Meter on the track and on the master: same checksum as without (0496bb147f6c). Live, 30 s of Drop 1 (beats 130 to 202.6, master limiter off so both see the same signal): Meter -9.05 LUFS integrated, -0.14 dBTP, LRA 1.0; master read-out -9.05, -0.14, 1.0. Offline render of the same beats measured in Python (BS.1770-5 written out in the test): -9.09 LUFS, true peak -0.31 dBTP with the Annex 2 filter and -0.37 with scipy's 4x resampling (the live and offline renders are not sample-identical, so 0.1 to 0.2 dB apart is expected).
- **Cost**: the app's own audio load reading while Drop 1 plays: 0.27 to 0.32 without and 0.32 to 0.33 with the Meter on the master in two runs (headless, noisy; 1 to 4 points). The worklet alone in Node: about 2 % of one core for 60 s of stereo audio with the Annex 2 filter, about 3 % with the extended readings (the Node harness also counts its own overhead). Pictures (`tools/ui-tests/meter.py`, headless, software drawing, Full effects): pinned and playing 31 per second (Overview 2.6 ms each, Minimal 0.2 ms), stopped right after input 34 to 41 per second, stopped and calm 2 per second, in the panel while another track is shown 0.
- **Exports unchanged** (`export_same.py`): Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo); Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom checksums equal to v0.185 (Plant, Sampler and Drum kit differ from run to run in both versions).
- `devices_all.py` for Meter, Spectrum and Loudness: nothing sticks out, title order as everywhere, the parameters slide shut and open (Meter 1244 > 796 > 1244 px).
- No page errors (the only console line is the service worker's fetch message, as before).
- Screenshots looked at: every view, every preset, pinned Overview over the arrangement, pinned Minimal with the parameters shut, pinned Loudness and Stereo; Show changed inside a pinned window.

### Not done, or different from what one might expect
- The integrated value, range and history run from the moment the Meter is created or Reset, live; there is no "measure the whole song offline" button yet.
- Mono tracks are counted as two equal channels (+3 dB against BS.1770's single-channel reading), as in the master read-out; the app is stereo everywhere.
- The reference spectrum is kept until the device is rebuilt or Clear is pressed; it is not saved with the project, and it is tied to the view it was kept in.
- No intelligibility meter, no surround, no codec preview (MasterCheck), no A/B against a loaded reference file (Metric AB). Width per band uses fixed band edges.
- The Show choice does not clear the preset name, so the menu can say "Minimal" while another view is shown (as on other devices).

### Not verified
- On the user's own screens, pinned while working; the colours on revealing monitors; nobody has compared the readings with a commercial meter on the same file.

### Left
- An offline "measure the whole song" button (render and read integrated, LRA, PLR, true peak of the export), a saved reference spectrum or a loaded reference track, and AES-17 RMS as a choice.

## 26 November 2026, v0.189: Bounce, a bouncing-ball instrument; chord steps and a rhythm per ball for Bouncy Notes; throwing, removing and weighing balls in Orbit (task F: what was left of tasks A and B)

### The user's words (task B and A of `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md`)
"Let's have a bouncing ball device as a instrument and also as an effect. And as the ball bounces, it makes sounds ... and also do like some harmonies and chords also" and, for Orbit, "you can drop in small little balls ... You can change their mass". Run as scheduled task F with no one present; no questions asked.

### Built
**Bounce, a new instrument** (names `bbi` / `BBI`, preset key `bball`; code before `askChoice`, search "Bounce, a bouncing-ball instrument"; `bbiInit();` runs after `bn2Init();`)
- Every played note drops a ball; every landing strikes a sound whose loudness and brightness follow the speed of the impact. In the instrument browser under Synths, after Pluck, with its picture.
- **The same formulas as Bouncy Notes.** `bbiCore().ball()` is the inner part of `bnBalls` (gravity, mass, friction, speed, Lifetime, Vel > Height; Rhythm Free or a note length, Bounce By friction, Steady, Halving, Two thirds, Whole round, with gravity solved so the first gap is exactly the note length). A test compares 7380 landing times and speeds of both: identical. Voices works as in Bouncy Notes (a new ball takes the place of the oldest). Wander lets a ball drift sideways, which moves its sound left or right; Key spread puts low keys left and high keys right.
- **The struck sound**: a small modal engine. Each ball gets a bank of up to 24 tuned resonators (each a decaying sine, run as a rotating pair of numbers, exact and cheap) pitched from the key. Materials: Wood (a marimba bar, modes 1 : 3.98 : 9.2 ...), Glass (1 : 2.32 : 4.25 ...), Metal (a free steel bar, 1 : 2.76 : 5.40 ...), String (a plucked string, 24 harmonics, plucked at 13% of its length, a little stiffness), Drum (a skin, 1 : 1.59 : 2.14 ...). A hit adds to what is still ringing, as on a real bar. Hardness sets how bright a hit is; Brightness how much of that follows the impact; Dynamics how much the loudness follows it (50% = like nature, half the speed gives half the level). Decay (time to fall by 60 dB, a little shorter for high keys), Damping (high modes die faster), Click (a short seeded noise at the contact), Tune, Fine. The ratios are taken from the usual tables for these objects (from memory, not from new research; nothing was looked up online for this run).
- **Ball stops**: when it rests (the ball goes on after the key is let go, until it lies still or Lifetime ends) or at note end. **Auto**: a held key drops a new ball every chosen note length.
- **One worklet per track** (`al-bbi`, `BBIWL`, in both module lists of `wlLoad`; `pvAny`, the clean preset switch `CHSENG` cover it). The landings are worked out and scheduled inside the worklet to the sample: the render is cut into pieces at every landing. A note that is picked up in the middle (Play inside it, a preset switch) keeps its ball where it would be: landings that are already over are skipped, not played at once.
- `chsCut` now takes its list of engines from `CHSENG` (it was a second copy of the same list that had to be kept in step by hand), so a new engine only has to be added once.
- Levels: one hard hit on middle C peaks at about -14 dBFS for every material (measured and set per material).
- Picture: the balls falling onto the struck thing (bars for wood, glass and metal, a string that shakes, a drum skin), a ring at every landing as big as the impact, the lit bar. While the song plays the balls come from the clip under the playhead, worked out with the same formulas; keys played by hand drop balls too; otherwise one test ball drops again and again. Click plays the last key.
- Pages: always Hardness, Brightness, Dynamics, Decay, Friction, Gravity, Speed, Lifetime. Sound: Material, Damping, Click, Tune, Fine, Key spread, Wander. Ball: Rhythm, Bounce, Ball stops, Auto and how often, Mass, Vel > Height, Voices, Seed. Tooltips in plain words on everything.
- **12 presets** in three groups: Free (Wood marimba, Ping pong, Marble on glass, Steel bar), In time (Kalimba every beat, Drum ratchet, One round in a bar, Bells two thirds, String on 1/8), Textures (Glass rain, Bouncing drum, Wind chimes).

**Bouncy Notes**
- **Chord steps** (Notes page): a row of 8 steps, each empty or a step of the scale I to VII, and Every (1/4, 1/2, 1 bar, 2 bars, 4 bars). Each bounce is moved to the chord of its step before Harmony adds its notes, so with Harmony "Chord (3 notes)" and I V VI IV the chords change every bar. Empty steps are left out. Steps count from the start of the clip. Stored as `pg0` to `pg7` and `prate` (handled by the generic select handler).
- **A rhythm per ball** (Balls page, "Next balls"): when Auto drops several balls, the 2nd, 3rd and 4th can bounce in another rhythm than the first (Same, or 1/16 to 2 bars); then round again. Only with a note length as Rhythm. Stored as `r2`, `r3`, `r4`. The picture follows, since it uses the same `bnBalls`.
- Three new presets in a group Chord steps: Chords I V VI IV one per bar, Minor steps on every half bar, Auto with three rhythms. Older presets set the new choices back to empty.

**Orbit**
- **Throw**: press in the picture and drag: a white line with an arrow shows direction and speed, and a dashed line the orbit the ball will get; let go and the ball starts with that speed. A click without dragging drops a ball as before. The speed is stored with the ball (as a multiple of the speed of a circle at distance 1, so a throw keeps its shape when Centre or Gravity change).
- **Remove one ball**: right-click on it. The others keep their seeded places (the removed slot still uses up its random numbers). The last ball cannot be removed; with One per key on it says why. One undo step.
- **Mass per ball**: scroll over a ball; a label shows "mass x1.25" for a moment. It counts like the Mass knob (Ball pull, Drag, and whatever Mass drives on the Vectors page; the ball is drawn bigger). One undo step per scroll gesture. Scrolling where there is no ball still scrolls the page.
- **Orbit paths**: each ball's own path is drawn faintly in its colour: the ellipse it would follow if only the centre pulled it, worked out from where it is and how fast it goes (a Kepler orbit); only the part inside the rim. While the song plays the engine now also reports each ball's speed for this (the sound is not touched).
- Clear dropped balls also forgets throws, removals and masses.

### Tested (headless, no page errors on load)
- `tools/engine-tests/bounce_instrument.py` (new): the landings of Bounce and Bouncy Notes are identical (7380 compared). All 12 presets with a phrase and a four-note chord at full velocity: no NaN, peaks -6.3 to -2.9 dBFS, the sound ends (last 0.5 s at -128 dB or below), two renders bit-identical. **Landings on the grid**: at 90, 120, 137 and 174 bpm, for 1/4, 1/8 triplet and 1/16 steady every hit starts exactly on the song's grid frame, and for third, halving and whole round every hit that can be told apart starts exactly on its formula frame. Halving at 1/4: hits at -16.4, -23.5, -31.7, -39.7 dB with brightness 997, 770, 553, 457 Hz; with Dynamics and Brightness at 0% all hits -16.4 dB. Velocity 1, 0.5, 0.25: -14.1, -20.1, -26.1 dB. Pitch from the key (130.7, 261.4, 440.0, 880.0 Hz for keys 48, 60, 69, 81, within the FFT bin). A phrase moved by 293 samples: identical for Wood marimba and Glass rain, below 1.2e-8 for the drum presets (a ringing tail is switched off below 1e-7 at the end of a piece of the render, so where that happens depends on the audio blocks). Ball stops at note end: hits at beats 1 and 2 for a note of 1.6 beats; when it rests: 1, 2, 3 ... 7. Auto: 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5. Voices 1: the first ball stops when the second comes. Cost: 64 notes, 32 s of sound in 1.3 to 2.4 s (0.9 s for the same length with one note).
- `tools/engine-tests/bounce_chords.py` (new): I V VI IV every bar gives C E G, G B D, A C E, F A C; every half bar with empty steps left out; minor I VI III VII with four-note chords on A; without steps exactly as before. Rhythm per ball: balls dropped at 0, 2, 4, 6 land after 0 1 1.5 1.75 / 0 0.333 0.5 0.583 / 0 0.5 0.75 0.875 / 0 1 1.5 1.75 beats; all Same as before. A render with chord steps is bit-identical twice.
- `tools/ui-tests/bounce_instrument.py` (new): nothing outside the device on both pages and shut (916 x 253, shut 408), no control without a tooltip, no dash characters, preset groups Free 4, In time 5, Textures 3, choices and five undos, materials, presets and three undos, playing, a key played by hand, the browser tile.
- `tools/ui-tests/orbit_throw.py` (new, real mouse events): a click drops (6 to 7 balls); a drag of 20 right and 70 up shows the throw line while dragging and gives a ball with speed 1.87 at 74 degrees (the drag's own angle), which the engine starts with exactly; right-click removes the ball under the mouse (8 to 7), all other balls keep their places, undo brings it back; four notches of scroll make a ball x1.57 heavier (the engine's mass and Mass value follow), two back x1.25, one undo step for the gesture; scroll over empty space is not taken; Clear forgets all; One per key ignores right-click; while playing the engine reports speeds.
- `tools/ui-tests/bounce_chords.py` (new): the chord step row and the Next balls row set the parameters through the page, show I V VI IV, undo, tooltips, the new preset.
- **Unchanged**: `bounce_rhythm.py`, `bouncy_walls.py` and `orbit.py` (engine) give the same results as on v0.188 apart from the timing lines; `tools/ui-tests/orbit.py`, `bounce.py` and `devices_all.py` the same apart from the new device, the new choice row and the new presets. Fractals demo, solo, beats 64 to 96: Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB.
- `preset_switch.py` with Bounce added (`ONLY=Bounce`): the old preset is gone after 16 ms; sixteenth notes all there once and on the grid; Play inside a long note sounds. The "heard again 20 to 50 ms after a switch" check fails for Bounce by nature: after a switch to a free-fall preset the ball of the held note is in the air and the next landing comes later.
- Screenshots looked at: Bounce on both pages, glass, ratchet, rain, playing, shut, the browser tile; Bouncy Notes Balls and Notes pages with the new rows; Orbit while throwing, with paths, with the mass label, playing.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it.** The materials, levels and presets were set by measurement and by reasoning; the mode ratios come from memory of the usual tables, not from a new search.
- Real mouse or trackpad (tests used Playwright's mouse), the Mac build, touch, MIDI learn and automation of the new knobs.
- Tempo changes inside a held Bounce note (the tempo is read when a note starts).

### Left
- Listen and tune Bounce (materials, Click, levels against other instruments).
- Bounce has no chord steps or rhythm per ball of its own (put Bouncy Notes in front of it for that); throwing balls onto pads; Bounce Delay and Orbit could share the new picture parts.

## 26 November 2026, v0.190: Psy Bass, an instrument for the psytrance rolling bass (task H, from the user's message)

### The user's request
"Let's also create a device with the same design language ... another one for psytrance bass do some deep research online ... what the best algorithms are, what the best presets are. And this is one of the most important devices because Audiolab is for electronic dance music mostly ... Psytrance bass is also very important. and decide yourself what research you should do to have the best outcome." The user makes melodic full-on at 145 to 150 BPM (reference: Electric Universe).

### What the Fractals demo uses today, and what it lacks
The demo's Bass track is **Plant** (one saw, `cut` 500 Hz, `res` 1.5, `fenv` 20, attack 1 ms, decay 70 ms, sustain 0, release 30 ms; notes of 0.145 beats = 60 ms, K-B-B-B on A at 55 Hz, velocities 0.8 / 1 / 1; EQ, the Sidechain pump and a second EQ on the track). Read from `plantPlay`: Web Audio nodes per note, a 12 dB biquad, a filter envelope from `setTargetAtTime` (time constant 55 ms, so it is still a third open at the end of the note), envelopes that never reach zero (the oscillators are stopped later), a random ±1 cent detune on every note (`RND()`), so no two notes are the same, the sub layer an octave under the note (27.5 Hz for A at 55 Hz), no key tracking, no gate against the kick, no tick. The demo's own track was **not** changed (its renders are bit-identical to v0.189).

### Research (three passes: the project's own research docs, a web pass on styles and producers, a web pass on algorithms; what is well supported, what is one source and what is a guess is said per line)

**Already in the project** (`claude/psytrance-rules-in-numbers.md`, `claude/psytrance-production-brief.md`, the two reference analyses, the Sub doc), used and not repeated: K-B-B-B; one saw, mono, retrigger; note length 50 to 75 ms at 145 BPM, at least 10 ms of silence (Myloops; another Myloops article says 80 to 100 ms); amp decay 60 to 90 ms, sustain 0, release 20 to 40 ms; two filter recipes (180 to 260 Hz, 12 dB, envelope 15 to 25%; or 400 to 700 Hz, 12 or 24 dB, about 40 ms, one octave); first note after the kick quieter (80 / 100 / 100 forum; -30%; Fractals measured about 10 dB); bass high-pass 30 Hz, mono, kick and bass on the same root (Model 2, matches both measured references); Fractals on A at 55 Hz, Imperial Army on F at 44 Hz, both K-B-B-B.

**Styles** (web pass; most named-producer interviews say nothing concrete about the bass, and that is the main finding):
- Full-on: "usually 140 to 148" (Wikipedia), "usually it's just 145" (Infected Mushroom, Audiofanzine). One KVR post: saw, no attack, full sustain, filter decay "around 100+ ms at 145 BPM", zero resonance, no key tracking, kick ends exactly where the first bass note starts. One psynews post: full-on basses need little or no sub. bonedo (Serum workshop): the filter closes "within a few milliseconds", the first of the three 16ths quieter. Elektronauts: lower the first B after each K.
- Progressive: 130 to 140 BPM (DoubleBlind magazine, one source); offbeat bass an eighth after the kick, held an eighth (bonedo); more sub and up to three phase-locked saws (psynews, one forum user). Vini Vici name TAL-BassLine 101 and Serum, no settings (WeRaveYou).
- Dark and forest: from about 150 BPM, often faster (Wikipedia); no synth values found anywhere.
- Hi-tech: 170 to 200 BPM (Melodigging, low reliability); one commercial bass synth (Eplex7 HBS1) says a hi-tech bass needs a separate transient with a "clicky start" and saturation. No 32nd-roll layout found.
- Goa: 130 to 150 BPM; saw through resonant filters; TB-303, Juno, Bass Station, MS-10, SH-101 (Wikipedia); classic basses ran free (no oscillator reset), so every attack differed (bonedo).
- Triplet and galloping rolls: named by bonedo and Computer Music, **no step layout published** in what could be read. The Triplet roll (K-B-B per beat) and the Galloping roll (K-.-B-B, from the Aciduss article already in the project) are the plain readings.
- Synths named: Sylenth1 (several forum users, Astrix seen using it), Serum, Virus, Zebra, Massive, TAL-BassLine. **No published psy-bass settings were found for Spire, Diva, Predator, Nord Lead, "Serum MG Low 24" or the Virus**; nothing from Electric Universe at all.
- Where sources disagree (new here): amp sustain 0 (Myloops) against full sustain with the pluck from the filter only (two forum users); filter decay "a few ms" (bonedo), 40 ms (Myloops), 100+ ms (KVR), about 500 ms (psynews); resonance near zero (forums) against resonant filters for Goa (Wikipedia); a sine sub one octave down (forum) against a single saw (forum) against a two-layer split at 200 Hz (forum).

**Algorithms** (web pass with measurements made for this run in Python; papers rarely give dB figures):
- Band-limited saw: Välimäki, Pekonen, Nam (JASA 2012) give the highest alias-free note by a masking model: 2-point PolyBLEP 2135 Hz, 4-point B-spline 7845 Hz. Measured against -80 dB (worst alias below 20 kHz): 2-point PolyBLEP at 1x -70 / -53 / -42 dB at 31 / 223 / 887 Hz; at 2x with a halfband -92 / -75 / -63; **4-point B-spline at 2x -117 / -100 / -88**. Chosen: 4-point B-spline PolyBLEP at twice the sample rate.
- Ladder: Zavalishin, "The Art of VA Filter Design" 2.1.0: TPT one-poles, the instantaneous solve u = (x - kS)/(1 + kG), self-oscillation at k = 4, pass-band gain 1/(1+k) (compensated here by (1+k), so the note keeps its level when resonance goes up); stays stable when the cutoff moves every sample. Huovilainen (DAFx 2004) and D'Angelo and Välimäki (ICASSP 2013) put tanh inside the stages and need 2x; not done here (see "drive"). 12 dB: Simper's (Cytomic) trapezoidal state-variable filter.
- Drive without aliasing: tanh of the raw saw before the filter aliased to -63 dB at 880 Hz even at 2x with ADAA (measured). Because the wave is a known function of its phase, the drive is now applied to the shape itself (tanh of the wave over one cycle): its smooth part has overtones that fall off exponentially, and its two jumps are corrected with the same B-spline BLEP. Measured -106 dB or lower on every key up to 880 Hz with the filter wide open. That this is equivalent to a driven oscillator is my own reasoning, confirmed by the measurement.
- Saturation after the filter: tanh with first-order antiderivative anti-aliasing (Parker, Zavalishin, Le Bivic DAFx 2016; Bilbao et al. 2017; safe form log cosh x = |x| + log1p(exp(-2|x|)) - ln 2). Fine at real cutoffs; with the filter wide open and 100% saturation it reaches -62 dB on high keys (see tests).
- Decimation: a 79-tap Kaiser halfband (designed and measured here): flat to 20 kHz at 48 kHz, -101 dB from 28 kHz.
- Envelopes: exponential segments (RC curves; earlevel, mystran on KVR); a k-rate parameter holds one value per 128 frames, 2.7 ms, which is 7 to 27% of a 10 to 40 ms segment (MDN), so everything is per sample here. Each segment is (exp(-c x) - exp(-c)) / (1 - exp(-c)), which reaches its end value exactly at the set time, so a note with sustain 0 ends at true zero at Decay.
- Start phase: Logic ES2: a free (random) phase makes the attack less punchy; "soft" starts at a zero crossing; "hard" at the peak gives more click (Apple docs). Ableton Operator: R restarts at the same phase (manual). Chosen: Restart on, start at the saw's zero crossing (0 degrees) with a 0.5 ms fade-in; 90 degrees gives the harder click. **No source puts a number on the click.**
- DC: the JOS DC blocker with R = 0.995 cuts at about 35 Hz (-3.7 dB at 30 Hz): wrong for psy bass. Here the Low cut (two-pole, at least 10 Hz) sits **before** the amp envelope and takes the DC of the saturation out, so notes still end at zero.
