Sources read this run: psynews.org (topic 72935), kvraudio.com forum (p=3272796, p=5823459, p=8915826, p=6838503), elektronauts.com (t/253875), bonedo.de (psytrance workshop 1), pressreader.com (Beat, 6 October 2022), feelyoursound.com, idmmag.com (Vini Vici and Astrix bass), weraveyou.com (Vini Vici gear), en.audiofanzine.com (Infected Mushroom), equipboard.com/pros/astrix, musicradar.com (Juno Reactor), Wikipedia (Psychedelic trance, Goa trance, Dark psytrance), doubleblindmag.com, melodigging.com (hi-tech, dark), kvraudio.com/product (Eplex7 HBS1), loopmasters.com (Forest DarkPsy), everand.com (Computer Music, preview), adsrsounds.com (Zebra psy bass, summary); mac.kaist.ac.kr (Välimäki, Pekonen, Nam JASA 2012), native-instruments.com (Zavalishin VAFD 2.1.0), dafx.de (Huovilainen 2004; Parker et al. 2016), D'Angelo and Välimäki ICASSP 2013 (abstract), aaltodoc.aalto.fi (two theses), levien.com/ladder.pdf, gist.github.com/hollance (Cytomic SVF), core.ac.uk (Bilbao et al. 2017), ccrma.stanford.edu (Chowdhury ADAA notebook; JOS DC blocker), pure.ed.ac.uk (Esqueda et al. 2016), mathworks.com (halfband design), developer.mozilla.org (AudioParam), ableton.com (manual), help.apple.com (Logic ES2), eenewseurope.com (envelopes). Not readable: Brandt's minBLEP paper, the Serum and Diva manuals, several KVR and Gearspace threads.

### Built (names `psb` / `PSB`, code before `askChoice`; `psbInit();` runs after `msuInit();`)
- **Psy Bass** (`PRESETS.psyb`, first in "Bass & acid" in the instrument browser, with its picture). Worklet `al-psb` (`PSBWL`, in both module lists of `wlLoad`; `pvAny`; `CHSENG.push` at init, so the clean preset switch and `chsCut` cover it).
- **`psbCore()`** is one engine shared by the worklet, the picture and the tests. `Eng.note`, `Eng.render`, `Eng.trig`, `vStart`, `vTick`; per voice at twice the sample rate: saw and square (mix) with the 4-point B-spline PolyBLEP (`blr`), drive on the wave shape, 24 dB ladder or 12 dB SVF with per-sample cutoff (cutoff, key tracking from 55 Hz, velocity, filter envelope in octaves, Tick = a 3 ms extra opening of up to 6 octaves, accent), saturation (tanh ADAA), Low cut, amp envelope, a sine Sub on the note or an octave down beside the filter. Two voice slots: a note that is still sounding fades in 1.5 ms when the next starts. Halfband down to the output; the start of every note is moved forward by the latency (41 samples at the inner rate), so it lands on its own sample.
- **Every note is the same**: notes and pattern steps start on whole output samples, with the wave, filter, saturation, low cut and envelopes all set back; Restart off lets the wave run on, its phase taken from the absolute time.
- **Gate to the kick** (on by default): a note keeps as many 16ths as it was written for, never past the next beat, sounds the Gate share of that, and its release is cut so it ends 0.5 ms before the step is over. So K-B-B-B notes stop before the next note and the next kick, and an offbeat eighth keeps its two steps.
- **Roll** (off by default): a held note plays a 16-step bar (or 12 triplet steps) of off / on / accent with an octave per step, worked out in the engine from the note's place in beats (`PSBB`, set by a wrapper on `synthB`; from the playhead for keys played live; from the note itself when stopped), at the tempo where the note starts. Patterns: K-B-B-B, K-B-B-B first note softer, Galloping, Offbeat, Triplet, K-B-B-B with octave jumps, Every 16th, K-B-B-B last step free. Roll on, Gate and the open tab stay as they are when the preset changes.
- A note picked up in its middle (preset switch while playing, Play pressed inside it) is left out when Gate is on and Roll is off (it was over after its first steps); with Roll on the roll carries on in time.
- **Picture** (`psbDraw`, `psbPaint`, `psbNote`): one beat from the kick to the next kick with the bass notes of that beat (the pattern's first beat, or K-B-B-B) as the engine makes them, the amp envelope (white) and the filter envelope (dashed yellow) over each, the steps, and the silence before the next kick in ms (red "rings into the kick" if there is none); below, the note and its overtones from 20 Hz to 5 kHz with the note named. Painted into a spare canvas and only copied until a setting, the key, the tempo or the size changes (0 paints at rest, measured). Click plays a note.
- **Controls**: top row Cutoff, Resonance, Opening (octaves), Close time, Decay, Release, Tick, Gate. Tab Roll and gate: Gate to the kick, Roll on, pattern menu, 16ths or Triplets, Accent, the step row (click a step: off, on, accent; click under it: octave up, down, none; beats marked). Tab Sound: Filter 24 or 12 dB, Restart, Sub on the note or an octave down, Octave; Square, Sub, Drive, Saturate, Key track, Low cut, Start phase. Tab Envelopes: a plain sentence on how long a 16th sounds at the song tempo; Open time, Stays open, Close curve, Attack, Sustain, Curve, Vel bright, Velocity. Every control and every preset has a tooltip (`PSBTIP`, preset `tip`). New value formats `tms` (ms with a decimal under 10 ms) and `ov` (octaves).
- **18 presets in 5 groups** (`PSBPRE`), each tooltip saying the tempo and key range: Full-on (Full-on roller 145 = default, roller 150 tight, Melodic full-on Fractals, Full-on on F Imperial Army, bright squelch, saw and square, with a clean sub), Progressive (roller 138, offbeat 136), Dark and forest (Dark roller 150, Forest squelch 155, Dark galloping 150), Hi-tech (short and clicky 180, deep sub 185), Goa (acid flavour 145, square drone 140, Triplet roller 145, Acid roller 147). A hidden level trim per preset (`lv`) keeps them within about 2 dB of each other in average level. Note names in the texts follow the app (55 Hz is A2 in the piano roll).

### Tested (headless, no page errors on load; `tools/engine-tests/psybass.py`, `tools/ui-tests/psybass.py`; 44.1 kHz renders through the app)
- **Tuning**: keys 24 to 57, off by at most 0.00026 Hz.
- **Every note the same**: 48 notes of a 16th roll, presets Full-on, Fractals, Dark (150), Hi-tech (180), Forest (155), Acid (147): largest difference from the first note **0** (from 1 ms before each note to 1 ms before the next step; inside the last 0.4 ms the next note's band-limited start adds about -127 dB). Restart off: the notes differ, as they should.
- **Silent before the kick**, every preset at its own tempo and pattern: the level in the last millisecond before each kick is -73 dB under the bass's peak at worst (Goa), exact zero for 14 of 18; quiet (under -60 dB) for 29 ms before the kick on the default, 2.4 to 13.5 ms on the long Goa and offbeat presets. For comparison, Gate off with long envelopes gives -14.6 dB.
- **Envelope times from the output**: Decay 40 / 75 / 150 / 300 ms ends at exact zero at 40.5 / 75.2 / 150.2 / 300.1 ms (the rest is the halfband's 0.9 ms); Release 10 / 20 / 50 / 150 ms ends 10.3 / 20.2 / 50.0 / 150.0 ms after the note; Attack 2 / 10 / 30 ms reaches full level at 3.6 / 10.4 / 30.8 ms (measured on a 440 Hz wave, so within one cycle); filter Close time 30 / 45 / 100 ms: the 4th overtone settles at 34 / 49 / 103 ms (6.8 ms window).
- **Aliasing**, every preset, keys 28, 40, 57 (41, 82, 220 Hz), note held, filter at rest and held fully open: strongest thing that is not an overtone, below 17 kHz, **-125 dB** under the strongest overtone at worst. Stress cases with the filter at 8 kHz: drive 100% and resonance 50% -147 to -89 dB (keys 28 to 81); drive 100% and saturation 60% -114 / -97 / -86 / -77 / -69 dB; drive, saturation 100% and resonance 60% -86 / -78 / -71 / -65 / -59 dB on keys 28 / 45 / 57 / 69 / 81. So **the -80 dB target holds for every preset, but not when Saturate is pushed high with the filter wide open on notes from about 110 Hz up**.
- **DC**: 186 dB or more under the peak on a held note of every preset; a whole rolling render has a mean 50 dB under the peak (short decaying notes are not symmetric; nothing under 10 Hz is made).
- **Roll**: one long note at 145, 138 and 150 BPM (Galloping), triplets at 145 and a note starting at beat 1.37: 48 / 48 / 32 / 32 / 44 notes, all as expected, every one starting the same 4 samples after its grid line (the point where the 0.5 ms fade-in crosses the detection threshold). Two renders identical.
- **Level** against the Fractals kick (peak -1.0 dBFS, average -9.8 dB) through a new track: default peak -8.1 dBFS, average -25.8 dB, which is the average of the demo's Bass track (-25.5 dB); presets -6.5 to -13.1 dBFS peak, -23.6 to -27.2 dB average.
- **Against the demo's Bass track** (Psy Bass put on that track with its EQ, pump and volume, same notes, beats 64 to 96): Psy Bass is about 10 dB louder there (that track's volume is set for Plant). Over a 16th the two rise the same way; Plant still rings at -19 to -39 dB from 45 ms to the end of the step, Psy Bass falls to -30 dB by 60 ms and then stops (the gate). Spectrum in octave bands from 30 Hz, against the lowest band: Plant 0, -9.1, -10.5, -12.8, -16.2, -29.9, -53.3, -83.4; default 0, -10.3, -13.4, -19.8, -29.5, -38.4, -53.5, -72.5; Fractals preset 0, -9.7, -13.0, -17.1, -24.0, -31.7, -44.2, -59.9. So Psy Bass is darker from 280 Hz to 1 kHz (the note is over sooner and the filter closes fully) and has more above 2 kHz (the tick and the band-limited saw, where Plant's biquad and browser oscillator roll off). The Fractals preset was set by these measurements.
- **Bit-identical**: two renders identical for six presets; a note moved by 77, 293 or 1000 samples sounds the same (difference 0); output the same for any block size (37 against 128 in the engine, by itself).
- **Cost**: 26.5 s of rolling bass rendered in 0.5 s more than the same project with one note: **about 20 ms per second of sound** in the app's offline render (9.8 ms per second for the engine alone in node); silent, the engine returns at once.
- **Live**: `preset_switch.py` with Psy Bass added: a switch is clean (nothing restarted off the grid), the 13 sixteenths after it all there once and on the grid; Play inside a long note stays silent (Gate on); a held key with Roll on plays; a preset change during a roll keeps the roll going.
- **Page**: nothing outside the device on all three tabs and shut (916 x 253, shut 408); title order as on Sub; no control or preset without a tooltip; no dash characters; 18 presets in 5 groups; presets, steps, octave row, pattern menu, 16ths or Triplets, Roll, Gate, Filter, Restart, Sub octave and their undos; a live key with Roll on; the browser tile. `devices_all.py`: every device as before, Psy Bass 916 x 253, nothing outside. Screenshots looked at: the three tabs, Dark, Offbeat, Triplet, Goa, shut, playing, the browser tile.
- **Unchanged**: Fractals demo solo Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96); `export_same.py` checksums equal for both and for Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom (Plant, Sampler and Drum kit differ from run to run on the old version too, checked again).

### Not verified, and what is a guess
- **Nobody has listened to it.** The presets come from the research and from measurement; the dark, forest, hi-tech and Goa values are my reading of thin sources (no published synth values exist for them).
- The tick (a 3 ms extra opening of the filter) and its amount, the 0.5 ms default attack, the 1.5 ms fade of a note cut by the next, the per-preset level trims and the accent amounts are my choices.
- The Triplet and Galloping layouts are the plain readings; no source shows a step layout.
- Tempo changes inside a held note with Roll on: the roll keeps the tempo of its start. Knobs turned while a note is held change the next note, not the one sounding.
- Live use with a real keyboard and mouse, the Mac build, MIDI learn and automation of the new knobs.

### Left
- Listen and tune the presets with the user, most of all the tick, the decay against the user's kick and the Goa and forest resonance.
- A swing control for the roll; the first note after the kick softer by its own amount; a fundamental swap (sine 3 semitones up, IDM Mag) as an option.
- Ducking that listens to the kick track's notes (today the app's Sidechain effect pumps on the beat).
- Task E (CPU and background freeze).

### Release
- v0.190: commit 1a98265, project doc `claude/audiolab-psy-bass-research-and-device.md`, artifact version 213.
- Built on top of v0.189 (Bounce, task F), which another session released while this one was testing: the Psy Bass block was put onto v0.189 (its engine joins the clean preset switch through `CHSENG.push`, as Bounce does) and the tests were run again there.
- **Desktop build**: run 45742963619 for this commit succeeded (2 min 22 s, started 02:31 UTC).

## 26 November 2026, v0.191: Fractal, a pulse-pattern synth and effect (task P, from a later message; first called Crackle)

### The user's request
"This synthesizer is going to create like a crackle, rattle, a noise, a rumble, like a grrr ... sound going from really loud ... to silent to really loud ... these little dials that you can turn ... you can speed up and speed down ... for EDM, baselines, sidechain ... put like phasers over it, or like a fractal effect, like a 3D effect ... And you can loop sidechains like this." With a drawing: rows of thin spikes at equal steps, a dial per spike height (A small, B tallest, C middle), a big SPEED dial: "SPEED DOES TIME FASTER/SLOWER = CRACKLE/RATTLE/NOISE/RUMBLE". Built as a scheduled run; while it was being built the user wrote: "we are going to call this device Fractal ... a nice little logo on the top where the title is ... stylized text". So the device is called Fractal (internal names stay `crk` / `CRK`).

### Research (what was read, and what was only searched)
- **Kilohearts Trance Gate** (kilohearts.com/products/trance_gate): 8 pattern slots; click steps on and off, drag to tie steps; an ADSR applied to each step; Resolution = length of one step; Mix. No per-step level, no Hz rate, no swing or randomness on that page.
- **FL Studio PoiZone Trance Gate** (image-line.com FL help): 16 steps synced to the tempo, 1/32 the fastest and 4/4 the slowest, one step per unit; SMOOTH sets how hard the gate starts (left faster); WET 0 to 100%.
- **Cableguys ShaperBox 3 manual** (Volume shaper; the LFO part was read only through a summary): "rhythmic gating/stuttering, a.k.a. trance gating"; loop length 1/128 to 32 bars or a speed in **Hertz from 0.02 Hz to 5.24 kHz**, note values straight, triplet and dotted; points hard, medium or soft; a Smooth control; triggers by sync, MIDI or audio; 6 ms of look-ahead for anti-click smoothing in MIDI and audio mode; Mix as amount. This is the only product found with a rate that goes from note lengths into audio rate, which is what the user drew, so Speed here goes from 1/1 to 1/64 and then on in Hz up to 4 kHz.
- **Gross Beat** (image-line.com): a 2-bar buffer with 36 time and 36 volume envelopes for glitch, stutter, gate; options for click reduction, remove DC offset and sinc resampling. Its volume and time pages were not read.
- **KVR forum, "Developing a Trance Gate"**: per-step attack and decay; producers prefer the rise to start at the step and peak a little later. That is how Pump comes back after each dip.
- **SuperCollider Dust** ("random impulses from 0 to +1", density = impulses per second) and **Crackle** (a chaotic generator that "crackles" near 2.0): crackle is random impulses, so Rnd time at audio rate turns the buzz into crackle; both are seeded here so exports repeat.
- **Theory**, CMU 15-322 notes on amplitude modulation: a modulator in the audible range is heard as sidebands at carrier +- modulator, not as tremolo. Worked out here for pulses (standard Fourier series, not from a page): a pattern of n steps at a pulse rate r repeats every n/r seconds, so everything it adds sits at multiples of r/n; when all steps have the same level only the multiples of r are left (the pulse rate is the pitch); different levels add the lower tone at r/n and its multiples (the user's "grrr"); shorter pulses spread the energy higher (brighter). A sound chopped by the pulses gets copies of itself shifted by every one of those lines.
- **Aliasing**, DAFx 2016 "Rounding corners with BLAMP" (only the abstract): jumps and corners need infinite bandwidth and are the main source of aliasing; band-limited ramp corrections reduce it by up to 50 dB. Here both jumps (BLEP) and corners (BLAMP) are corrected, and the chop runs at twice the sample rate so the sum tones above half the sample rate are filtered away instead of folding back.
- Searched but **not read**: Effectrix, Looperator, LFOTool, VolumeShaper's own page, Polyverse Manipulator, Tantra, Phase Plant, Serum and Vital audio-rate AM, Bitwig Grid, Reaktor, Output Thermal, velvet noise papers, and articles on very fast gating in psytrance. The controls chosen from the pages that were read: steps, per-step level (the user's drawing; none of the products read has it per step except as point height in ShaperBox), length (duty), shape (sharp to soft), smoothing (PoiZone SMOOTH, ShaperBox Smooth), swing, randomness with a seed, note values with triplets that continue in Hz (ShaperBox), mix.

Sources: kilohearts.com/products/trance_gate · image-line.com/support/FLHelp/html/plugins/Poizone_TranceGate.htm · cableguys.com/downloads/Cableguys-ShaperBox-3-Manual.pdf · image-line.com/support/FLHelp/html/plugins/GrossBeat_MainOptions.htm · kvraudio.com/forum/viewtopic.php?p=4529497 · docs.supercollider.online/Classes/Dust.html · depts.washington.edu/dxscdoc/Help/Classes/Crackle.html · cs.cmu.edu/~15322/book/ch06/03.html · dafx.de/paper-archive/details/xRM0GiuF-JmeN0_aaP9VNg

### Built (code before `askChoice`, between `/*CRK-BEGIN*/` and `/*CRK-END*/`; `crkInit();` runs after `psbInit();`)
- **Fractal, an instrument** (`PRESETS.crk`, in "Bass & acid" of the instrument browser, with its picture) and **Fractal, an effect** (`FXDEF.crk` through `ND`, in Modulation of the effect browser and of the old menu). One engine for both.
- **The pattern** (`crkCore()`, shared by the page and the worklets): 2 to 8 steps, a level dial per step named A to H as in the drawing (`l1`..`l8`), Length (part of a step, 2 to 100%), Shape (0% square, more rounds both edges), Smooth (the shortest edge in ms: 0 clicks on purpose), Swing (every second step later, up to a third of a step), Rnd level and Rnd time with a Seed (the same numbers for the same step every time). Kind: **Pulse** (spikes with silence between) or **Pump** (each step dips to silence at its start and comes back to its level, with a curve set by Shape: a sidechain loop; per-step levels make variations).
- **Speed**, one knob: 0 to 60 are note lengths 1/1, 1/2, 1/2T, 1/4, 1/4T, 1/8, 1/8T, 1/16, 1/16T, 1/32, 1/32T, 1/64 per step, locked to the song time; from 60 it carries on smoothly in Hz from the rate of 1/64 at the song's tempo up to 4000 pulses a second (so the Hz shown at a given knob position depends on the tempo). In Hz the rate glides over 10 ms when the knob turns. The value reads "1/16" or "112 Hz (A3)".
- **Instrument**: the key sets the pitch of the source (Saw, Square, Sine, Noise, Two saws with Detune, Saw and sub with Sub level), up to 8 voices, Tone (two-pole low pass before the chop), Attack, Release, Velocity, Octave. **Pulses follow key**: Off (Speed), Pulse = key (the pulse rate is the note, the buzz is in tune), Pattern = key (one round of the pattern is the note; pulses come steps times faster). With key following, or with **Restart** on, every note starts the pattern at step A; otherwise the pattern runs with the song. Notes, ids and tags work as in Sub (`pvState`, `CHSENG`, `pvAny`, voice keys, picking up notes).
- **After the chop** (inside the engine): Phaser (six all-pass stages per side, 180 Hz to 5 kHz, sides a quarter turn apart, sweep from the song time), Phaser rate, Phaser fb; **3D spread** (steps placed left and right in turn, wider for later steps) and **3D depth** (one side up to 10 ms later). One click adds the app's own effects after it: Phaser, Vector Delay (particles, a 3D space of echoes), Chorus / Flanger, Stereo Imager (instrument page After) and + Phaser, + Vector Delay, + Chorus (effect page After). There is no fractal effect in the app (Fractals is the demo song); Vector Delay and the 3D controls stand in for "fractal or 3D".
- **Without aliasing**: every pulse edge and corner is drawn as a band-limited jump or corner (tables from one Kaiser-windowed sinc, 32 taps each side, 256 sub-positions; edges longer than 16 samples round off into a raised cosine); the chop runs at twice the sample rate between two 91-tap half-band filters (pass to 0.432, stop from 0.568 of the sample rate, about 95 dB). The saw and square of the instrument use the same band-limited jumps. The effect hands the sound on 45 samples later; `fxLat` reports it, so the app's delay compensation lines the other tracks up; at Mix 0 the effect passes the input on untouched and with no delay. The instrument makes its sound 78 frames ahead (`AHEAD`), so a note comes out exactly on time with no delay to make up for. Instrument output has a 3 Hz DC blocker (a saw chopped at its own pitch leaves an offset); the effect has none.
- **Exports**: everything is worked out per sample from the song's own frame count; the instrument wakes 256 frames before a note needs it and sleeps on an exact frame, never on block edges. Two renders are bit-identical, and the block size does not matter (128, 512 and 100 sample blocks identical in a direct test of the engine).
- **Picture** (black, left; `crkPaint`): the user's drawing come alive: two rounds of the pattern as spikes, each as high as its dial, letters A B C ... under the steps and the dial value over each; it moves with the song while it plays (white line on the current step). Below: at rhythm speeds one bar of the song at the real speed with the playhead; from 20 pulses a second the tone the pattern makes (its overtones as bars against Hz, worked out from one round with an FFT; the pulse rate dashed). Painted into a spare canvas and only copied until a setting, the size or the song position changes.
- **Pages**: Pattern (Kind, Pulses follow key; Steps, A.., Speed, Length, Shape, Mix), Feel (Smooth, Swing, Rnd level, Rnd time, Seed), Sound (instrument only: source, Restart, Octave; Tone, Attack, Release, Detune, Sub, Velocity), After (Phaser, rate, fb, 3D spread, 3D depth, Add after buttons). Only as many level dials as there are steps (redrawn when the mouse is let go). Tooltips in plain words on everything; no dashes.
- **The name**: a small FRACTAL wordmark in the title row of the instrument and the effect (italic heavy capitals in a pink to amber gradient, the user's spikes A B C in a row under it, a soft glow; `crkMark`), as asked during the run. The logo tile in the browsers shows the spikes and FRACTAL.
- **20 presets** in 7 groups, the same patterns for both (the instrument adds a sound to each): Your drawing (A B C as drawn 1/16; A B small and tall), Sidechain pump (every beat, every 1/8, hard pump deeper on 1 and 3), Trance gate (1/16, swung 1/16, triplet 1/8T), Stutter (1/32, machine gun 1/64), Rattle, growl and buzz (Rattle, Growl, Buzz, Soft rumble; on the instrument the first three follow the key), Crackle and noise (Crackle, Noise bursts, Psytrance rattle 1/64 rolls), Wild (Phased growl, 3D crackle cloud, 3D spiral gate).

### Tested (headless, no page errors on load; `tools/engine-tests/fractal.py`, `tools/ui-tests/fractal.py`)
- **On the grid**: effect with square pulses, Smooth 0, a steady input, at 90, 120, 137 and 174 bpm for 1/16, 1/8T and 1/32: every onset found (30, 22, 62 per case), at most 0.057 samples from the grid after the 45 samples the app makes up for (the limit of reading a band-limited edge with a straight line between samples).
- **Levels against the dials**, measured in the middle of each pulse: 35/100/65, 100/0/50/25/80 and 10 to 80 come out exactly (to 4 decimals). Pump on every beat: level 1.00 just before the beat, 0.50 after 1 ms, 0.03 after 5 ms, 0.38 after 50 ms, 0.84 after 150 ms, 1.00 after 300 ms.
- **In tune and the lower tone** (instrument, sine source, keys 45, 52, 57): with Pulse = key everything is on multiples of the expected spacing (anything else 147 dB or more under); 2 equal steps: no tone at rate/2 (more than 190 dB down); 2 steps 100/40: rate/2 at -6.1 dB against the pulse rate; 3 steps 30/100/60: rate/3 at -8.4 dB; 4 steps 100/40/80/20: rate/4 at -4.8 dB. Pattern = key: the note is the lowest line (19 dB over the pulse rate), anything else 132 dB or more under.
- **Aliasing**, effect on a sine (everything not at sine +- k x pulse rate, below 0.43 of the sample rate): 5 kHz with 1500 Hz pulses -131.9 dB, 9 kHz with 3100 Hz pulses -123.3 dB, 2 kHz with 777 Hz 10% pulses -117.6 dB, 15 kHz with 2900 Hz -128.6 dB; the same chop done the plain way: -27.2, -18.2, -20.9, -18.3 dB.
- **DC**: instrument presets 79 dB or more under the peak (Buzz the most); effect presets on a saw pad 52 to 107 dB under the peak (3D crackle cloud the most, the pan per step on a saw).
- **Clicks**, largest jump between samples against the steady 110 Hz sine: square with Smooth 0 (asks for clicks) 22.3; Smooth 0.6 ms 1.37; Smooth 2 ms 0.50; the default (shape 15%, Smooth 0.6 ms) 0.40; shape 60% 0.40.
- **Mix 0** against no effect: identical sample for sample. Mix 100 with the gate always open: the input 45 samples later, within 0.0001 dB per third octave from 20 Hz to 18 kHz; -0.03 dB at 19 to 20 kHz, -0.87 dB at 20 to 21 kHz, -4.6 dB at 21 to 22 kHz at 44.1 kHz (the half-band filters; at 48 kHz the edge is 1.6 kHz higher).
- **Presets**: all 20 on the instrument (a phrase with a chord) peak -16.0 to -6.6 dBFS, no NaN; all 20 as an effect on a saw pad: no NaN, no clip, never louder than the dry pad (-12.5 dBFS).
- **Same every time**: instrument presets and effect presets bit-identical in two renders; a note moved by 293 samples with Restart on gives the same sound exactly, except the noise presets (the noise is seeded from the note's start, so a moved note gets other noise, on purpose).
- **Cost** (test machine, 32 s of sound): instrument 2.0 to 2.4 s over the same project with one short note, that is 63 to 75 ms per second of sound; the effect 1.4 to 1.8 s over the same audio without it, 43 to 57 ms per second. Heavier than Sub (8 to 25 ms); most of it is the two half-band filters and the pulse edges at twice the sample rate.
- **Page**: nothing outside the device on all pages of both (instrument 916 x 253, effect 868 x 253), title order as on the other devices with the wordmark in the name's place, preset groups 2/3/3/2/4/3/3, four presets and four undos, Kind, Add Phaser and + Vector Delay with undo, latency 45 and 0 at Mix 0, playing; `devices_all.py`: Fractal as instrument and effect, nothing outside, presets 20, shut and open. `preset_switch.py` with Fractal (`ONLY=Fractal`): old preset gone after 14 to 18 ms; sixteenths all there once and on the grid; Play inside a long note sounds; the "heard again 20 to 50 ms after a switch" check reads silent because that window falls in the gaps of the 1/16 pattern (as for Bounce, by nature).
- **Unchanged**: Fractals demo, solo, beats 64 to 96: Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB.
- Screenshots looked at: instrument on all four pages, Growl, Crackle, Pump on every beat, 3D crackle cloud, playing; effect on its three pages, Buzz, Trance gate.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has listened to it.** Presets, levels, the Pump curve and the phaser were set by measurement and by reasoning.
- Real mouse or trackpad, the Mac build, MIDI learn, automation and modulators on the knobs; a tempo change inside the song (the device uses the song's main tempo, as the other tempo-locked devices do).
- Live: notes played by hand start 3 ms after the key; a note that reaches the engine less than 78 frames before its time starts a little late (exports are not affected).
- The effect's 45-sample delay with the app's delay compensation was checked only through `fxLat`, not by ear on a full song.

### Left
- Listen and tune with the user; maybe a drawing mode for the spikes (drag the tops in the picture instead of the dials), ties between steps, a tempo map.
- The cost (about 50 to 70 ms per second of sound per device) could come down with a lighter edge path for slow patterns.
- Task E (CPU and background freeze).

### Release
- v0.191 (v0.190 was taken by Psy Bass, released by another run during this one; this was rebuilt and tested again on top of it).
- v0.191: commit 46bafa9, artifact version 214, project doc `claude/audiolab-crackle-pulse-synth.md`.
- **Desktop build**: run 45747286773 for this commit succeeded (1 min 59 s, started 03:11 UTC).

## 26 November 2026, v0.192: arrangement edges, playhead look and Follow (task R, from the user's message)

### What the user asked
No blurred boxes at the top left (under the project tabs, above Controls and the track headers, left of Intro) or the bottom left (below Master, above Settings and Assistant, and the thin blur above Master): the arrangement should carry on behind them. No line behind the Instruments, Audio and return-arrow buttons. The time over the playhead as 04:12 (two digits each side, colon on the line), hidden where it passes behind the project tabs and the plus button. A Playhead section in Settings with shapes, colours and thicknesses from research. A clearer Follow button with a right-click menu of follow modes.

### What the boxes were
- Since the glass rebuild the arrangement canvas (`#arr`) is clipped away under the whole header column whenever the header glass is taller than half the arrangement (`gbLayout`), and a half-size copy of it (`#arrBack`, made for the blur under the glass) shows there. Above the first track and below the last one that half-size copy was seen directly: the "blurred boxes".
- The canvas also darkened everything left of the song start (0.42 black) plus everything left of beat 0 again (0.3 black), from x = 0, so with the song start at the header edge the whole column under the headers was a dark block, and the grid lines started at beat 0.
- The header glass (`#hglass`) reached from the caption row to the middle of the add buttons, so its right edge and bottom edge ran through "Instrument" and the plus signs; the add buttons were 72% see-through with their own 8 px blur.

### What was built
- **Header glass** from the top of the first track to the bottom of the last track (`HGR` keeps its place for the canvas code). The caption row (Tracks, Controls), the automation buttons, the add buttons and Master float over the arrangement with only their own small backgrounds.
- **No darkening under the header column**, and no darker block left of the song start any more: the song start is a thin line like the song end; the grid carries on into negative beats (as 4/4). The darker area after the song end stays, only over the arrangement itself.
- **`gbClip` / `gbCol` and a new canvas `#arrCol`**: the arrangement is still clipped under the whole header column with a plain rectangle (`inset`), and a canvas as wide as the column shows the two strips above the first track and below the last one, copied from `#arr` after every drawing (also playhead-only frames). Nothing under the header glass changes, so the glass is not recalculated. A first try clipped `#arr` with a polygon around the glass; it cost about 8 ms more per frame while playing in the test browser (polygon clips are masks), so it was replaced. `#arrCol` leaves the page in Lightest with the other glass layers (`pmLayers`), and is mirrored for headers on the right.
- **Add buttons**: solid background, no blur, the plus signs dropped (the tooltips say "Add ... track"), so "Instrument" fits.
- **Time over the playhead** (`tmTime`, `tmFlag`, `#phFlag`): `00:00`, `04:12`, `12:05`, `1:02:03`; three grid cells (minutes, colon, seconds) in a fixed 112 px box so the colon sits on the middle of the playhead line (measured 0.04 px off), tabular figures. `tmClip` hides it where it passes behind any project tab, the plus button or the buttons on the right of the tab bar (a partial overlap cuts it with `inset`; the rectangles are cached and refreshed by a ResizeObserver and a MutationObserver on the tabs). The read-out now also moves on playhead-only frames.
- **Playhead look** (Settings, Zoom and playhead, new "Playhead look"; `UI.ph = {s, c, w, t}`, saved with the app settings): shapes Line, Triangle head, Handle, Glow, Beam, Double line, Comet tail, Scanning bar, Dashed (`PHSH`, `phDraw`, a few rectangles or one gradient each, no shadows or filters); colour Accent (the theme's playhead colour) or eight swatches or any colour; thickness 1 to 6 px; time above the playhead on or off; a live preview (`phPrevDraw`, runs only while the page is open) and small pictures of each shape; "Back to the standard playhead".
- **Follow button** (`fbDraw`, `FBW`): a pill with a small playhead icon and the word Follow, in the playhead colour when on, dark when off; left-click toggles as before. **Right-click menu** (`followMenu`): Follow on or off; Keep the playhead in the middle; Stay in place (new mode `stay`: the playhead keeps the spot where it was when Play was pressed and the song scrolls past, `FSTAYF`); Look ahead; Page by page; Push at the edge, smoothly; Off; Follow again 3 s after I stop scrolling; Jump back to the playhead; Playhead look in Settings. Settings has a "Stay in place" tile next to Keep centred, Page by page and Don't follow. The old menu label with a dash is gone.

### Research (sources)
- Cubase and Nuendo: Auto-Scroll button with two settings, Page Scroll (the cursor runs to the right edge, then the view jumps) and Stationary Cursor (the cursor stays in the middle and the ruler scrolls), plus Suspend Auto-Scroll when Editing. archive.steinberg.help/nuendo/v12/en/cubase_nuendo/topics/playback/playback_autoscroll_r.html, forums.steinberg.net (stationary cursor threads).
- Logic Pro: Catch button per window; with View > Scroll in Play the playhead stays in the middle and the content scrolls; Catch preferences "when Logic starts" and "when moving playhead". help.apple.com/logicpro/mac/10.2.1/en.lproj/lgcp5cbf1727.html. Logic's playhead has a handle in the ruler.
- Pro Tools: Edit window scrolling options (No Scrolling, After Playback, Page, Continuous); a Playback Cursor locator in the ruler when the cursor is off screen (red when a track is armed, blue otherwise). apps.avid.com/proToolsFirstHelp/version12.3/enu/Pro Tools First Help/rec1.playback.19.11.html, production-expert.com Pro Tools scrolling options.
- Ableton Live: Follow (paging); forum threads on follow behaviour and its frame rate. forum.ableton.com. REAPER: play cursor colour and look come from the theme; cockos wiki Preferences Appearance (the page did not load), reaper.fm theme docs.
- Shapes from memory of the DAWs (not looked up page by page): thin line with a point (Ableton, Bitwig, Cubase, REAPER), large triangle head (Studio One, FL Studio), handle (Logic); the creative ones (glow, beam, comet tail, scanning bar, double, dashed) are our own.

### Tests
- `tools/ui-tests/playhead.py` (new): time read-outs `00:00 04:00 12:05 1:02:03`; colon 0.04 px from the line; read-out hidden at every place behind the tabs (230 to 480 px); the arrangement clip is a rectangle and the strips canvas is on; Follow 58 px wide, a click toggles, a right-click opens the menu with the modes; frame time while playing for every shape (19 to 25 ms in the software-rendered test browser, all within noise of each other); no page errors.
- Corners looked at (2x screenshots) at the top left and bottom left with the arrangement scrolled to 0, right, left of the song start and down, in Full effects, Optimised, Lighter and Lightest; headers on the right; every shape on the arrangement; Settings page; the menu.
- Frame time against v0.191 while playing (same test, twice each): centred 23 to 26 ms against 22 to 27 ms, page 20 to 21 ms against 21 to 22 ms.
- Unchanged: Fractals demo solo Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96).

### Not verified
- A real Mac GPU (the test browser draws in software); the look in other DAW themes than the standard one; touch.
- Right after a vertical scroll the strips are copied in the same frame; while resizing the window they may lag one frame.

### Left
- An option to follow while stopped (when the playhead jumps); a playhead locator arrow in the ruler when the playhead is off screen (Pro Tools style; today there is the small jump button).

### Release
- v0.192, built on v0.191 (Fractal, task P), which another session released while this one was testing; one merge of two additions in index.html.
- **Artifact**: version 215 of artifact 1b07d888 (index.html only, lines 3 to 10 removed).
- **Desktop build**: run 45749404233 for commit 8c0c7b4 succeeded (2 min 19 s).

## 26 November 2026, v0.193: the MIDI panel rework (task Q, from a later message)
> "Let's rework the MIDI panel. Right now, when you expand the MIDI panel at the same time with the devices, a lot of buttons are cut off and it takes over the whole screen. [...] I want the MIDI panel above the devices panel at the same height as the devices panel. [...] when you minimize the devices panel, I want the top part of the MIDI window to stay there and the bottom part to expand [...] window mode [...] the same top head that we have with the mixer panel [...] the rest of the window to be borderless [...] buttons to be different and smaller [...] make it resizable [...] bigger buttons like you had before [...] glass effects on the top of the devices panel"

### What was wrong
- The docked piano roll was 46% of the window high and sat on top of the devices panel inside the bottom dock, which is anchored to the bottom of the stage. At 1280 x 720 its top (the whole toolbar) was pushed 21 px above the stage and the track tabs of the devices panel covered the bottom 25 px of its velocity lane.

### Built (new block "v0.193: the MIDI panel rework (task Q)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`)
- **Docked** (`prDockFit`, `prAdj`, `prFxOpenH`, state `PRDK`): with the devices panel directly under it, the piano roll is exactly as high as the devices panel (262 px by default) and ends where the track tabs start (25 px gap, class `prgap`), so nothing covers it. Its height comes from `--prh` (class `prdk`). It is never taller than what is left of the stage (the toolbar can no longer leave the screen).
- **Devices hidden**: the piano roll's top edge stays where it is and it grows down by the devices panel's height (class `prcol`); showing the devices puts it back (checked to the pixel).
- **The border between them** (`#prDiv`, a small bar at the bottom edge of the piano roll): drag it to move the border; the piano roll grows and the devices panel shrinks by the same amount, the top stays. Remembered in `UI.prDH` and `UI.fxDH` (the devices panel's height is only changed while the piano roll is docked above it; with the piano roll closed it is the normal 262 px). Double-click: both the same height again. Devices at least 215 px (below that the channel strip's A/B row is cut), piano roll at least 150 px.
- **The top edge** (`#prTopH`): drag to make the piano roll taller or shorter without touching the devices; double-click to follow the devices height again. With the devices hidden the same drag keeps the top-stays rule (it stores the height for the shown state).
- **MIDI alone** (devices popped out into their window, or placed elsewhere in the layout): same default height (`UI.prDH`, else the devices panel's height).
- **Window mode** (`prHeadSync`, `prClampW`): a head like the mixer's (`#prHead`, same look as `#mixWin>.fwh`: grip dots, the clip's name, then the Dock, Full screen and Close buttons moved in from the toolbar, solid `rgb(var(--gtr2))`, 34 px, sitting on the top right edge); the rest is borderless (no border, rounded corners, only the shadow). Drag the head to move; it cannot leave the top of the screen. Resizable from every edge and corner (the app's `rzScan` handles, min 380 x 200). Position and size remembered (`UI.prW`, as before).
- **Compact buttons** (`prCmpSync`, class `prcmp`): smaller buttons (23 px instead of 28 to 30), no key letters on the tools, Ghost and Feel show only their icons, narrower lists, a smaller head. Used in a window smaller than **1000 x 460 px** (`PRCW`, `PRCH`); at that size or larger the normal buttons come back. Docked, compact is used when the panel is narrower than **1240 px** (`PRCDW`), so the toolbar stays on one row down to about 1000 px; narrower windows wrap to a second row (all buttons visible).
- **Toolbar**: every control listed (title, 9 tools, Timing, Length and Shape menus, Snap, Scale root and Scale, Scale lock, Chord, Ghost, Feel, Devices (full screen only), Window, Full screen, Close, and the Velocity list in the lane): 26 counted in the test. Scale lock now has a drawn lock icon instead of the emoji; buttons without a tooltip got one (`prWords`).
- **Glass** (`fxBandSync`, `fxBandR`; wrappers of `fxPic`, `gbClip`, `abDraw`, `fxGlassSync`, `applyTrans`): a glass band (`#fxband`) behind the track tabs on top of the devices panel (below it when the devices are at the top). It shows the same ready-made blurred picture of the arrangement as the devices panel (the arrangement canvas is clipped away under it, so there is no blur pass of its own), refraction on top when that effect is on (Full effects), a live blur only when there is no picture, a lit top rim that follows the edge light strength. Solid in Lighter and Lightest (and with blur and refraction off). Hidden in full screen, split view and when the devices are a window. `UI.fxBand=false` turns it off (no setting for it yet).
- Wrappers: `prMode`, `openPR`, `closePR`, `devHide`, `devPop`, `applyLayout`, plus a ResizeObserver on the piano roll, the stage and the devices panel.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/midipanel.py`, headless, Fractals demo)
- At 1280 x 720, 1440 x 900, 1920 x 1080 and 2560 x 1440 (also 1024 x 700 and 900 x 640): both open, devices hidden, MIDI alone, window 640 x 330, window as large as fits (up to 1500 x 760), docked again. In every case all 26 controls are visible, fully inside the panel or its head, on screen, not covered by anything (checked at three points each) and have a tooltip. 77 checks, 0 failures.
- Top edge stays when the devices are hidden (to the pixel) and comes back when they are shown. Small window compact, large window normal buttons.
- Mouse drags: the divider 40 px down (piano roll +40, devices -40, top unchanged); the top edge 80 px up (piano roll +80, devices unchanged); devices hidden after that keeps the top; sizes after a reload; double-click on the divider; a window corner from 700 x 380 to 1120 x 540 (compact to normal buttons); the head (moves, cannot leave the top); window position and size after a reload.
- Glass at each stop: Full effects (picture plus refraction, `backdrop-filter: url(#rf..)` only), Optimised (picture, no filter), Lighter and Lightest (solid, no filter).
- Frame time while playing (1600 x 1000, software drawing), band on against off, alternated 4 times: Full effects 27.0 against 28.3 ms, Optimised 17.5 against 18.0 ms: no measurable cost.
- No page errors on load at any size. Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).
- Screenshots looked at: all of the above at the four sizes, the divider drag, the band at each stop.

### Not verified
- A real GPU and the user's Mac (the refraction on a 25 px high band, full width, should be cheap: the map is small and the picture behind it changes only when the arrangement is redrawn).
- Touch and pen. Theme colours other than the default on the new head and band (they use the theme's panel colour).

### Left
- A setting to switch the glass band off (only `UI.fxBand=false` for now).
- The full-screen piano roll keeps its old layout (devices 222 px under it), unchanged.
- Artifact version 216 (1b07d888). Desktop build run 45750489821 dispatched for commit 530d246. Project doc `claude/audiolab-ui-run-Q.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.194: the mixer strip rework (task S1, from a later message)
> "When you double click a channel, it should open the devices on the channel ... if you double right click, it should instead open the mixer panel on the channel ... rework the stereo slider ... show how wide it is, but at the same time also show the stereo field inside of the slider ... under like 80 px, the pan slider should become a half dial ... a half dial, like in a car dial or a pressure gauge dial, and also a full dial ... make a very intuitive pan dial that also gives you the information visually if the sound is in the center or not ... the flat bars are now going to be the standard ... make an extra flat bar with the new redesign ... master stay pinned ... a pin button, on by default ... compact ... also from the bottom up ... the master docks back on the right so it doesn't overlap the last two channels." (shortened; the mixer cables are task S2 and were not touched)

### Research (sources read this run)
- **Pan and balance**: Logic gives mono channels a Pan knob and stereo channels a Balance knob, with Stereo Pan (two handles on a ring for the left and right position, drag the ring for the spread, the centre to move both) and Binaural as other modes; balance only lowers one side, it cannot move a source across (help.apple.com Logic Pro 10.4.5 "pan, balance"; ask.audio "Logic Pro's Balancing Act"; Direction Mixer: Spread 0 = mono, above 1 wider, Direction rotates). Pan law: -3 dB keeps loudness on speakers, -6 dB keeps mono level, -4.5 dB is the usual compromise and DAWs differ (soundonsound.com "What pan-law setting should I use"). RME TotalMix can re-centre pan and balance when a channel switches mono/stereo (docs.rme-audio.de preferences). Also looked at from memory of the programs: Ableton and Bitwig use a small round pan knob or a split stereo pan; Reaper offers several pan modes per track (balance, stereo pan, dual pan); hardware consoles (SSL, Neve) use a plain rotary pot with a centre detent. None of them shows where the sound really sits on the pan control itself; that information is only in separate meters.
- **Width and the stereo field**: Ozone Imager has one width slider per band (-100 = mono) and a scope with Polar Sample (dots, two 45 degree "safe lines": inside is in phase, outside out of phase), Polar Level (rays: length = level, angle = position) and Lissajous, a correlation meter (+1 to -1) and a correlation trace (downloads.izotope.com Ozone 8 Imager). The goniometer is L/R turned 45 degrees so mono is a vertical line; correlation is green from 0 to +1 and red below 0 (soundonsound.com stereo metering, free part). The VSL PowerPan page did not load (only the manual index).
- **Meters**: RME TotalMix: peak hold time 0.1 to 9.9 s, over after 1 to 10 full-scale samples, RMS +3 dB option (docs.rme-audio.de). Studio One / Fender Studio Pro: Peak or Peak/RMS per channel, peak hold set globally by right-click, K-System scales, a clip counter above the main meter that resets on click (fenderstudiopromanual.fender.com Metering). Ardour: digital peak, K20/K14/K12, IEC PPM, VU, all as bars; peaks over the threshold turn red; click the peak indicator to reset (manual.ardour.org/meters). Common to the readable ones: a dB scale beside the bar, a firm peak-hold line, colour zones that stay visible, a clear 0 dB mark, a numeric read-out.

### Decisions from the research
- The pan control gets a **live balance marker** instead of a separate meter: from the left and right energy of the channel's own analysers, b = (R² - L²) / (R² + L²), drawn at the pan position that gives that balance with the app's equal-power StereoPannerNode (p = 2/π · asin b). So a mono sound sits exactly under the handle, and a stereo sound that leans shows the marker apart from the handle. Green when it is within the centre (4 %, leaves at 6 % so it does not flicker), amber off centre, hidden when the channel is silent (below about -70 dB). This is the "centre of the sound" the user asked for; a separate centre dial was not needed.
- The width slider uses the Ozone Polar Sample idea folded into a line: every sample pair's angle of (mid, side) from -90° to +90°, energy weighted, 40 bins; ±45° (only left, only right) falls exactly on the 25 % and 75 % marks, which is also where 100 % width ends, and the red outer quarters are out of phase. So the bracket (the setting) and the field (the sound) use the same scale.
- Flat bars stay flat but take the readability rules above: the zone colours dimly visible behind the bars, a 0 dB line across, a solid white peak line, wider bars, a flat grip tab with the track colour.

### Built (new block "mixer strip rework (task S1, v0.194)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`)
- **Pan styles** (`s1PanKind`, `s1PanBuild`): `UI.mxs.panst` = `auto` (default: slider, half dial when the strip is under 80 px; compact strips are 46 px so they get the half dial), `slider`, `half`, `full`. Same control id `mx:<track>:pan`, so header, device strip and mixer stay linked. In thin strips the slider runs edge to edge with a 6 px handle (class `mxpc`, wrapper of `paintCtl`). Compact channels now show pan too (small). Setting "Pan control" in the mixer menu and in Settings, Interface.
- **Balance marker** (`s1Bal`, `s1Mark`, `s1MixTick` from a `drawMixer` wrapper, visible channels only, every second frame; device strip pan bar from `s1Loop` at about 30 per second while visible): a dot on the slider handle, a dot on the rim of the dials, a triangle under the device strip pan bar; centre tick turns green.
- **Width slider** (`ctl` wrapper for kind `ws`, `s1Field`, `s1FieldDraw`): 16 px strip with the in-phase middle and red out-of-phase ends, 100 % marks, a white bracket for the setting, the live field drawn on a canvas behind it only while playing and visible.
- **Flat bars 2** (design 22, appended to `FDS`, header look appended to `HFD`): the default (`MXSD.fd`, and the AudioLab theme). Once per profile (`UI.fd22`) a saved Classic (1) or Flat bars (7) choice moves to 22; Flat bars 7 is still there.
- **Master pin** (`s1Pin`, button `.mxpin` in the master's name row, `UI.mxs.pin`, default on, also "Keep the master in view" in the settings): off lets the master scroll with the channels (class `mxunpin`). Below 600 px (360 px with compact channels) it unpins by itself and the button goes off; clicking it then pins anyway until the mixer is wide again; wider again re-pins. In compact mode at the end of the scroll the master sits after the last channel (no overlap, checked).
- **Compact** (`s1CmpH`, `cabH` wrapper, fixes in `mxFit`): the window gets about 26 % shorter (top stays, at least 280 px; the old height comes back when compact is switched off), the cable lane 20 % lower, gaps 3 px, master 112 px without the monitor buttons and extras. `mxFit` no longer reserves room for the hidden effect slots in compact mode, so the faders keep their height.
- **Transport** (`#mBtn`): an icon, bolder, its own background, still joined to the level meter in one group; while the mixer is open it lights up blue and the group glows (`s1Watch`, MutationObserver on the window's `hidden`). In the narrow transport the icon stays.
- **Double-click / double right-click** (`s1MixFocus`): double-click a track header (also its name) opens that track's devices; Alt and double-click on the name still renames (also in the track menu). Double-click a mixer channel already opened its devices. Two right-clicks within 450 ms on a header or a mixer channel close the menu the first opened and open the mixer, scrolled so the channel is in view, selected and lit up for 1.5 s.

### Tested (headless, software drawing; `tools/ui-tests/mixstrip.py`)
- No page errors on load or in any step. Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).
- Balance marker: Hat (panned L10) reads -0.100, Open hat (R10) +0.100, centred tracks 0.000. Test signals into the selected track's analysers: mono sine 0.000 and field peak in the middle bin; inverted right channel 0.000 balance and the field at the red ends; a sine panned -50 reads -0.500 and its field peak left of centre; noise (wide) spread over the middle with red tails.
- Mouse: drag in slider, half dial and full dial changes pan; double-click centres; compact slider drag; double-click on a header and on a strip opens that track's devices; double right-click on a header and on a strip opens the mixer on it, the menu closed; a single right-click still opens the menu.
- Pin: on by default, off and on by click, auto off at 454 px and back at 1252 px, forced on while narrow. Compact: 470 to 348 px and back to 470; at the end of the scroll the last channel ends at 1124 px and the master starts at 1135 px.
- Screenshots looked at: mixer at strip width 64, 66, 70, 96 and 120 in each pan style, compact in each style, scrolled to the end, unpinned, narrow, the flash; the width slider with mono, wide, out-of-phase and panned signals; the transport button on and off at 1600 and 1920 wide; Settings, Interface.
- Frame time with the mixer open and playing (1920 x 1080): 31.4 ms mean before, 32.0 after (software drawing, within noise); `drawMixer` 2.12 ms before, 2.09 ms after.

### Not verified
- The user's Mac, real GPU, trackpad. The look of Flat bars 2 with the other meter colour sets and themes (only Classic colours looked at).
- The balance marker on the header pan controls (not added: the headers are small and already busy; the mixer and the device strip have it).

### Left
- Header pan controls could get the same marker. The cable lane (task S2) was not touched.
- Artifact version 217 (1b07d888). Desktop build run 45753812908 dispatched for commit e649c00. Project doc `claude/audiolab-ui-run-S1.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.195: visualizer, glass preview, splash, zoom (task U, from a later message)

### What the user asked
The visualizer should dock at the top too and be sharper on high resolution screens without costing CPU; right-click on the map or the visualizer gives a menu to turn each on or off. The Glass and light preview should show more scrolling clips and real UI items, as a prerecorded loop per setting, not live. No "press to skip" on the splash: it is the real loading screen, appears at once, is quick on a fast computer and leaves right after everything is ready. The zoomed-out overview should show clip names, and zooming back in should land in the arrangement at the same scale and position so it reads as one fluid zoom.

### Built (new block "v0.195: visualizer, glass preview, splash, zoom (task U)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`)
- **Visualizer at the top**: option Top in the visualizer's own place menu and "Docked at the top" in Settings; it sits in `#topOut` right above the arrangement (`vizApply` edit, `applyLayout` wrapper keeps it last there), resized from its bottom edge.
- **Sharp**: the canvas is drawn at device pixels times the app zoom (`vzDp`, at most 3; it used to ignore the app zoom, so any zoom above 100 % was blurry). The spectrogram was a picture at one pixel per point, scaled up with smoothing (the blur) and copied onto itself every frame. It is now a ring buffer (`VZD.spec`): one new column per frame, drawn in two parts without smoothing, so it is crisp and cheaper. The buffer stays at one pixel per point on purpose: a buffer at screen pixels cost 4 to 6 times more per frame in the test (sending the changed picture to the screen dominates). Frequency rows are now 1 point instead of 2.
- **Cost** (headless, software drawing, screen at 2 device pixels per point, playing, All view): one visualizer frame 0.70 ms before, 0.47 ms after; Spectrogram alone 3.5 ms before, 1.0 to 1.4 ms after.
- **Right-click menu** (`pvMenu`, on `#mapw` and `#vizp`): switches for Map and Visualizer (the menu stays open), and where the visualizer sits (top, bottom, left, right, own window). Settings checkboxes follow (`pvSync`).
- **Overview clip names** (`ovwNames`): each block gets its clip's name when the row is at least 8 px high and the block wide enough; the name sticks to the left edge of the view, shortened with an ellipsis (no text measuring), dark text on light colours.
- **One fluid zoom** (`ovwEnter` wrapper, `ovwGeo`, `ovwZoom`, `ovwLeave` edits, `ovwRh`, `ovwPpbMin`): entering, the overview starts at exactly the normal view's scale and position (it used to jump to "whole song fits", often a different scale), and its zoom range is extended to include that. Rows grow from the overview height to the normal view's smallest track height as the overview zooms in. Zooming in past the top leaves at once (no 180 ms wait, no extra push) with the normal view at the overview's scale, anchored under the pointer. A double-click zooms in twice as far. Esc, the chip and Shift+O still go back to exactly where you were.
- **Splash = loading screen**: the splash card is now plain HTML at the top of `<body>` with a few lines of script, so it is on screen at the first paint, before the 2.7 MB of code is read. While the code loads (the page cannot draw then) its bar runs on the compositor for as long as the last start took (`localStorage` `audiolab-boot`). Then `bootRun` (replaced) follows the real steps: interface built, audio engine (worklet loaded), fonts, first picture of the tracks, and the page settled (no long task over 50 ms for 120 ms, at most 2.5 s; where long tasks cannot be watched, three quick frames). The panels and controls appear as the bar passes them, the clips draw in during the last quarter, and the card fades in 0.26 s the moment the bar is full. "Click to skip" and the click are gone. The timings are in `BOOTU.at`. Turning the splash off in Settings, and child windows, still show no splash.
- **Glass and light preview** (`gpDraw` replaced, `gpvContent`, `gpvSheet`, `gpvGet`, `gpvFrame`, `gpvSetup`): a page of the app scrolls under the pane in a 1280 px loop: ruler with bar numbers, four sections, four tracks of clips with names (waveforms, notes, automation, one selected clip with a white border), device cards with knobs, meters, M/S/R buttons with a fader, the tempo pill. Buttons under the preview: Your setting, Full effects, Optimised, Lighter, Lightest. **How the loops are made (the cheapest way that looks right):** because everything scrolls together, a scrolled glass picture is the same as the glass picture of the page, shifted. So for each setting the page is blurred, saturated and tinted once into one picture (about 20 ms, blurred pictures at 1x since blur hides detail, sharp ones at screen pixels), and playback copies parts of the page and of that picture 30 times a second: the pane, the refraction bands (a 12 px lens at each edge squeezing the picture from just outside the pane, in 6 slices), and the edge light from a tiny brightness map. No filters, no backdrop blur, no DOM glass while it plays; the old live pane and canvases are hidden. Up to 3 settings stay in memory (about 3 MB each). Playback cost measured: 2 to 5 ms per second.

### Load time (headless; `tools/ui-tests/loadtime.py`, normal start = second and later runs in the same profile)
- Normal CPU: splash on screen at 0.65 to 0.93 s before, 0.20 to 0.25 s after. Splash starts to fade at 4.2 to 4.5 s before (a fixed 2.8 s animation after the code), 1.5 to 1.7 s after (0.03 to 0.13 s after the page settled).
- CPU slowed 4 times: splash at 2.3 s before, 0.45 to 0.48 s after; fade at 6.6 to 7.1 s before, 4.6 to 4.9 s after.
- First start ever (new profile) takes longer in both, because the new demo project is set up then; the splash waits for that.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskU.py`, headless)
- No page errors on load at device pixel ratio 1 and 2. Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).
- Screenshots looked at: visualizer at the top and bottom at ratio 1 and 2, both menus, the preview for each setting, the overview with names, the splash at 150, 400, 700 ms and later.
- Menus: Visualizer off from the map's menu, Map off and the visualizer to the top from the visualizer's menu, Map back on, Settings boxes follow.
- Zoom (Ctrl+wheel around a clip, "Lead low 57"): entering, beat 64 at 657.5 px before and after; leaving, the clip at x 1014.0 px width 87.3 px just before and just after (scale 2.729 both), top 475.6 to 478.0 px and height 32 to 29 px (the normal view draws clips 3 px inside their row).
- `tools/ui-tests/overview.py` passes; it needed more zoom-in key presses (the overview now starts at the normal view's scale, so from a far zoom-out it takes more steps to come back); its loop limit was raised to 40.

### Not verified
- The user's Mac: real GPU, retina, trackpad pinch, Safari (no long-task timing there, so the frame-gap fallback is used).
- How the loading bar looks on a very first start of a new version (the time of the last start is the guess for the bar).

### Left
- The splash cannot animate the logo while the code is being read (the page cannot draw then); only the bar moves, and a soft glow stands in for the logo until the code is ready.
- Artifact version 218 (1b07d888). Desktop build run 45754961657 dispatched for commit 4224859. Project doc `claude/audiolab-ui-run-U.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.196: device layout with side headers (task T, from a later message)
> "Let's make the show and hide devices button [...] a little bit bigger and look a little nicer and make it look more part of the box under it [...] when it's in the scrolling mode, on each side it should be more clear that you're scrolling and that there are more tabs [...] make the top bar with the name of the device together with the hide parameters button and the on-off button sideways, vertically on the left of each device, to save vertical space [...] I want it consistent throughout all the devices [...] another vertical header where the hide and show parameters button will be, and that's where the parameters come out [...] The pin button stays on the top right. And we should always have a remove button in the same place [...] Do some research on that."

### Research (sources)
- Ableton Live 12 manual, Working with Instruments and Effects (read): the title bar holds the Activator (on/off), hot-swap and save preset, and an arrow for expanded views; a device folds by double-clicking its title bar; there is **no delete button on the device**: you select it and press Delete. https://www.ableton.com/en/live-manual/12/working-with-instruments-and-effects/
- Bitwig Studio user guide, Introduction to Devices (read): the header has the Device Enable button, the name, a Remote Controls button that shows and hides that pane, and the Modulators button; presets are in the browser, not in the header. https://www.bitwig.com/userguide/latest/introduction_to_devices
- Reason 13 manual, Working with the Rack (read, second half through a summary): the Fold/Unfold button sits **to the left of the device panel**; devices are deleted with Backspace/Delete or the context menu, with a confirmation, not with a button on the device. https://docs.reasonstudios.com/reason13/working-with-the-rack
- Nielsen Norman Group, "Dangerous UX: Consequential Options Close to Benign Options" (read): destructive actions far from benign ones, extra distance is fine (Fitts' law used on purpose), plus redundant cues (colour, icon). https://www.nngroup.com/articles/proximity-consequential-options/
- SSW Rules, delete buttons (read): red for destructive actions only, a trash icon rather than a cross (a cross reads as Cancel or Close), not next to primary actions, a tooltip on icon-only buttons. https://www.ssw.com.au/rules/destructive-button-ui-ux
- Mozilla bug 1701976, "Hard to see that there are more tabs at the ends of the tab strip" (read): after a redesign the overflowing tab strip looked like it ended; the arrows at the ends are the real cue, a shadow alone is not enough. https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=1701976
- Searched, not read: Ableton help on navigation and view options (show and hide the detail view), Apple's tab bar guidance, Chrome's tab component guide, Telerik and SAP scrollable tab strips (arrows at both ends).
- Studio One and FL Studio mixer slots were not read; nothing here rests on them.

### Decisions
- **Remove**: one place on every device, the bottom of the left strip, as a trash icon that turns red on hover. It is as far as the device allows from on/off (top left) and from pin (top right), which follows the NN/g distance rule; the trash icon follows the "cross means cancel" finding (the ✕ also looked like closing a window). Undo brings a device back, as before; no confirmation for effects (the DAWs above delete at once with undo too).
- **Remove on an instrument**: the instrument is the track, so the same button asks "Delete the track?" (Keep it / Delete track) and then deletes the track (undo brings it back). The tooltip says to use Change to keep the track and try another instrument. Before, instruments had no remove at all.
- **Presets and Change**: in a row **on top of the parameters** (the user's first choice), not floating in the picture. Pictures keep their own labels in their top corners (OSC A, FILTER, readouts), which a floating bar would cover, and the parameters side has the height to spare. The row goes away with the parameters when they are hidden (as the presets did before). Only Grid, which has no parameter side, has the row floating at the top left of its patch.
- **Show and hide parameters**: a strip on the **right** edge of every device: the pin at the top (top right, as the user asked), below it the button over the rest of the height with the word "Parameters" (blue "Show parameters" when hidden). The parameters slide out of it to the left. Placing it at the right end keeps pin and button at the same place whether the parameters are open or not (between picture and parameters the pin would have had to move).
- **Devices tab**: the old 30 x 22 icon became a tab of the panel itself: panel icon, the word Devices and a chevron that points where the panel will go; the same fill as the panel and no line under it, so it reads as part of the box (the track tabs and Master pick what is shown; this tab folds the panel). Hidden, it stays on the edge as the handle in a light blue. Drag it up still pops the devices out.
- **Overflow of the track tabs**: the fades stay, and each end that has more tabs shows a solid button with an arrow and how many tabs are hidden on that side (Mozilla's finding: the arrows are the cue). A click scrolls by most of a width; dragging the tabs still works.

### Built (two blocks "v0.196: device layout with side headers (task T)" and "v0.196: the devices panel's own tab" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`)
- `dsDev(D)` runs from a wrapper of `pinButtons` (it is called for the panel and for every pinned window's render, right before the pinned devices are taken out), so panel, pinned windows and the Devices window share one layout. It turns `.devh` into the left strip (`.dsl`: `.pw` or the logo, the name upright with `writing-mode`, `.dsrm`), moves the other title items into `.dspr` at the top of `.dfs` (or `.eqside`; Grid: `.dspr.dsfl`), and builds `.dsr` (the `.pinbtn` moved from the title, the `.duar` arrow with a label). `duFit` runs again after the move; the slide-in animation is taken off while measuring (it starts at width 0, which made a reopened device 54 px too wide). A `pinHarvest` wrapper lights the pin in windows (the old code looked in `.devh`).
- The Fractal wordmark is turned upright in the strip. Particle devices are 54 px wider (922, Orbit and the other instruments 970, shut 462), so their picture keeps its old width and drawing code.
- Instrument remove: `[data-dsrm=inst]`, a capture click listener, `askChoice`, `delTrack`.
- Note for CSS: `pinCss()` skips selectors that contain "pinb", which includes `.pinbtn`; rules for the pin button must be written with `:is(#fxin,.pinb)` by hand.
- Tab: `dsHideSync()` (wrappers of `devHide`, `devPop`, `applyLayout`); `--dshw` keeps the track tabs clear of it. Overflow: `dsOvWire()` / `dsOvUpd()` from a `renderFx` wrapper, a scroll listener and a ResizeObserver.
- Nothing in the sound code, the presets or the engines was touched.

### Tested (headless; `tools/ui-tests/sideheaders.py` (new), `devices_all.py`, `design.py`, `pin.py`, `change_live.py`, `midipanel.py`)
- `sideheaders.py`: 7 tracks and the master at three sizes (panel 262 px, the smallest 215 px with the piano roll docked above, the Devices window), every device: left strip at the left edge with on/off or logo on top, remove within 8 px of the bottom, right strip with the pin within 8 px of the top, the row on top of the parameters, nothing outside the device; all devices of Bass shut at each size (row hidden, remove still there). Mouse: on/off, show and hide parameters (effect and instrument), next preset (Plant, EQ), Change (effect and instrument, Escape), remove of an effect and undo, remove on the instrument asks and Keep it keeps the track, pin two devices, the layout and lit pin in both windows, show and hide parameters inside a window, unpin from the windows, the Devices tab hides and shows, its tooltip; at 900 px the tab bar shows 5 hidden on the left and 16 on the right, the right button scrolls and does not fold the panel, at the start only the right button shows. **0 failures, no page errors.**
- `devices_all.py`: all 70 instruments and effects: nothing outside the device, parameters shut and open again to the same width. No errors.
- `design.py`, `pin.py`, `change_live.py`, `midipanel.py` (1280 x 720, 1920 x 1080): same results as before (the timing of a Change swap is the same order on the old file on this machine).
- Space: the picture of Plant, Sidechain, Kick designer and Compressor is 238 px high instead of 208 (+14 %), Pro EQ 238 instead of 196 (+21 %). Devices are 54 px wider (the two strips), Compressor and Sidechain 64 to 108 px wider because the preset row needs about 160 px.
- Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).
- Screenshots looked at: every device open, several shut, the panel at the three sizes, the master chain, pinned windows open and shut, the tab bar at 900 and 1600 px, the Devices tab shown and hidden.

### Not verified
- The user's Mac, a real mouse and trackpad, touch. Themes other than the default on the strips and the tab.
- Long names on very short panels are cut with dots in the strip (the full name is the tooltip).

### Left
- The placeholder a pinned device leaves in the panel and the tiles in the two browsers keep the old horizontal title.
- Narrow effects (Compressor, Sidechain) wrap Change to a second line in the row.

### Release
- v0.196: commit d216367. Artifact version 219 (1b07d888). Desktop build run 45755589112 dispatched for this commit. Project doc `claude/audiolab-ui-run-T.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.197: preset library, part 1: the synth instruments (from the user's message: "redesign every preset ... the best possible sounding presets from your research")
Part 1 of 3 (part 2: the other instruments, part 3: the effects). Only preset data changed: no engine and no sound calculation. Released on top of v0.191 (Fractal, task P) to v0.196, which other sessions released while this one worked; Fractal (`crk`) was left alone as asked.

### How presets are stored (found)
- Instruments: `PRESETS[key]` is the default, `IPRE[key]=[table, paramsFn]`; a table entry is `{n:name, ...settings}`. Basic instruments: `DUBI[key]` (made into `IPRE` by `duInit`); Plant `PLPRE`/`plantParams`; Acid `ACPRE`/`acidParams`; Wavetable `WTPRE`/`wtParams` (on `wt0`); Venom `VNPRE` (names "Group · Name", its window groups by them); Growl `GWPRE` (field `g`); Vector FM `VFMPRE`/`vfmParams`; Orbit `ORBPRE`/`orbParams`. The menus of Sub, Bounce and Psy Bass group by `p.g` through their own `instShell` replacements; all others showed a flat list. Effects use `"Group|Name"` (`preOpts`).
- Projects store the settings, not the preset, so old projects sound the same. Code that uses preset keys: the demos (Plant `lush`, `pluck`, `psybass`, `supersaw`; Acid `psy`) and the defaults (`wtParams('init')`, `grwParams('yoi')`, `vfmParams('glass')`, `orbParams('sun')`, `VNPRE.init`). These keep their exact values.

### Instruments in the browser (`IBG`), engine, parameters, presets before and after
| Key | Name | Engine | Parameters (range) | Presets before | After |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| saw, square, pad, strings, pluck, keys, sub, bass, reese | Saw lead, Square lead, Soft pad, Strings, Pluck, Soft keys, Sub bass, Square bass, Reese bass | pooled `al-poly` voice: one wave (sine, saw, square, triangle) as two oscillators at plus and minus Detune, 12 dB low-pass whose own envelope starts at 2.5 times the cutoff, ADSR | wave, det 0 to 40 ct, cutoff 60 to 18000 Hz, res 0.1 to 18, a 0.001 to 2 s, d 0.01 to 2 s, s 0 to 1, r 0.01 to 4 s, gain 0 to 1 | 5, 5, 5, 5, 6, 5, 5, 5, 5 | 20, 16, 18, 15, 20, 15, 11, 15, 14 |
| plant | Plant | `al-poly`: two waves with Morph, up to 7 voices, Width, Stereo, filter with Bloom envelope, FM with ratio, Sub, Noise, LFO to cutoff (Motion), Space send | cut 60 to 18000, res 0.1 to 15, fenv 0 to 100, uni 1 to 7, det 0 to 60, width, morph, fm 0 to 100, ratio 0.5 to 8, sub, noise, a 0.001 to 4, d 0.01 to 4, s, r 0.01 to 6, lfo 0.05 to 12 Hz, lamt, space, gain | 35 | 68 |
| wtab | Wavetable | `al-poly`: 12 tables of 8 frames, Position, scan LFO, position envelope, up to 5 voices, LP/HP/BP filter with envelope, sub, noise | pos, lfoRate, lamt, penv -100 to 100, pd, uni 1 to 5, det, width, cut, res, fenv, sub, noise, ADSR, gain | 30 | 48 |
| acid | Acid | `al-poly` acid voice: one wave, two filter stages, accent above velocity 0.88, slide, drive | wave, cut 60 to 8000, res 0 to 30, env, dec 0.03 to 1.5 s, acc, slide 0.01 to 0.3 s, dist, gain | 15 | 31 |
| venom | Venom | `al-venom` worklet (Serum-like): two wavetable oscillators with warp, sub, noise, 7 filter types, 2 envelopes, 2 LFOs, 8-slot mod matrix, 4 macros, distortion, chorus | about 90 settings (see `VNP`) | 55 | 72 |
| growl | Growl | rendered per note: FM pair, three-band mouth (vowels) moved by a tempo LFO, scrape, squeal, drive, fold, crush, squash | see `GWP` | 24 | 36 |
| vfm | Vector FM | `al-vfm` worklet: FM pairs (1 to 4 operators) moved by particles | see `VFMP` | 10 | 27 |
| orb | Orbit | `al-orb` worklet: FM pairs moved by balls orbiting a mass | see `ORBP` | 9 | 25 |
| Not in this part | Sub (`sbass`), Psy Bass (`psyb`), Bounce (`bball`), Fractal (`crk`), drums, samplers, Emit, Vector Grain, FX, Grid | | | | |
Total in this part: 229 presets before, 451 after. Sub, Psy Bass and Bounce were left: their presets are a day old, already grouped, with tooltips (Psy Bass) and made from their own research.

### Research (sources; the full text is in the project doc `claude/audiolab-preset-research.md`)
- What makes a good preset and library: developers in a MusicRadar roundup (u-he: "quality is more important than the sheer number"; Serum: about 450 presets kept from about 1000 submitted), [MusicRadar](https://musicradar.com/news/tech/must-a-synth-come-with-a-large-supply-of-good-presets-639042); counts: Serum 2 626 ([Xfer](https://www.xferrecords.com/products/serum-2)), u-he Diva 1354, Zebra2 551 ([u-he](https://new.u-he.com/support/faq/product-info/preset-count)), Vital 43 factory ([forum](https://forum.vital.audio/t/number-of-presets-and-waveforms-in-vital-plus/5164)); categories ([Vital](https://forum.vital.audio/t/categories-for-arps-brass-etc/6362), [NKS advice](https://community.native-instruments.com/discussion/comment/46421)); naming and gain staging ([Steinberg HALion preset guideline](https://steinbergmedia.github.io/halion-script-api/HALion-Tutorials-Guidelines/pages/HS-Preset-Guideline.html)); init preset and effects-heavy factory presets ([Gearnews](https://www.gearnews.com/?p=259059), [MusicRadar](https://musicradar.com/how-to/how-to-strip-back-a-synth-preset-to-make-it-more-usable)); bread and butter plus showcase ([NI](https://blog.native-instruments.com/massive-x-lab-sound-design/)); hot presets ([KVR](https://www.kvraudio.com/forum/viewtopic.php?p=4900736)); psytrance packs: Elements Psytrance 100 presets, leads 45 ([Loopmasters](https://www.loopmasters.com/genres/58-Psy-Trance/products/18716-Elements-Psytrance-Serum-Presets)).
- Recipes with numbers: JP-8000 supersaw ([gist](https://gist.github.com/audionerd/fe50790b7601cba65ddd855caffb05ad)); Syntorial preset recipes for a trance lead, a supersaw pad, trance, Goa and house plucks, acid, strings ([Syntorial](https://www.syntorial.com/preset-recipe/mark-sherry-imbecile-lead/) and siblings); reese ([MusicRadar](https://www.musicradar.com/how-to/classic-reese-bass-renegade-terrorist)); 303 ([MusicRadar](https://www.musicradar.com/news/producers-guide-roland-tb-303), [Attack](https://www.attackmagazine.com/technique/synth-secrets/acid-synth-uhe-diva/)); FM bell, brass, clarinet, wood drum ([Chowning 1973](https://web.eecs.umich.edu/%7Efessler/course/100/misc/chowning-73-tso.pdf)); DX7 electric piano tine at 14 ([Yamaha](https://yamahasynth.com/learn/synth-programming/fm-101-part-five-expanding-your-sound/)); wobble, hoover, house organ, string machine, Juno pad (MusicRadar); psytrance lead, pad and rolling bass numbers from the project doc `claude/psytrance-rules-in-numbers.md` (Myloops).
- Guesses (no published numbers): zap sweep times, growl and wavetable positions, big room leads, impacts, the Vector FM and Orbit motion settings, every level of loudness between groups (pads 3 dB, keys 1.5 dB, plucks 1 dB under leads and basses is my choice).

### What was built
- New block `/*PSLIB-START*/ ... /*PSLIB-END*/` before `askChoice` (`psLib()` is called once after `/*VN-END*/`, when every instrument exists). It rebuilds the preset tables of the 16 instruments in group order with Init first, keeps the demo and default keys exactly, and fills `PSMETA[inst][key]={g group, t tooltip, p psytrance, r role for the tests, tr transposition for the tests}`.
- Groups: Init, Lead, Bass, Sub, Pad, Strings, Pluck, Arp, Keys, Organ, Bells, Stab or Chord, FX and Psytrance (Acid: Classic, Techno, Psytrance, Bass, Lead, FX; Growl keeps Dubstep, EDM, Drum and bass and gets Psytrance). Every synth that can make it has a Psytrance group (68 presets in all): full-on leads (3 saws a few cents apart, 24 dB filter at 650 to 700 Hz with resonance, 75 % sustain), melodic full-on leads, squelch mids, Goa leads, acid lines, rolling basses, zaps, plucks and arps, atmospheres.
- Every preset has a tooltip in the preset menu (what it is for and where it sounds best, for example "Play 16ths on F1 to A2"); the menu shows the tooltip of the chosen preset too. Presets made for psytrance outside the Psytrance group carry "· psy" and the tooltip ends "Made for psytrance."
- Menus: `psMenus()` runs after every `renderFx` and turns the flat preset list of these 16 instruments into groups with tooltips (found by matching the option keys to a table); Venom's own window keeps its groups and gets the tooltips.
- Levels: `PSLV[inst][key]` holds a trim in dB per preset, applied to the preset's own level (`gain`, Venom `vol`) when the tables are built. Measured, trimmed, measured again (three rounds).

### Tests
