- `tools/engine-tests/presets_all.py` (new; later parts add their instruments): all 451 presets, rendered twice each with a phrase in their own register (bass C1 to G1, sub an octave lower, acid 16ths, lead melody C4 to C5, pluck 16ths C4 to D#5, pad and keys chords C3 to C4, fx one held C4; Vector FM and Orbit basses two octaves higher because they play two octaves down): no NaN, none silent, none clips (highest peak 0.79, Vector FM bells), every one ends after its notes (last second at least 70 dB under the loudest part), every render the same twice (all bit-identical except 34 Plant presets and one Growl, which differ by at most 6e-8 from run to run, as on v0.190). Attack against the role: every lead, pluck, bass and keys preset reaches half its level within 60 ms except Venom "Lead · Reese Lead" (95 ms, an old preset whose unison swells); every pad needs more than 60 ms except the ones meant to be quick (Short warm pad, Medium strings). Bass energy: every bass and sub has at least 10 % of its energy under 200 Hz (most 80 to 100 %). Level, loudest 100 ms against the default preset of the instrument (or a demo preset of the same role): all presets within 2 dB of their target in Saw lead, Square lead, Soft pad, Strings, Soft keys, Reese bass, Acid, Growl; Pluck 19 of 20, Sub 10 of 11, Square bass 13 of 15, Wavetable 46 of 48, Venom 67 of 72 (the others are those whose level is at its top: short psy rollers, a sync lead, a square arp, 3 to 6 dB under); Plant: leads at the Supersaw lead, pads at the Lush pad (which is 3 dB above the leads by this measure, so Plant pads sit a little above its leads; Lush keeps its value for the demos), basses within 1.5 dB of the leads except the demo's Psy bass (8 dB under, kept) and the Square rolling bass (level at its top); Vector FM 15 of 27 and Orbit 18 of 25 (basses, Init and risers are one voice against six, with Level at its top). Closest pairs per instrument (mean difference over 24 bands and 12 time slices of the probe): 1.7 dB (Sub: Init and Long sub) to 3.6 dB (Soft pad); none under 1.5 dB after four rounds of changes (the first round found 19 pairs under 1.5 dB, among them 8 old Acid presets that were nearly the same)
- `tools/ui-tests/preset_menus.py` (new): every menu of the 16 instruments opens with groups, Init first, every entry has a tooltip, no dash characters, choosing an entry applies it and the menu keeps its groups; pictures of the Plant and Saw lead menus opened as lists, looked at.
- Unchanged: `export_same.py` Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96) with the same checksums; Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom default renders identical to v0.190; Plant, Sampler and Drum kit vary from run to run on the old version too (checked again: about 1 dB under load). The two demo songs built by code: every track's instrument and effect settings identical to v0.190 (new `tools/engine-tests/demos_same.py` renders them; their checksums vary from run to run on the old version as well, so the settings were compared).
- No page errors on load.

### Not verified, and what is a guess
- **Nobody has listened to any of it.** The presets come from published recipes where they exist and from my own choices where none exist, then from measurement.
- Velocity and the mod wheel: only Venom has macros (Macro 1 cutoff, 2 wave position, 3 detune in every preset) and velocity routes (new Venom presets send velocity to cutoff); the other engines have no mod wheel route, so presets cannot add one without an engine change (left for later).
- Level is matched by a K-weighted measure of the loudest 100 ms of a phrase per role, not by ear; Vector FM and Orbit basses and Init stay 5 to 9 dB under their instrument's default because their level control is at its top (one voice against six); a few short plucks in Plant are the same.
- The similarity check compares spectra over time on one probe; it does not hear detune or stereo, so two presets that differ only there count as close.

### Left
- Part 2: the other instruments (samplers, drums, Emit, Vector Grain, FX, Grid; Sub, Psy Bass, Bounce, Fractal only if needed). Part 3: the effects.
- Listening with the user; a mod wheel route for the basic synths, Plant and Wavetable (engine work); a preset browser with search and tags across instruments.

### Release
- v0.197: commit 1424e8c, project doc `claude/audiolab-preset-research.md` (same text as `docs/preset-research.md`), artifact version 220.
- Desktop build: run 45756249392 for this commit succeeded (2 min 36 s).

## 26 November 2026, v0.198: the mixer cables (task S2, from a later message)

> "When it comes to the cabling system at the bottom of the mixer, when you resize, it resizes the bottom of the window. The window should stay the same size ... Let's also have a slider on the bottom that only makes the cable portion bigger or smaller instead of the whole window. But the whole window resizes if you go all the way to the bottom right corner ... more text per cable ... more and more levels the more cables you have ... like 5 levels with text showing where everything goes ... shows direction ... modulators or side chaining, you should be able to make out from the animation inside the cables ..." (shortened). Also: in compact mode the cable area may shrink a little but must not collapse.

### Research (how other programs show cables)
- **Max (Cycling '74)**: cords are told apart by "stripe patterns and colors": event cords thin and plain, signal (audio) cords striped, multichannel and Jitter cords each their own pattern. Probing shows what flows in a cord on hover. Source: docs.cycling74.com/userguide/patch_cords/
- **Reason**: audio and CV are told apart by the jack ("large quarter inch jacks" for audio, "smaller mini jacks" for CV); cables run from an output to an input only; MIDI has no cables at all. Source: docs.propellerheads.se/reason9/RoutingAudioAndCV.15.2.html
- **VCV Rack**: no fixed colour code; users colour by type (often red for audio, green for modulation) and keep "all the same color" per type. Source: medium.com/modular-synthesis/cabling-conventions-in-vcv-rack-a-user-interface-note-a13e7453d957
- **Bitwig**: modulation has no cables; while mapping, the source button flashes and its targets light up, and a modulated control shows its current value as a moving marker. Source: bitwig.com/userguide/latest/the_unified_modulation_system/
- **Node editors (React Flow, Unreal Blueprints)**: direction is shown by movement along the line: animated dashes (stroke-dashoffset) or small dots that travel from output to input; Unreal shows execution flow as pulses along the wire while it runs. Animated dashes on hundreds of lines were a measured CPU cost, so one author animates only the highlighted lines. Sources: reactflow.dev/examples/edges/animating-edges, liambx.com/blog/tuning-edge-animations-reactflow-optimal-performance, forums.unrealengine.com/t/animate-lines-between-nodes/9090
- **Reaper**: routing lives in dialogs and a routing matrix (sends, sidechain on channels 3/4), no drawn cables; the source is where you start the drag. Source: soundonsound.com/techniques/reaper-using-tracks-internal-channels
- Taken from this: type by line style (solid audio, thicker bus for group outputs, dashed sidechain, wave for modulation) as in Max and Reason, colour by channel as before; direction by an arrow into the target plus movement from source to target as in node editors; activity only while something plays, and only for lines in view (the React Flow cost lesson).

### Built (new block "v0.195: mixer cables (task S2)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`; names `cs2*` / `CS2*`; `cabDraw`, `cabGrip`, `cabLegend`, `cabPlan`, `cabH` replaced, `cabList`, `cabWhat`, `cabRemove`, `cabPop`, `drawMixer` wrapped)
- **Sizes**: `UI.mxs.cabH` is now the total height of the cable area (`cs2T`); `cabH()` is the part the channels give room for (down to 378 px of channels, 230 px in compact, `cs2ChMin`); anything beyond slides over the lower part of the channels as an opaque panel (`.cpan`, nothing under it is clickable), at most the window minus 70 px. The top grip moves only this split (the window keeps its size). The bottom edge of the window (`#mixWin>.rz-s`, cables on) is caught before the general resize code and changes the cable area and the window together, so the channels keep their size; it has a small grip bar and a tooltip. Corners and the other edges resize the whole window as before. Compact: 0.8 times the height, at least 38 px (as in v0.194).
- **Levels and labels**: `cabPlan` takes a limit; up to 5 levels as the room allows (about 14 px each), lines that do not fit go onto the level where they overlap least. Each line gets a label from where to where ("Send: Kick, Open hat → Plate reverb", "LFO on Snare → Impact · Pan"), shortened step by step ("Kick +2 → Plate reverb", "→ Plate reverb") to fit the free room on its level, kept inside the part of the mixer in view (not under the master or the legend), and hidden when there is no room. Width measured with a canvas, cached.
- **Looks**: send solid with white chevrons pointing to the target; output to group a thicker bus with a dark core; sidechain dashed; modulation (new kind `mod`: a modulator on one track with targets on another) a wave. Arrow into the target as before. Legend shows each look; Modulation appears in it when there is such a cable. Clicking a modulation cable lists its targets and can remove the links (one undo step).
- **Animation** (`cs2Tick`, from `drawMixer`, every second frame, only while the mixer is open): sends and outputs: light dots flow from the source to the target, speed and brightness follow the source level (and the send amount); silent: nothing. Sidechain: a pulse runs along the cable on every hit of the source (followers, from the mixer's own hit detection) or on every pump of a Sidechain effect (its sync value, in time with the song). Modulation: the wave's dashes move while the modulator moves, brightness follows its value. Only cables in view; a page write only when the value changed; off in Lighter and Lightest, with reduced motion and when animations are off (`svg.cstill`), the still look stays.

### Tested (headless, software drawing; `tools/ui-tests/cables3.py`)
- No page errors on load or in any step (the only console line, "An unknown error occurred when fetching the script", is the test server's missing service worker and is there on v0.197 too).
- Top grip dragged up 60 and 200 px: window 1252 x 470 unchanged both times; cable area 68 → 128 → 328 px (channels 392 → 370 px, the rest slides over them). Bottom edge down 120 px: window +120, cable area +120, channels unchanged (370 px); up 80: same. Corner se, edges n, e and corner sw: the window changed and the cable area did not (edge w: the mixer snaps back to the width its channels need, as since v0.167).
- 2, 8 and 20 cables (sends, group outputs, sidechain followers, Sidechain effects, LFOs on other tracks) at automatic, 46 px and 260 px, playing, full effects: levels 2 / 5 / 5, labels 2 / 7 / 11 when large; all screenshots looked at, also compact (348 px window: automatic 93 px with 4 levels, dragged to 200 px as a drawer), Curved, hover on a modulation cable, its pop-up, Lightest (still look, no animation). Animation values change between frames while playing.
- Frame time at 1920 x 1080, 20 cables, playing, mixer open, three runs of 240 frames: v0.197 80.5 / 91.4 / 90.7 ms and 84.2 / 90.0 / 92.1 ms, v0.198 86.4 / 90.5 / 96.4 ms and 87.7 / 89.9 / 96.9 ms (software drawing, about 3 % more, within the spread); `cs2Tick` 0.04 to 0.5 ms. Mixer closed: 47 to 52 ms both, `cs2Tick` not called.
- Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo). `cables2.py` runs as before (its grip drag now keeps the window at 470 px).

### Not verified

## 26 November 2026, v0.199: mixer menus, return channels, pan centre, effect dragging (task W, from a later message)

> "On the mixer, you should be able to right-click the stereo field or level marker or spectrum or any of the other ones and change them individually per channel ... the same drop-down window as the strip windows drop-down menu ... redesign the whole drop-down menu so it shows you literally which one is which ... much more compact ... live so you can actually see it while music is playing. It'll just show the master ... right-click and choose the options further, like with pan ... and also the colors. Return channels should look like return channels ... while grabbing an effect ... the box itself is not where the cursor is ... it glitches a little bit ... I want the animation to be smooth and snappy ... the pan center ... visible always ... a pixel further away ... a very faint subtle stereo field effect in the shape of the half turn ... for the sliders ... make the button transparent with a frosted glass blur." (shortened)

### Research
- **SortableJS** (wiki "Swap Thresholds and Direction", github.com/SortableJS/sortablejs/wiki/Swap-Thresholds-and-Direction): a swap happens when the pointer enters a "swap zone" on the target, a fraction of the target's size (swapThreshold); invertSwap puts the zone at the edges. The decision is about the target item, not about the gap.
- **dnd-kit** (docs.dndkit.com/presets/sortable, collision detection algorithms page): sortable strategies move the other items by the active item's size with CSS transforms (no layout change during the drag); collision by closest centre or by rectangle intersection; the layout of the items is measured, not re-read while they animate.
- **react-beautiful-dnd** (from what is known of the library, not re-read this run): the home list closes the gap when the item leaves it, items move by the dragged item's size, the drop animates the item into its slot.
- **FLIP** (aerotwist.com/blog/flip-your-animations, Paul Lewis): measure First and Last, Invert with a transform, Play by removing it; only transform and opacity animate, so it stays on the compositor.
- **Apple UIKit** (UICollectionView reordering, from memory): the item under the finger is lifted and follows the finger at the point it was picked up; the others animate aside.
- What went wrong in AudioLab before: the ghost box was placed in viewport pixels while the page is scaled by the app zoom (`html{zoom}`), so with any zoom other than 100 % it sat far from the cursor and was scaled twice; and the drop place was read from `getBoundingClientRect` of boxes that were in the middle of their slide animation, so the middle of a neighbour moved while it was compared, and the placeholder jumped back and forth.
- **Return channels in other programs**: Ableton Live (live manual, Mixing chapter): return tracks sit at the right of the Session mixer, named with letters (A Reverb, B Delay), fed by the Send knobs of every track. Sound On Sound "Ableton Live audio FX returns" and "Studio One: managing large sessions": Studio One FX channels sit in their own section with their own colour and can be shown or hidden as a group. Bitwig effect tracks and Logic aux channels (from memory): effect tracks sit in their own area after the instrument and audio tracks; Logic labels them "Aux" with the bus they listen to. Common idea: a separate place, a different colour or tint, a label that says what it is and what feeds it.

### Built (new block "v0.199: mixer menus, return channels, pan centre, effect dragging (task W)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`; names `w9*` / `W9*`)
- **Picture per channel**: right-click a strip's picture opens the picture menu for that channel only; the choice is saved with the project (`t.mxv`, `S.mxvM` for the master); without it the strip follows the mixer's choice (`UI.mxView`). `drawMixer` reads the picture per canvas (`w9VOf`), the side meters hide per strip in the meter picture (`.mx.nobgm`), `mxsTick` drives side meters per strip.
- **Picture menu** (`#w9m`, `w9Menu`): compact (3 x 2 tiles of 60 x 36 px, the name under each), each tile a live preview of the master drawn by `drawMixer` itself (own history per preview, `data-hk`), only while the menu is open and at 15 frames a second. Right-click a tile for its colours and options (the existing per-picture menu); the pan styles are a row in the same menu; footer "On every channel" / "Like the mixer" for one channel, "Clear N own choices" for the mixer-wide menu (the Waveform button in the mixer bar now opens the same menu for every channel).
- **Right-click pan** in a strip: pan styles with small pictures, Centre this channel, Stereo field behind pan on or off. **Right-click a fader**: the fader and meter designs.
- **Return channels**: tinted in their colour with a stronger border, the name in italics, a "Return" badge with the number of tracks that send to it (tooltip lists them), a dashed divider and a gap before the first return.
- **Dragging effect boxes** (one engine `w9DBegin`/`w9DMove`/`w9DEnd` for mixer slots and for devices in the device chain, where dragging the left strip of a device is new): the ghost keeps the exact grab point and size at any app zoom; the boxes' positions are measured once per list from layout (`offsetTop`/`offsetLeft`, never from moving boxes); the drop place changes when the dragged box's leading edge passes the middle of the neighbour it moves over (where that neighbour is at that moment), with 2 to 6 px hysteresis, so boxes of any size swap once and never back; the others slide by the dragged box's size with a 150 ms ease-out on transform only; the home list closes its gap when the box leaves for another channel; the drop lands with a 160 ms FLIP from the ghost; Shift or Alt copies; Escape cancels; tooltips hidden while dragging; the device chain scrolls when you drag towards its edge. The old slot drag is switched off (`window.W9DRAG`).
- **Pan centre**: the centre tick crosses the pan track on dials and stands across the slider, always visible (green when the sound is centred); the live marker sits 1.8 px further out than before on the dials and stays visible when silent (grey); a faint stereo field behind every mixer pan control (`w9PanTick`, every fourth mixer frame, visible channels only, fades out once when silent and then costs nothing): a fan in the shape of the dial, or bars along the slider, red where the sound is out of phase. Pan sliders have a frosted glass handle (blur off in Lighter and Lightest) with the centre tick, marker and field visible behind it.

### Tested (headless, software drawing; `tools/ui-tests/taskW.py`)
- No page errors on load or in any step.
- Menu while playing: 6 live previews, all drawn (lit pixels in each). Per channel: Bass stereo, Clap meters (its side meters hidden), Hat spectrum, Open hat spectrogram, Percussion fun, saved in the project (5 own choices after reload of the saved state). Right-click on a tile opens "Spectrum colours"; right-click pan opens "Pan control"; right-click a fader opens the fader designs.
- Drag, mixer slot (Sidechain over the next Pro EQ, 1 px per step, then wiggling 2 px around the end): grab offset constant (68.0, 6.0 at 100 %; 85.4, 7.5 at 125 %, ghost size equal to the box) over every move; exactly 1 reorder change each way. Device chain (Sidechain over the 982 px Pro EQ): grab offset constant (15, 114.5 at 100 %; 18.5, 143.1 at 125 %), exactly 1 change, order right, undo right. Across channels: Bass's Sidechain dropped into Clap before its Pro EQ.
- Before the fix the same test at 125 % put the ghost 70 to 100 px away from the cursor.
- Screenshots looked at: the menu for one channel and for every channel while playing, the options menu over it, five channels with different pictures, pan slider, half and full dial playing and silent, compact strips, the return channel next to normal ones, the drag in the mixer and in the device chain.
- Frame time at 1920 x 1080 x 2, mixer open and playing, 240 frames: menu open 45.3 ms, closed 43.9 ms (software drawing); `drawMixer` 3.1 ms with the menu open at full rate before limiting the previews to every fourth frame, 1.6 ms closed.
- Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).

### Not verified
- The user's Mac, real GPU, trackpad; how the frosted handle and the faint field look on a real screen at 120 Hz.
- Colours and options of a picture stay mixer-wide per picture type (a channel picks its picture, not its own colour scheme).

### Left
- Per-channel colour schemes; the header pan controls have no field or marker; pinned device windows have no drag reorder.

### Release
- v0.199: commit a096302 on main. Desktop build run 45761961643 succeeded. Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 222 (it was at v0.198). Project doc `claude/audiolab-ui-run-W.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.200: Voice, an AI voice changer with note editing (task Z, from a later message)
> "Let's make a new instrument or effect. I'm not sure what it actually is. The concept is you have a microphone and you sing in the microphone or talk in the microphone. And it has a voice changer, so it has like an AI voice changer. It can be like a woman or a man, or maybe a choir, multiple people, or typical comped pop music vocal effects, like already auto-tuned. Can you find online some good neural networks or AI that's good for this kind of stuff? And then create a device based on that deep research. And I want to be able to see when you sing in it or talk, I want to see your voice and notes in the same way Melodyne does. And then you can move certain parts up or down, like Melodyne. That's going to be part of the whole AI neural network. Let's figure out a way to make this device."

What shipped: the whole device without the neural network part. Note view and editing, pitch correction, man, woman, child and other voices, choir, doubler and pop chains all run on signal processing, in the browser and the desktop app, live and in exports. The AI voices are researched and planned (model, licences, sizes, how to add them) but not built: they need the desktop app, a model runtime and a voice model, and none of that could be tested here (see "Left").

### Research (full text with a source for every claim: `docs/voice-research.md`, project doc `claude/audiolab-voice-device.md`)
- **Voice conversion models.** RVC v2: MIT code, base models MIT trained on VCTK (CC BY 4.0), needs a trained model per voice (about 10 minutes of audio), 170 ms end-to-end (90 ms with ASIO) in its own README, 190 ms on CPU in an independent paper. Applio (RVC, MIT), OpenVoice V2 (MIT, zero-shot, speech only, ONNX 34 to 66 MB), FreeVC (MIT), DDSP-SVC (MIT, real-time singing, per-voice training), so-vits-svc-fork (MIT, unmaintained). Not usable in this app: Seed-VC (GPL-3.0, 430 ms on a laptop GPU), so-vits-svc and Diff-SVC (AGPL-3.0), w-okada voice-changer and kNN-VC (licence "other", parts proprietary).
- **Sizes**: ContentVec (RVC's content encoder) as ONNX 360 MB, 91 MB as int8; RMVPE 185.8 MB, 94 MB int8; FCPE 47.8 MB; onnxruntime-node 218 MB unpacked (CoreML on macOS, DirectML on Windows); onnxruntime-web's WebGPU file about 24 MB.
- **Pitch trackers**: CREPE (MIT, 22M parameters), SwiftF0 (MIT, under 100k parameters, CPU), PESTO (LGPL-3.0), FCPE (MIT, 10.6M), RMVPE (Apache-2.0 code, MIT weights in RVC, 90M), pYIN and YIN (no weights). Paper accuracies are not comparable across papers (each trains on different sets).
- **How the editors show notes**: Melodyne blobs at the pitch centre with the pitch line, Pitch Center and Pitch Drift percentages in Correct Pitch, Pitch Modulation (vibrato) and Drift tools (100 % as sung, 0 % flat), a Formant tool and an Amplitude tool, double-click to split; Logic Flex Pitch hotspots (fine pitch, drift in and out, vibrato, gain, formant); Auto-Tune Retune Speed 0 ms for the hard effect, 10 to 50 ms natural; Waves Tune speed 15 ms and note transition 120 ms by default.
- **Choir and doubler**: Waves Doubler 8 ms by default, 2 to 12 cents sound in tune, voices panned up to ±45°, Waves suggests 10 and 20 ms for two doubles. 10 to 40 ms is common advice, only 8 to 20 ms was confirmed in a source.
- **Voices**: speaking pitch about 115 to 125 Hz for men, 200 Hz for women, 245 to 275 Hz for children (Peterson and Barney, Hillenbrand); formants of women about x1.15 to x1.20 of men's (2.4 to 3.2 semitones), children x1.3 to x1.47 (4.5 to 6.7 semitones).
- **Pop vocal chain**: high-pass at 80 to 100 Hz, compression around 4:1, de-essing at 5 to 8 kHz, a plate of 2 to 2.5 s with 30 to 90 ms pre-delay, slap or 1/8 delays.
- **Guesses**: the exact numbers of the Man, Woman and Child voices (octave rule, 2.7 and 5.5 semitones of formant), the pop chain settings and every preset are my choices from the ranges above; nobody has listened to them.

### Architecture (chosen from the research)
- **Web app (everywhere)**: one shared DSP core `vxCore()` used three ways: in the page and a background Worker (analysis of clips and rendering of note edits, so a long take does not freeze the screen), and in the worklet `al-voice` (live). Pitch: YIN on a 4x lower rate copy low-passed at 4 kHz, parabolic refinement, octave check against the neighbours, median of 3, runs under 30 ms dropped. Notes: a new note where the voice starts, where the pitch settles on another note for 70 ms or more (more than 0.8 semitone away), or at an onset (+9 dB); slides shorter than 120 ms join the note they lead into. Voices: TD-PSOLA (two-period Hann grains one period apart at pitch marks, laid down one changed period apart; a grain read faster or slower moves the formant; the output divided by the window sum, which makes "no change" exact, and multiplied by the square root of its slow average, which keeps the level when the pitch moves). Chosen over the existing phase vocoder (`al-pshift`, 2048 samples of delay) because it keeps the formant by itself, has no smearing on a single voice and needs only two periods of delay.
- **Edits are rendered, not played live**: a moved note changes the clip into a rendered copy (new buffer, `c.buf`); the recording stays as it was in `c.vx.buf` and the notes in `c.vx.notes`. Undo restores both. Rendered copies are not written to the browser storage (they can be large and change with every edit): after a reload `vxRestore()` makes them again from the recording and the notes, with the same samples. Project files keep the recording and the rendered copy (`packProject` collects every `buf`).
- **Live**: the microphone goes into the worklet's second input; the worklet sums it with the track's own sound, follows the pitch every 5 ms, places marks one period apart and plays every voice D samples late, D = two periods of the lowest pitch of the range (Find out 85 Hz: 1129 samples, 23.5 ms at 48 kHz; Low voice 65 Hz: 30.8 ms; High voice 140 Hz: 14.3 ms). `fxLat` reports D, so the app's delay compensation lines the other tracks up.
- **Desktop (later, for AI)**: onnxruntime-node in the shell through preload (CoreML on Mac, DirectML on Windows), models downloaded on demand into userData with a progress bar, never in index.html. Rendered after recording, since 90 to 430 ms is too late for live. Planned in "Left".

### Built (block "Voice: a voice changer with note editing" before `askChoice`, CSS "Voice (v0.200)" before `.dacv`; `vxInit();` after `crkInit();`; `VXWL` added to `wlLoad`)
- **Voice** (`ND('voice')`, effect browser: Pitch & voice, first entry). Picture on the left (600 px), parameters on the right, presets, Change, pin, remove, side strips as every device.
  - **Picture**: the note view of the chosen clip (keys on the left, scale notes lighter, bars of the song, blobs at their pitch centre shaped by the level, the name and cents, the pitch line that will be heard drawn through them, a dashed outline where a moved note was sung, the playhead), or the live view (your pitch grey, what comes out pink, the note, your voice class, the voice and its shift, the delay and an input level bar).
  - **In the picture**: click a note to select it, Shift-click to add, drag on the background to select several, drag up or down to move (snaps to semitones, Alt free), double-click to split.
  - **Editor** (button, large window): Snap (Semitones, Scale, Free), Key, Scale, Correct pitch with Centre and Drift percentages, Drift, Vibrato, Formant and Level for the selected notes (all when none is selected), Split (at the playhead or the middle), Join (J), Reset, Keep formant, Fit, Close (Escape); arrow keys move by a semitone (Shift an octave), wheel scrolls up and down, Shift+wheel sideways, Ctrl or Cmd+wheel zooms; Cmd or Ctrl+A selects all.
  - **Buttons**: Live (green when on), Input (microphone and channel: Input 1, Input 2 or both; the Zoom AMS-24 shows up as its own entry), Record (red while recording; starts the song if stopped, records the dry microphone as 32-bit float, mono, at the engine's rate, puts the take on this audio track where it was sung, compensated by Settings > Recording latency offset or the reported output delay, then shows its notes), Notes (finds the notes of the selected clip, the one at the playhead, or the first), Editor, AI (says plainly that AI voices are not in this version yet and how they will come).
  - **Knobs**: Correct (0 to 100 %), Speed (Hard to 400 ms), Pitch (±24 st), Formant (±12 st), Width, Doubler, Tuning (±50 cents), Mix; bars: Voices (1 to 8), Time spread (0 to 80 ms), Pitch spread (0 to 50 cents), Formant spread (0 to 4 st). Choices: Voice (Natural, Man, Woman, Child, Deeper, Brighter, Giant, Small creature, Robot), Key, Scale, Choir (Unison, Octaves, Octave below, Thirds, Fifths, Chord, Sixth below; harmonies follow the scale), You (Find out, Low voice, High voice: sets the tracking range and so the live delay), Keep formant. Every control has a tooltip.
  - **Man, Woman, Child** adapt to your voice: the class (low, high, child) comes from the middle pitch of 1.5 s of voiced sound (or from You), the pitch moves by whole octaves only, and only when the ideal move is 6 semitones or more (so a song stays in its key), the formant by the difference of the classes (0, 2.7, 5.5 st). Robot sings everything on the key note nearest your voice.
  - **Choir**: voices spread in time (each a fixed share of Time spread), pitch (evenly across ±Pitch spread plus a slow drift of a quarter of it plus 2 cents), formant and stereo; level 1/√voices. **Doubler**: two voices at 11 and 19 ms, -7 and +8 cents with a slow drift, left and right; the total level is kept (Wide double was 3.7 dB louder before this).
  - **23 presets**: Natural (Natural, Gentle tuning, Tuned (pop), Hard tune, Melodic rap), Character (Man, Woman, Child, Deeper, Brighter, Giant, Small creature, Robot), Group (Doubler, Wide double, Small group, Choir, Octave choir, Harmony in thirds, Chord stack), Pop chain (Pop lead, Hard-tuned lead, Airy pop: these also add the app's De-Esser, Compressor and Pro EQ after Voice and sends to two return tracks "Vocal plate" (Convolution Reverb plate, 2.2 s, 40 ms pre-delay) and "Vocal delay" (Echo delay 1/8 or 1/4, feedback 28 %, 300 Hz to 6 kHz); choosing another pop preset replaces the chain; one undo step each).

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/voice.py`, new, headless with Chromium's fake microphone; `node` harnesses in the run's scratch space for the engine alone)
- **Pitch tracking** on synthetic sung notes (additive harmonics through three formant resonators, known pitches A3 +30 cents, C♯4 -40, E4 -20 with ±0.6 st vibrato at 5.5 Hz, D4 after a 150 ms glide from 59.5): steady notes median 0.08 cents, 95 % within 0.16; vibrato note median 12.5 cents (the 33 ms window averages the wobble); glide median 11.5 cents; no voiced frame missed inside notes, 10 frames marked voiced at edges. Spoken-like phrase falling from 150 to 100 Hz in three syllables: median 2.4 cents, 253 of 260 voiced frames found. Cost: 28 ms per second of audio, in the Worker.
- **Notes**: the four notes found with start and end within 15 ms and centres within 1 cent (A3 57.30, 60.60, 63.80, 61.99 for 62.00).
- **Moving a note by a semitone** (arrow key): +100.01 cents measured by an FFT method that does not use the tracker; the other notes 0.00 and +0.64 cents. 5 semitones up: +500.0 cents; formant envelope -0.56 st with Keep formant, +2.93 st without (it follows the pitch); Formant +3 st on a note: pitch +0.0 cents, envelope +2.27 st (the envelope measure reads low for big moves).
- **Correct pitch 100 %** (Centre and Drift) in C major: A3 +30 → A3 +0.11 cents, C♯4 -40 → C4 -0.11, D4 → +0.11; the vibrato note E4 -20 → mean +0.59 cents with its vibrato kept.
- **Mouse**: a drag of 2.1 rows in the device picture moved A3 +30 to B3 exactly (snapped); double-click split one note into two; Join made one; undo after each puts the clip back on the recording.
- **No clicks at joins**: legato notes moved by +3, -2 and +5 semitones: largest sample step 0.13 against 0.20 in the input; in the app, after a move, 0.046 against 0.21.
- **Bit-identical**: rendering edits twice, the Natural preset twice and Hard tune twice: identical. A rendered copy deleted and made again (as after a reload): identical.
- **Live engine in exports**: Hard tune (Correct 100 %, Speed Hard): +0.07, -0.13, +0.12 cents off the scale notes. Choir of 2 voices with 30 cents and 40 ms: left -25.9 and -21.6 cents, right +36.4 and +26.9 cents (±30 plus the drift), right starts 25 ms after left (27 ms by design). Woman on the spoken phrase with You: Low voice: +1200 and +1201 cents (an octave); its formant is set 2.7 st up (not measured on this phrase).
- **Latency of the live path**: 1129 samples (23.5 ms at 48 kHz) measured as the delay of the output against the input, equal to what `fxLat` reports; Low voice 30.8 ms, High voice 14.3 ms. Plus the audio interface's own delay, not measured here.
- **Cost of the live engine** (Node, this run's 2.1 GHz Xeon; an M4 is faster): pass-through 0.8 % of one core, Hard tune 1.3 %, pitch and formant 1.9 %, choir of 8 voices 5.3 %, 8 voices and the doubler 6.2 %. Rendering edits: 8 ms per second of audio. Silence costs nothing after 0.36 s.
- **Presets**: all 20 without a chain render finite, levels within -31.5 and -34.2 dB against -31.7 dB for Natural; pop chain presets add the three effects and two returns, a second pop preset replaces them, two undos remove all.
- **Fake microphone**: Live receives it (its 400 Hz beeps are found), Record made a 2.2 s take on the track at the place it was sung and showed it (no notes in a beep, as expected).
- `devices_all.py`: all 73 devices, Voice included: nothing outside, parameters shut 1116 → 670 → 1116 px. No page errors on load or in any step. No dash characters in any text. Every control of the device has a tooltip (the editor's Close was reported without one after it had been clicked: the app's own tooltip takes the title over on hover).
- **Demo unchanged**: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo), same checksums; the other `export_same.py` lines as before.
- **Screenshots looked at**: the device with notes (a moved note in yellow with the dashed outline of where it was sung), the editor before and after Correct pitch, the live view, a recorded take, the choir settings.

### Not verified
- **Nobody has heard it**, and no real voice was used: the tests use synthetic sung vowels. A real voice has breath, consonants, creak and reverb; the tracker and the note finder may need tuning on real takes (thresholds: aperiodicity 0.3, level 45 dB under the loudest part).
- The Zoom AMS-24 and the Mac: not tried. Monitoring delay on a real interface: 23.5 ms plus the interface.
- The quality of Man, Woman and Child on real voices (PSOLA formant moves sound good up to about 3 to 5 semitones; Giant and Small creature are meant to sound unnatural).

### Left
- **AI voices** (the plan): RVC v2 (MIT code and base weights, VCTK CC BY 4.0) as ONNX: ContentVec int8 (91 MB) + FCPE (47.8 MB) + the user's own voice synthesizer (made by Applio or RVC from about 10 minutes of their own singing or a voice they have the rights to; no voice imitating a real person is shipped), through onnxruntime-node (CoreML on the Mac, DirectML on Windows) in the desktop shell. Steps: (1) desktop/package.json: `onnxruntime-node`; (2) main.js: IPC `voiceAI.status`, `voiceAI.download(name, url, sha256)` into userData/voice-models with progress events, `voiceAI.load(modelPath)`, `voiceAI.convert(Float32Array 16 kHz, f0 curve, transpose)` returning audio; (3) preload.js: `window.audiolabDesktop.voiceAI`; (4) index.html: when `voiceAI` exists, the Voice choice lists the installed AI voices and "Load a voice model…", and Record renders the take through the model after recording into a new clip (live only if the measured latency is under about 60 ms, which RVC's numbers do not promise); when it does not exist, the AI button says "Update the app to use AI voices"; MIN_SHELL stays. Credits screen: RVC, ContentVec, FCPE, VCTK, LibriSpeech.
- A neural pitch tracker (SwiftF0, MIT, tiny) as an option for the note view; timing edits (moving and stretching notes in time); a sibilant and breath balance; drawing the pitch line with a pencil; a formant line over the blobs like Melodyne's Formant tool.
- Warped clips (`c.warp`): the note view reads the recording, not the warped version.
EOF
python3 - <<'E'
s=open('docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md').read()
a="- Preset library, part 1 of 3"
i=s.index(a);j=s.index("\n",i)
line="\n- Voice, AI voice changer with note editing (task Z, from a later message): **done without the AI part**, v0.200 (record from the microphone or use a clip; the voice as notes with the pitch line like Melodyne: move, split, join, drift, vibrato, formant, level, Correct pitch; pitch correction from natural to hard tune; Man, Woman, Child and other voices; choir up to 8 voices; doubler; pop chain presets with the app's own De-Esser, Compressor, Pro EQ, plate and delay; live with 14 to 31 ms delay). Left: the AI voices (RVC through onnxruntime-node in the desktop app; plan, licences and sizes in the log and `docs/voice-research.md`)."
s=s[:j]+line+s[j:]
s=s.rstrip('\n')+"""

## Task Z: Voice, an AI voice changer with note editing (from a later message, 26 November 2026)
> "Let's make a new instrument or effect. I'm not sure what it actually is. The concept is you have a microphone and you sing in the microphone or talk in the microphone. And it has a voice changer, so it has like an AI voice changer. It can be like a woman or a man, or maybe a choir, multiple people, or typical comped pop music vocal effects, like already auto-tuned. Can you find online some good neural networks or AI that's good for this kind of stuff? And then create a device based on that deep research. And I want to be able to see when you sing in it or talk, I want to see your voice and notes in the same way Melodyne does. And then you can move certain parts up or down, like Melodyne. That's going to be part of the whole AI neural network. Let's figure out a way to make this device."

Status: done in v0.200 without the AI part, see "Where it stands" and the log.
"""
open('docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md','w').write(s)

### Release
- v0.200: commit 23ec3c9 on main. Desktop build run 45765956244 succeeded (2 min 27 s). Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 223 (it was at v0.199). Project doc `claude/audiolab-voice-device.md` (this entry and the research). The desktop shell is unchanged (MIN_SHELL stays 0.147.0); the AI voices will need a new shell.

## 26 November 2026, v0.201: light devices and background freeze (task E of `docs/device-backlog-2026-11-25.md`)

> "I want all these devices to be optimized for CPU use, so everything needs to stay snappy. And for any like heavy physics or any other heavy device, we need a way to dynamically freeze it in the background so it doesn't, so it's not taking up CPU all the time, unless there is too many changing parameters. Maybe we can figure out an algorithm or a way to do things like that."

All numbers: headless Chromium on this 2-core test machine, no GPU. Audio-thread load is the processor time of Chromium's real-time audio thread read from Linux (`/proc/<pid>/task/*/stat`), in % of one core; the app's own meter cannot measure in headless (the buffer is too small for it). Offline cost is the time `renderMix` takes for a fixed phrase. The test machine is much weaker than the user's Mac: the ratios are the result, not the absolute numbers.

### Part 1: what each device costs, and the waste that was removed

**Measured first.** A fixed phrase (8 bars at 120 bpm: a 3-note chord and four eighth notes per bar, 19 s of sound with the tail) rendered offline three times, the fastest kept, with the device and without it. "ms per s" = render time per second of sound.

| device | offline cost, ms of work per s of sound (best of 9 renders; v0.199 = v0.201) | stopped, never played, 10 tracks: v0.199 → v0.201 (the selected track stays awake) | 6 armed tracks after playing, stopped (armed never sleep), v0.201 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Saw lead (the reference) | 11 | 6.0 → 2.2 | 7.0 |
| Plant | 103 | 3.8 → 2.2 | 13.0 |
| Emit | 64 | 3.5 → 2.2 | 2.5 |
| Vector Grain | 21 | 3.8 → 2.3 | 5.3 |
| Vector FM | 53 | 3.3 → 2.3 | 5.8 |
| Orbit | 53 | 3.5 → 2.5 | 6.3 |
| Bounce (instrument) | 84 | 3.7 → 2.0 | 7.7 |
| Growl | 7 | 3.3 → 2.3 | 6.2 |
| Wavetable | 17 | 3.7 → 2.3 | 8.0 |
| Sampler | 18 | 3.7 → 2.2 | |
| Sub | 9 | 3.7 → 2.3 | 6.2 |
| Psy Bass | 15 | 3.7 → 2.0 | 7.2 |
| Fractal (instrument) | 45 | 3.5 → 2.3 | 7.2 |
| Venom | 49 | 3.5 → 2.2 | 8.0 |
| Bouncy Notes (MIDI effect, on Saw) | +21 | | |
| Reverb | +37 | 5.5 → 2.2 | 35.7 |
| Space (convolution) | +14 | 13.0 → 2.3 | 20.5 |
| Vector Delay | +24 | 49.0 → 11.7 | 20.3 |
| Bounce Delay | +6 | 25.7 → 3.7 | 12.8 |
| Fractal (effect) | +38 | 45.2 → 8.2 | 30.5 |
| Delay | +7 | 14.5 → 3.0 | |
| Tape delay | +9 | 14.7 → 3.7 | |
| Blur | +9 | 15.0 → 3.7 | |
| Phaser | +20 | 21.2 → 3.8 | |
| Chorus | +26 | 8.7 → 2.8 | |
| Multiband | | 18.3 → 3.5 | |
| Resonator | | 17.5 → 2.7 | |
| Meter | | 27.5 → 4.5 | |
| Pitch shift | | 14.0 → 2.8 | |
| Vocoder | | 12.3 → 3.2 | |
| Limiter, Crusher, Gate, Imager, Transient | | 8.8 to 11.8 → 2.7 to 3.5 | |
| EQ, Compressor, Drive, Filter, Clipper, Sidechain, OTT | | 4.2 to 9.3 → 1.8 to 2.7 | |
| (an empty project) | | 2.4 → 2.4 | |

Effects: the cost over Saw alone. Load in % of one core of the audio thread. Vector Delay, the Fractal effect and the Meter still cost something with the selected track awake (one of the ten): about 9, 6 and 2 % for that single track on this machine.

Single offline renders move by up to ±30 % between runs on this machine (other work on 2 cores), so the best of three runs each of v0.198, v0.199 and v0.201 is given; the code that makes the sound was not changed and the renders are bit-identical (below), so this column has no before and after. Vector Map is a modulator with no audio of its own: its offline cost is inside the renders it moves (`vector_map.py`), and it adds nothing while stopped.

**Waste found (and fixed):**
1. **Nothing slept while the song was stopped.** Every delay, tape delay, blur, phaser, chorus, sidechain, multiband, resonator, Vector Delay, Bounce Delay, Fractal effect, Meter and reverb kept computing silence. A delay with a feedback loop and a wobble LFO never counts as silent for the browser, and a worklet effect is called every block. Fractals demo, stopped: **21.6 to 23.9 % of one core before, 2.8 to 3.2 % now** (an empty project is 2.4 %).
2. **Analysers kept unplugged chains running.** The browser keeps every analyser that has no output running on its own, and it pulls everything in front of it. Effects keep analysers for their pictures, so a track unplugged by "Sleeping tracks" still computed its whole effect chain. Measured on the demo with every track unplugged: 23.6 % without the fix, 2.8 % with it. This also made sleeping during playback save much less than it was meant to: Fractals playing from bar 1: 23.0 → 14.9 %, from beat 64: 15.5 → 10.6 %; from beat 160 (everything plays) 29.5 → 29.4 % (sleeping cannot lower the busiest moment, as the research said).
3. Effects taken off a track kept their old chain running for good through their analysers (since long before this version): now their analysers are unplugged.
4. The Meter plugged its own silent sink straight into the output, which kept its track's whole chain running even while asleep: such connections made while an effect is built now go into the effect's sink too (Meter, 10 tracks stopped: 15.3 → 4.5 %).

**Checked and found clean:**
- **Instrument engines with no notes**: the worklets of Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM, Orbit, Sub, Bounce, Psy Bass, Fractal, Venom and the voice engine do almost nothing without notes (column 4: about the cost of a plain track); the grain engine and the voice engine return early when empty. Instrument tracks also sleep now, so their engines are not called at all.
- **Pictures while nothing moves**: with each device's panel open and the song stopped, the page's main thread used 10.7 to 13.7 % for every device, the same as a plain Saw (11.3 %): the particle pictures only redraw when their engine sends positions (Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM and Orbit send nothing while no particle exists), nothing to fix there. Vector Delay and Fractal send their picture data 30 times a second while their panel is open (that is how they show the particles moving).
- **Timers**: the 47 intervals of the app were left alone; the new ones are cheap (500 ms background freeze, 200 ms sleep check that only reads the level of tracks that could go to sleep, 2 s analyser clean-up).

**How sleeping works now** (`slzTail`, `slzPeak`, `slzQuiet`, `slzWake`, `slzSinks`, `slzSweep`; `slpTick` replaced):
- Every analyser an effect makes (collected while `mkFxNode` runs in the live context) is plugged into a silent sink (a gain of 0, which adds exact zeros) that is plugged in while its track is awake and pulled out while it sleeps.
- **Stopped**: a track goes to sleep when its own output (after its fader, both channels, every sample, two analysers of 16384 samples read every 200 ms) has been below -100 dB for longer than its effects can ring: 1.5 s, reverbs 2 s + 1.5 x decay, delays, blur and resonator 12 s, vocoder, Fractal, pitch effects 4 s, counted from the stop or the last sound. Groups wake with their tracks, returns with the tracks that send to them.
- **Playing**: as before (a clip within 2.5 s ahead or within 6 or 12 s behind), and new: a track (also a group or return) whose sound has not died away yet stays awake, so a long delay or reverb tail is never cut.
- Never sleep: the selected track, armed, recording and monitored tracks, the track the Change browser is working on. Playing a key, a preview, a pad, MIDI input wakes the track before the note is made (the hooks are installed after the devices add their own versions of `voiceOn` and `preview`, and again if anything wraps them later). Play wakes every track with something to play, as before.
- The setting is the same "Sleeping tracks" switch (Settings › Performance › Savers), its text now says that it also works while stopped.

### Part 2: background freeze (`bgf*`, `BGF`; new block before `askChoice`)

**What the app already had and what this builds on**: the Freeze button of the track (renders the whole track to one audio clip, by hand, `freezeTrack`, `trackRender`), `freeze_match.py`, the note cache (single notes of Plant and the basic synths played from buffers), the clip render of "Bounce to audio" (`bounceRender`), sleeping tracks, the clean switch of v0.184 (`chsTrack`: the old input fades out at a sample-exact moment while the new one takes over and picks up the notes that are sounding).

**What it does**
- When an instrument track has not changed for 8 seconds and nobody plays it by hand, each of its MIDI clips near the playhead (within 90 s ahead, and every clip inside the loop) is rendered once in the background: an offline render on its own thread, one clip at a time, followed by a rest as long as the render took (at most half of one core). From then on a clip that is played from its start plays that recording into the track instead of the live instrument. The track's effects stay live, so effect knobs, effect automation, modulated effects, tails and sidechains work as always and do not thaw anything.
- **What is recorded**: exactly what the clip sends into the track: the instrument with its MIDI effects (Bouncy Notes, chord, arp) and the clip's own volume, pan and filter lines, made by the same code as an export (`clipChain` and `synthB`), from the clip start until its tail has died away (the instrument's release + 2 s; if the sound has not died away by then the clip stays live). A recording that is the same on both sides is kept once and played through a two-channel input that copies it to both sides exactly. It starts on a whole sample (a start between two samples would be interpolated and lose treble).
- **Only from the start**: a clip entered in the middle (Play inside it, a jump, a loop start inside it) plays live, and a clip cut by the loop end plays live, so a recording only ever stands for exactly the notes the live instrument would play.
- **Thaw, at once**: a change to the instrument (any knob, a preset, MIDI learn), a MIDI effect, the tempo, the clip or its notes, the loop cutting it, arming, recording or monitoring the track, playing its keys or a preview. Checked every screen frame and every 50 ms while recordings play, and on every edit. The thaw is the clean switch of v0.184: the recording fades out a few milliseconds ahead at a sample-exact moment and the live instrument takes over at the same sample, picking up the notes that are sounding. After a change the track waits 8 seconds again before it is recorded again.
- **Stays live**: the selected track (where the keys go), armed, recording, monitored tracks, the track the Change browser works on; an instrument that a modulator, Vector Map, a macro or automation moves; an instrument that refers to another track; mono and legato instruments (Acid, Psy Bass, Sub, any with mono, legato, glide) where the clip touches another clip of the track (one note would cut or slide into the next); and every instrument that is not on the measured list (below).
- **Measured list** (`BGFOK`): Vector Grain, Vector FM, Orbit, Wavetable, Sub, Bounce, Acid, Fractal, Growl, Saw lead, Square lead, Sub bass, Square bass, Reese bass, Soft pad, Strings, Pluck, Soft keys, Kick and the four drum synths. Not on it, and why: **Plant, Emit, Psy Bass, Venom** change with the position of the 128-sample blocks by -24 to -3 dB (see the test), so a recording would not match the live sound closely enough; **SFX, drum kit, percussion, Sampler, Grid** make every hit at random. A new instrument stays live until it is measured.
- **Memory**: at most 384 MB (`UI.bgfMB`), the least recently played recordings go first; recordings of an old sample rate are dropped when the audio engine restarts; switching the setting off drops them all.
- **Settings › Performance › Savers › Background freeze**: on by default (the tests below show it does not change the sound). Off: everything plays live, recordings are dropped.
- **Mark**: a small snowflake in the track header (blue, before the name) while the track plays from recordings, or, stopped, would play its next clips from them; the tooltip says what it is and that any change plays it live again.
- Exports, freeze, bounce never use any of this (`BGF.tst`, the hook in `renderMix`, is only set by the test).

### Tests (all on this machine; files in `tools/engine-tests/`)
- **`bgfreeze_match.py`** (the mix with and without background freeze, rendered by the app's own `renderMix`, 120 bpm, every instrument on two clips with a clip volume line plus a track with tape delay and reverb after it, beats 4 to 40): with the recording placed at the same block phase as in the live render (B), the mix is **bit-identical** for Vector Grain, Vector FM, Orbit, Wavetable, Sub, Bounce, Acid, Saw lead, Square lead, Sub bass, Square bass, Reese bass, Strings, Pluck, Soft keys, Soft pad, Kick, the snare, clap and hat synths, Bouncy Notes; Fractal differs by at most -94 dB, Kick at -178 dB, Growl and the tom synth by -85 and -80 dB, which is exactly how much they differ from themselves between two renders (they have a random part). With the recording made from the clip start and started on the nearest sample as live playback does (C), the difference is what the block phase changes, the same as between two live plays: below -130 dB for most, Saw lead with Bouncy Notes -77 dB, Bounce -103 dB, Fractal -92 dB, Growl -85 dB, tom -78 dB, Kick -102 dB: far below hearing. Plant (-12 dB), Emit (-24 dB), Psy Bass (-4 dB), Venom (-8 dB) and SFX (random) differ by much more and so stay live.
- **`bgfreeze_live.py`** (live playback, VFM, Orbit and Acid tracks, a recorder on the VFM track's output): recordings made 13 s after building (8 s still, then rendering), marks on all three tracks while stopped and while playing; an EQ knob on the frozen VFM track: no thaw; an instrument knob: thawed after 1 to 7 ms, 4 notes picked up live, the clip now live; around the thaw the level never fell more than 2 to 3 dB below the 100 ms before (no gap) and the largest step from one sample to the next was 0.080 to 0.105 against 0.091 in the frozen part before (no click); a key played on Orbit thawed it; arming VFM thawed it.
- **`bgfreeze_cpu.py`** (audio-thread load while playing, with and without, alternating twice): **six particle and wavetable tracks (Vector FM, Orbit, Bounce, Vector Grain, Wavetable, Fractal), dense notes: 58.9 % without, 7.1 % with background freeze**. **Fractals demo**: from beat 160 29.5 → 27.8 %, from beat 288 32.7 → 31.6 %: only Kick, the drum synths, Snare roll and Acid freeze there (they were already cheap), the Plant tracks that make most of its load stay live (see above), so the demo gains little. 190 clip renders took 27.7 s for the demo's sections (146 ms each on average).
- **Exports bit-identical**: every device of the table rendered with v0.199 and v0.201 with the same project seed: identical checksums for Saw, Plant, Vector FM, Orbit, Wavetable, Sub, Bounce, Psy Bass, Fractal, Venom, Bouncy Notes, Reverb, Space, Vector Delay, Bounce Delay, Fractal effect, Delay, Tape delay, Chorus, Phaser. Emit, Vector Grain, Growl, Sampler and Blur give a different checksum on every page load also in v0.199 (they have random parts that the project seed does not fix), so they cannot be compared this way; their engine tests give the same values.
- **Engine tests**, v0.199 against v0.201: `emit.py`, `vector_grain.py`, `vector_fm.py`, `vector_delay.py`, `vector_map.py`, `orbit.py`, `bounce_rhythm.py`, `growl.py`: the same values except timings and the lines that also change between two loads of v0.199 (Emit and Vector Grain levels, Vector Map volume by distance: v0.199 gave -36.98 and -37.48 dB on two loads). No errors.
- Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo). No page errors on load.
- `tools/ui-tests/fxlevels.py` (the Performance tab): same results as before, the only console line is the known 404. Screenshots looked at: the Savers list with the new Background freeze row, the track headers with the mark on Vector FM and Orbit (not on the selected Lead, not on Emit, which stays live).

### Not verified
- The user's Mac: how much the 384 MB and the background renders cost there, and the audio load on a real audio device (the app's meter works there).
- Background renders while playing on a 2-core computer compete with the audio thread for the second core; they rest as long as they work, but nobody has listened for dropouts on a weak computer.
- Nobody has listened to a thaw.

### Left
- Plant, Emit, Psy Bass and Venom depend on where the 128-sample blocks fall (live playback has the same differences between two plays); making their engines independent of it would let them freeze too, and Plant is most of the Fractals demo's load.
- Vector Delay, the Fractal effect and Bounce Delay still compute silence while their track is awake (selected, or playing with long gaps between notes): 2 to 4 % of one core each here. A silence skip inside those worklets must keep their moving particles in step to stay bit-identical; not done.
- A whole-song recording of a track (one buffer across clip boundaries) would also freeze back-to-back clips of mono instruments.
- The idle load of the page itself while nothing moves (about 11 % of one core here, the same with any device) was not looked at.

### Code (for the next person)
- One block "v0.201 (task E, part 1)" and one "v0.201 (task E, part 2)" before `askChoice`; CSS `#hdrin .bgfm` before `.dacv`. Names `slz*`/`SLZ` (sleeping) and `bgf*`/`BGF` (background freeze).
- Wrapped or replaced: `mkFxNode` (live context only: collects analysers and output connections into a sink), `BaseAudioContext.prototype.createAnalyser` and `AudioNode.prototype.connect` (only while an effect is built in the live context), `slpTick` (replaced), `stop`, `syncGraph`, `changed`, `renderHeaders`, `restartAudio`, `pmBuild`; `voiceOn`, `preview`, `padHear` through `bgfHooks` (installed after the devices' own wrappers, re-checked every second).
- Two lines changed inside `schedule`: a clip played from its start may get `bgfPlay(...)` instead of `clipChain(...)`, and the note loop skips such a clip (`!I.bgf`). One in `renderMix`: `BGF.tst` (null except in the test) may stand in for a clip's notes.
- Measured with v0.199 plus this work; rebased onto v0.200 (Voice, which arms its track for the microphone, so it never sleeps) before release.
