## 26 November 2026, v0.202: preset library, part 2: drums, percussion, samplers, particle instruments, FX, Grid, Sub, Psy Bass, Bounce and Bouncy Notes (from the user's message: "redesign every preset ... the best possible sounding presets from your research")
Part 2 of 3 (part 1: the synths, v0.197; part 3: the effects). Only preset data changed: no engine and no sound calculation. Released on top of v0.198 to v0.201, which other sessions released while this one worked; the block was inserted again on v0.200 (checks below) and once more on v0.201 (load and demo checks repeated, see Release).

### Instruments in scope, presets before and after
| Key | Name | Before | After | What changed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| kick | Kick designer | 24 | 48 (47 in the menu) | 24 new, old values kept (drum pads store a kick by key); groups and tooltips; one old near copy hidden |
| drums | Drum kit | 6 | 19 | 13 kits per genre with the same pad layout; a Kick designer on the kick pad in the psytrance kits |
| zsnare, zclap, zhat, ztom | Snare, Clap, Hat, Tom designers | 10, 8, 8, 8 | 23, 17, 19, 16 | Init, classic machines, genres |
| perc | Percussion | 10 | 23 | mallets, bells, drums, plucked, psytrance; levels matched |
| sampler | Sampler (with its grain mode) | 6 | 24 | "Your sample" settings that keep the loaded sample; vocals, pads, granular, keys, FX on the built-in sources |
| emit | Emit | 10 | 17 | Init, pads, shimmer, sparks, bursts, a psytrance spray |
| vgrain | Vector Grain | 9 | 16 | Init, tempo chops, pads, octave layers, glitch, pluck |
| sfx | FX | 26 | 39 | a preset now sets Tone, Length, Wobble, Space, Level and the curves; psytrance riser, zap, dive marked |
| grid | Grid | 66 | 66 | tooltips, Init group, level trims on the Output module, one name with an artist in it renamed |
| sbass | Sub | 22 | 24 | Init group, tooltips, trance offbeat sub, UK garage sliding sub |
| psyb | Psy Bass | 18 | 21 | Init, "kick owns the sub" model (bass an octave up, low cut 85 Hz), twilight roller |
| bball | Bounce | 12 | 19 | Init group, tooltips, 7 new (3/16 echo, psy ratchet, drum roll, triplets, glass) |
| bnotes | Bouncy Notes (MIDI effect) | 23 | 28 | tooltips, a Psytrance and EDM group |
Total 258 before, 419 after. Fractal (`crk`, 20 presets, a day old, grouped) was left as it is.


### Research (sources read for this part; also in `docs/preset-research.md` and the project doc `claude/audiolab-preset-research.md`)
- **Kicks, general numbers.** Kick fundamentals sit in the 30 to 70 Hz range, sweet spot 40 to 50 Hz (E0 41.2 Hz to A0 55 Hz in standard naming); 808 kick: sine, pitch decay 20 to 70 ms; 909 kick: triangle through a low-pass with a 200 to 500 ms pitch and filter decay ([MusicRadar, classic analogue drums](https://musicradar.com/how-to/how-to-recreate-classic-analogue-drum-sounds-in-your-daw-and-with-hardware)). Kick pitch sweep decay 20 to 80 ms (Vital example about 30 ms), body decay house 200 to 300 ms, techno 400 ms or more, click noise decay 5 to 15 ms ([Beat Kitchen](https://beatkitchen.io/guides/electronic-music/02-drum-design/)). Kick at about 58 Hz, pitch envelope attack about 1 ms and decay about 50 ms ([ModeAudio](https://modeaudio.com/magazine/drum-synth-sound-design-kick-snare)). Original 808 tuning 56 Hz ([MusicRadar, analogue-style kick](https://musicradar.com/how-to/make-analogue-style-kick)). House kick in Kick 2: length about 500 ms, drive about 60 % ([Computer Music via MusicRadar](https://musicradar.com/how-to/how-to-synthesise-a-house-kick-using-sonic-academy-kick-2)). Kick 2, BigKick and Punchbox are built the same way (a sine body with a pitch envelope, a click or sample layer, drive), which is what the Kick designer does; no factory preset values of them are published.
- **TR-808 bass drum circuit** (Werner, DAFx-14): trigger pulse 1 ms, an attack where the pitch is raised by more than an octave for about 6 ms, centre frequency about 49.5 Hz (Roland's chart: 56 Hz), a slow "pitch sigh" of about 48 to 58 Hz over the first 300 ms ([paper](https://dafx14.fau.de/papers/dafx14_kurt_james_werner_a_physically_informed,_ci.pdf)).
- **Psytrance kick** (project doc `claude/psytrance-rules-in-numbers.md`): sine with a fast fall plus a click high-passed at 1 kHz; shorter than 130 ms (Aciduss), full-on body 80 to 150 ms (Myloops), at most one 8th (Bonedo); fundamental E to A (41 to 55 Hz), tuned to the key; the rolling bass comes on the second 16th (103.4 ms at 145 BPM). The Fractals demo kick lasts to about 190 ms.
- **Hardstyle**: pitch from about C4 to C5 falling to F1 to C2 over 100 to 200 ms, a 5 to 15 ms "tok", heavy distortion, 150 to 160 BPM ([Music Production Wiki](https://musicproductionwiki.com/articles/how-to-recreate-the-hardstyle-kick), unsourced starting points; [EDMProd](https://www.edmprod.com/how-to-make-hardstyle/) gives no numbers).
- **808 bass**: Vital patch decay 1.44 s, sustain 55 %, release 0.25 s, pitch amount 15 to 25 % ([EDMProd](https://www.edmprod.com/808s/)).
- **Snares**: 808 snare a sine near 160 Hz, amp decay 250 to 350 ms, noise decay 100 to 600 ms; 909 snare pitch decay 100 to 200 ms; snare tone 100 Hz (beefy) to 200 Hz (thin) (MusicRadar above); tone 150 to 250 Hz with decay 80 to 150 ms and noise 100 to 250 ms (Beat Kitchen); about 200 Hz, short decay for tight genres (ModeAudio). Psytrance snare: three layers (909 punch, clap, stereo noise), no low end (Bonedo, in the project doc).
- **Claps**: 3 to 4 hits 10 to 20 ms apart, band-pass 1 to 3 kHz, tail 100 to 200 ms (Beat Kitchen); 808 clap band-pass 800 Hz to 1.5 kHz, amp decay 450 to 800 ms (MusicRadar).
- **Hats**: closed hat high-pass 5 to 8 kHz, decay 10 to 40 ms; open hat 200 to 500 ms; crash 1 to 3 s (Beat Kitchen); closed under 200 ms, open about 1 s (MusicRadar). The 808 metal: six square oscillators at 205.3, 304.4, 369.6, 522.7, 540 and 800 Hz, band-passes at about 3.44 and 7.1 kHz, high-pass near 10.5 kHz ([ICMC 2014 paper on the 808 cymbal](https://speech.di.uoa.gr/ICMC-SMC-2014/images/VOL_2/1453.pdf)); the Hat designer uses the same six frequencies. Psytrance closed hat must die within one 16th (project doc).
- **Toms**: 909 toms two oscillators a fifth apart, pitch envelope 100 to 200 ms; 808 cowbell 555 and 835 Hz, clave 1.5 to 3.5 kHz with 150 to 250 ms (MusicRadar).
- **Granular and sampler presets**: grains of 1 to 100 ms are typical; an ambient pad from about 600 ms grains every 40 ms with jitter, slow attack and release; LFOs under 0.1 Hz on size and interval for an evolving pad; small grains at low density for glitch ([Native Instruments](https://blog.native-instruments.com/granular-synthesis/)); large grains at high density blur into pads, small grains at low density give glitches; density as notes (1/16 gives a hat-like part) ([Sound On Sound](https://www.soundonsound.com/techniques/granular-synthesis-practical-introduction)); pads from long grains with spray and a slow scan, glitch with spray the length of the sample and random pitch ([EDMProd](https://www.edmprod.com/granular-synthesis/)). Simpler-style modes (Classic, One-shot, Slice) and warped playback ([Ableton help](https://help.ableton.com/hc/en-us/articles/209072629-Warping-in-Simpler)) are why the Sampler got settings-only presets that keep the user's own sample.
- **Risers, impacts, zaps**: from the project doc (noise riser band-pass 500 Hz to 8 kHz over 8 bars; downlifter: saw falling 2 octaves over a bar into a 4 to 6 s reverb; impact: sine falling about an octave plus body and crack; FX blip every 4 bars at -15 to -20 dB).
- **Sub and Psy Bass**: checked against their own research docs (`claude/audiolab-sub-bass-research-and-device.md`, `claude/audiolab-psy-bass-research-and-device.md`). Gaps filled: an Init in each; the "kick owns the sub" model (bass an octave above a kick on the root, high-passed at 80 to 90 Hz, Myloops in the project doc) as a Psy Bass preset; a twilight roller; a trance offbeat sub and a UK garage sliding sub.
- **Guesses (no published numbers)**: every value of the particle instruments (Emit, Vector Grain, Bounce, Bouncy Notes), the kit levels per pad, the FX variants' curves, the level trims between groups, and the genre kick values outside psytrance where only ranges exist.

### What was built
- New block `/*PSLIB2-START*/ ... /*PSLIB2-END*/` before `askChoice`; `psLib2()` runs once after `psLib()`. It extends part 1's `PSMETA` (group, tooltip, psy mark, role for the tests) and `PSLV` (level trims), reorders every table so Init comes first and groups follow each other, and keeps every old key with its old values (only names change).
- Menus: part 1's `psMenus()` now covers these instruments too (grouped menu, tooltip per entry and on the menu); `psMenus2()` groups the Kick designer's own menu and a drum pad's kick menu, and puts tooltips on the Bouncy Notes menu.
- Kick designer: a group "Psytrance" of rolling kicks made so the kick is at least 20 dB down when the first bass note of a K-B-B-B bass comes (103.4 ms at 145 BPM; hi-tech and forest ones shorter), tuned on E, F, F#, G, G# and A; "Progressive" down before an offbeat bass (207 ms). The old psy kicks keep their values (demos and drum pads use them) and moved to "Psytrance, long body", with a tooltip saying their body overlaps the first bass note. "Full-on punch" measured nearly the same as "Hard, long body": it stays for old projects but is hidden from the menu (shown only when it is the one in use).
- Drum kits: each kit sets the kick (a kick preset, or a Kick designer on the pad: Full-on 145, Forest and dark 150, Progressive psy 138, Goa 140), a kit-wide tune, decay and tone, and per-pad settings. The pad layout is the same in every kit, so a beat written for one plays on all.
- Sampler: the "Your sample" presets name no sample, so the loaded one stays (the playback modes of other DAWs' samplers); the preset function now keeps a preset's Reverse on (it was always switched off).
- FX: a preset sets the whole sound and puts the curves back.
- Grid: patch levels trimmed through the Output module (its knob stops at 1); "Eminent strings (Jarre)" renamed "Wide string machine, phased" (no artist names in preset names).

### Tests
- `tools/engine-tests/presets_all.py index.html part2` (extended): every preset rendered twice in a phrase for its role (one kick hit; two drum hits; a 16-pad pass for kits; a mallet line; a held FX note, all 12 keys for "By note"; a 145 BPM K-B-B-B roll for Psy Bass; one dropped ball for Bounce). **No NaN, none silent, none clips, every one ends, no page errors.** Bit-identical twice: all of the Kick designer, drum designers, Emit, Vector Grain, Sub, Psy Bass and Bounce; Percussion, kits, Sampler, FX and Grid differ by at most 6e-6 from run to run (the same happens on v0.200 for the Grid reese, 4e-7; the browser's own nodes).
- Kicks (table printed by the test): measured end pitch equals the set note on every kick (for example Full-on G 49.0 Hz, Melodic A 55.0 Hz, Dark night F 43.7 Hz, Forest E 41.2 Hz); pitch 5 ms after the hit 76 to 400 Hz on the rolling psy kicks, 400 to 720 Hz on the hard dance ones; level at 103.4 ms: Full-on -22.9 dB, Melodic -23.0, Snappy -21.0, Dark night -32.5, Forest -78, Hi-tech silent, Goa -22.6; Progressive -4.4 at 103 ms and silent at 207 ms; the old long psy kicks -0.3 to -8.9 dB at 103 ms (as their tooltips say). Length to -20 dB: rolling psy kicks 74 to 102 ms, techno 260 to 400, hard dance 360 to 540, 808s 420 to 1600. Every kick has the same peak (the designer scales each one).
- Closest pairs: kicks are compared as "the same" only when they end on the same note (half a semitone) and are as long (15 %): the closest are Goa soft and long / House punch 1.6 dB, none under 1.5 except the hidden one. Drum designers 1.7 (snare), 2.0 (clap), 1.8 (hat), 2.0 (tom); Percussion 2.5; kits 1.5 (two old kits: Techno and Tight and dry); Sampler 3.1; Emit 4.3; Vector Grain 5.0; Grid 3.4; Bounce 1.8; Psy Bass: old Full-on 145 and old Imperial Army 0.9 (the same shape, meant for different keys: both were played on A); Sub: old Pure and old Drum and bass sub 1.2 with the same overtones (they differ in glide and key level, which this phrase does not play); FX: old Tension and old Filter sweep 1.2. In the work, 11 new presets were removed or reshaped because they measured too close to another (a house snare, a drum and bass clap, three hats, "House, classic" kit, six FX variants, "Full-on, first note softer" which only differs with Roll on, "Full-on, plays your key" which was the tight G kick with Key track).
- Levels (loudest 100 ms against the instrument's default preset, pads 3 dB, keys 1.5 dB, plucks and mallets 1 dB, FX 2 dB lower): Percussion and FX all within 2 dB of their target where the gain could reach it; Grid 63 of 66; Sampler 21 of 24; Emit 14 of 17; Vector Grain 13 of 16. Presets whose gain reached the top of its knob stay quieter: Sampler presets on the glass and rain sources (up to 7.6 dB), Emit and Vector Grain on the rain (up to 12 dB), FX whoosh, sweep, vowel, wind, snap and rim (3 to 6 dB), Grid pluck, harp, Karplus, rolling bass, hat, clap, rim, vowel (up to 11 dB). The drum designers and the Kick designer keep equal peaks, so their loudest 100 ms differ by nature (hats 13 dB, snares 11 dB). Sub, Psy Bass and Bounce keep their own levels (set from their research a day ago): spreads 8, 12 and 14 dB in this test's phrases (the triplet roller is quiet in a 16th phrase; Bounce in-time presets play fewer hits in the window).
- `tools/engine-tests/bnotes_presets.py` (new): all 28 Bouncy Notes presets in front of Soft keys: no NaN, none silent or clipping, every one ends, identical twice, no two make the same notes; gaps and keys printed (the 3/16 echo 0.75 beats apart, the ratchet 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625).
- `tools/ui-tests/preset_menus2.py` (new): all 15 instrument menus, the pad kick menu and the Bouncy Notes menu: groups with Init first (Bouncy Notes: Balls first), a tooltip on every entry, no dash characters, every preset shown, choosing applies; pictures of the Kick designer and Drum kit menus looked at. Part 1's `preset_menus.py` passes too.
- Unchanged: Fractals demo solo Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96), same checksums; Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom renders identical to v0.200; the two demo songs built by code have identical instrument and effect settings on every track; a new track of every instrument starts with the same settings as before (the Grid differs only in random module ids).

### Not verified, and what is a guess
- **Nobody has listened to any of it.** Kick and drum values come from the published ranges above and then measurement; the particle instruments, kit levels, FX curves and many tooltips' "best keys" are my choices.
- "Ends before the first bass note" is measured as 20 dB under the kick's peak at 103.4 ms. Sources also give longer full-on kicks (150 to 220 ms), and the Fractals kick runs to about 190 ms, so the long-body group stays.
- The similarity check is a spectrum over time on mono; it does not hear stereo width, glide or velocity response, so presets that differ only there count as close.
- Levels of one-shot drums are by peak, not by loudness.

### Left
- Part 3: the effects. Fractal's presets (grouped, no tooltips through the library).
- Listening with the user, above all the psytrance kicks against Psy Bass, the kits and the levels of the quiet sources.

### Release
- v0.202 (first built as v0.201; another session released its own v0.201, task E, in the meantime, so the block was inserted again on top of it). Checked again on this base: no page errors on load; the two demo songs' settings and every instrument's new-track settings identical to v0.201; Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB with the same checksums; all menus (`preset_menus2.py`) pass; the Kick designer and Psy Bass renders give the same numbers as above.
- v0.202: commit 104f304 (research doc heading fixed in dfc18d7); desktop build run 45771160780 for this commit succeeded; project doc `claude/audiolab-preset-research.md` updated (same text as `docs/preset-research.md`); artifact version 225.

## 26 November 2026, v0.203: app icon, icon picker and .audiolab file icon (task V, from a later message)
> "Let's implement the default icon. And let's have all the other icons as a choice in the settings. Let's just pick all of them with their real name, so not like the numbers like VA3, FE5. Just have the normal name and implement it in the settings. And with the .audiolab file, on Mac right now it's just like a question mark on the paper. Can we have our logo there?"

Ran after task E (v0.201) was finished. Icon files from the branch `assets/app-icons` (now in `desktop/icon-assets/`, branch deleted).

### Default icon: Ocean Yellow
- Mac: `desktop/build/icon.icns`; Windows: `desktop/build/icon.ico` (`win.icon` was `build/icon.png`, now the .ico with its own small drawings), also the NSIS installer and uninstaller icon; `build/icon.png` and `dock-icon.png` replaced. The icns/ico files are used as delivered: each holds separately drawn small sizes (four tiers), nothing regenerated from one PNG.
- Website: `favicon-16.png`, `favicon-32.png` (the small drawings), `favicon-48.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png` (180), `icon-192.png` and `icon-512.png` (the manifest already named these two but they did not exist). The favicon links sit after `<title>`, outside lines 3 to 10.

### Settings, Colours & clips, App icon
- Seven groups from `icons.json`, Main logo colours first with Ocean Yellow marked Default, then Spirograph & fractal, Realistic 3D, Decades, 1950s illustration, Scientific illustration, Atom models in history. 49 tiles, each with its name under it and a tooltip; never the design codes. The chosen tile is highlighted; a click applies it at once and it is remembered. A sentence under it: on a Mac the chosen icon shows in the Dock while AudioLab Pro runs; Finder and Launchpad keep the default icon (Windows and browser have their own sentence).
- The pictures are not in index.html. They load only when the Colours page is first shown (an IntersectionObserver), from 128 px thumbnails (`desktop/icon-assets/picker/thumb/`, made from the 512 px pictures with the Apple margin cropped, 0.8 MB for all 49, lazy). Settings opens as fast as before.
- Settings search: "app icon" finds it first; "dock", "logo", "favicon" find the page.
- Browser: the tab icon changes (all favicon links point at the thumbnail), stored in localStorage `audiolab-app-icon`, applied at load.
- Desktop app: `preload.js` adds `listAppIcons()` and `setAppIcon(id)`. `main.js` keeps the choice in `app-icon.json` in the user-data folder, serves the pictures at `audiolab://app/app-icons/` (from `icon-assets/picker/` inside the app; paths outside it are refused), and applies it: macOS `app.dock.setIcon` (the fixed `dock-icon.png` call at start now uses the saved choice; Ocean Yellow still uses `dock-icon.png`), Windows and Linux `setIcon` on every window and on new windows (the picture without the Apple margin). Older desktop apps (no `listAppIcons`) show "Update the app to change its icon". MIN_SHELL not raised.
- Windows Start menu and desktop shortcut: done, because it is small and only touches what it should. Only `AudioLab Pro.lnk` in the user's Start menu Programs folder and on the desktop, only when the shortcut points at this very program, only the icon field (`shell.writeShortcutLink(..., 'update', {icon, iconIndex: 0})`). The icon is a PNG-in-ICO (256, 48, 32, 16) written to the user-data folder with a new name per icon so Windows does not show a cached picture; back to Ocean Yellow points the shortcuts at the program itself again. An app update recreates the shortcuts, so a non-default choice is applied again 4 s after start. Not tried on a real Windows computer (no Windows here); every step is in a try and a failure leaves the shortcut as it was. Pinned taskbar shortcuts are not touched (they live elsewhere and Windows keeps its own copy).

### .audiolab file icon on the Mac (the question mark)
- electron-builder 25.1.8 first applies `mac.extendInfo` (Object.assign onto Info.plist) and then appends one document type per `fileAssociations` entry to `CFBundleDocumentTypes`. A document type in `extendInfo` plus the top-level `fileAssociations` would give two entries. So `fileAssociations` moved into `win.fileAssociations` (Windows keeps using it, with `build/doc.ico`), and `mac.extendInfo` holds exactly one document type: name "AudioLab Pro project", role Editor, `LSHandlerRank` Owner, `LSItemContentTypes` [com.audiolab.project], extension audiolab, icon doc.icns; plus `UTExportedTypeDeclarations`: com.audiolab.project, "AudioLab Pro project", conforms to public.data and public.content, icon doc.icns, extension audiolab. `doc.icns` goes to Contents/Resources through `mac.extraResources`. Double-click opening is unchanged in main.js (`open-file`).

### Checks
- `node --check` main.js and preload.js; package.json valid; `electron-builder --linux dir --publish never` builds; the app.asar holds the 49 pictures, 49 thumbnails and icons.json.
- The built Linux app run headless (Playwright's Electron driver): `listAppIcons` gives 7 groups and 49 icons without design codes; a thumbnail is served (200, image/png); `app-icons/../../main.js` refused (403); an unknown id refused; `setAppIcon('sputnik')` writes `{"id":"sputnik"}` and applies; a restart with `teal-coral` saved reports `teal-coral`; the picker in the app shows 49 tiles with all images loaded; clicking Ocean Yellow writes it back. No page errors.
- Browser (`tools/ui-tests/appicon.py`): no page errors on load; nothing loaded before the page is shown; 49 tiles, 7 headings in order, Ocean Yellow on and marked Default, every tile has a tooltip, no design codes; click Sputnik: highlighted, stored, all favicons changed; reload: favicon and highlight still Sputnik; back to Ocean Yellow restores the original favicons; search finds App icon; with a pretend desktop host a click calls `setAppIcon` and leaves the favicon alone; an older host shows the update sentence. Screenshot looked at.
- Fractals demo unchanged: solo Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96).

### Release
- Update notes say the new icon needs the new desktop app, and that macOS caches icons: if .audiolab files or the app still show the old icon after installing, log out and in once.
- v0.203: commit bdde4ed; desktop build run 45772449717 succeeded (release v0.203.0: Mac dmg, Windows setup). The Mac dmg was opened (7z): Info.plist has exactly one document type (AudioLab Pro project, Editor, Owner, LSItemContentTypes com.audiolab.project, extension audiolab, icon doc.icns) and the UTExportedTypeDeclarations entry; Contents/Resources holds doc.icns and icon.icns identical to `desktop/build/`; app.asar holds the picker pictures. MIN_SHELL stays 0.147.0.
- The branch `assets/app-icons` could not be deleted from here (the connection drops on a branch delete); its files are on main in `desktop/icon-assets/`. Delete it on GitHub (Branches) when convenient.
- Next: the user tries the new app on the Mac (Dock icon, .audiolab icon in Finder after one log out and in) and on Windows (shortcuts).

## 26 November 2026, v0.204: loop bar fix (task AC, from a later message)
> "Sometimes when your song is playing and you start selecting, it starts playing really fast: when you drag the selection and it's still small, it starts looping really fast inside. [...] it sounds like it's glitching."

### What was wrong
- A drag on the ruler (drawing a loop), on the loop (moving it), on its edges (resizing) or on a looped section wrote `S.loopRange` on every mouse move, and the transport reads `loopB()` every 25 ms in `tick()`. A loop drawn slowly from just behind the playhead is, for most of the drag, a short loop whose end is behind the playhead or inside the look-ahead. The scheduler then saw `play.sched >= end` and wrapped, up to three times per tick (the `k<3` loop), with notes already handed to the engine past the end: fast looping, notes twice, glitches.
- Reproduced in the new test on v0.202: 10 rounds of a loop in a 2.4 s drag; resizing let a quarter-beat loop be active (the old minimum was 0.25 beat).
- The same wrap happened when the loop button was turned on while the playhead was past the loop.

### Research
- Ableton Live, Bitwig, REAPER, Cubase: the loop brace or locators can be dragged while playing; the transport only takes the loop over once it is set, and a loop is never thrown at the playhead while the mouse is still moving. Logic Pro: the cycle area is drawn in the ruler and the playback follows the finished cycle; with the playhead outside it, playback goes into the cycle. These are from memory of the programs plus the pages found (not read in full): forum.ableton.com/viewtopic.php?p=451858 (should the play position jump when adjusting the loop), forum.ableton.com/viewtopic.php?p=1791144 (moving the loop brace while playing), help.apple.com/logicpro/mac/10.2.1/en.lproj/lgcp9ede81be.html (cycle area), help.apple.com/logicpro/mac/9.1.6/en/logicpro/usermanual/chapter_5_section_6.html (Using Cycle Mode), cockos.com/wiki/index.php/Preferences_Audio_Playback (REAPER seek and loop options).
- Taken over: the loop is applied on release; a minimum length; no restart of the transport; the jump into the loop keeps the beat.

### What was built
- `LPV` (the pending loop) and `loopSet(R,on)`: while the song plays a drag only draws the new loop (`loopShow()` shows `LPV`), the transport keeps the old one; when stopped the loop follows the mouse as before. `loopCommit()` on pointerup or pointercancel of the arrangement (capture listeners) writes it.
- `LMIN = 1`: a loop is at least one beat, snapped to the grid (draw, move, both edges). A drawn drag shorter than that shows and changes nothing.
- `loopSync()`, first thing in `tick()`, for any change of the loop while playing (drag, loop button, undo, assistant, looped section): if what the scheduler has already handed over (`play.sched`) is inside or before the new loop, nothing happens and the scheduler goes round at the new end. If it is past the new end, the notes already handed over play out and the song carries on inside the loop at the same place in the beat (`a + (sched - a) mod length`): the same step the scheduler takes at every loop end (`t0`, `b0`, `sched`, `wrapped`, `first`, `pass`), so no stop and start, no gap, nothing scheduled twice, and at most one step per change. `start()` records the loop it plays (`play.la`, `play.lb`, `play.lo`). Cost: three comparisons per tick.
- Edits outside the new block: `loopA` line (declarations), `loopShow`, `tick`, `start`, the ruler, loop and section drag lines in the arrangement's pointermove.

### Tests
- `tools/ui-tests/loopbar.py` (new): plays the Fractals demo, records the transport every frame and every scheduler call. Draw a loop slowly from one beat behind the playhead (slower than the playhead, then faster), move it by one bar, shrink it towards nothing and grow it: during each drag no active loop under one beat, the transport's loop unchanged, 0 restarts, the old loop comes round only at its own pace; after release the loop is the new region, the first jump lands inside it, every later jump goes from the end to the start (error 0.001 to 0.033 beats with frame time taken out), 0 restarts, nothing scheduled twice; stopped: the loop follows the mouse; no page errors. Passes on v0.204 (on v0.202 it failed 8 checks).
- Loop button turned on while the playhead is past the loop: one step into the loop, then normal rounds.
- `tools/ui-tests/playhead.py` and `tools/ui-tests/play.py` pass. Fractals demo unchanged: solo Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96).

### Not verified
- Listening on real speakers (the test browser has no audio output; the check is on the schedule, which is what decides double notes).
- A clean preset switch (`chsResched`) inside the 0.15 s right after a loop change that jumped: its pending-round branch assumes the round ended at the loop end. Rare, not seen in tests.
- Touch.

### Release
- v0.204, built on v0.203 (task V, app icon) released by another session while this one worked; one merge of two new blocks in index.html, both kept.
- Commit 24001d9; desktop build run 45773245351 succeeded. Artifact 1b07d888: version 227 (index.html only, lines 3 to 10 removed; it was at v0.203). Project doc claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AC.md.

## 26 November 2026, v0.205: mixer sidechain window and cable menus (task AB, from a later message)
> "When it comes to the mixer, when you right-click one of the cables, or you connect, let's say you connect kick to pad, then it shows you what you want to do. And let's say sidechain, it should be much clearer which direction you're sidechaining ... once the sidechain is connected, it should open the sidechain window. It's like a small window connected to the line, but you can move it around ... how loud you want the input to be, how much you want the pad to duck ... the faders aren't that reactive and smooth ... both of the sliders should light up a little bit ... the return options in a drop-down menu should be more visual with icons ... for the resizing ... I only want it where you grab the tab. Everywhere else on the bottom that's not the tab resizes the whole window like normal." (shortened)

### What was there before
- Choosing "sidechain" on a cable made either a Sidechain effect (a pump in time with the song that never listened to the other track: the cable only marked it) or a Sidechain follower (a modulator that read the other track with an analyser once per screen frame and moved the volume fader). The follower stepped at the screen's frame rate, moved the fader itself (so the fader jumped) and did nothing in an export. The menu was a plain list.

### Research
- **Ableton Live 11 Compressor, Sidechain section** (ableton.com/en/live-manual/11/live-audio-effect-reference/): a source chooser, Gain for the trigger level, Dry/Wet, an EQ that limits the trigger to one band, a headphones button to hear only the trigger; threshold, attack, release, knee and look-ahead (0, 1, 10 ms) on the compressor. Direction is shown only by the name in the chooser.
- **FL Studio Fruity Peak Controller** (image-line.com, online manual): turns the input's level into a control for another track's volume; Base, Volume (negative values duck), Tension (the curve of the way back), Decay. The link is made in a dialog, nothing on the track shows it.
- **Cableguys Nicky Romero Kickstart 2** (manual PDF, cableguys.com): Mix, 16 curves, Slope, Shift, Length, a band split; triggered by the song, MIDI or audio; the kick is drawn over the curve ("Kick View").
- **Eventide H9000 Ducker tutorial** (cdn.eventideaudio.com, vsig ducker): threshold, amount, release, gain; gain reduction on a meter.
- **Wavesfactory Trackspacer** (MusicTech review): one Depth control, high- and low-pass limits for the range that is reduced, attack and release in v2.
- **Bitwig Audio Sidechain modulator** (bitwig.com user guide 4.4, Modulators): gain, high- and low-pass filters, attack and release; the source can be any audio in the project.
- **Studio One** (Sound On Sound, "Studio One: how do I side-chain"): source chosen in the plug-in list, a sidechain filter, pre or post fader; a send per ducked track.
- Fader input (from what is known of the Pointer Events and requestAnimationFrame specifications, MDN; not re-read this run): capture the pointer on the control, take only the latest position once per frame, never ease behind the pointer, re-anchor when the fine key is pressed so the value does not jump, double-click to reset.
- Taken from this: amount, trigger level, trigger gain, attack, hold, release, curve, look-ahead, listen to (everything, lows, one band, highs) with a frequency, and hear the trigger; the trigger drawn over the ducking (Kickstart's Kick View, Eventide's meter); the direction written out in words everywhere, since none of these programs shows it on the routing itself.

### Built (block "v0.205: mixer sidechain window and cable menus (task AB)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`; names `abx*`, `ABX*`; `abxInit();` after `vxInit();`)
- **Ducker** (new effect `duck`, also in Add effect under Dynamics): worklet `al-abxduck` with two inputs (`ABXWL`, added to `wlLoad`); the trigger is the source track after its effects and before its fader (`N.ut.out`), so a muted or not soloed trigger still ducks; wired after every `syncGraph` (`abxWire`). Peak detector (10 ms fall), a 6 dB soft knee above the trigger level, linear attack, hold, linear release shaped by Curve (gain = 1 - amount x envelope^p), look-ahead delays the track and is reported to delay compensation (`fxLat`), Listen to filters the trigger with a state variable filter, Hear the trigger plays the trigger through the track. Five presets. In `renderMix` a track that triggers a Ducker is built even when it is not heard (fader at 0) and wired the same way, so exports duck too. A sidechain that would make a loop (the ducked track already plays into the trigger) is not offered (`abxReach`).
- **Dragging a cable**: a box next to the pointer with both names, an arrow and one line ("Pad will duck when Kick plays", "Kick will send to Plate reverb", "Kick will play through Drums"), and an arrow head at the end of the cable.
- **Menus** (`#abxM`, `abxMenu`): both names as coloured chips with an arrow between them, a swap button (start the cable at the other end), one line saying what the choices are, and per choice an icon, a bold title and a plain description. On letting go (`abxOpts`): sidechain first for normal tracks ("Pad ducks when Kick plays"), send first for returns, play through for groups, then the pump effect and the follower with what they really do. Right-click on any cable (`abxCabMenu`): open the window, pause, swap direction, show the device, remove (sidechain); change amount, show, stop sending (send); and so on for the other kinds; "Make it a real duck" turns an old pump or follower cable into a Ducker.
- **Sidechain window** (`.abxw`, `abxOpen`): opens when a sidechain is made and on a click on its cable; a dashed leader line from the socket of the ducked channel to the window (`#abxLead`); drag the title to move it, the offset is kept per cable with the project (`f.abw`), double-click the title to put it back; live picture of the trigger (filled, its colour) and the ducking (line, the ducked track's colour) with the trigger level dashed and the reduction in dB; knobs Duck (amount), Trigger level, trigger level gain, Attack, Hold, Release, Curve, Look-ahead, Focus; Listen to as four buttons; Hear the trigger, Presets, Swap, Device, Remove, pause. A tooltip on every control. Windows hide with the mixer and close when their Ducker goes.
- **Linked faders**: while a Ducker or a follower is active both channels' faders get a soft outline in the trigger's colour that glows with the trigger (source) and with the reduction (ducked track), plus a thin bar beside the ducked fader showing how far it is down (`abxLights`, every mixer frame, written only when the value changes).
- **Faders**: in the mixer and the sidechain window a drag sets the value once per frame from the latest pointer position, with no easing behind the pointer (the old knobs eased by a third of the distance per frame); Shift is fine control (one fifth) and starts from where the control is, so pressing or letting go of Shift never jumps. Mixer faders used to be set on every pointer event.
- **Bottom edge**: a tab in the middle (`.abxtab`) changes only the cable area (the S2 code now listens to the tab); the rest of the bottom edge and the corners resize the whole window.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskAB.py`, headless Chromium, software drawing)
- No page errors on load or in any step.
- Drag Kick to Pad: the box reads "Kick → Pad, Pad will duck when Kick plays, Let go to choose", the arrow head is there; the menu shows the sidechain first with icons; choosing it adds a Ducker on Pad with Kick as trigger and opens the window with its leader line.

Hear the trigger: the Kick comes out of the Pad track.

## 26 November 2026, v0.206: AI voices for Voice (task Z2, from a later message)

> "We need AI voices to be a follow-up task because that's the most important thing of this device: that you can change your voice into any other voice for singing or talking. And you should be able to use an example clip, then use that as a voice, and then you can sing, and it will use the example clip to morph your voice into that. With AI, do research on the best AI voices and neural networks that are open source, and let's implement the best one or make our own better one. You can choose which is the best."

Reading: a zero-shot voice (from one example clip, no training), for singing and talking, that keeps the notes the user sang or edited in the note view. Section 8 of `docs/voice-research.md` (task Z) had chosen RVC, which needs a model trained per voice, so it did not fit this message; the research was redone (section 9 of `docs/voice-research.md`, with sources, licences, sizes and measurements).

### Decision
- **OpenVoice V2** (MyShell; code and weights MIT): the only zero-shot model found whose licence allows using it in this app. Seed-VC is GPL-3.0, Vevo2's weights are CC BY-NC-ND, so-vits-svc and Diff-SVC are AGPL, RVC and DDSP-SVC need training per voice, kNN-VC (MIT) needs WavLM-Large (about 1.2 GB) and takes the pitch from the example. Newer singing papers (YingMusic-SVC, R2-SVC) have no confirmed weights or licences yet.
- ONNX fp16 build (66 MB, moonbox-dev, pinned revision), **checked against the original PyTorch model: 47.4 dB SNR** (the int8 build only 4 dB, not used; an own fp32 export only 10 % faster). Downloaded on first use from Hugging Face with a SHA-256 check per file, kept in userData; nothing is bundled in index.html or the app.
- **Own pipeline around it**, because the model moves the pitch toward the example's range (measured: a woman singing into a man's voice came out 2,340 cents lower): the example's spectrogram is redrawn at the singer's pitch (voiced frames only) before its embedding is taken, a short stretch is converted first and the range corrected (at most three tries), and the Voice device's TD-PSOLA puts the result onto the edited pitch line. So note edits and Correct pitch still work after conversion, and moving a note does not need the AI again.
- Runs in the desktop app with onnxruntime-node 1.31.1 in an Electron **utility process** (its `run()` blocks JavaScript: measured), closed after 3 minutes without work. Processor by default; "Faster (test)" tries Core ML (Mac) or DirectML (Windows) and falls back by itself.
- **Live AI voice is not offered**: the model needs 0.5 s of context after each moment to sound the same as a whole-file conversion (measured: 0.08 dB log-spectral distance at 0.5 s, 1.83 dB at 0.25 s). Recordings are converted after recording, with a progress bar, into a copy (the recording is kept).

### Built
- Desktop: `desktop/voice-ai.js` (download with progress and checksum, saved voices in `userData/voice-ai/voices`, the utility process, IPC `al:vai-*`), `desktop/voice-ai-worker.js` (spectrogram identical to OpenVoice's, max relative error 7e-5; spectral redraw at another pitch; embedding; chunked conversion with 4 s chunks, 0.75 s context and noise fixed per frame so chunks join without a seam), one line in `main.js`, `voiceAI` in `preload.js`, `onnxruntime-node` in `package.json` with only the Mac arm64 (45.6 MB) or Windows x64 (66 MB) binaries packed and unpacked from the asar. MIN_SHELL not raised: the page checks for `audiolabDesktop.voiceAI` and says "Update the app to use AI voices".
- Page (block "AI voices for Voice" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`): the Voice device's **AI voice** button opens the AI voice window: model status and Download (66 MB), the voice list (built in, saved; use, choir, Rename, Delete), Make a voice from a clip of the project or a file (also by dropping it on the window) with the note "Only use voices you have the right to use"; Convert this clip: Amount, Notes (Keep my notes, Nearest octave of the voice, The voice's own range), Pitch range, Formant, Breath and consonants, Speed; Convert, Convert again, Back to the recording; progress bar and message; a tooltip on every control. `vxRender` hands AI clips to `vaiRender` (stage 2 in a worker: pitch lock, formant, breath and air band from the recording, level match, choir layers), so converted clips also play and can be note-edited in the web version.
- Choir: up to 3 more voices (saved or built in) sung with the lead, detuned, delayed and panned.
- Built-in voices: three blends of many VocalSet singers (CC BY 4.0): Low blend (11 men), High blend (6 women), Mixed blend (all 17); they are nobody in particular (measured 0.52 to 0.65 against three of their own members, the same singer against herself 0.74).
- Web version: says plainly that AI voices run in the desktop app and what still works there.

### Measured (`tools/ui-tests/aivoice.py`, `tools/ui-tests/aivoice_electron.py`; build machine: 2 x86 cores, no GPU)
- Speaker similarity (WavLM-base-plus-sv cosine to the example): woman singing into a man's voice 0.628 before, 0.736 keeping her notes, 0.904 at the nearest octave of the voice; man talking into a woman's voice 0.378 before, 0.486 keeping his pitch, 0.740 at the voice's own range.
- Held notes on the edited pitch line: median -2.8, +4.2, -0.7 cents (10 to 90 %: -8 to +9.5); after moving a note +2 semitones the same within 1.5 cents. Real sung phrase against the sung pitch: median +10.9 cents, median absolute 17.3.
- No clicks (largest sample step 1.6 to 1.9 times the 99.9th percentile, the recordings 1.5 to 1.7); level within 0.4 dB of the recording; recordings unchanged (checksum); a rendered AI copy is made again after a reload.
- Time: the model about 1.0 times real time here; a whole conversion with the pitch fitting 1.0 to 3.2 times the clip length; making a voice 2.4 s. Apple silicon not measured; guess 4 to 8 times faster on an M4. Model process memory 634 to 661 MB.
- Electron 45 with the real `main.js`: the page sees the bridge, the model downloads in 3.4 to 3.6 s with progress, a wrong checksum deletes the file, a voice is made and a clip converted end to end; no page errors.
- Voice device unchanged (`tools/ui-tests/voice.py` all as before), demo renders unchanged (Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB, beats 64 to 96).

### Left
- Live AI voice (needs a streaming model with a licence that allows shipping), Core ML and DirectML speed and real times on an M4 and on Windows, AI voices in the web version (onnxruntime-web), importing trained RVC voices.

### Release (v0.206)
- Commit d0da3a5 on main, then d4e48b0: the first Mac build (run 45774082916) failed because `voice-ai-worker.js` was listed for unpacking from the asar and electron-builder linked it twice (EEXIST); Windows built. Fix: the worker stays inside the asar (Electron's utility process reads it there), only `onnxruntime-node/bin/**` is unpacked. Checked before pushing with a packed Linux app (electron-builder `dir`): the model downloaded, a voice was made and a clip converted from inside the asar, no page errors.
- Desktop build run 45774597676 succeeded (3 min 9 s). Sizes: Mac dmg 148.6 MB (v0.204: 127.4 MB, +21 MB for ONNX Runtime), Windows installer 150.6 MB (v0.204: 122.0 MB, +28.6 MB with DirectML). The Mac app could not be opened here (no Mac); it is built and ad-hoc signed by the same steps as before, and the same Electron 45 code ran end to end on Linux.
- MIN_SHELL stays 0.147.0; the page says "Update the app to use AI voices" in older apps. Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 229. Project doc `claude/audiolab-voice-device.md` updated (AI voices first, then the v0.200 device and the research).
## 26 November 2026, v0.207: update window (task Y, from a later message)

> "The update notification should be a window popping up in the center with an X and some more information. And let's have the logo on the top."

Reading: every update notice (a new program version downloaded over the air, and a new desktop app on Windows or Mac) becomes one centred window instead of the small card at the top right and the system dialogs. Task V (app icon, v0.203) was finished, so this run went ahead.

### Built (block "v0.207: update window (task Y)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`; names `uyw*`, `UYW*`, `#uywM`, `#uywB`)
- **Window** (`uywOpen`, `uywPaint`): glass like the other windows, centred, a short fade in. At the top the logo: the icon chosen in Settings, App icon (desktop app: `listAppIcons`, its picture from `audiolab://app/app-icons/thumb/`; browser: the picker pictures) or Ocean Yellow, which is built in (a 4.7 KB WebP) so it shows even offline and in the published copy; a picture that fails to load falls back to it. Then a title, the version you have and the new one, What is new (the manifest notes from `update-notes.txt`), Recent releases (the first lines of the last five release commits, two lines each, click to read all), What to do as numbered steps, the buttons, and an X at the top right. Escape closes. A tooltip on every button.
- **Over the air** (`audiolabHost.updateReady`, every app build): Later / Restart now (saves, restarts the window; the "AudioLab was updated" card afterwards is unchanged).
- **New desktop app** (`audiolabHost.appUpdate`, called by `main.js` from app v0.207 on): Windows: "needs a new app, downloading by itself", then "Restart and install" (new IPC `al:install-app`, asks to save like quitting, then `quitAndInstall`); Mac and other cases: the three steps (download the .dmg, drag into Applications and Replace, open again) with Download the new app, plus the note "log out of your Mac and log in again" when the app being replaced is older than v0.203 (the icon changed there, `UYW_ICON`).
- **Playback**: while something plays, records or exports the window never opens; a small badge with the logo shows at the top right ("Update 0.207", "New app 0.300") and opens the window on a click; the window opens by itself after 1.5 s of quiet. If playback starts while it is open (Space reaches the transport, the window takes no key except Escape and focuses nothing) it folds back into the badge and opens again when the sound stops.
- **Later** is stored per version and kind (`al-upd-later`, e.g. `ota::0.207`, `app:ready:0.300`): that version then only shows the badge. The X closes it for this session (the badge stays).
- "Install updates automatically" keeps its own small card (it installs by itself when safe). The Updates window (Check for updates) shows the same logo in its header.
- `main.js`: `update-downloaded` (Windows) and `needNewApp` go to the page first (`appUpdateToPage`), with the system dialogs only when the page is older; `preload.js` gets `installApp`. MIN_SHELL stays 0.147.0.

### Tested

Screenshots of every window looked at.
- `tools/engine-tests/updater/test_window.js` (Electron 45 under xvfb with today's `main.js`, local update server): the hourly check downloads 0.207 and the window shows "AudioLab 0.207 is ready, 0.206 → 0.207, Later/Restart now"; Restart now runs 0.207 with the "updated" card; a manifest asking for a newer app shows "Version 0.207 needs a new app, Later/Download the new app"; no system dialog in either.
- No page errors on load (the only console message is the test server's missing service worker file, the same on v0.206). Demo unchanged: `export_same.py` Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96) with the same checksums; Plant, Sampler and Drum kit vary from run to run on v0.206 as well.

## 26 November 2026, v0.208: change several channels and tracks together (task AE, from a later message)
> "I want to be able to select multiple channels and move multiple related parameters at the same time. So let's say I select kick, bass, and riff, and then I slide down on one volume level. I want them all to follow, and do some research online how it should follow, in proportion or relative or any other way. I want the same for the panning, mute, and all the other buttons that are the same over and over again on the channels. And let's do exactly the same for tracks as well ... One small thing on the mixer: the tab to pull it down is a little bit overlapping over 'output to group' ... I like that it's thin and not taking up a lot of space. Let's keep that."

### What was there before
- The track headers already had a multi-selection (`TSEL`: Cmd or Ctrl-click, Shift-click for a range, light blue outline), used only for "Group the selected tracks". The mixer had no multi-selection, and every control changed only its own track. The cable legend sat 3 px above the window's bottom edge, and the bottom tab (v0.205) reached 7 px into the window, over "Output to group".

### Research
- **Ableton Live 12 manual, Mixing** (ableton.com/en/live-manual/12/mixing/): "With multiple tracks selected, adjusting the volume of one of them will adjust the others as well", and differences between them are kept ("this difference will be maintained"); the same for pan. Toggling one Track Activator toggles the others, and pressing a Solo or Arm switch of a selected track solos or arms all of them.
- **REAPER**, Sound On Sound "Reaper: VCA grouping and linking" (soundonsound.com/techniques/reaper-vca-grouping-linking): tweak a control on one selected track "and the others will all follow", keeping the level relationship; selection by Shift or Cmd/Ctrl-click; covers level, pan, mute and solo.
- **Studio One / Fender Studio Pro manual, Groups** (fenderstudiopromanual.fender.com, Mixing Topics, Groups): group faders move "relative to one another, maintaining the correct dB value relationships"; mute, solo and sends are linked; Alt/Option-click suspends the group so only one track changes.
- **Cubase Pro 10, Using Quick Link** (steinberg.help, MixConsole): Q-Link synchronises "all touched parameters of selected channels"; the page does not say relative or absolute (Cubase has both modes in its link settings, not re-read this run).
- **Logic Pro, Edit multiple tracks** (support.apple.com, lgcpe53254fa): swiping across Mute or Solo buttons sets all of them to the same state (the page summary, the verbatim text was not seen). Pages on selection-based processing and Bitwig's Mix View do not cover multi-channel controls.
- **Pro Tools** (relative groups in dB, offsets kept while one fader is at its limit) and Bitwig were searched; no primary page was read, so they are not used for the decision.

### Decision
- **Faders and sends: relative in dB.** Every selected channel moves by the same number of dB as the one being dragged (Ableton, REAPER, Studio One). The offsets are measured from where each channel was when the drag started, so a channel that reaches +6 dB or the bottom stops there and gets its old difference back when the drag comes back (it is lost only if you let go there). If the dragged fader goes to silent, all go silent. A channel that was silent stays silent (there is no offset to keep), and if the dragged one started silent only the silent ones follow it. A send moves only on the channels that already send to that return.
- **Pan and width: relative** (same offset, each stops at its own end), like Ableton.
- **Option (Alt) during a drag: all get the same value** (absolute), a mode the research shows some programs offer. **Cmd or Ctrl held when the drag or click starts: only that one changes** (Studio One uses Alt for that, but Alt was already free here for absolute, and Cmd/Ctrl-click is how the selection is made, so holding it on a control reads as "this one").
- **Buttons** (mute, solo, record arm, read automation, write automation): all selected tracks get the clicked track's new state, not each one flipped (Ableton, Logic), so a mixed selection ends up the same.
- **Double-click** on a control of a selected channel resets all of them, the **mouse wheel** moves all of them relative.

### Built (block "v0.208: change several channels and tracks together (task AE)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`; names `ae*`, `AE`)
- One selection for both views: `selT` (the focused track) plus `TSEL`. The mixer now selects like the headers: click (clears), Shift-click (range, folded tracks skipped), Cmd or Ctrl-click (add or remove). `hdrMark` marks both the headers and the mixer strips (`.msel`: light blue edge and tint), runs after every mixer render, and shows "N selected ✕" in the mixer title bar (click to clear; tooltip explains the modifiers). A tip appears once per session when two or more are selected.
- Controls: on pointer down on a control of a selected track (`mx:<id>:vol|pan`, `mx:<id>:s:<return>`, `hd:<id>`, `hp:<id>`, `fx:vol|pan|width`), the start values of all selected tracks are taken and the control's setter is wrapped once (`aeWrap`), so every frame of the drag sets the others (`aeFollow`, then one `changed(false)`). The existing first-move `pushUndo` already covers all tracks: one undo step.
- Buttons: a capture click on header and mixer buttons applies the state to all selected with one `pushUndo` (`pushUndo` is silenced inside, `AE.noU`); arm skips groups and returns and asks for the input per audio track as before.
- Right-click a selected track (header or mixer strip): a menu for all of them: colour, mute, solo, arm, duplicate (the copies become the selection), freeze or unfreeze (one question for all), group, the menu for that track alone, clear the selection, delete. Duplicate and delete are one undo step.
- Bottom of the mixer: the cable legend moved up from 3 px to 8 px above the edge (the cable rows keep 5 px more room for it) and the tab is 11 px instead of 13 px and sits 1 px lower, so there is a 4 px gap between them; the tab is as thin as before.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskAE.py`, headless Chromium)
- No page errors on load or in any step; version 0.208.
- Kick, Bass, Clap at -3, -9, -14 dB selected with Ctrl-click in the mixer (3 strips and 3 headers marked, "3 selected"). Dragging the Kick fader: -23.81, -29.81, -34.81 (all down 20.81 dB); far up: 6, 0, -5 (Kick stops at +6, the others keep going by the same step); back down: -8.24, -14.24, -19.24 (offsets of 6 and 11 dB back). One undo step, and one undo gives -3, -9, -14.
- Pan 0, -0.2, 0.5 dragged right: 0.75, 0.55, 1 (Clap stops at the right); with Alt: 0.25 for all; with Ctrl: only Kick moved.
- Mute clicked on Bass in the mixer: all three muted, one undo step; Ctrl-click on Solo: only Bass; Solo on Clap: all three on; three undos bring it all back.
- Arrangement: the same three headers marked after closing the mixer; the Bass header volume dragged left: all three down by 13.56 dB, one undo step; the wheel moved all three by 6.47 dB; a double-click reset all three to 0 dB; header mute on Kick: all three; a plain click cleared the selection; Shift-click gave the range Kick to Clap (4 tracks).
- Right-click menu with "4 tracks selected": Duplicate made 4 copies (30 → 34 tracks, the copies selected), one undo back to 30; Delete 3 tracks (30 → 27), one undo back to 30.
- Bottom tab: legend bottom at 551 px, tab top at 555 px (it was 552 over a legend ending at 556); screenshots of the strips, the menu and the tab looked at.
- Demo unchanged: `tools/engine-tests/export_same.py` Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96), same checksums.

### Not verified
- The user's Mac and trackpad (Cmd is the key there; the test used Ctrl); moving several channels while automation Write is on (the followers write their own lanes through `awTouch`, not tested while playing); the devices panel's own buttons (Mono, phase, FX on) are not linked, since that panel belongs to another run.

### Left
- A saved link mode (groups that stay linked without selecting, like Studio One groups or Cubase links) and a choice of absolute as the default; linking the devices panel's buttons and the effect knobs of the same effect on several tracks; the Delete key for selected tracks.
### Release
- v0.208: commit e97ce44 on main, on top of v0.207 (task Y, released by another session while this one worked; the version moved from 0.207 to 0.208 and both sides of the conflict were kept). Desktop build run 45776476015 succeeded. Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 231 (it was at v0.207). Project doc `claude/audiolab-run-AE.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.209: About window showcase, "Under the hood" (task AD, from a later message)

> "Let's also add some small things for the About tab. Let's also explain or advertise all the advanced features that our audio workstation has, like voice pooling, block timing, the neural network for splitting stems, the neural AI voice changer, stuff like that, and add all the advanced features in the About, just to brag and make the software look very intelligent and complex."

Reading: the About window (opened by the logo atom; it is the one that credits Ilja V. Jakovliev) gets an "Under the hood" section. Settings has no About tab of its own, only an About line in General, so that line gets a button "What's inside AudioLab" that opens the same window. Run order checked first: task Z2 (v0.206) and task V (v0.203) were released.

### Research (small)
- Baymard Institute, "Structure descriptions by highlights" (https://baymard.com/blog/structure-descriptions-by-highlights): 2 to 6 highlights, each an icon or picture, a short headline and one paragraph; the rest in an "additional features" part; plain bullet dumps are "rarely inspiring".
- FabFilter Pro-Q 4 release (https://www.fabfilter.com/news/1733994000/fabfilter-releases-pro-q-4-equalizer-plug-in): bold feature names with a short paragraph, numbers inline ("up to 24 bands"), then a long one-line "Key features" list.
- Bitwig Studio 5.0 release notes (https://downloads.bitwig.com/5.0/Release-Notes-5.0.html): numbered short titles, details below, grouped by part of the program.
- Applied: six highlight tiles (icon, name, number) first, then "Show all 34 features" opens six groups with an icon, a short name, one plain sentence and the measured number per feature; a small note that the numbers come from our own tests.

### Built (block "About: Under the hood showcase" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`; names `AHF`, `AHF_ICO`, `ahfSvg`, `ahfHtml`, `.ahf*`, `#genHood`)
- The About window is taller (up to 860 px, 92 % of the screen) and scrolls; the logo, wordmark, credits, the version grid, Check for updates and the copyright are kept; the close button stays on screen while scrolling.
- Collapsed: "Under the hood", "What runs inside AudioLab Pro, with the numbers we measured", six tiles: Pooled voice engine, Sample-exact block timing, Clean preset switch, Background freeze, Neural stem splitter, AI voices. Hovering a tile shows its plain sentence.
- "Show all 34 features": two columns of groups (Audio engine 8, Mixing and metering 5, Desktop app 3 | AI 5, Sound design 8, Interface 5), each in its own colour (ocean blue, yellow, teal, purple, green, blue), and scrolls to the section. "Show less" closes it.
- Animation: tiles and groups fade up once (0.4 to 0.5 s, staggered) when shown; CSS only, nothing runs afterwards or while the window is closed; none in Light mode or with reduced motion. Light mode also drops the gradients of the tiles.
- No em dashes in the text (checked by the test).

### Every claim and its source
Audio engine
- Pooled voice engine, "32 voices per track, up to 14 oscillators each": older than this log (v0.145); code `PWL` "AudioLab pooled voice engine (v0.145)" (`LIVE=32`, `NOSC=14`) and the Settings, Performance tooltip "one engine per track with 32 reusable voices".
- Sample-exact block timing, "A note moved by 293 samples: difference 0": L738 (pieces of 64 samples counted from the start, does not depend on the block size, 128 live, 512 in an export), L600, L632.
- Bit-identical renders, "Same checksums in every release": the export check in every entry (e.g. L1180), L1325, L1643, and this release's `export_same.py`.
- Look-ahead scheduler, "Clock tick every 20 ms": older than this log; code "scheduler clock in a worker" (`setInterval(...,20)` in the worker), look-ahead also at L1084.
- Clean preset switch, "Old sound gone in 13 to 19 ms": L972, L1084.
- Every note on time, "23 instruments: 0 missed, 0 doubled": L1115.
- Sleeping tracks, "Stopped demo: 22 % of a core down to 3 %": L2124 (21.6 to 23.9 % before, 2.8 to 3.2 % after).
- Background freeze, "Six heavy tracks: 59 % to 7 % of the audio thread": L2160 (58.9 % without, 7.1 % with); thaw L2159.
AI
- Neural stem splitter, "4 or 6 stems, offline after one download": older than this log; code, the "Split into stems" window ("HTDemucs v4 by Meta ... Downloads 166 MB once, then works offline", 4 and 6 stems), also named in docs/voice-research.md ("the app already loads it for the neural stem split").
- AI voices, "Held notes within about 4 cents": L2387 (median -2.8, +4.2, -0.7 cents), v0.206.
- Checked AI model, "47.4 dB match to the original": L2373.
- Voice note editing, "Steady notes read to 0.08 cents": L2029; YIN L2012.
- Live voice changer, "23.5 ms delay; a choir of 8 uses 5.3 % of a core": L2014, L2038; TD-PSOLA L2012.
