## 26 November 2026, v0.210: mixer flicker and sidechain window layout (task AG, from a later message)
> "Whenever you turn a device on and off on the mixer, it looks like some elements flicker, like the stereo field and the level meters ... Let's fix that. And let's improve the sidechaining cable window a little bit when it comes to arranging the buttons, and maybe improve the style of the buttons a little bit. The delete button is in the wrong place, I think. Look online how a sidechain like this should look ... make it a little bit more clear what is what, and use more illustrations and icons, but not too much, just subtle."

### What was there before (found by measuring)
- The power dot on an effect slot did `changed(true)` and then `renderMixer()`; `changed(true)` runs `renderHeaders()`, which also calls `renderMixer()`. So one click rebuilt the whole mixer twice. Every rebuild made new canvases: for one frame each channel picture was empty (transparent, pixel sum 0), the fader meters had no clip (drawn full height), the master loudness and peak showed "–" until the slow read-out caught up (about a quarter of a second, longer when stopped: then it stayed "–"), and the stereo field lost its trails. The audio analysers were not the cause: they stay in `NODES` and the effect chain is reconnected in one step.
- The same happened on every other mixer rebuild (mute, solo, adding an effect, the devices panel's power button).
- Sidechain window (v0.205): names with an arrow, then 8 knobs in two rows in no particular order, Listen to, the Focus knob alone on a row, then Hear the trigger (an emoji), Presets, Swap, Device in one row and the delete button alone at the bottom right, with no undo note.

### Research
- **FabFilter Pro-C sidechain section** (fabfilter.com/help/pro-c/using/sidechainsection): the trigger controls are a separate section under the main controls, shown with a Side Chain button; level of the trigger, Audition (click, or hold for a moment) to hear "the filtered and stereo-linked signal that will be used to trigger compression", input choice, stereo link, then the trigger EQ.
- **Ableton Compressor sidechain** and **Bitwig Audio Sidechain modulator** (read for task AB, see the v0.205 entry): source first, gain, filter and listen; dynamics settings on the main panel.
- **Cableguys ShaperBox / Kickstart 2 / VolumeShaper**: the curve is the centre of the window and the amount (Mix) is the main control; ShaperBox switches to audio trigger with an "External Sidechain" button and keeps the trigger detection in a small separate setup panel (Cableguys ShaperBox 3 support page; the VolumeShaper manual page that was asked for returned the ShaperBox FAQ instead, so VolumeShaper's own layout was not read). Kickstart's picture of the curve over the kick was already the model in v0.205.
- **Trackspacer** (v0.205 entry): one big amount, the frequency range second.
- **NN/g, "Dangerous UX: consequential options close to benign options"** (nngroup.com/articles/proximity-consequential-options/): "Avoid placing highly consequential actions ... directly next to options that are benign"; add space and other cues (colour, icon), it is fine to make the dangerous one a little harder to hit.
- **Smashing Magazine, "How to manage dangerous actions in user interfaces"** (2024): undo is smoother than a confirmation for reversible actions; confirmations get clicked "on autopilot"; dangerous actions in a separated area at the bottom. (The last part of the page came through as a summary only.)
- Taken from this: who triggers whom first; the picture of the duck next; then the amount and shape, the timing, and what the trigger listens to (the sidechain filter and audition grouped together, like Pro-C); Remove in a footer, on the left, away from the other buttons, red only as text, no question first but an Undo button right where the window was (removing is one undo step).

### Built (block "v0.210: mixer flicker and sidechain window layout (task AG)" before `askChoice`, CSS before `.dacv`; names `ag*`, `AG`)
- **Device on and off**: the slot's power dot and "Turn on / Turn off" in the slot menu run inside `agOnly`: the mixer is not rebuilt, `agSlotsOnly` only switches the `off` look of the slots (and redraws the cables); if anything else differs it falls back to a full rebuild. The pause button of the sidechain window does the same.
- **Every other rebuild** (`renderMixer` wrapper, outermost): before the rebuild it keeps each channel's canvas and fader meter; after it, the old canvas (with its drawing, trails and cached state) and the old meter (with its peak line) are moved into the new channel in place of the new ones (attributes copied, never width or height, since setting them clears a canvas), and the master loudness, peak and clip light keep their last values. `MXV` is updated.
- **Sidechain window** (`abxBuild` is now `agBuild`):
  - Header: small captions Trigger and Ducks over the two coloured names, the arrow, a swap button next to it; pause and close on the right.
  - A line in words (orange "Paused: ..." when paused).
  - Picture: while playing the live picture of v0.205; when stopped or paused a preview of one duck (`agPaint`): the hit of the trigger, the level of the ducked track with attack, hold and release marked under it, the depth in dB, "preview · play to see it live". Drawn only when a setting or the size changes.
  - Sections with a small icon and a heading: How much (Duck amount, Curve, Trigger level, trigger gain, and Presets on the heading's right), Timing "after each hit" (Attack, Hold, Release, Look-ahead), Listen to "what part of Kick makes it duck" (All, Lows, Band, Highs, each with a small filter shape; Hear it with a headphones icon; Focus as a slider on its own line with a label).
  - Footer: Remove (bin icon, red text, no frame until hover) on the left, Show device on the right.
  - Remove: the sidechain goes at once (one undo step) and a small bar appears where the window was: "Pad no longer ducks under Kick." with Undo (and ✕), for 8 seconds; Undo brings back the Ducker and opens its window again; if something else changed meanwhile it says so instead of undoing the wrong step.
  - Every button and control has a tooltip; no em dashes in the new text.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskAG.py`, headless Chromium; the same test on v0.209 for comparison)
- No page errors on load or in any step.
- Stereo field view, Kick channel with a neutral Imager added for the test, playing from beat 72, Imager off and on, every frame recorded (canvas element, pixel sum, fader meter clip, master loudness): v0.210 one canvas element, no blank frame, no full meter, no "–", no mixer rebuild, biggest change next to a toggle 28467 against 34350 for the music alone. v0.209: 3 canvas elements, a blank frame and a full meter at the toggle, "–" in the loudness, 2 rebuilds, a change of 212329 next to the toggle (the music alone 28881).
- Meter view, Bass channel: v0.210 one canvas, nothing blank, 298641 next to a toggle against 372910 for the music; v0.209 3 canvases, a blank frame, "–" twice.
- Stopped: v0.210 nothing changes; v0.209 new canvases and the loudness stays "–" after the toggle.
- Mute on and off (a full rebuild): v0.210 one canvas element, no blank frame; v0.209 3 elements and a blank frame.
- Sidechain window (Kick to Pad): sections How much, Timing, Listen to; footer Remove, Show device; 0 controls without a tooltip. Remove: the Ducker is gone, the window closes, the bar reads "Pad no longer ducks under Kick.", one undo step; Undo: the Ducker is back and its window open, the bar gone. Pause: off, line "Paused: Pad plays at full level". Band shows the Focus slider. Screenshots before and after (stopped with the preview, playing, paused, the undo bar) looked at.
- Demo unchanged: `tools/engine-tests/export_same.py` Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96), same checksums.

### Not verified
- The user's Mac, trackpad and real GPU; the devices panel redraws its own pictures when its power button is used (another run's area, not touched).

### Left
- Clicking and holding Hear it to listen only while held (Pro-C does this); a preview curve in the devices panel's Ducker.
### Release
- v0.210: commit 7abff48 on main. Desktop build run 45781476825 dispatched through the GitHub connector (the gh dispatch was refused by the proxy: numeric-ID repository path); it was running when this was written. Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 233 (it was at v0.209). Project doc `claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AG.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.211: devices panel fixes (task AA, from a later message)
> "Every time you change a preset in the devices, it resets the scrolling [...] it scrolls all the way to the left. [...] when you change presets, especially with instruments, it's not that instant and there are some bugs [...] you still have to fix the weirdness when you change from one preset to the other. [...] whenever you change a parameter on the big black box, it plays like a sample sound or a demo sound, even when the song is playing. We don't want that at all. [...] the devices button on the top right to collapse it and make it bigger is a little bit too low. It should look like it's part of it, not intruding into it [...] more professional by design rules. You can do some research online".

### What changed for the user
- **The panel stays where you left it when a preset changes.** The row of track tabs no longer jumps back (it was drawn again on every change and centred on the shown track, which looked like "scrolled to the left" when you had scrolled the tabs elsewhere). It now only moves when you pick another track whose tab is out of view, and then just far enough to show it. In the Devices window, where devices wrap into rows, a preset change no longer moves the view up and to the left.
- **No sounds from clicking devices.** Clicking, dragging, double-clicking or choosing a menu entry on any device (picture, knobs, sliders, wave and mode buttons, drum pads, preset menus and arrows, the big Venom and Grid windows, pinned windows) no longer plays a note, playing or stopped. The actions still happen (a pad is selected, a wave is chosen). The explicit play buttons stay: Play the kick, Play this pad, and the ▶ of the instruments that have one. A preset change no longer plays a test note either. The Change browser keeps its short test sound while stopped (it is a browser for hearing instruments, not a control on the device).
- **Cleaner preset switches.** On the synths with the voice engine (Saw, Square, Pad, Strings, Keys, Bass, Sub bass, Reese, Acid, Plant, Wavetable, Sampler in grain mode) a note that carries on after a switch no longer starts with all its unison oscillators in phase, which gave a loud zap (+10 to +13 dB for 50 to 100 ms on supersaw sounds); the oscillators continue where they would be. A key held on the keyboard while stopped now carries on at its age (it used to start its attack again: Pad and Strings dipped by 18 to 27 dB). On Emit, Vector Grain, Vector FM, Orbit, Sub, Bounce, Psy Bass and Fractal a held key was lost for good at a preset switch (silent afterwards); it now carries on with the new preset.
- **The devices panel has a real header.** The track tabs, Master and the Devices button now sit in one header strip that belongs to the panel: the same surface as the panel, a hairline on top, a line under it that the shown track's tab breaks. The Devices button is an ordinary header button centred in the strip with a divider before it, so it no longer hangs down into the devices. Folded away, the strip stays as a thin header bar on the edge with the tabs (a click on a tab opens the panel) and the button pointing up, tinted blue.

### Research (sources read)
- Apple Human Interface Guidelines, Disclosure controls: a disclosure button points down when the content is hidden and up when it is shown; "Place a disclosure button near the content that it shows and hides"; "Use no more than one disclosure button in a single view". https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/disclosure-controls
- Apple Human Interface Guidelines, Panels: panels are less prominent than the main window, have a title bar with a short noun as the title; nothing about docked bottom panels. https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/panels
- Bitwig Studio user guide, The Window Footer and Anatomy of the window: panel toggles are icon buttons in a strip that belongs to the window (lit when the panel is shown), grouped on the side where the panel opens; header, body and footer are separate slices. https://www.bitwig.com/userguide/latest/the_window_footer , https://www.bitwig.com/userguide/latest/anatomy_of_the_bitwig_studio_window
- Ableton help, Navigation and View Options in Live 12: the Mixer view control sits at the bottom right corner of the window and folds the mixer in or out; the same from the View menu and a key. https://help.ableton.com/hc/en-us/articles/12243771208092
- Figma, Our approach to designing UI3: floating panels were tried and reversed, "panels are fixed, but still resizable", because floating panels cramped the canvas and slowed people down. https://www.figma.com/blog/our-approach-to-designing-ui3/
- Decisions taken from these: the toggle is a button inside the panel's own header (Apple: near the content it hides; Bitwig: in a strip that is part of the frame), not a tab glued to the edge; one toggle per panel; the chevron points down while shown (the panel folds down) and up while folded away; the header is a fixed part of the panel, not floating pieces over the arrangement (Figma's lesson); when folded away the header stays as a thin bar so the control never jumps. Studio One and Logic pages were not read; nothing here rests on them.

### Part 1: scrolling, measured (`tools/ui-tests/preset_scroll.py`, new; Fractals demo, 1280 x 900)
Before (v0.202): the device row in the panel already kept its place for every way of changing a preset (menu, arrow key on the menu, arrows of an effect and of the Kick designer, an effect's menu, Change with clicks, keys, Escape and Enter, stopped and playing). What moved:
    track tabs scrolled to the start, preset menu changed:   tabs 0 > 544 (jump to the shown track's tab)
    track tabs scrolled to the end, preset menu changed:     tabs 1200 > 544
    Devices window scrolled down, any preset changed:         down 619 > 242 (and 46 > 0 sideways)
After: every case keeps both positions (16 checks stopped and playing, 0 failures).
How: `renderFx` keeps the tab row's position (`tabsKeep`), `fxswWire` no longer centres the shown tab on every redraw; an outermost `renderFx` wrapper, installed after all the others, puts the device row back in both directions at once and once more in the next frame before it is painted.

### Part 2: preset switch on every instrument, measured (`tools/engine-tests/preset_switch2.py`, new)
Method: two tracks play the same notes; A starts on preset k0 and is switched through its preset menu, B plays the new preset all along; both recorded on the audio thread with a marker at the click. "new sound": ms from the click until A's band levels stay within what A and B differ by 0.6 to 1 s later (+2 dB) for 100 ms. "over/dip": the loudest and quietest 10 ms of A against B 30 to 300 ms after the click. Cases: one long note playing, sixteenth notes playing, a key held while stopped. Full tables before and after for all 32 instruments are in `docs/preset-switch-AA.md`; the instruments that changed:

    case / instrument            new sound before > after   over / dip before          > after
    long note, Plant (supersaw)  207 > 37 ms                +13.1 / -7.2  > +1.3 / -0.9
    long note, Wavetable         307 > 47 ms                +10.8 / -11.0 > +3.5 / -5.7
    long note, Pad               182 > 37 ms                +5.4 / -89.7  > +1.2 / -1.1
    long note, Reese             247 > 12 ms                +5.1 / -96.6  > +3.7 / -4.2
    long note, Saw lead           27 > 32 ms                +4.6 / -5.9   > +0.9 / -1.0
    key held, Saw lead           297 > 22 ms                +12.5 / -1.7  > 0.0 / 0.0
    key held, Pad                227 > 27 ms                -0.6 / -19.6  > 0.0 / 0.0
    key held, Strings            577 > 27 ms                +3.2 / -17.8  > 0.0 / 0.0
    key held, Sub bass           717 > 27 ms                +4.3 / -0.2   > 0.0 / 0.0
    key held, Emit / Vector FM / Orbit / Sub (sbass) / Fractal: the held key silent after the switch (-92 to -108 dB, measured with the test note removed) > it carries on with the new preset
- "key held" before: the extra "over" was the test note of the preset change playing on top of the held key; it is gone.
- Unchanged and fine: drums, Kick designer, Snare, Clap, Hat, Tom, Perc, Growl, Grid, Sampler, SFX switch in 12 to 37 ms with no over or dip worth hearing.
- Still a restart (not fixed): Venom, Vector FM, Emit, Vector Grain, Orbit and Fractal start a picked-up note with their own attack (Vector FM's bell is +8 to +12 dB louder than the held note for about 0.6 s; Venom dips 5 to 6 dB for 50 ms); Bounce is a physics simulation and never matches a reference. Sixteenth notes with long-release sounds (Pad, Strings, Plant) lose the release tails of the notes before the switch, by design of the clean switch.
- Time in the change event (picture drawn after it): 11 to 40 ms for all instruments, stopped and playing; the 367 ms seen once for Venom in the before run (the first use of a wavetable) did not come back.

### Part 3: no preview sounds (`tools/ui-tests/quiet_devices.py`, new)
- Before: on v0.202 a click or drag on the picture of Plant, Wavetable, Venom (and its big window), drum pads, the Kick designer picture, knobs of the Drum kit, wave menus and every preset menu or arrow played a note (-1 to -22 dBFS on the output tap).
- How: `DQ`, a capture listener on the window for pointer, click, double-click, menu and wheel events inside a device (panel, pinned windows, Devices window, Venom and Grid windows); it is on from the event until the end of the task it runs in (a heavy rebuild can take longer than a time window) and 150 ms after; `preview`, `padHear` and the held preview `prevSet` do nothing while it is on. Events on an explicit play button turn it off. `instApply` and `kickApply` no longer play a test note. The drum pad tooltip says "click to select and edit it (the play button hears it)".
- Test: every instrument (32) and every effect (41), stopped and while the transport runs past the end of the song: the picture clicked at five points, dragged and double-clicked, every knob and slider dragged, every own button clicked (not remove, Change, pin, show parameters, on/off, play), every menu set to its next entry; the whole output tapped on the audio thread, anything above -90 dBFS counts. Result: all 32 instruments and all 41 effects silent, stopped and playing (146 device runs). The first runs were split in chunks because one long browser session ran out of breath; the test now unpins windows and closes the Change browser between devices.
- Found by this test and fixed: a click on the small buttons of Spectrum, Loudness, Shaper, Arp, Pitch correct, Space reverb, Tape stop and Meter threw "NACT[f.type] is not a function" (their actions are a list by name, the click handler only knew single functions), so those buttons never worked. They do now.
- Explicit play buttons still sound (Plant -11 dB, Venom -7 dB, Orbit -5 dB and the others).

### Part 4: the panel header (`tools/ui-tests/devices_header.py`, new)
- CSS block "v0.211: the devices panel's header (task AA)" before `.dacv`: `#fx.dsh>#fxTitle` spans the panel (left 0, right 0) with the panel's surface (glass where the panel is glass, solid in Light mode, Lighter and Lightest), hairline on top, a line under it; `.fxbtns` is centred in the strip with a divider; `#fxHide.dshb` is a 19 px header button with rounded corners (the old tab shape hung 1 px into the devices and drew a 3 px patch over the panel's edge); folded away, the strip stays with the tabs.
- Test: normal and Light mode, open and folded away: the button inside the strip (strip 566 to 591, button 569 to 588; folded 827 to 852 and 830 to 849), the strip spans the panel, tooltips on every control in it, the button folds and opens the panel, a click on a tab of the folded panel opens it, no page errors. Screenshots looked at (2x): open and folded, left and right ends, normal and Light mode; also with the piano roll docked above and with the devices in their own window.
- `tools/ui-tests/sideheaders.py`: the check of the Devices button's height now allows the new 19 px button (it asked for 24 px, the old tab).

### Other tests
- `sideheaders.py` (all devices, three panel heights, pin windows, Change, remove): 0 failures. `preset_scroll.py`: 0 failures. `devices_header.py`: 0 failures.
- Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo). No page errors on load.

### Not verified
- The user's Mac with a real mouse and trackpad; a native preset menu on macOS (the change event is the same).
- The quiet test clicks every visible control once; controls inside sub-tabs that are not open were not all reached; on Orbit, Vector FM, Bounce and Psy Bass a click changed the device's layout and the test went on with the next device.

### Code (for the next person)
- New JS blocks before `askChoice`: "the track tabs keep their place", "the device row keeps its place too", "held keys on every engine carry on after a preset switch" (`chsVo`, an outermost `voiceOn` wrapper so the engines that answer a key themselves are listed in `CHSK`), "quiet devices" (`DQ`, `dqOn`, `dqHit`, `DQOK`).
- Direct edits: `renderFx` (keeps the tab row's position), `fxswWire` (no centring), `instApply` and `kickApply` (no test note), the al-poly worklet's picked-up note (oscillator, sub and FM phases at their age), `chsKeys` (age for al-poly and al-grain, plays through `chsVo`), the drum pad tooltip.

### Release
- Commit c822696 on main. Desktop build run 45788160099 dispatched (through the GitHub connector; the gh dispatch was refused by the proxy). Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 234 (it was at v0.210). Project doc `claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AA.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.212: Shift-drag duplicates clips, arrow keys move clips (task AJ, from a later message)
Research: Ableton Live duplicates with Ctrl/Cmd+D and Ctrl/Option+drag, Logic with Option+drag, Cubase with Alt+drag, FL Studio and Bitwig with Ctrl/Alt+drag; in all of them the copy is what you drag, the original stays, and a plus appears on the cursor. Nudging with the arrows: Ableton and Cubase move by the visible grid step, so the step changes with the zoom; Shift gives a bigger step, Alt/Cmd a fine one. Here Shift is the duplicate key (as asked); Alt-click (split), Alt as fine snap and Cmd/Ctrl+D (duplicate after the clip) work as before.
- Shift-drag: a Shift press on a clip waits for 5 px of movement; a click without movement still toggles the selection as before. With movement the clip (or the whole selection when the clip is part of it) is copied; the copies are dragged with the usual snap, relative positions and track changes, the originals stay. A dashed green outline and a small green plus on each copy, and the copy cursor, show it. One Undo step removes all copies.
- Arrow keys: Left/Right move the selected clips by the smallest musical step that is at least 12 px wide on screen (1/64, 1/32, 1/16, 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1, 2, 4, 8 beats; 1/64 note zoomed far in, 4 beats at 3 px per beat); Shift 4 times that, Alt always 1/64 note. Never before time 0 (the group stops with its first clip at 0). Up/Down move to the track above or below (skipping folded ones); at the first or last track nothing moves and a hint says so; a clip whose target track is the wrong kind (MIDI on audio, audio on instrument, group, return, frozen) stays where it is, with a hint. Group moves rigidly. The view follows the clip.
- Only when the arrangement was the last place clicked (arrangement or track headers), no text field, slider, dialog, menu, settings, Devices window, piano roll, Overview, tutorial or drag is active; the computer-keyboard piano keeps its octave keys. One redraw and one change per key press (key repeat is applied at once and committed 90 ms after the last repeat); a press is one Undo step, a held key (repeats) is one step.
- Shortcuts window: rows for Shift+drag, arrows, Shift+arrows, Alt+arrows (fixed keys). The clip tooltip mentions them.
- Test tools/ui-tests/taskAJ.py: single and group duplicate, Undo/Redo, shift-click still selects, steps at 300, 80, 24, 12 and 3 px per beat (Right, Shift+Left, Alt+Right), time 0 limit, up/down and blocked cases, held key = one step, text field and focus guard, 354 clips moved 20 times in 5 ms, no console errors. Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB unchanged.
- Release: commit on main, app copy synced, desktop build not dispatched (as asked). Project doc `claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AJ.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.213: Devices button and Clap designer (task AF, from a later message)
> "Let's improve the button for the devices when you want to expand devices. [...] move it a little bit up and have it rounded as a full button on the bottom. [...] it can stick out of the bar when it's collapsed. Actually, when it's collapsed, there should not be a blurred bar to the left. [...] when you scroll the devices and you have like the colour on the top, so let's say you are in Clap and the top is orange, the devices button overlaps with that. [...] It does not need the blurred out bar on the left. [...] just have all the tabs, the master, and then just nothing, just free, no blur or anything. And the Clap designer looks a bit broken when it's in the devices. All the buttons look wrong."

### What changed for the user
- **Folded away:** only the Devices button is left, a fully rounded pill at the right, raised 6 px off the edge so it floats over the song instead of sitting in a bar. No tabs and no blurred bar along the edge any more: open space where the panel was. A click opens the panel (the track tabs come back with it).
- **Open:** the track tabs and Master stand on the panel's top edge with nothing behind them; the blurred band that ran across the whole width is gone and the song shows through the gaps. The Devices button is the same pill at the right end of that tab row, above the panel's edge, so no device ever scrolls under it: a device's coloured top (Clap's orange, any other colour) stays clear while scrolling sideways or up and down.
- **Clap designer:** it had four flat bars (Hands, Spread, Burst, Tail), a Room fader next to Level and three dials stacked in a column, so it looked unlike the other drum designers. It now has eight dials in two rows (Hands, Spread, Burst, Tail; Tone, Focus, Room, Drive) and the Level fader, like the Tom designer. The Hat designer's preset row wrapped Change and Play onto a second line; the drum designers now share one width (270 px) so the row is one line on all four.

### Why it looked broken
- The device layout picks a control by the parameter's name: "time-like" names (Hands, Spread, Burst, Tail, Room) become bars, and a later pass turns Size, Length and Room into a fader beside the gain. The Clap designer is the only drum designer whose names hit those rules, so it got bars, a second fader and only three dials. The drum designers (Snare, Clap, Hat, Tom) now keep every setting as a dial in their own order, with Level as the only fader, and up to 8 dials go in two rows.

### Built
- CSS block "v0.212: the Devices button and the tab bar without a strip (task AF)" before `.dacv` (written while v0.212 was the next number; released as v0.213): `#fx.dsh>#fxTitle` has no background, blur or shadow (normal, Light mode, Lighter, Lightest, panel on top); `#fxband` (the glass band behind the tabs from v0.193) is hidden, which also stops the arrangement being clipped under it; `#fxHide.dshb` is a 22 px pill (radius 999 px, opaque panel colour, small shadow; blue tint while folded away); open, `.fxbtns` sits at 24 px above the panel (1 px clear of the edge); folded away, `#fxTitle` is hidden and the pill sits 28 px above the edge (6 px gap); mirrored for a panel at the top. `.dev.du.pzd .dfs` min-width 270 px.
- `devLayout`: for `.pzd` devices every control but Level is a dial, in the preset's order. `duDev`: the Size/Length/Room-to-fader pass skips `.pzd`. `duFit`: `.pzd` with up to 8 dials uses at most 2 rows.
- Nothing in the sound code or the presets was touched.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskAF.py`, new; Fractals demo, 1600 x 1000)
- Normal (glass on) and Light mode, open and folded away: the button is a full pill (radius >= half its height); open: tabs shown with a transparent background, no blur, no shadow, the pill's bottom 589 above the panel's top 590; folded: no tabs, no bar, the pill's bottom 846 with the edge at 852; `#fxband` 0 px high (it was 25 px); tooltips on every control in the row; the pill folds and opens the panel.
- Overlap check in code: for all 26 tracks and Master, the devices scrolled sideways in 13 steps and up and down (top, middle, end), every device's rectangle against the pill's: no overlap. Also with a 200 px panel.
- Drum designers: Clap 8 dials, Snare 11, Hat 6, Tom 8; no bars; only Level as a fader; the preset row one line (24 px; Hat was 50 px); nothing outside the device; tooltips on every control.
- The same test on v0.212 fails in 12 checks (the strip, the 19 px button with 6 px corners, the folded bar, the band, Clap's Room fader and bars, Hat's two-line row), so it does catch the old look.
- `devices_header.py` (task AA) changed to the new design (button above the edge, tabs hidden when folded; the "click a tab of the folded panel" check removed, there are no tabs then): 0 failures. `sideheaders.py`: 0 failures. `devices_all.py` (all instruments and effects, nothing outside, shut and open again): all 74 instruments and effects, nothing outside, 0 errors.
- Panel at the top (fx before the arrangement) open and folded, and the Devices window, looked at: pill in the right place, no errors.
- Screenshots looked at (2x): open and folded, left and right ends, normal and Light mode, Clap's orange top passing the pill's x range, the four drum designers.
- No page errors on load. Demo unchanged: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96, solo).

### Not verified
- The user's Mac with real glass on a real GPU (headless renders the glass, but the refraction effect was not checked). Themes Lighter and Lightest were not looked at (the rules cover them).
- With the strip gone, the empty part of the tab row (after the last tab when there are few tracks) still catches clicks as before; it is 25 px high.

### Release
- v0.213: commit c4085e4 on main (v0.212 was taken by task AJ while this ran; merged, both sides kept). Desktop build run 45792913920 dispatched through the GitHub connector (the gh dispatch was refused by the proxy). Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 236 (it was at v0.212). Project doc `claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AF.md`. Note: GitHub reports the repository moved to Audio-lab-DAW/Audio-Lab-Pro-Alpha (pushes to the old address still work).

## 26 November 2026, v0.214: right-drag selects partly covered clips, MIDI on an audio track is bounced (task AK, from a later message)
> "We should also be able to right-click, drag, select, even if half a clip is selected. We should be able to hold in Shift and also duplicate like that as well. [...] It should actually drop the MIDI on the audio on the correct place and then bounce it automatically. So you can place MIDI on audio. Actually, that should be everywhere. If I copy or drag MIDI over audio, it should just automatically bounce it. And it becomes audio where you drop it."

Research (from what is known of the other programs, not re-checked online in this run): Logic, Cubase, Bitwig and FL Studio (select tool or Ctrl+drag) select every clip a rubber band touches, a partly covered clip counts; Ableton selects a time range instead. A rubber band on the right button is this app's own choice (the left button on empty space pans here). Bitwig bounces a note clip when it is dragged to an audio track; the others need a Bounce in place command first.

### What changed for the user
- **Right-drag = selection rectangle.** Press the right button on the arrangement (on empty space or on a clip) and drag: a dashed blue rectangle follows, and every clip it touches is selected, also a clip that is only half inside. Same highlight as any selection. With Shift held the touched clips are added to what was selected. Releasing the right button without dragging (less than 5 px) opens the usual menu. Near an edge the view scrolls along. The old right-drag (a range box that cuts clips at its edges and can be moved or inserted) is now Alt+right-drag.
- **Moving and Shift-drag duplicating** such a selection works like any selection (task AJ), across several tracks.
- **MIDI on an audio track is bounced.** A MIDI clip that is dragged, Shift-drag copied, pasted, moved with Arrow Up/Down, or dropped from Capture onto an audio track becomes an audio clip there, at the same position and length: the notes are rendered through the instrument and effects of the track they came from (what the clip sounded like), with the clip's own gain, fades and clip automation. A reverb, delay or release that rings on makes the clip longer (up to 8 s; the silence after the last audible sample is not kept; if it still rings after 8 s the clip ends in a short fade). While dragging, the clip shows "becomes audio"; after the drop the new clip shows moving stripes, a progress bar and a percentage until its waveform appears. Playback and the screen keep running meanwhile. A move removes the MIDI clip, a copy or paste keeps it. One Undo brings everything back (audio clip gone, MIDI clip back); Redo brings the audio back without rendering again.
- **Rows are kept.** Three clips on three MIDI tracks dragged down over an audio track and more MIDI tracks land on the tracks at the same row distance: the ones on MIDI tracks stay MIDI, the one on the audio track is bounced. If a clip would land above the first or below the last track, the row move is blocked for the whole selection (a hint says so); the clips still follow in time.
- **Not changed:** an audio clip on an instrument track is still refused (in a group drag it stays on its own track and a hint says so, a single clip offers a new audio track as before, the arrow keys show the hint). Dragging clips between two project windows still wants a track of the same kind (the other project's instrument is not here to render with). Pasting onto rows that do not exist still adds tracks, as before.

### Built
- `bounceRender(t,c,prog,rq,seed,tail)`: three optional settings (render block size as in exports, the export's seeding of random numbers, tail length). Without them it works as before (the Bounce window is unchanged).
- Block `ak*` before `askChoice`: `akBounceInto(dst,midiClip,srcTrack)` is the one place where MIDI becomes audio (puts the busy clip, queues the render one after the other, trims the tail, fills the clip in). `akOk` says whether a clip may sit on a track; `akCommit` converts after a drag; `akMarq`/`akAuto` are the rectangle and its edge scrolling; `akDraw` draws the rectangle, the "becomes audio" mark and the progress. The arrangement drag (single clip and group), `ajMove` (arrow keys), `clipPaste` and `capInsert` call these.
- A busy clip carries its job number, so a state brought back by Undo/Redo, or a project that was not in front when the render finished, is filled in when it is shown. A page closed in the middle of a render leaves a clip marked "Bounce did not finish".
- Shortcuts window: two new rows. Clip tooltip (tutorial mode): right-drag and the bounce are mentioned.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskAK.py`, new, 41 checks, all pass; headless, 1600 x 1000)
- Rectangle: two partly covered clips selected, an untouched one not; Shift adds a third; a rectangle over nothing clears; plain right-click and a 3 px wobble still open the clip menu.
- Layout 3 MIDI, 1 audio, 3 MIDI tracks; three clips dragged 3 rows down and 4 beats right: clip 1 became one audio clip on the audio track at the right beat, clips 2 and 3 are MIDI on the two tracks below, the three source tracks are empty; one Undo step; Undo restores exactly; Redo returns the audio clip with its audio. Shift-drag copy: the same, originals stay, one Undo removes all. Five rows down (beyond the last track): rows unchanged, hint shown.
- Paste onto the audio track: audio clip at the playhead, original stays, one Undo step. Arrow Down onto the audio track: bounced, MIDI removed; Arrow Down of that audio clip onto an instrument track: refused with the hint. A single clip dragged onto the audio track: bounced, original removed, Undo restores.
- Sound: the bounced clip against a direct render of the same clip through the same chain: identical samples (peak and RMS equal). A clip on Venom against the app's own export render of that range (solo track, times fader and master gain): null at -145.5 dB. Pluck with a 3 s reverb: the clip grew from 4 to about 6.6 beats, the tail after the MIDI clip is there (-70 dB RMS); its level equals the export render within 0.5 dB. A dry clip keeps exactly 4 beats.
- Playing while a bounce runs: playback continued, progress was shown. Hovering a MIDI clip over the audio track during playback and dragging it back changes nothing. No console errors.
- Extra checks by hand in the same headless browser: the Bounce window still renders; Capture onto an audio track bounces; a stale state is filled in; Alt+right-drag still makes the range box; the piano roll closes when its clip becomes audio; edge scrolling moves the view; `taskAJ.py` still passes entirely.
- Fractals demo: Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (unchanged; the master -10 dB of task AI is not in yet).

### Not as asked, or left
- **"Bit-identical regardless of block size" holds only for some instruments.** The bounce renders in the export's block size with the export's seeding, so bouncing the same clip twice gives identical samples, and it equals a direct render. But a render in blocks of 128 against 512 differs for most instruments, in the app's exports too (measured in this run, export 128 against 512: Venom and Wavetable identical, Pad -129 dB, Saw -84 dB, Pluck -69 dB, Acid -57 dB, Plant -37 dB, Drum kit -22 dB; the same clip through the bounce: Pluck -88 dB, Saw -96 dB, Acid -11 dB). That is in the instrument engines and was not touched here.
- Instruments with free-running random phases (Saw, Pad, Pluck, the reverb's noise) sound the same in a bounce as in an export but are not sample-equal to it (different seed string); only the level was compared for those.
- The bounce uses the tempo at the start of the song (as the Bounce window always did); a song with tempo changes is not handled specially.
- The old right-drag range box moved to Alt+right-drag: a change of an existing gesture, made because the rectangle was asked for on the right button.
- Esc during a drag does not cancel it (it never did).
- Not checked on the user's Mac; themes other than the default were not looked at (the marks are drawn in fixed colours on the arrangement).

### Release
- v0.214: commit cb81148 on main, app copy synced, update note written, desktop build not dispatched (as asked). Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 237. Project doc `claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AK.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.215: Shift and scroll makes clips wider or thinner, zoom and scroll settings (task AN, from a later message)
> "When you hold Control, you zoom in and out. When you hold Option, it makes the tracks taller or less tall, and I want it so when you hold Shift, the clips become wider or thinner and you can scroll. And I want some similar controls in the settings where you can change the way it zooms in and out. The way you zoom in and out should be part of the layout under settings under interface. It should be part of the layout block on the top under scaling."

### Research: what the wheel does in other DAWs
- Ableton Live 12 (manual, Arrangement View and keyboard shortcuts): Ctrl/Cmd + scroll zooms time; Alt/Option + scroll (or Alt + pinch) changes the track height; Shift + scroll scrolls left and right.
- Logic Pro (Sound On Sound, "Logic Pro customisation: project navigation"): Option + scroll zooms vertically, Shift + Option + scroll zooms horizontally; Shift + scroll moves along the timeline (the Mac's own behaviour).
- FL Studio (manual, Playlist): Ctrl + wheel zooms time at the pointer; Shift + wheel over the track names reorders tracks; middle-button drag scrolls. Alt + wheel as the vertical zoom is what FL users know, but the manual pages read in this run do not state it, so that part of the preset is from memory.
- Cubase and Bitwig: the pages found gave no table (Cubase forum threads ask for a vertical zoom key; G and H zoom). Not used for a preset.
- All of them use Shift + wheel for scrolling sideways. Here Shift is the clip width key because that was asked, so sideways scrolling moved (see below).

### What scrolling over the arrangement does now (default)
- Ctrl + scroll (Cmd too on a Mac): zoom, as before. Time and track height together, like zooming a picture (with the Ableton zoom style in Settings > Zoom and playhead it zooms time only, also as before).
- Option/Alt + scroll: track height, as before. All tracks get taller or shorter and the track under the pointer stays where it is. New: it also works with the pointer over the track names.
- New, Shift + scroll: clip width. Only time is zoomed: clips become wider or thinner, the track height stays. Anchored at the pointer. This is the difference from Ctrl: Ctrl is the overall zoom (both directions), Shift only changes the width.
- Scrolling sideways: two fingers sideways on a trackpad (or a tilt wheel) with no key, as before. With a plain mouse wheel it is Ctrl + Shift + scroll, because Shift alone now has the clip width job. If Shift is given no job in the settings, Shift + scroll scrolls sideways again (what browsers and the other DAWs do).
- Plain scroll: up and down (and sideways on a trackpad), as before. The map follows the same keys.
- A trackpad pinch zooms like Ctrl (it can be switched off). A pinch reaches the app as a scroll with Ctrl set although no Ctrl key is down; the app follows the real key so the two can be told apart.
- Smooth: every delta is added up and eased once per frame (the existing view animator); 30 small trackpad deltas give exactly the same zoom as the sum; the point under the pointer stays within a pixel (measured 0.00 px for time, 0.33 px for rows).
- Limits are the view's own: 600 px per beat zoomed in; zoomed out until the song is a small part of the width, and pushing on past that end opens the Overview as before. Track height 34 px to the height of the arrangement.
- Task AJ's arrow step follows the zoom reached this way (1/64 note at 567 px per beat, 4 beats at 5.8).

### Settings > Interface > Layout > Zoom and scroll
First thing in the Layout box, directly under the scale. Saved with the other settings (`UI.zs`), applied at once.
- Presets: AudioLab default (Ctrl zoom, Option track height, Shift clip width, Ctrl+Shift sideways), Ableton style (Ctrl clip width, Option track height, Shift sideways, no overall zoom key), Logic style (Option track height, Option+Shift clip width, Shift sideways), FL Studio style (Ctrl clip width, Alt track height; Shift has no job and scrolls sideways, since there is no reordering by wheel here).
- Four jobs, each with its key: Zoom, Track height, Clip width, Sideways. Keys: Ctrl (Cmd on a Mac too), Option/Alt, Shift, Ctrl + Shift, Option/Alt + Shift, None. Choosing a key that is taken swaps the two. Ctrl + Shift and Option + Shift were added to the four that were asked for, because Logic and the sideways scroll need them.
- Zoom speed 25% to 300%; Zoom around: mouse pointer or playhead (the playhead while it is in view, else the pointer); Invert direction (zoom, track height, clip width; pinch keeps its direction); Trackpad pinch zooms on or off; Reset. Every control has a tooltip.

### Code
- `zsWheel(e,beat,row,key)` decides for the arrangement, the map and (track height only) the track names; `ZS()` reads `UI.zs` over the defaults `ZSD`; presets `ZSPRE`; the settings group is built by `zsBuild()` after `stfBuild()`. The old Ctrl and Alt branches of the two wheel handlers were replaced by one call. Mixer, piano roll, devices panel and MIDI panel were not touched.

### Test
`tools/ui-tests/taskAN.py` (66 checks, all pass): each key with mouse wheel steps and trackpad-like deltas, the browser's Shift swap to a sideways delta, anchoring within a pixel, easing over frames without going backwards, both limits, the Overview still opening, track names, pinch, the arrow step, the four presets live, swap, None, Reset, speed 200% and 50% (exactly 2x and 0.5x), invert, playhead anchor, pinch off, saved and back after a reload, and the wheel with every key over the devices panel, mixer, piano roll and MIDI panel leaving the arrangement view alone; no console errors. taskAJ, taskU, overview, playhead and setfind still pass. Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB unchanged. Screenshots of the Layout block looked at, normal and Light mode.

### Left
- Not tried on a real trackpad or on the user's Mac: the feel of the speed, pinch against Ctrl + scroll on macOS and Windows, and whether macOS itself turns Shift + trackpad scrolling sideways (both directions are handled).
- Zoomed out with Shift alone the song can become about a tenth of the width before the Overview opens; that is the view's existing limit, not changed.
- Inside the Overview the keys are as before (Ctrl or pinch zooms).
- The shortcuts window lists keys only, so these scroll keys are not in it.

### Release
- v0.215: commit 09da3d0 on main, app copy synced, update note written, desktop build not dispatched (as asked). Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 238. Project doc `claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AN.md`.

## 26 November 2026, v0.216: audio dropped on an instrument track is turned into MIDI, and the neural stem splitter now really runs (task AL, from a later message)
> "Let's have it when you drop audio clips on top of MIDI tracks. Let's have it automatically ask if you want to convert melody or [...] harmony or chords [...] a pop-up window and you can choose whatever you want or cancel. It should also have an option to stem split [...] with the neural engine stem splitter that is already built in."

### Fact check (what existed before this run)
- **Stem splitter: it existed in the code, but the neural one never worked.** `stemSplit` (quick DSP split: percussive/harmonic, bass band, centre voice, v0.1xx) and `neuSeparate` (HTDemucs v4 by Meta, ONNX fp16, 4 stems 166 MB or 6 stems 136 MB, onnxruntime-web in a worker, model from Hugging Face and kept in the browser cache) were built. Running the real model for the first time in this run found two bugs that stopped it every time: (1) the worker declared `let ort`, the engine script `ort.min.js` declares `ort` too, so `importScripts` failed ("failed to load"); (2) with the graph optimiser on (`'all'`) the engine fails to create the session (unreadable number as error, `'basic'` crashes the page: out of memory turning the half-size weights into full-size). Fixed: variable renamed, optimiser off. Real 6-stem and 4-stem models now load and split in headless Chromium here (4 s of sound in about 35 s on one processor core; the user's Mac with several threads will be faster).
- **Audio to MIDI: existed** as the "Convert to MIDI…" window (clip menu): YIN pitch tracking (melody), harmonic salience (several notes), onset based drums, with sliders and a preview. Used here as the base. No Basic Pitch, no CREPE, no chord recognition existed.
- The About tab did not claim anything wrong; it listed the splitter ("offline after one download"), which is now true. Added a measured detail and a tile for this task, in honest wording.

### Research (from known material, one search; not re-checked in depth)
Basic Pitch (Spotify, Apache 2.0, small network, TS and ONNX ports exist) is the best known polyphonic note transcriber with pitch bends; it needs a model file to be downloaded and a TF.js or ONNX runtime. pYIN/CREPE are the monophonic references; Ableton's Convert Melody / Harmony / Drums to MIDI, Logic Flex, Melodyne, Bitwig and Cubase's chord track decide the same way: monophonic pitch for melody, chroma and salience for harmony, onsets for drums. Decision: use the engine already in the app (offline, no download, tested here) and improve it; Basic Pitch stays an option for later (would need one more model download and a test on real music).

### What changed for the user
- Dropping, Shift-copying, pasting or arrow-nudging one or more audio clips onto an instrument track no longer refuses or makes a new track: the clip is replaced at once by a striped MIDI clip ("Choose how to convert") and a centred glass window asks. It listens first (in a worker; the screen and playback stay free), recommends a way in plain words ("Sounds like a single melody line (quite sure)") and highlights that card.
- Cards: Melody (one note at a time), Harmony (several notes at once), Chord symbols only (one held block chord per beat, root as a bass note, clip named after the chords), Drums (standard drum notes 36 kick, 38 snare, 42 hat; on a drum kit track in place, else on a new drum track below; melodic results never go on a drum kit track), Split into stems first then choose, Keep as audio (new audio track below). Cancel, the X and Escape change nothing (the state before the action is restored, no undo entry is left). A click beside the window is ignored.
- Stems step: pick Neural 6 stems (guitar and piano), Neural 4 stems or the built-in Quick split (the window says "Not downloaded yet: about 136 MB are downloaded once" or "Downloaded: works offline"; offline and not downloaded says so; no WebAssembly disables the neural ones). Then every part has a play button, a level, a tick and a mode pre-selected by type (drums > drums, bass and vocals > melody, piano, guitar, other > harmony). The first part goes on the dropped track (its own instrument), the others on new instrument tracks below.
- Several clips: one window, a row per clip with its own recommendation; "Same choice for all N clips" (default) or an own choice per clip. Rows are kept (as in AK).
- Result: MIDI clip at the same position and length, notes in beats from the audio's own timing (project tempo; warped clips are read as heard; a tempo map is not followed). Timing is kept unless "Tidy up timing" (snap to sixteenths) is ticked. Velocity from the level, noise notes removed, glides merged, same-pitch repeats kept apart. Moved audio is replaced; a copy or paste keeps the original. If no notes are found or the analysis fails, the audio goes to a new audio track and a message says so. One Undo step brings everything back; Redo returns the MIDI.
- Also better in the old Convert to MIDI window (shared code): note starts were about 60 to 80 ms early (the analysis window is seen before the sound starts); now moved to the sound's own rise (error under 5 ms on test sounds); drums found from a fine level curve so quiet hi-hats are found next to kicks; ghost notes an octave below removed in the chord finder; a note struck again is split; repeated same-pitch melody notes no longer fuse.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskAL.py` + `taskAL_synth.js`, 76 checks, all pass; with `--neural` the real model too)
- Synthesised material (saw melody C major scale, Am F C G progression, drum loop, drums + bass + piano mix): recommendations Melody, Harmony, Drums, Split into stems, all "quite sure". Melody precision 1.0 recall 1.0 timing error 4.9 ms; harmony precision 1.0 recall 1.0; chord symbols Am F C G (16 notes, 4 beats each); drums 4 kicks, 4 snares, 8 hats found, precision and recall 1.0 (3.5 ms).
- Honest limit: these are clean synthetic sounds and the thresholds were tuned on them. Real recordings (noisy, reverb, vibrato, dense mixes) are not tested; expect mistakes, which is why the recommendation says "quite sure / fairly sure / not sure" and every way stays selectable.
- Flows: drop (move) of one clip: popup, listening, recommendation, convert, original removed, position and length equal, one undo step, Undo restores the exact state, Redo returns the MIDI; Shift-copy keeps the original; Cancel by button, X and Escape restores everything (no undo entry, no redo entry); paste and Arrow Down open the pop-up; Keep as audio; drums on a pluck track make a new drum track, on the drum kit in place; two clips dropped together, own choice per clip, same choice for all, cancel; Tidy up snaps to the grid; playing while hovering and dropping back changes nothing, playback continues while the pop-up is open; every control has a tooltip, no em dashes; no page errors.
- Stems: quick split for real (4 parts, players, modes by type, picked parts converted, Undo restores including added tracks); stand-in for the neural engine (6 parts incl. guitar and piano, Cancel after the split changes nothing); **real HTDemucs 6-stem model**: downloaded from Hugging Face, ran, six parts (drums 0 dB, bass -8, other -7, the rest silent for the synthetic mix), bass stem became the bass line (precision 1.0, recall 0.75: two repeated notes fused), drums on a new drum track, one Undo restores. The 4-stem model also loads and splits. The test harness reached the model and the engine files over the network here.
- `taskAK.py` updated (audio on an instrument track is no longer refused); AK and AJ tests rerun. Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB, Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96) unchanged.

### Not verified
- Real music of any kind; the Mac app (the stem model download inside the asar/Electron build was not run, the splitter is browser code and needs no shell update); long songs (split time grows with length: about 9 s per second of sound on one core in this container); the other music of the user's own projects.
- File drops from the operating system onto an instrument track still import to a new audio track (not changed).

### Release
- v0.216 on main (v0.215 was task AN, merged). Desktop build dispatched only if listed below. Project doc `claude/audiolab-audio-to-midi.md`; artifact 1b07d888 updated.

## 26 November 2026, v0.217: preset library, part 3: every effect and MIDI effect (from the user's message: "redesign every preset ... the best possible sounding presets from your research")
Part 3 of 3 (part 1: the synths, v0.197; part 2: the other instruments, v0.202). Only preset data, the preset menus and the level of presets changed; no effect's sound processing. Released on top of v0.216 (other sessions released v0.214, v0.215 and v0.216 while this one worked; the block was inserted again on v0.215 and v0.216, the full measurement ran on v0.215 with the block, and the load, menu and demo checks were repeated on v0.216).

### Effects in the browser and MIDI effects, their parameters and presets before and after
| Group | Effect (key) | Parameters | Presets before | After |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dynamics | Compressor (comp) | threshold, ratio, attack, release, makeup | 32 | 39 |
| Dynamics | OTT (ott) | depth, time, up, down, lo, mid, hi, out | 19 | 22 |
| Dynamics | Sidechain (side) | depth, shape, punch, sustain | 19 | 23 |
| Dynamics | Limiter (limit) | gain, ceil, rel, look, out | 16 | 18 |
| Dynamics | Soft Clipper (clip) | gain, thr, ceil, bias, mix, out | 15 | 18 |
| Dynamics | Gate (gate) | thr, hyst, floor, att, hold, rel, look, hpf, lpf, ratio | 18 | 20 |

MIDI effects: Chord, Bouncy Notes, Arpeggiator, MIDI Toolkit (group MIDI). Analysis effects (Spectrum, Loudness, Tuner, Meter) do not change the sound; their presets are views and targets.

### Research (sources read for this part)
Numbers marked "general knowledge" are widely published starting points that I did not find on a page read in this run; "guess" means my own choice.

- **Compression per source.** Sound On Sound (drum compression): snare 5:1, fast attack, medium release, 4 to 5 dB of gain reduction; close mics 3 to 4 dB each, then smaller amounts on the drum bus and "a touch more" on the mix bus; kick outside mic 6 dB or more with fast attack and medium release; a room mic 8:1 with fast attack and release, driven ([SOS](https://www.soundonsound.com/techniques/drum-compression)). Bass: 3:1 to 4:1, attack about 100 ms backed off until the transient survives, release 20 to 40 ms, 5 to 8 dB of reduction; serial stages of 2 to 3 dB at 2:1 to 3:1 ([EDMProd](https://www.edmprod.com/compress-bass/)). Kick and bass group in psytrance: 1.5:1, 30 ms attack, 1 to 2 dB (project doc, Myloops). Bus glue (general knowledge, SSL-style bus compressor): 2:1 or 4:1, attack 10 or 30 ms, release 0.1 s or auto, 1 to 3 dB. Vocal: fast 1176-style 4:1 to 12:1 with 3 to 6 dB, or optical 3:1 with slow release (general knowledge).
- **Sidechain pumping.** Reason compressor at 123 BPM: threshold -36 dB and the fastest attack for full pumping, release about 39 ms, threshold -14 dB for a subtle bounce ([MusicRadar](https://www.musicradar.com/tuition/tech/how-to-create-classic-pumping-sidechain-compression-624915)); "a few dB" of bass ducking with fast attack and medium-fast release ([Attack Magazine](https://attackmagazine.com/technique/tutorials/ten-tips-better-drums/3)). Psytrance (project doc, Myloops): light 2 to 4 dB back to 0 in 40 to 70 ms; medium 4:1 to 8:1, 6 to 10 dB, release 80 to 120 ms; drawn volume shape silent on the beat, hold 30 to 50 ms, back over 60 to 80 ms; the whole duck must end inside one 16th (103.4 ms at 145 BPM).
- **Multiband.** Crossovers for a full mix 120 Hz and at least 2.5 kHz, heavy top work above 6 kHz; bass guitar 50 to 60 Hz and a few hundred Hz; voice 150 Hz and 4 kHz; a mastering mid band 1.1:1 at -35 dB; bass release about twice the mid release and the top band half of it ([SOS](https://www.soundonsound.com/techniques/multi-band-compression-tips)). Glue start for all bands 2:1 with 100 ms attack and release, crossovers 200 Hz and 2 kHz ([iZotope](https://www.izotope.com/en/learn/multiband-compression-basics-izotope-mastering-tips.html)). OTT (general knowledge): Xfer's free OTT is Ableton's Multiband Dynamics "OTT" preset, crossovers 88 Hz and 2.5 kHz, depth usually 20 to 50 % on a mix and up to 100 % on sound design.
- **Limiting and clipping for club masters.** A limiter usually does no more than about 2 dB, ceiling -1.0 to -0.1 dBFS; heavy settings (3 to 5 dB) audibly distort vocals; a loud dance master example ends near -8 LUFS ([iZotope limiters](https://www.izotope.com/en/learn/an-introduction-to-limiters-and-how-to-use-them)). A soft clipper before the limiter takes peaks first and can give up to about 6 dB ([iZotope soft clipping](https://www.izotope.com/en/learn/what-is-soft-clipping)); 2 dB of clipping plus 2 dB of gain sounds cleaner than 4 dB of clipping ([Slate Bus Clipper manual](https://docs.slatedigital.com/Bus_Clipper/Bus_Clipper.html)); clip after EQ and before the limiter, high-pass or leave the bass out of a clipper ([Mastering The Mix](https://masteringthemix.com/blogs/learn/how-to-use-a-clipper-when-mastering)). Psytrance master (project doc): mix peaks -6 dBFS into the limiter, at most 4 dB of limiting, ceiling -1 dBTP, about -8 LUFS integrated, -6.5 LUFS short-term in the drop; Electric Universe albums measure DR5 to DR3.
- **EQ per source.** Kick 60 to 80 Hz punch, 200 to 500 Hz box cut, 1 to 5 kHz beater; snare 150 to 250 Hz body, 400 to 700 Hz ring, 2 to 3.5 kHz attack; hats 3 to 7 kHz harshness; bass 300 to 500 Hz mud, 800 Hz to 1 kHz attack; vocal high-pass 100 to 250 Hz, 500 Hz to 1.2 kHz nasal, 5 to 8 kHz harsh, 10 kHz air shelf ([iZotope EQ cheat sheet](https://www.izotope.com/en/learn/eq-cheat-sheet)); kick 50 to 60 Hz, 400 Hz hollow, 3 to 5 kHz; bass 50 to 80, 700 Hz, 2.5 kHz; vocal 120, 240, 5 kHz presence, 10 to 15 kHz air (Owsinski via [Produce Like A Pro](https://producelikeapro.com/blog/eq-cheat-sheet/)). Psytrance (project doc): high-pass pads and FX at 300 Hz, reverb returns at 500 Hz, everything except kick and bass at 120 Hz at least; leads lose their side signal under 300 Hz; mono under 120 to 150 Hz.
- **Reverb.** Cut the low end of a reverb from 100 to 200 Hz down, tame metallic highs from 4 to 8 kHz, a little pre-delay keeps the dry sound in front ([MusicRadar](https://www.musicradar.com/how-to/explainers-reverb-parameters-and-how-they-work)). Gated reverb: hold about 70 ms, release about 140 ms ([SOS gates](https://www.soundonsound.com/techniques/production-techniques-using-gates-expanders)). Psytrance atmospheres: pads with heavy reverb in the background; long tails printed and cut ([Bonedo](https://www.bonedo.de/artikel/psytrance-produzieren-3-psy-fx-atmosphaeren/)). Pre-delay and decay against the tempo (general knowledge): pre-delay a 1/64 or 1/32 note (26 or 52 ms at 145 BPM), decay that ends before the next beat or bar (one bar is 1.66 s at 145 BPM).
- **Delay.** Psytrance FX and leads: long ping-pong delays ([Bonedo](https://www.bonedo.de/artikel/psytrance-produzieren-3-psy-fx-atmosphaeren/)); lead delay at a low mix ([IDM Mag](https://idmmag.com/tech/tutorials/waio-style-psytrance-leads-transitions/)); a tape delay at 1/16 on percussion and a reverb after a delay at about 35 % mix on an arp ([Attack Magazine](https://www.attackmagazine.com/technique/tutorials/adding-atmosphere-with-delay-and-reverb/)). Dotted eighths (0.75 beat, 310 ms at 145 BPM) and 3/16 echoes are the trance and psytrance default; slapback 70 to 120 ms; doubling 10 to 30 ms (general knowledge).
- **De-essing.** Sibilance 2 to 12 kHz, most offensive 5 to 8 kHz; 3 to 4 dB of reduction normal, up to about 10 dB for strong esses ([Splice](https://splice.com/blog/?p=23640)).
- **Gates.** Drum gates: attack from zero up until the click goes, release for a natural fade, hold allows short releases ([SOS gates](https://www.soundonsound.com/techniques/production-techniques-using-gates-expanders)). Psytrance bass gate: attack 0.01 to 0.1 ms, hold 100 to 130 ms, release 15 to 40 ms, at least -40 dB (project doc; treated as suspect because it mutes the first bass note).
- **Lo-fi.** E-mu SP-1200: 12-bit linear at 26.04 kHz ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-mu_SP-1200)), so a rate divider of about 1.7 at 44.1 kHz.
- **Chorus.** Roland Juno-60 chorus: two buttons with modulation rates of 0.4 Hz and 0.6 Hz, both together a stronger chorus ([Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juno_60)).
- **Guesses (no published numbers)**: the particle and drawn effects (Vector Delay, Bounce Delay, Fractal, Blur, LFO Shaper curves), Frequency Shifter amounts, vocoder band counts per style, pitch-correction speeds outside the classic hard-tune, every tooltip's "best on" advice, and the level targets between groups.

### Which real device each AudioLab effect is closest to
| AudioLab | Closest real devices | What that means for its presets |
|---|---|---|
| Compressor | a VCA or FET channel compressor (SSL bus, 1176), Web Audio's compressor with its own make-up gain | presets by source (drums, bass, vocals, synths, bus); matched to each other, not to bypass (see rules) |
| OTT | Xfer OTT (Ableton's Multiband Dynamics "OTT" preset) | the classic full setting first, lower depth for mixes, upward-only and downward-only |
| Multiband Dynamics | FabFilter Pro-MB, Ozone Dynamics | crossovers 120 Hz and 2.5 kHz for a mix, slower lows and faster top |
| Limiter, Soft Clipper | FabFilter Pro-L, Ozone Maximizer; StandardCLIP, KClip | clip a little before limiting; at most 4 dB of limiting, -1 dB true peak for psytrance |
| Sidechain, Ducker, LFO Shaper, Fractal | Kickstart, a ducking compressor, LFO Tool and ShaperBox | duck lengths against the beat; psytrance: back within one 16th (103 ms at 145 BPM) |
| Gate, Transient Shaper, De-Esser, Resonance Suppressor | drum gates, SPL Transient Designer, FabFilter Pro-DS, soothe | by source; a gated-reverb snare (hold 70 ms, release 140 ms) |
| Echo delay, Timeless delay, Vector Delay, Bounce Delay | a stock delay, Soundtoys EchoBoy or Valhalla Delay in tape mode, multi-tap delays | note values against the tempo; dotted 8ths for trance and psytrance; filtered and low in the mix on leads |
| Space reverb, Convolution reverb, Blur | Valhalla Room and Plate, Altiverb, a freeze or granular cloud | rooms, plates, halls, chambers; psytrance: short pre-delay on leads, long dark washes with the low end cut for atmospheres |
| Drive, Bitcrusher, Low-pass filter, Auto Filter | Decapitator or Saturn, Decimort, a resonant low-pass, Ableton Auto Filter | by amount (subtle, medium, heavy) and use; the 12-bit, 26 kHz sampler sound |
| Phaser, Chorus / Flanger, Frequency Shifter | a pedal phaser, the Juno and Dimension choruses, a jet flanger, a Bode shifter and ring modulator | classics plus slow, resonant psy sweeps on hats and leads |
| Pitch & Formant, Pitch Correction, Voice, Vocoder | Little AlterBoy, Auto-Tune, a classic vocoder | voice changes, hard-tune, spoken psytrance samples |
| Pro EQ | FabFilter Pro-Q | per source; a psytrance group from the project's mixing rules |
| Arpeggiator, MIDI Toolkit, Chord, Bouncy Notes | the arpeggiators and MIDI tools of every DAW | patterns by style; E phrygian, phrygian dominant, dotted 8th note echoes, 3/16 accents |

### Rules added in part 3
1. Every effect: groups by use (source, task or character) and a "Psytrance" group; a one-line tooltip per preset says what it is for, and the menu adds the main settings in brackets (for example "4:1 from -18 dB, attack 30 ms, release 120 ms"). Presets made for psytrance outside the Psytrance group show "· psy".
2. Effects that started with no preset of their own (Compressor, Echo delay, Space reverb, Drive, Low-pass filter, Phaser) get "Init: Default settings" first, equal to a new effect. For every other effect the first preset, which a new effect starts on, keeps its sound, except the Timeless delay (see 5) and two levels that were clearly wrong (Multiband "Master glue" was 9.7 LU louder than bypass, the Vocoder "Classic robot" 13 LU quieter).
3. Every preset is filled with the settings of a new effect for the keys it does not set, so it sounds the same whichever preset was chosen before; choosing a preset copies its values (the effect no longer shares drawn shapes and arrays with the preset table).
4. Level: where an effect has an Output, presets are trimmed so the loudness against the dry sound stays within 1 LU (the Limiter and the Soft Clipper's mastering presets are louder on purpose). The Compressor adds its own make-up gain and Make-up cannot go below 0, so its presets are matched to each other at about +4 LU. Effects without an Output (Drive, Sidechain punch and sustain) keep their own levels.
5. Feedback: the Timeless delay's drive stage raises the loop gain to k/tanh(k) with k = 1 + drive x 0.06, and each filter adds about 1 dB, so its feedback is set from the drive for a chosen loop gain (0.45 to 0.78, each repeat 2 to 7 dB down). Its old default "Warm tape" (key tape) and "Ping-pong 1/8" (ping) build up for ever; the demos use them, so they stay, in a group "Endless (repeats build up)", and a new Timeless delay starts on a stable "Warm tape". The Echo delay is linear: at most 80 % feedback (90 % ran away and clipped 22 times over).
6. Old keys stay with their old values unless a value was wrong; renamed where a name had an artist, a brand or a product in it ("Dotted 1/8 (The Edge)", "Hard-tune (T-Pain)", "Kick tight 1176", "SP-1200 style", "Daft chord", "Kraftwerk", "Juno-style", "Dimension D", "NES", "Game Boy"), and where it promised something the effect cannot do (Compressor "Parallel crush" has no mix knob: now "Drums crushed, for a send"; reverb "Infinite freeze" stops at 8 s).
7. Tests for part 3: `tools/engine-tests/fx_materials.py` (test material), `fx_presets.py` (every preset), `fx_trims.py` (level trims), `fx_demo_same.py` (demos and new-effect settings), `tools/ui-tests/preset_menus3.py` (menus).

### What was built
- New block `/*PSLIB3-START*/ ... /*PSLIB3-END*/` before `askChoice`; `fxLib()` runs once after `psLib2()`. `FXMETA[type][key]` holds group, tooltip, test material, psy mark and hidden flag; `FXLV` the level trims; `FXSPEC()` the data of every effect (old keys renamed, regrouped and fixed where a value was wrong; new keys added); `EQLIB()` groups the Pro EQ presets and adds a Psytrance group; `CHLIB()` groups the Chord shapes with tooltips (new: Minor add 9, Minor with flat 2nd, Octave down and fifth, Minor and octave, 7 sus4, Half-diminished).
- Every preset is filled with the settings of a new effect for the keys it does not set, so a preset sounds the same whichever preset came before.
- Menus: `preOpts()` (every effect's preset menu and the Pro EQ menu) puts a tooltip on every entry (what it is for, then its main settings in brackets), leaves hidden near copies out and marks psytrance presets outside the Psytrance group "· psy"; the Chord menu is grouped (`chOpts()`). Choosing a preset now copies its values; before, an effect shared drawn shapes and arrays with the preset table, so drawing on an LFO Shaper changed the preset itself.
- Psytrance group on every effect where it makes sense (35 effects plus the Pro EQ and Chord), from the project's numbers: Sidechain, Ducker and LFO Shaper shapes that are back within one 16th at 145 BPM (103 ms) for a rolling bass, a light 3 dB version, pads under the kick; Compressor kick and bass glue 1.5:1 with 30 ms attack, a release of one 16th on the drum bus; dotted 8th (310 ms) delays on leads at a low mix with low cut 500 Hz, half-note ping-pong FX throws, quarter-note dark atmospheres; reverbs with a 1/64 pre-delay (26 ms) on leads, two-bar (3.3 s) vocal sample halls with 1/32 pre-delay, 6 s atmosphere washes high-passed at 500 Hz; Pro EQ high-passes at 120, 300 and 500 Hz and the two ways to share the low end (kick owns the sub: bass high-passed at 85 Hz; shared root); a psy master chain (clip about 2 dB, limit about 4 dB into -1 dB true peak, mono under 150 Hz, multiband split at 120 Hz and 2.5 kHz); E phrygian, phrygian dominant and dotted 8th note echoes in the MIDI Toolkit; 3/16 accents and a K-B-B-B roll in the Arpeggiator; hat swirls on the Phaser, Chorus and Frequency Shifter.
- Fixes in old presets: Timeless delay feedback set from its drive (20 of 23 built up into an endless loop; see the rules); Echo delay "Self-oscillating dub" went over 22 times full scale, now 80 % feedback ("Dub, nearly endless"), three ambient echoes shortened so they end; Auto Filter "Choppy 1/16" and "Acid squelch" clipped (resonance and drive lowered); Multiband presets clipped and were 8 to 13 LU louder than bypass; Pitch Correction "Hard-tune A minor" and "Minor scale gentle" used the same notes as the C major ones (now A harmonic minor, and C minor); Pitch & Formant "Add octave below (mix)", "Add octave above (mix)" and "Dreamy fifth" were the same as "Octave down", "Octave up" and "Fifth up" (that effect has no mix knob), now three different sounds; names with artists, brands or products renamed; Compressor "Parallel crush" (it has no mix knob) renamed "Drums crushed, for a send", Space reverb "Infinite freeze" (stops at 8 s) renamed "Longest tail, 8 s".

### Tests
Material (`tools/engine-tests/fx_materials.py`, rendered by the app at 145 BPM, 8 beats, peak -6 dBFS): a psytrance kit beat, the kit's kick alone (the Ducker's trigger), a K-B-B-B rolling bass on A (Psy Bass), a held A minor pad, a saw lead line, three of the Fractals voice samples, the Fractals demo (beats 64 to 72), a 4 ms click, pink noise and a saw gliding an octave up and down. The app renders all of these centred, so the mix and the pad were given a side signal like a real mix (mono under 300 Hz, a 7 ms copy at -9 dB above). The Limiter plays the mix at -1 dBFS peak.

`tools/engine-tests/fx_presets.py index.html materials.npz` renders every preset of every effect on the material that fits its group, twice, with a tail (16 s for space effects), each effect in a fresh browser (hundreds of renders in one page ran it out of memory). Final numbers (full run on v0.215 plus this block; Compressor, Sidechain, Vector Delay, Arpeggiator and Tape Stop again after the last changes):
- **No NaN, no preset clips (highest peak 0.94), every tail ends** (last second 50 dB under the loudest 400 ms) except the three marked to hold on purpose (Timeless delay "Frozen loop" and the two "Endless" presets, Blur's freeze presets). Before: 20 Timeless delay presets and 4 Echo delay presets did not end, the Echo delay "Self-oscillating dub" peaked at 22.5 (27 dB over full scale), Auto Filter "Choppy 1/16" 1.54 and "Acid squelch" 1.05, three Multiband presets 1.07 to 1.14.
- **Analysis effects** (Spectrum, Loudness, Tuner, Meter): output equal to the input for every preset.
- **Bit-identical twice**: all but OTT (15 presets) and Multiband (15), which differ by at most 9e-8 (Web Audio's own compressor node), Auto Filter (7, at most 3e-6), and Blur (22) and Echo delay ping-pong (8), which differ by up to 0.4 and 0.2 from the first repeat on. The Blur and Echo delay differences are the same on v0.213 (two renders of the old presets differ the same way), so they come from the engine, not from the preset data; not fixed here (no processing changes in this task).
- **Delay times against the tempo**: the first repeat of the click is on time (within 0.02 beat) on all 34 Echo delay presets and 22 of 28 Timeless delay presets; the other Timeless delay ones come early on the very first repeat because its delay time glides in over 50 ms when playback starts (engine), plus the two that build up.
- **Reverb decay against the setting**: measured RT60 (fit from -5 to -35 dB) is 0.86 to 1.00 of the set decay on all 36 Space reverb presets and 0.86 to 0.91 on all 23 Convolution presets.
- **Psytrance ducks on a rolling bass**: on the K-B-B-B bass itself, Sidechain "Rolling bass gap (145)" changes the loudness by +0.5 LU and "Bass under the kick" by -0.3 LU: the duck is over before the first 16th of the bass, as the rule asks. On a held pad (where the presets were compared) they cut 2 to 4 dB.
- **Levels** (table below): loudness against the dry sound over the 8 beats. Trims were measured in two passes (`fx_trims.py`) and written into `FXLV`. Compressor presets were -2.2 to +12.5 LU from bypass and are now +3.3 to +7.2 (Web Audio adds its own make-up, so they are matched to each other); OTT -2.8 to +8.2 now -0.9 to +1.4; Multiband +0.0 to +13.5 now -0.1 to +1.5; Vocoder -21 to -6 now -9.5 to +0.1 ("Lush detuned" and "Parallel" are at the top of the Output knob); Soft Clipper drum and colour presets +4 to +9 now 0 to +5 (its Output works before the clipper on some shapes, so "Wavefolder crunch" stays +7.3); De-Esser, Transient Shaper, Bitcrusher, Frequency Shifter, Convolution and Stereo Imager within about 1 LU where the knob allows. Left with their own levels (no Output knob): Drive (+3.5 to +11.7 on the rolling bass, the shaper brings a quiet bass up), Sidechain "Drum snap" and "Fat sustain" (+9 to +10), Auto Filter (the band and comb presets take most of a pad away), Resonance Suppressor, Fractal, Ducker (a deep pump is quieter by nature), Pro EQ (the curves are the point) and the Limiter and Soft Clipper mastering presets (louder on purpose).
- **Closest pairs** (spectrum over time of left and right, 24 bands by 12 slices, level taken out; space and modulation effects compared on what they add): table below. Pairs at 0.00 are presets that do the same thing on that material by nature: modes with the same notes (C major and D dorian in the MIDI Toolkit), gates that stay open on a full drum loop, a clipper that does not reach its threshold on a -6 dBFS mix, the analysis effects. Voice "Lower voice", "Higher voice" and "Child" render the same offline (its character voices are not available in this test). Changed because they were the same sound: Pitch Correction "Hard-tune A minor" and "Minor scale gentle" (same notes as the C major ones), Pitch & Formant "Add octave below (mix)", "Add octave above (mix)" and "Dreamy fifth", Tape Stop "Double-speed fill" (the same beat as "Double up on 4"), and four new presets that measured too close to an old one.
