- Menus (`tools/ui-tests/preset_menus3.py`): all 40 effect menus, the Pro EQ and the Chord menu: 957 entries in groups, every entry has a tooltip, no dash characters, every visible preset is in its menu, choosing the 4th entry applies its values without sharing arrays with the table, a new effect shows its first preset. Pictures of the Compressor, Space reverb and Sidechain menus opened were looked at (groups and names show). Parts 1 and 2's `preset_menus.py` and `preset_menus2.py` still pass.
- Unchanged (`tools/engine-tests/fx_demo_same.py`, v0.216 against v0.217): no page errors on load; Fractals demo solo Kick -9.75669 dB and Bass -25.48699 dB (beats 64 to 96) with the same checksums; the effect settings of every track of the Fractals demo, "Psytrance demo" and "Shrine Legion" are identical. `demos_same.py` renders of the two code-built demos: Kick, Rolling bass and Vocals identical; the tracks with Blur, delays and reverbs differ from run to run on v0.215 itself (Atmosphere pad -35.34 then -35.96 dB in two runs of the same version), so they cannot be compared bit for bit. A new effect starts with the same settings as before except the Timeless delay (stable "Warm tape"), the Multiband Dynamics (Output -9.5 dB) and the Vocoder (Output +12 dB), on purpose.

### Not verified, and what is a guess
- **Nobody has listened to any of it.** Values come from the sources above and from measurement; tooltips' "for what" advice, the particle and drawn effects, the Frequency Shifter, Vocoder and Fractal values, and the level targets between groups are my choices.
- The similarity measure takes level out, so two gentle compressor settings on the same material can measure close (Compressor "Bass even" and "Sub steady" 0.08) while they act differently on other material.
- The Limiter test mix peaks at -1 dBFS; the psy master presets were set for a mix that peaks near -6 dBFS (9 dB in, about 4 dB of limiting), the level the project's mixing rules ask for.
- Pitch Correction and Voice were measured on a gliding saw and on spoken samples, not on singing.

### Left
- Listening with the user, above all the Psytrance groups (ducks against a real kick and bass, the master chain, delay and reverb levels on leads).
- Engine issues found, not fixed (no processing changes in this task): the Timeless delay's drive makes the loop gain larger than its Feedback knob says (the stable feedback is now computed per preset, but a user turning Feedback up with Drive on still gets a runaway loop); Blur and the Echo delay's ping-pong do not render the same twice; the Timeless delay time glides in over 50 ms at the start of playback; Web Audio's compressor adds its own make-up gain, so the Compressor and Multiband are louder than bypass at Make-up 0.
- Drive has no Output knob, so its presets keep different levels.

### Release
- v0.217 (first built on v0.213 as v0.214; other sessions released v0.214, v0.215 and v0.216 in the meantime, so the block was inserted again on v0.215 and on v0.216; all checks above were repeated on v0.216 as noted).

## 26 November 2026, v0.218: logos that keep moving, and a freeze while AudioLab is in the background (task AO, from a later message)
> "The animated AudioLab logo on the settings and the animated AudioLab settings button pauses after a bit. I don't want it like that. I want it to be always animated, unless AudioLab is in the background. And let's have a system where AudioLab doesn't take up any CPU in the background, unless it's playing. But if nothing is playing and you go to a different desktop, or you start using another application, I want AudioLab to go into some sort of freeze mode where it stops everything temporarily until you return. And it needs to be dynamic."

Not to be mixed up with "Background freeze" of v0.201 (task E): that one plays tracks from recordings while the app is in use. This one is about the whole app when it is not in use.

### Why the logos stopped
- **The settings button (top bar).** Its logo was painted from the frame loop only while "not calm". Calm means: nothing plays and 3 seconds have passed since the last click, drag or movement over a device picture (`frame0`: `if(!calm)drawAtom(...)`, added in v0.170 so the glass of the top bar is not recalculated for it). So it stood still 3 seconds after you stopped working. Reproduced on v0.214: mouse at rest, Settings open, 150 s: the button logo moved in 1 of 30 samples.
- **The logo in Settings.** It has its own loop (`setLoop`, v0.170) and in the same 150 s test on v0.214 it moved in 30 of 30 samples. **I could not make it pause here**, so its cause on the Mac is not proven. Two things that do keep both logos still are known: the Lighter and Lightest looks (and the automatic switch to Lighter on a slow screen) drew them as a still picture, and the loop was only started by `openSettings`.
- **Now:** the button logo is painted from the part of the frame loop that always runs (30 pictures a second, 15 in Lighter and Lightest); the Settings logo's loop is started again whenever Settings is open without it; and the lighter looks no longer keep the logos still. Only the system setting "reduce motion" does. Both stop with everything else when the app is in the background.
- Cost, measured (headless, software drawing): one button picture 0.10 ms, so 0.3 % of one core at 30 a second; the Settings logo 3.7 to 4.3 ms a picture here (only while Settings is open, as before). Whole page in front, idle, before and after: Full effects 27.8 / 27.7 %, Optimised 26.0 / 28.4 %, Lighter 20.8 / 21.4 % of one core. Putting the logo on its own layer above the bar did not lower it (28.1 %), so it stays where it is.

### What was built
**One activity state for the whole app** (block "v0.218: app activity" before `askChoice`, names `ao*` / `AOS`; a few lines "the app activity gate" (`AO`) at the very top of the script, see below).
- **In front**: the window is visible and has the focus, or is visible and the mouse is over it (a move within the last 60 s), or another AudioLab window (a torn-off project) has the focus. A click, a key or the wheel always counts as in front.
- **Away**: hidden, minimised, on another desktop, or another app has the focus. Read from `document.visibilityState`, focus and blur, pagehide and pageshow, mouse enter and leave, a `BroadcastChannel` between AudioLab windows, and in the desktop app the window events of `main.js` (below). 150 ms wait before "away" counts, so the focus moving between two AudioLab windows changes nothing.
- A page that never had the focus and was never seen going away (opened with the cursor still in the address bar) is left alone. Inside another page (an embedded copy, such as the published artifact) only being visible counts, because the focus says nothing there. Nothing freezes in the first 15 s after the start.

**Three levels**
| | when | what stops | what runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0, in front | | nothing | everything |
| 1, drawing off | away, and something plays, records or works (export, bounce, freeze, render, conversion, download, AI job), or a key was just played, or the 2 s grace, or the audio has to stay on | every screen frame (`requestAnimationFrame`), the 16 repeating timers that only update the screen or prepare things ahead (meters, loudness read-out, clock, glass, tutorial, track background freeze, note cache), CSS animations (class `aobg` on the root), the song map analysis | audio at full quality with all its timers (scheduler, look-ahead, dynamic EQ, grain pump, sleeping tracks), and the three things the frame loop does for the sound (parameter automation `paTick`, modulators `modTick`, the reference track `refSync`) from a timer of their own, 30 times a second |
| 2, frozen | away for 2 s with nothing playing or working, and the master silent | also every other repeating timer (all 39 of the app), timers that come due wait, and the audio engine is suspended (`ctx.suspend`, after a 30 ms fade) | one check a second by the freeze itself |

- **While a job runs in the background** the frames that are waiting are run 4 times a second, so code that waits for a frame carries on.
- **Back in front**: one call does everything in this order: `ctx.resume()`, the timers are put back, waiting timers run, the class comes off, the arrangement is marked for a full redraw, then the waiting frames are released. No loop is restarted, each one continues where it stood.
- **Play, a key or MIDI while frozen** (from a controller or a shortcut): the audio part wakes at once (level 1), the drawing stays off. After Stop: 2 s, then frozen again.
- **The audio engine stays on (level 1 instead of 2)** when: a track is armed, recording or monitored (its input stays live); "Keep the audio engine awake" is on in Settings; keys or a MIDI controller were played in the last 30 s; or a tail still rings (waited for, at most 20 s). The reason is written to the audio engine's log ("Audio kept running in the background: the track "Kick" is armed or monitored, so its input stays live") and shown in Settings.
- **Hover effects**: there is no hover without the mouse over the window, and with the mouse over it the app is in front, so nothing had to be switched off.
- **Settings › Performance › Savers**: "Freeze when in the background" (on) and "Keep visuals in the background" (off; with it on the drawing and timers run on and only the audio engine pauses), both with tooltips, and a line under them that says what happened the last time ("Last time in the background (41 seconds): frozen after 2.3 seconds.").
- `aoHold('name')` and `aoRelease('name')` let other code keep the app awake during a job of its own.

**How everything is stopped without touching every loop** (`AO`, the first lines of the main script, the one place where this task could not follow "new JS before askChoice": timers made before that point would not be known): `requestAnimationFrame`, `setInterval` and `setTimeout` (and their cancel functions) go through a small gate. Shut, a frame that is asked for or comes due waits in a list and keeps its number; repeating timers are remembered so they can be taken away and put back under the same number; a one-shot timer that was set before the freeze and comes due during it waits, one that is set during the freeze (a job still working) runs. With the gate open the only cost is one small function per call.
- Work is counted in one place: every `OfflineAudioContext.startRendering` (export, bounce, freeze, render, clip renders), every `fetch` and body read (downloads), messages to and from workers (stems, voice, AI, time stretch; not the playback clock), plus the app's own flags (`EXFZ`, `FRZBUSY`, `RB.act`, `BUSYN`, `VAI.busy`).
- In place, one condition each: `mapAnalyze` stops between two chunks while the app is away (`!AOS.fg`) and carries on after the return. Wrapped: `start`, `stop`, `noteOn`, `midiCC`, `ccLive` (wake, and tell the desktop app whether something plays), `wdTick` (the watchdog must not take a wanted pause for a broken audio device), `hxTick`, `pmBuild`, `pmSync`. Replaced: `drawAtom`, `setLoop`.

**Desktop app** (`desktop/main.js`, `desktop/preload.js`; the page checks for `audiolabDesktop.activity`, the old app keeps working with the web events only; MIN_SHELL not raised)
- `main.js` listens to blur, focus, hide, show, minimize and restore of every window (and the app becoming active or not on the Mac) and sends `{focused, visible, minimized}` to all windows: `focused` is true when any AudioLab window has the focus.
- The page answers `{playing, frozen}`: `powerSaveBlocker` ("prevent-app-suspension") is held only while something plays; `backgroundThrottling` is switched on for a frozen window and off again the moment it is not frozen (it was always off before).
- `audiolabDesktop.metrics()` returns Electron's own processor numbers per process.

### Processor use, before and after
Test computer: 2 cores, no GPU (drawing in software), Fractals demo, Full effects, nothing playing unless said, 20 s per number, % of one core. The Mac is much faster; the ratios are the result.

**Desktop app (Electron 45, `app.getAppMetrics`, `tools/ui-tests/taskAO_electron.js`)**
| | v0.214 app: page | main | GPU process | all | v0.218: page | main | GPU process | all |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| in front | 10.71 | 0.10 | 15.61 | 26.82 | 11.59 | 0.07 | 15.53 | 27.62 |
| another app in front, window visible | 10.09 | 0.07 | 15.43 | **26.00** | 0.02 | 0.02 | 0.00 | **0.07** |
| hidden | 10.02 | 0.02 | 15.24 | **25.68** | 0.02 | 0.02 | 0.02 | **0.07** |
| hidden and playing | | | | | 10.49 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 10.94 |
| back in front | 9.79 | 0.05 | 15.01 | 25.22 | 11.66 | 0.07 | 15.11 | 27.27 |

**Headless Chromium (processor time from Linux, `tools/ui-tests/taskAO_cpu.py`)**: page process + everything else (browser, GPU process, audio service)
| | v0.214 | v0.218 |
|---|---|---|
| in front | 27.03 + 46.66 | 25.97 + 43.26 |
| another app in front, window visible | 26.48 + 45.36 | **0.05 + 0.00** |
| hidden or minimised | 25.57 + 41.26 | **0.05 + 0.05** |
| hidden, playing from beat 64 | 78.92 + 89.80 | 22.78 + 1.00 |
| in front, playing from beat 64 | 91.48 + 85.38 | 90.92 + 85.22 |

Frozen: 0 frames, 0 of the app's 39 repeating timers, 0.0 to 0.2 one-shot timers a second; what is left is the freeze's own check once a second. In front nothing got slower.

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskAO.py`, headless Chromium, 61 checks, all pass; visibility and focus emulated, every native frame and timer call-back counted underneath the app)
- Logos: mouse at rest, Settings open, 130 s: button logo moved in 26 of 26 samples, Settings logo in 26 of 26; in Lighter 5 of 5 each; still in the background, CSS animations paused, moving again after the return.
- Another app in front, and hidden: drawing stops at once (0 frames a second), frozen 2.5 s later, audio engine suspended; back: the first frame starts 1.4 to 2.5 ms after the return and the whole first picture is done after 34 to 50 ms, the audio engine was told to resume in the same call and ran again after 51 to 77 ms; the same audio engine, the watchdog did not step in, position, selection, view and project unchanged; the arrangement and the mixer are drawn again.
- Mouse over the unfocused window: in front again; mouse leaves: frozen after 2.2 s.
- Playing in the background (solo Kick from beat 64, 4 s from the first hit recorded from the master through a worklet): sent to the background 1 s after Play, and Play started from the frozen state: 0 frames a second, the recording differs from a play in front by -39.5 and -39.9 dB while two plays in front differ by -40.2 dB (live plays are never identical, also in front; over ten background plays in all runs the figure was -32.6 to -39.9 dB, in front -38.2 to -42.2 dB), the audio clock ran at 0.9997 of the wall clock, the browser counted no new underruns (in one earlier run of five it counted 2 at the moment the audio device restarted), and automation and modulators were still applied 30 times a second without the frame loop. After Stop: frozen again after 2.4 s.
- A MIDI key while frozen: audio runs again, no drawing; stays on for the 30 s, then freezes. An armed track: drawing off, audio on, the log says why; disarmed: frozen after 2.1 s.
- Settings: both switches with tooltips, the line with the last time; Keep visuals: 180 frames a second in the background, audio suspended; Freeze off: nothing stops.
- An export (Kick alone, beats 0 to 384) sent to the background 60 ms after its start carried on (the app stayed at level 1, "working") and finished in 3.4 s, bit-identical to two exports made in front (4fc1ce241dac).
- Desktop events mocked: focus lost with the window visible, another AudioLab window focused (in front), minimised or hidden, restored (first frame after 0.8 ms); the page tells the app when it plays and when it is frozen. A page that never had the focus is not frozen.
- Real desktop app (`taskAO_electron.js`, Electron under a virtual screen): `main.js` told the page about blur and hide, the page froze 2.6 s later, Play while hidden: sound on, drawing off, power blocker on; Stop: frozen after 5.5 s, power blocker off; shown again: in front. The virtual screen has no window manager, so blur and focus were emitted on the window by the test; hide and show were real.
- Regression: `taskAK.py`, `taskAF.py`, `taskAG.py`, `taskAN.py`, `play.py`, `updwin.py`, `appicon.py`, `setfind.py`, `fxlevels.py`, `loadtime.py` pass on the build with this change. `export_same.py`: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB (0496bb147f6c) and Bass -25.48699 dB (09e0415bec5e), Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom the same checksums as v0.214 (Plant, both Samplers and the Drum kit give another checksum on every page load, in v0.214 too). The master -10 dB of task AI is not in.

### Not verified
- **A song with automation or modulators in the background was not listened to or compared.** The frame loop used to apply them; in the background a timer does now, and the test only counts that it runs. In a hidden browser tab they stood still before this version (the browser stops frames there), so this is better than before in the browser and should be the same as before in the desktop app.
- **The Mac.** Whether macOS sends blur when you switch desktops with AudioLab not focused, how fast `ctx.resume()` is on a real audio device (51 to 77 ms here on a silent test device), and whether the first note from a MIDI keyboard after a long freeze comes late by that much. Nobody has listened to a return or looked at it.
- The Settings logo pausing on the Mac was not reproduced (above). If it still pauses in v0.218, the next thing to look at is the automatic performance step on that screen.
- Real MIDI and audio interfaces after a freeze (none here). The audio engine object is the same one after the return, and nothing reopens a device, but that is all the test can say.
- Two torn-off project windows in the browser (the channel between windows is built, not tested with two real windows).

### Seen, not changed
- **After Stop a small steady offset stays on the master until the track goes to sleep** (v0.214 too): solo Kick, 0.018 right after Stop growing to 0.15 after 3.75 s, then exactly 0. It is not sound (no zero crossing), but it is why the test reads silence only after taking a straight line away, and it is cut hard when the track sleeps.
- **Long exports are not always the same twice** (v0.215 before this work, in front): the whole mix, beats 64 to 192, gave three different checksums in three renders of one page; Kick alone over beats 0 to 384 gave another checksum in 1 of 4. The short regression render (beats 64 to 96) is stable. Once, on v0.215 without this work, `export_same.py` gave Bass -26.74483 dB instead of -25.48699 dB right after the page loaded. Worth its own look.
- The idle page costs about 11 % of a core in the desktop app and 26 % in headless Chromium while in front (the frame loop and 39 timers), as before.
- Auto-install of updates waits for the return when the app is frozen (its timer is one of those that stop).

### Release
- v0.218 (built on v0.214; other sessions released v0.215, v0.216 and v0.217 in the meantime, so it was applied again on each; the full test ran on the v0.217 base, the quick one and the desktop app test on the final v0.218; the timer for automation and modulators was added after the full run and is covered by the quick one). App copy synced, update note written. `desktop/main.js` and `desktop/preload.js` changed: desktop build as noted below.
- v0.218: commit 3704a12 on main. Desktop build run 45900585940 dispatched for this commit (no other build was running; it needs to finish before the new desktop app with the window events is out). Artifact 1b07d888 updated to version 241. Project doc `claude/audiolab-background-freeze.md`.
- The repository now answers under the name Audio-Lab-Pro-Alpha; `gh api` with the old name fails through the proxy (it is redirected to a numeric path), so the build list and the dispatch were done with the GitHub tools instead.

## 26 November 2026, v0.219: W7 shortcut icon (task AH, from a later message)

The shortcut icon of the desktop app is now the "W7" design: the Ocean Yellow logo zoomed 1.5 times into its top right, tilted 6 degrees. Only icon files and the desktop app changed; `index.html` has a new version number and nothing else.

### Done
- `desktop/build/icon.icns`, `desktop/build/icon.ico` and `desktop/dock-icon.png` replaced by the files from the branch `assets/shortcut-icon` (commit 504a506), byte for byte (not regenerated): the normal logo at 16 to 20 px and at 1024 px, W7 at 24 to 512 px. The sources and their README are kept in `desktop/icon-assets/shortcut/`.
- Settings > App icon > Ocean Yellow (default). Mac: the Dock gets the new `dock-icon.png` (the code already read that file). Windows: the program, the taskbar and the shortcuts already take the program's own `icon.ico`; what was wrong was going back to Ocean Yellow after another icon, which put the picker picture (the normal logo) on the windows until the next start. `main.js` now sets `icon.ico` there (shipped in the Windows build as `resources/icon.ico`; on Linux, which cannot read an .ico, the Dock picture without its margin).
- Not touched: `doc.icns`, `doc.ico`, `build/icon.png`, the web favicons and manifest icons, the in-app logos, the 49 picker pictures and thumbnails (Ocean Yellow in the picker stays the normal logo), MIN_SHELL (0.147.0).

### Tested
- `tools/ui-tests/taskAH_electron.js` (new; real Electron with `main.js` under a virtual screen, 18 checks, all pass). Started as a Mac (platform name faked, a Dock that records): the Dock picture at the start and after choosing Ocean Yellow is `dock-icon.png`, pixel for pixel, and is not the picker picture; Ink Electric still gives its picker picture. Started as it is (the Windows code path): no window icon set at the start, Ink Electric as before, back to Ocean Yellow sets the W7 picture. No page errors in either start.
- `tools/ui-tests/appicon.py`: 49 choices, Ocean Yellow the default, favicons unchanged, no page errors on load.
- `tools/engine-tests/export_same.py`: Fractals Kick -9.75669 dB (0496bb147f6c), Bass -25.48699 dB (09e0415bec5e), unchanged.

### Not verified
- Nobody has looked at the icon on a real Mac or a real Windows computer. macOS caches app icons: if the old one still shows, log out and back in once (this is in the update note).
- Windows reading `resources/icon.ico` at run time (going back to Ocean Yellow without a restart) was not run on Windows; the test computer is Linux and used the stand-in picture. If the file could not be read, the window would show the picker picture until the next start, as before.

### Release
- v0.219: commit 7144f77 on main. App copy synced, update note written ("New shortcut icon; needs the new desktop app. If the old icon still shows on a Mac, log out and back in once."). MIN_SHELL not raised.
- Desktop build run 45902988517 (success), release v0.219.0. Both installers downloaded and unpacked with 7z:
  - Mac (`AudioLab-Pro-0.219.0-arm64.dmg`): `Contents/Resources/icon.icns` is the new file byte for byte (sha256 23beac77...), `Info.plist` names it as the app icon; `doc.icns` unchanged.
  - Windows (`AudioLab-Pro-Setup-0.219.0.exe`): all ten pictures of the new `icon.ico` (16 to 256 px) sit byte for byte in `AudioLab Pro.exe`; `resources/icon.ico` and the uninstaller icon are the new file (sha256 62cbbba8...); the packed app holds the new `dock-icon.png` and the new `main.js`.
- The repository answers as Audio-Lab-Pro-Alpha; the `gh api` dispatch with the old name is refused by the proxy, so the build was started with the GitHub tools (first try).
- Branches `assets/shortcut-icon` and `assets/app-icons` could **not** be deleted from here (the push and the API call were both refused; this session may not delete branches). Everything on them is on main in `desktop/icon-assets/`, so they can be deleted on GitHub by hand.

## 26 November 2026, v0.220: starts with the Fractals song only, master 10 dB down (task AI)

Three changes to what a fresh install and every start look like. The audio engine is not touched; no desktop file changed (no desktop build needed).

### Done
- **One open song at every start: the Fractals song.** At a start every tab that would be restored is closed (also torn-off windows, the old "Demo song", "Psytrance demo" and "Shrine Legion" tabs, which are no longer added by themselves), and the Fractals song opens as the only tab, at beat 0, not playing, without an unsaved mark. There is no empty untitled song next to it: the short stand-in that holds the place while the song is unpacked (under the start screen) is removed when the song is in.
- **Which Fractals song.** The newest remake that is in the repository: the embedded "Fractals remake v6" (`DEMO_FRACTALS`, 30 tracks plus the audio track "Vocals (original)", 289 bars at 145 BPM), the one every regression figure refers to. The v7 and v8 remakes of 26 November exist only as project docs (their sessions could not push), so there is no v8 file to ship; see "Open".
- **Ships inside the app.** The project was already inside `index.html`. Its audio track was not: the ten vocal samples were fetched from `vox/*.wav` next to the page, which the desktop app does not have, so there the track was missing (and in the browser v0.219 added the track a moment after the song opened, and dropped it when another demo tab took the front in that moment). The samples are now inside `index.html` as FLAC (lossless, the same samples as the WAV files; 1.67 MB, 2.2 MB as text), decoded at the start; the WAV files stay as a fallback. `index.html` grows from 3.58 MB to 5.81 MB. The decoded samples are stored once under fixed names, so they do not pile up in the browser storage from start to start.
- **Unsaved work is not thrown away.** A closed tab that is marked unsaved is first put under New project > Saved projects (same name; two with one name get "(2)"; an edited Fractals song is kept as "Fractals_remake (edited)"), and a notice says how many were kept. A tab saved in a file is closed without a copy (it is on disk). Playing a song also marks it unsaved (the work timer), so the Fractals song is compared with the shipped one and only kept when something else differs; the v0.219 Fractals tab (master 0 dB) counts as the shipped one. If the browser storage is full, the unsaved projects stay open next to the Fractals song instead. The stored tabs are only removed once the Fractals song is ready, so quitting during the start loses nothing.
- Closing the Fractals song and opening others works as before (it cannot be closed while it is the only tab, as for any last tab). "Demo: Fractals remake" in the + menu opens it again, or switches to it when it is open.
- **Master 10 dB down.** The master of the song is -10 dB (was 0 dB). Nothing else in the project file changed: the edit script checks that the project with the master put back is identical to the old one, and the test compares the open song with the v0.219 one (tracks, notes, devices, levels, sends, sections, tempo: equal).

### Measured (`tools/engine-tests/taskAI_levels.py`, offline render at 44.1 kHz with the app's -6 dB output trim, whole song in 32-bar pieces)
| | v0.219, master 0 dB | v0.220, master -10 dB | change |
|---|---|---|---|
| Whole song, sample peak | +0.08 dBFS | -3.24 dBFS | -3.32 dB |
| Whole song, true peak | +0.42 dBTP | -2.92 dBTP | -3.34 dB |
| Whole song, loudness | -9.69 LUFS | -18.98 LUFS | -9.29 LU |
| Whole song, RMS | -10.93 dB | -20.34 dB | -9.41 dB |
| Mix, beats 64 to 96: peak / loudness | -1.05 dBFS / -10.55 LUFS | -7.99 dBFS / -20.27 LUFS | -6.94 dB / -9.72 LU |
| Mix, beats 640 to 704: peak / loudness | -0.15 dBFS / -8.71 LUFS | -4.54 dBFS / -18.04 LUFS | -4.39 dB / -9.33 LU |
| Solo Kick, beats 64 to 96 | -9.75669 dB (0496bb147f6c) | -19.05560 dB (fb577dd5202b) | -9.30 dB |
| Solo Bass, beats 64 to 96 | -25.48699 dB (09e0415bec5e) | -35.48685 dB (392ec52590c7) | -10.00 dB |

- **The master effects behave differently, and the song is not exactly 10 dB quieter.** In AudioLab the master fader sits before the master effects (tracks > master fader > Compressor > Soft Clipper > output). With the fader 10 dB lower the Compressor (threshold -12 dB, 1.5:1) and the Soft Clipper (gain 6 dB, threshold -4 dB) get 10 dB less and do much less: the loudness falls by 9.3 LU instead of 10, the peaks only by 3.3 dB, and the sound is less squashed (peak to loudness: 9.8 dB before, 15.7 dB after). If the old clipped sound at exactly 10 dB less is wanted, the way is the Soft Clipper's Output at -10 dB with the master back at 0 dB; that is a change to a device, which this task did not allow.
- **The regression figures are measured after the master**, so they change: as the song opens, solo Kick is -19.05560 dB and Bass -35.48685 dB (not exactly 10 dB lower, for the reason above: the clipper worked on the kick). With the master put back to 0 dB they are the old -9.75669 dB and -25.48699 dB with the old checksums, which shows the tracks are unchanged. `export_same.py` now prints both pairs; `taskAN.py`, `taskAL.py` and `taskAO.py` accept either.
- The safety limiter on the output is not part of an export and was not measured; live it sees 3.3 dB lower peaks, so it has less to do.

### Tested
- `tools/ui-tests/taskAI.py` (new, 31 checks, all pass): fresh start with no vox files served (as in the desktop app): one tab, 31 tracks, 10 vocal clips with their audio, master -10 dB in the song and in the read-out, not playing, start screen gone, no page errors; song equal to the v0.219 one; the FLAC samples decode to the same audio as the WAV files; Space plays and stops; after only playing, the next start keeps nothing; another song opens, the Fractals song closes, the menu opens it again and a second click only switches; coming from v0.219 (three demo tabs, Fractals played): one tab, nothing kept; four stored tabs and a torn-off one with real unsaved work: one tab, three projects under Saved projects, notice shown, one opens again from there; another start: still one tab, 10 stored samples, not more; storage full: the unsaved project stays open.
- `tools/engine-tests/export_same.py` on v0.219 and v0.220: Saw, Pad, Acid, Wavetable and Venom the same checksums (Plant, both Samplers and the Drum kit give another one on every page load, as before).
- `tools/ui-tests/play.py` and `taskAN.py` (66 checks) pass. `loadtime.py`, normal start, 3 runs: start screen visible at 0.49 s (0.27 s before: the file is larger), start screen gone at 2.1 to 2.4 s (2.6 to 4.7 s before: one song is prepared instead of three).
- `taskAK.py`: one check ("reverb clip: level within 0.5 dB") fails at random, in v0.219 too (1 of 2 runs there); `appicon.py` times out at its page load here, in v0.219 too. Neither comes from this task.

### Not verified
- Not run in the real desktop app (no build was needed). There the page is the same; a .audiolab file opened by double-click at the start opens next to the Fractals song and keeps the focus (by the code, not tested).
- Nobody has listened. The numbers say quieter and less clipped.

### Open
- The v8 remake (`claude/audiolab-remake-v8-fractals.md`) is not in the repository. To ship it instead, its `.audiolab` file has to be pushed or attached; the start code takes any project put in `DEMO_FRACTALS`.
- Not touched, as told: the mixer fader default ("Flat bars", task D1).

### Release
- v0.220: commit 0b0a8de on main (rebased twice on other sessions' doc and glass-lab commits; `index.html` was not touched by them).
- v0.220. App copy synced, update note written ("Starts with the Fractals song as the only open song, 10 dB quieter on the master."). MIN_SHELL not raised, no desktop build. Project doc `claude/audiolab-fixes-run-AI.md`. Next in the chain: D2.

## 27 November 2026, v0.221: sidechain outline only when the sender or receiver is selected (task D2)
> Plan `docs/glass-engine-plan.md`, section 1: "the sidechain outline (sender and receiver strips light up) should show only when the sender or the receiver is the selected channel. Cables stay visible as now, unless 'Selected only' is on."

Started after task AI (v0.220) was in the log. Only the mixer's outline code changed; the audio engine and the cables are not touched; no desktop file changed (no desktop build needed).

### What was there before
- `abxLights` (run once per mixer frame from `drawMixer`) lit the strips of every sidechain link (Sidechain effect with a source, and Sidechain modulators) all the time, whatever was selected.

### Built
- `abxLights` now skips a link unless one of its two ends is selected: the focused channel (`selT`) or a member of the multi-selection (`TSEL`), which the mixer and the track headers share since task AE. The check sits before the link's meter is read, so unselected links cost less per frame than before, not more.
- Block "v0.221 (task D2)" before `askChoice`: `selectTrack`, `hdrMark` and `selMaster` call the lights once right after the selection changes, so the outline appears or goes in the same step and does not wait for the next frame. No CSS changed; strips that lose the outline are cleaned by the existing code (classes and the two style values removed), so nothing flickers.
- What counts as "nothing selected": AudioLab always has a focused channel except when the master is selected, so "no outline with nothing selected" is the master case. At a start the first track is focused; if that track is a sender or receiver, its links are outlined.
- Edit script `tools/edits/taskD2.py` (every replacement asserted unique).

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskD2.py`, headless Chromium, Kick to Pad sidechain made by dragging the cable socket)
- Master selected (no channel): no outline. Kick selected: Kick and Pad outlined (sender mark on Kick, receiver mark on Pad). Pad selected: both outlined. Bass selected: none.
- Same step: reading the strips directly after `selectTrack` gives 0, 2, 0 outlined strips for Bass, Kick, Bass, with no frame drawn in between. Over 90 frames each state stayed the same, stopped and playing (no flicker).
- Shared selection: Bass and Kick selected together with Ctrl-click: outlined; Kick taken out again: none. Pad clicked in the arrangement header: outlined; Clap clicked there: none.
- Cables: the 86 cable paths are identical in every selection state. "Selected only": 9, 13, 5, 5 paths for Kick, Pad, Bass, master, and 86 again when turned off; the same numbers on v0.220.
- The same test on v0.220 fails exactly the "no outline" checks, as expected.
- No console or page errors. Existing tests pass on this build: `taskAE.py`, `taskAB.py`, `taskAG.py`, `taskAI.py` (32 checks), `taskAF.py`, `taskAN.py`, `taskAK.py`, `cables3.py`, `mix.py`, `mixstrip.py`, `play.py`.
- Levels (`tools/engine-tests/export_same.py`, beats 64 to 96): measured after task AI's master change, Fractals as it opens (master -10 dB): Kick -19.05560 dB, Bass -35.48685 dB; with the master put back to 0 dB: Kick -9.75669 dB (0496bb147f6c), Bass -25.48699 dB (09e0415bec5e). All four are the v0.220 figures.

### Not verified
- On the user's Mac with a real GPU. The light level of the outline while playing read 0.00 in the headless run on both v0.220 and v0.221 (no live sidechain meter there), so the moving glow was not seen, only that the outline is on or off.

### Release
- v0.221: commit 02555dc on main (rebased once on another session's commit; `index.html` was not touched by it).
- v0.221. App copy synced, update note written ("The sidechain outline on the mixer strips shows only when the sending or the receiving channel is selected."). MIN_SHELL not raised, no desktop build. Project doc `claude/audiolab-task-D2.md`. Next in the chain: D1.

## 27 November 2026, v0.222: "Flat bars" is the standard fader design again (task D1)
> Plan `docs/glass-engine-plan.md`, section 1: "Fader design 'Flat bars' (the original, design 7), not 'Flat bars 2'. It becomes the standard again."

Started after task D2 (v0.221) was in the log. Only the default and one start-up step changed; the 22 designs themselves, the half pan dial, the mixer header, the stereo displays, the cables and the audio engine are not touched; no desktop file changed (no desktop build needed).

### What was there before
- Since v0.194 (task S1) the default was Flat bars 2 (design 22): `MXSD.fd` and the AudioLab theme. Once per profile (`UI.fd22`) a saved Classic (1) or Flat bars (7) was moved to 22.

### Built
- New installs: `MXSD.fd` is 7 and the AudioLab theme sets design 7, so a profile with no saved design shows Flat bars (also people who never picked a design and only had 22 because it was the default).
- The old one-time move is removed: a profile that has not run it yet keeps its saved Classic or Flat bars.
- Block "v0.222 (task D1)" before `askChoice`, once per profile (`UI.fd7`): a profile that went through the old move (`UI.fd22` set) and has Flat bars 2 saved goes back to Flat bars. Other saved designs are left alone. Flat bars 2 picked after this step stays.
- **Choice made, as the task told for this case:** the app could not tell "moved by the migration" from "picked Flat bars 2 by hand". The old move saved only the flag `UI.fd22` (set for every profile, moved or not) and the design number; there is no time stamp and no note of who set the number, and clicking the AudioLab theme also wrote 22. So everyone who had Flat bars 2 saved goes back to Flat bars, including anyone who picked it by hand before v0.222. One click in the mixer settings brings it back for them. People who were moved from Classic (1) also land on Flat bars, not Classic: which of the two they had is not saved either.
- No UI text and no CSS changed. Edit script `tools/edits/taskD1.py` (every replacement asserted unique).

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/taskD1.py`, headless Chromium, 18 checks, all pass; 13 of them fail on v0.221 as expected)
- Fresh profile: Flat bars in the mixer, nothing written into the saved settings, the design button in the mixer settings reads "7 Flat bars" and the list marks 7; all 22 designs still there.
- Profile as the migration left it (flag set, Flat bars 2 saved, another mixer setting saved): back to Flat bars, saved, the other setting kept, track headers follow, the same after a second start.
- Deliberate later choice: Flat bars 2 picked by hand (click in the list on the fresh profile, and again on the moved-back profile) is still Flat bars 2 after a restart.
- A v0.221 profile with no saved design gets Flat bars; a saved LED ladder stays; a profile from before the old move with Classic or Flat bars saved stays as it is.
- Screenshots of the mixer (fresh and moved-back profile) and of the design list looked at: the strips show the original flat bars with the plain grip, the list marks 7.
- No console or page errors. Existing tests pass on this build: `themes.py`, `mix.py`, `mixstrip.py`, `taskD2.py`, `taskAE.py`, `taskAI.py`, `taskAN.py`, `play.py`.
- Levels (`tools/engine-tests/export_same.py`, beats 64 to 96): Fractals as it opens (master -10 dB): Kick -19.05560 dB (fb577dd5202b), Bass -35.48685 dB (392ec52590c7); with the master put back to 0 dB: Kick -9.75669 dB (0496bb147f6c), Bass -25.48699 dB (09e0415bec5e). The same figures and checksums as v0.221.

### Not verified
- On the user's Mac, and in the real desktop app (the page is the same there). The user's own saved profile was not seen; it is expected to be the "migrated" case.

### Release
- v0.222: commit 527b6d5 on main (rebased once on other sessions' doc and glass-lab commits; `index.html` was not touched by them).
- v0.222. App copy synced, update note written ("The mixer fader design 'Flat bars' is the standard again; if you were on 'Flat bars 2' you are back on 'Flat bars' and can pick 'Flat bars 2' again in the mixer settings."). MIN_SHELL not raised, no desktop build. Project doc `claude/audiolab-task-D1.md`. Next in the chain: G1.

## 27 November 2026, v0.223: the glass engine draws the docked panels (task GS1, glass step 1)
> Plan `docs/glass-engine-plan.md`, sections 3.1 to 3.5 (the user's Mac tests are binding): "the engine draws its own backdrop on the GPU", levels Glass / Light / Solid, "backdrop-filter is not used for these surfaces at all", defaults lens Wide 45 px and blur Light, "on this Mac at this window size it must deliver 100 % of frames with room to spare".

Started after task D1 (v0.222, commit 527b6d5) and task G2b (engine 2.0, commit 6765edd) were on main. While this run worked, tasks G2c, G2d and G2e put engine 2.1.0, 2.2.0 and 2.3.0 on main, with the user's Mac runs of the test page (plan 3.6 to 3.8: with 2.1 every row holds 240 fps there; square panels showed a rounded band in their corners, fixed in 2.2; 2.3 changes rounded panels only). The app got the newest engine (see below), so no separate port ("GS1b") is needed for that. The audio path is not touched; no desktop shell file changed (no desktop build needed).

**Not proven here: the frame rate.** The cloud machine has no graphics card (Chromium's software renderer). Every time below says only that less work is asked for, not what the Mac will show. The user's check on the Mac (3840x2027 at 240 Hz) decides whether step 1 is good.

### What was there before
- Track headers (`#hglass`) and transport (`#tr`): `backdrop-filter` with the SVG lens (`feImage` map) and a live blur. Device panel (`#fxglass`): a blurred picture of the arrangement (`#arrBlur`, made by `abDraw` on a 2D canvas after every arrangement change). Behind live glass a half-size copy of the arrangement (`#arrBack`, `gbCopy`, up to 120 copies a second).
- A frame in which only the playhead moved copied the whole arrangement canvas back from a snapshot and drew the playhead on it; the snapshot was a full canvas copy after every full drawing. With Follow centre no snapshot was kept, so every playing frame was a full drawing.

### Built (edit script `tools/edits/taskGS1.py`, app code `tools/edits/taskGS1_block.js`, every replacement asserted unique)
- **The engine in the app**: `tools/glass-lab/glass-engine.js` (2.3.0 from task G2e, with one method added for the app, `setBackdropColor`; the version number is not raised because the module's own checks ask for it) is pasted unchanged into the block "v0.223 (task GS1, glass step 1)" before `askChoice`. One WebGL2 canvas `#glassCv`, first child of `#main`, under `#arr`. Faces for `#hglass`, `#tr`, `#fxglass` and `#fxband` (the tab band is switched off by the app's CSS since an earlier version, so it has no face today; it gets one by itself if it is shown again).
- **Backdrop drawn on the graphics card** (rule 1): `gleBuild` makes the rectangle list from the values `drawArr` and `drawClip` use (lane backgrounds, per clip a body and a title band in the track colour, selected and muted clips as `drawClip` paints them, the darker area below the last track and after the song's end, the playhead as one rectangle). `gleSig` is one number over all of it; after a full `drawArr` the list is sent again only when that number changed. Scroll and horizontal zoom are one uniform, the playhead is one uniform (in 2 px steps, 4 px at Light: under frost a finer step cannot be seen). **No canvas is copied or uploaded on this path**: `#arrBack` is an empty 2 x 2 layer that only passes clicks on, `#arrBlur` is not made, `gbCopy` and `abDraw` copy nothing.
- **Levels** (rule 2): Glass = engine with lens; Light = the same engine, lens off, an eighth-size backdrop refreshed 30 times a second; Solid = no engine at all (canvas and context freed), solid panels. Solid is also what a computer without WebGL2 gets. `#hglass, #tr, #fxglass, #fxband` have `backdrop-filter: none !important` at every level; `rfUpdate` returns at once for them, so the old lens makes no map and no filter for them. `rfAttach` / `rfBuild` / `rfMap` / `rfStyle` and the `feImage` lens stay for windows, sheets and menus (step 3 removes them).
- **Settings > Performance, "Docked panels"**: Glass level (Glass / Light / Solid), Lens (Off / Fine / Apple / Wide), Frosting (Light / Regular / Heavy), plain buttons with tooltips, no sliders. Defaults Wide and Light (rule 3). The level follows the Light mode slider (Full effects and Optimised = Glass, Lighter = Light, Lightest = Solid, which is also how existing choices are carried over) until one is picked here; moving the slider puts it back on the slider. Saved in `UI.gl`. The texts of the slider stops say what they mean now; the old effect switches are headed "Windows, sheets and menus".
- The docked panels keep their own see-through tint on top of the engine's picture (the Transparency slider still works); it has its own value `--gad`, so they are glass at Lighter too, where everything else is solid. A panel with nothing of the arrangement behind it (Solid, no WebGL2, the piano roll in full screen, the transport in a layout where it sits above the map) is solid (`glsol`).
- **Playhead as its own layer** (`#arrPhW`, G1): a thin canvas with the line and the Follow button, painted only when its look changes and moved with a transform in whole screen pixels; the small arrow at the edge is a second tiny canvas. The arrangement canvas no longer contains the playhead, so a playhead-only frame does not touch it and no snapshot is made after a full drawing. The overview keeps its old way.
- **Follow centre** (G1): frames in which the view did not move a pixel are now playhead-only frames (nothing drawn). A full drawing still happens for every pixel the view moves; at a deep zoom that is still every frame (see "Not done").
- **Transport** (G1): the logo glow, the meters, the time text and the CPU graph no longer sit inside a filtered element, because the transport has no filter any more: its glass is the engine's canvas, a separate layer under it. Nothing had to be moved in the page for that.
- **Window drags** (G1): a floating window's title bar keeps the pointer once the drag has really started (`setPointerCapture`), and a cancelled pointer ends the drag.
- **Edge light of windows and menus** used to read the blurred picture; it now reads a 384 x 216 copy of the arrangement, only while such a panel is open, at most 11 times a second.
- **Background freeze** (task AO): the engine is drawn at the end of `frame0`, so held-back frames stop it.
- Engine module: `setBackdropColor` added (the colour outside the track rows follows the theme). Everything else is task G2e's 2.3.0 as it is; the app does not use `setOrigin`, `setBackdropRate` or `setBounds` (plan 3.7: not needed). The app has one engine canvas.
- **Corners and edges** (engine 2.2 and 2.3, plan 3.7 and 3.8): the docked panels are square, so they get the square lens (no rounded band in the corners). The app names the free edges itself (`gleEdges`), because its panels lie against things that are not glass: the track headers bend only on the edge towards the arrangement, the device panel only on its top edge under the track tabs, the transport only while no device panel lies against it (devices folded or popped out).

### Measured (`tools/glass-lab/bench.py --capped --reps 2 --win 3 --scen a,b,e`, software renderer, 1600x1000, 60 Hz cap; compositor time per presented frame = viz + gpu thread)
| Scene, "Full effects" | v0.222 | v0.223 (the app code as released, with engine 2.2 in it) |
|---|---|---|
| idle, stopped | 0.33 + 0.47 = 0.80 ms | 0.15 + 0.14 = 0.29 ms |
| playing | 0.41 + 0.66 = 1.07 ms | 0.15 + 0.27 = 0.42 ms |
| scrolling and zooming the arrangement | 0.43 + 0.52 = 0.95 ms | 0.27 + 0.29 = 0.56 ms |

Two earlier runs of the same app code with engine 2.0 and 2.1 in it gave 0.28 / 0.41 / 0.30 and 0.30 / 0.32 / 0.32 ms, so the spread between runs on this machine is about as large as the last row shows. "Optimised" (picture blur before, the engine now): idle 0.28 to 0.34 ms, playing 0.46 to 0.38 ms, scrolling 0.35 to 0.30 ms (no real difference). "Lighter" (solid before, the engine at Light now): idle 0.22 to 0.24 ms; its playing row stalled for seconds in all three runs of the new build while the trace was recorded and cannot be used. Checked apart, without a trace, 5 s playing at Lighter, three runs each: 184, 216 and 238 frames shown on v0.222, 169, 178 and 202 on v0.223, the longest gap under 100 ms in both. So **on this machine, which has no graphics card, Lighter got about a tenth slower while playing**: Lighter used to be solid, and the engine at Light is worked out by the processor here. On a computer with a graphics card that work goes to the card; where the browser itself reports software WebGL the engine is refused and the panels are Solid, as before. At Full effects the same check gives 113 and 121 frames in 4 s on v0.222 and 171 and 172 on v0.223. bench.py now accepts a build with the engine (it used to stop when the header had no SVG lens).

The app's own work per frame (`FPS.js`, same machine, Fractals song): playing without Follow 4.3 to 2.0 ms; playing with Follow centre at the start-up zoom 9.6 to 4.6 ms (full drawings 95 of 95 frames before, 15 of 116 now); Follow centre zoomed in to 40 px a beat 12.6 to 9.2 ms (still a full drawing on every frame, without the snapshot and the copies).

### Tested (`tools/ui-tests/glass_gs1.py`, 65 checks, all pass on the released file with engine 2.3, headless Chromium with SwiftShader)
- Engine on at Full effects: level Glass, lens Wide, blur Light, nothing saved; one canvas in `#main` before `#arr`; `#arrBack` 2 x 2, no `#arrBlur`.
- No `backdrop-filter` (computed and inline) on the four docked surfaces at Full effects, Optimised, Lighter, Lightest, at Solid picked by hand, with the context lost and without WebGL2. The old lens made no map and no filter for them; the old lens code is still there.
- Pixels of the engine canvas: faces opaque under transport, device panel and headers, empty elsewhere; **middle frosted** (fine detail along a row falls from 2.45 to 0.12 of 255 while the colour spread stays 61 and 62); **rim refraction** on the track headers' edge towards the arrangement (18 of 255 between lens Wide and Off inside the band, 0 in the middle) and on the transport's top edge while the devices are folded (39, middle 0); with the devices open the transport's top edge and the device panel's side and bottom edges are closed (0 difference), as engine 2.2 intends. **No corner seam and no arc**: with a picture whose colour is its place fed in for this check, the device panel's top rim is bent 22 px inward 3 px from the edge, its left edge and its middle not at all, and the place a glass pixel is taken from differs by at most 2 across the corner diagonal.
- **Upload counter 0** while scrolling, zooming and playing; the rectangle list is not sent again by scrolling, zooming in time or playing; an edit sends it once; a new track height sends it once.
- **Playhead-only frames**: while playing, more than 80 % of the frames are playhead-only, with 0 lists built and 0 sent; the arrangement canvas has no playhead painted in; the Follow button on the playhead still takes the click.
- Follow centre: full drawings in 11 of 94 frames, 0 uploads, 0 lists. While playing without Follow: 138 of 139 frames playhead-only, the backdrop under the glass drawn 5 times.
- The overview takes the small-copy route: the copy that goes up is a sixteenth of the canvas (86,000 of 1,376,000 pixels), and the engine is back on the rectangle scene afterwards.
- Levels: mapping from the slider (Glass, Glass, Light, Solid); **Light has no lens** (the lens preset changes no pixel), an eighth-size backdrop at 30 a second, panels still see-through; Lightest and Solid: no canvas, no engine, solid panels, nothing cut out of the arrangement; a level picked by hand holds until the slider moves.
- Settings: the three rows with the right names, the defaults marked, a tooltip on every new control, no em dash, clicking Light switches and saves.
- **Context loss and restore**: the engine shows its plain solid look by itself, after the restore the glass is the same picture as before (difference at most 2 of 255).
- **WebGL2 switched off**: Solid at Full effects, no canvas, Settings says why and offers only Solid, the app plays as before.
- Background freeze: 0 engine draws while the frames are held, running again afterwards. Window drag: the title bar holds the pointer during the drag.
- A 2x screen and the interface at 125 %: faces in the right places, 0 uploads. No console errors in any run.
- Screenshots looked at: Glass and Light (dark theme), Solid, the Settings page, headers on the right, the "Ableton-style" layout (transport above the map: solid there, as it has no arrangement behind it), devices popped out, the Ableton light theme with the mixer open.
- On the released file: `glass_gs1.py` (65), `engine_test.py` (191), `devices_lab.py` (17), `play.py`. On the same app code with engine 2.2 in it, an hour earlier (for the square docked panels 2.3 is the same lens to the pixel, plan 3.8): `glass.py`, `glass2.py`, `glass5.py`, `overview.py`, `loopbar.py`, `themes.py`, `fxlevels.py`, `taskAI.py`, `taskAO.py` (63) pass; and with engine 2.0 in it, earlier still, `taskD1.py`, `taskD2.py`, `mix.py`, `taskAN.py`, `taskAE.py`. `playhead.py` stops at its check "read-out hidden behind the tabs" on v0.222 too (only one project tab is open since v0.220); with that one line skipped the rest passes (all nine playhead shapes, the Follow button and its menu), with about 17 ms a frame while playing for every shape (the 60 Hz cap) against 23 to 30 ms on v0.222.
- Levels (`tools/engine-tests/export_same.py`, beats 64 to 96, measured three times during this run, last on the app code with engine 2.2 in it; the audio code is not touched): Fractals as it opens (master -10 dB, task AI): Kick -19.05560 dB (fb577dd5202b), Bass -35.48685 dB (392ec52590c7); with the master put back to 0 dB: Kick -9.75669 dB (0496bb147f6c), Bass -25.48699 dB (09e0415bec5e). The same figures and checksums as v0.222.

### Not done, not verified
- **Frame rate on the Mac, on Windows and in the desktop app (Electron, Chromium 156).** To look at there: Settings > Performance shows the frame rate; in the console `AudioLab.run('JSON.stringify(GLE.g.stats)')` must show `uploads` 0 and a `sceneSends` that does not grow while scrolling or playing.
- Follow centre at a deep zoom still draws the arrangement on every frame in which the view moves a pixel. Avoiding that needs the arrangement to be moved as a picture and only its new edge drawn; that was too large a change of `drawArr` for this step.
- **The mapping Lighter = Light** is as the task says. It means a weak computer that put itself on Lighter now runs the engine at Light where it had solid panels (see the measurement above). If Lighter should stay without any glass, that is one line in `gleLevel`.
- Floating windows, sheets and menus still use `backdrop-filter` and the SVG lens (task GS2). The device panel over the full-screen piano roll is solid until then.
- A real driver context loss (sleep and wake, GPU restart) and real Windows drivers.
- The rectangle picture leaves out waveforms, notes, grid, ruler and automation lanes on purpose. If something is visibly missing under the glass on the Mac, it is added as more rectangles in `gleBuild`.

### Release

Please tell us the frame rate you see on your Mac."). MIN_SHELL not raised, no desktop shell file changed, no desktop build. Project doc `claude/audiolab-glass-step-1.md`. ## 27 November 2026, desktop app: the Mac icon stays blue under the Dark icon style (task IC1)

Reading: on the user's Mac in dark mode the app icon had a black background (white rings, yellow dots). Wanted: always the blue square with the Ocean Yellow W7 logo. `index.html` is not touched; only `desktop/`, `.github/workflows/`, `tools/` and `docs/`. Full write-up with sources: `docs/app-icon-mac-styles.md`.

### Found
- The black icon is drawn by macOS 26 itself. Under System Settings > Appearance > Icon & widget style > Dark it makes its own dark version of every app icon that has none: background colour out, black tile in, light parts kept. The app shipped only `icon.icns`, so it got that. **Reproduced** on GitHub's macOS 26.7.2 machine with the released desktop app (DMG 0.219, the newest one): kept in the Dock and closed, style Dark, the tile is black (`docs/app-icon-mac-styles/dock-macos26.png`, first row).
- The released app while it is **open** is blue under Dark: the picture the app sets on the Dock (`dock-icon.png`) is left alone by macOS (second row). So the black one is what shows in Finder, Launchpad, Spotlight and on the Dock tile of the closed app.
- No old build and no wrong file is involved: `icon.icns`, `dock-icon.png` and the picker pictures are as the scheduling chat said.

### Built
- An Icon Composer icon, written by hand: `desktop/icon-assets/mac-icon/AppIcon.icon` (`icon.json` + `Assets/mark.png`, made by `make_icon.py` from the W7 source). Fill = Ocean blue for the default, the dark and the tinted appearance; one layer with the white rings and the yellow dots; glass, shine, shadow and translucency off.
- `desktop/scripts/build-mac-icon.sh` compiles it with `actool` (picks the newest Xcode 26 or later; the release runner `macos-15-intel` has one beside its default Xcode 16.4) into `build/Assets.car`. `package.json`: `CFBundleIconName = AppIcon` and `Assets.car` into `Contents/Resources`. `icon.icns` and `CFBundleIconFile` stay as they were. `afterPack.js` stops a release build before the DMG if either icon or either key is missing.
- `desktop/main.js`: on macOS 26 and later with `Assets.car` in the bundle the default icon sets no Dock picture any more (the bundle icon shows, blue in Default and Dark); coming back from one of the other 48 icons hands the Dock back with `setIcon(null)`. The other 48 icons, and macOS 15 and older, work as before.
- `.github/workflows/desktop.yml`: the Mac build compiles the icon first; new job "Mac icon proof" on `macos-26` takes the finished DMG, checks Info.plist, `icon.icns` byte for byte and `Assets.car` (the three appearances, each with the blue tile), lets macOS draw the icon in the four styles, photographs the Dock, uploads the pictures (artifact `mac-icon-proof`) and fails if Default or Dark is not blue. `.github/workflows/mac-icon-check.yml`: the same look without a build, run by hand.
- Two traps, both seen on the build machine and written into the doc: a plain `fill` key beside `fill-specializations` makes the dark entry ignored (black again); and the first answer of the icon service for a new app can be the old automatic picture.

### Proof (on GitHub's macOS 26.7.2 machine, no Mac here)
- `assetutil --info Assets.car`: the layered AppIcon for `NSAppearanceNameAqua`, `NSAppearanceNameDarkAqua` and `ISAppearanceTintable`, the tile of each the colour 0.078, 0.314, 0.659.
- macOS drawing the icon of the really built app (`NSWorkspace`, style set with `AppleIconAppearanceTheme`): Default blue, **Dark blue, 0.00 difference to Default** at 512 px, tile colour 21, 80, 168 (wanted 20, 80, 168). The app with `icon.icns` only: Dark black. Picture: `docs/app-icon-mac-styles/styles-macos26.png`.
- A real Dock under Dark, both apps kept in it: released 0.219 black, new build blue, closed and open (`dock-macos26.png`, rows 3 and 4).
- Default against today's `dock-icon.png` at 512 px: the tile box is the same (50 to 461 px, so no grey plate, same margin); mean difference inside the tile 1.6 of 255, 1.4 % of pixels off by more than 24, on ring edges and the thin light rim macOS 26 adds. The old `.icns` as macOS 26 draws it is further from the reference (3.2).
- `tools/ui-tests/taskAH_electron.js`: 21 checks, all pass (4 new: macOS 26 start sets no Dock picture, another icon sets its picture, back to Ocean Yellow gives null, no page errors).
- Windows: `desktop/build/icon.ico` not touched (sha256 62cbbba8..., the same as in v0.219); `icon.icns` not touched (23beac77...); the Windows part of `package.json` and the Windows path in `main.js` unchanged.

### What macOS decides (cannot be blue)
- **Tinted** and **Clear**: macOS removes every app's colours by design (one tint colour, or grey glass). The shape stays W7.
- macOS 26 draws an Icon Composer icon at every size from the same layers, so the hand-drawn small versions in `icon.icns` (simple logo at 16 to 20 px) are not used there any more; small icons show W7 scaled down.
- macOS 15 and older take flat pictures that `actool` also puts into `Assets.car` instead of `icon.icns` (seen on the macOS 15 machine: the same W7 with a faint light rim). The switch that should leave them out did nothing with Xcode 26.6.

### Not verified without a real Mac
- The user's Mac itself (Finder, Launchpad, Spotlight, Dock) and its icon cache after replacing the app.
- `setIcon(null)` against a real Dock (tested against a recording stand-in; if Electron refused it the code sets the old blue picture).
- Tinted and Clear in a real Dock.

### For the user to check
1. Install the newest DMG. With the style on Dark (or Automatic in dark mode) the icon in Applications, Launchpad and the Dock (app closed and open) is the blue square.
2. Still black right after installing: log out and in once (icon cache).
3. Settings > App icon: another icon, then Ocean Yellow again: the Dock is blue again.

### Release
- Commit d4f8378 on main (no version change, `index.html` untouched, `update-notes.txt` left alone: the note belongs to the program version and there is no new one).
- Desktop build run 45933179423 dispatched with the GitHub tools at 03:16 UTC (the last release commit, v0.223, was 22 minutes old; no other build was running). All four jobs passed: release, Mac build (the icon compiled on `macos-15-intel`), Windows build, and "Mac icon proof" on macOS 26. It made the release **v0.223.0** (the newest desktop app before it was 0.219.0): `AudioLab-Pro-0.223.0-arm64.dmg` and the Windows installer.
- The proof job on the published DMG: `CFBundleIconName = AppIcon`, `icon.icns` byte for byte the file in the repository, `Assets.car` with the three appearances and the blue tile in each, signature valid, and the icon macOS draws for the app is blue in Default and in Dark (checked by the tile colour; its pictures are in the run's artifact `mac-icon-proof` and were not looked at by eye, the pictures in `docs/app-icon-mac-styles/` are from the same code built an hour earlier on the test branch).
- The test branches `ic1-test` and `ic1-proof` are left over if this session may not delete branches; nothing on them is needed.

## 27 November 2026: task NM1 (a truly native Mac app: plan, plug-in hosting, first tests)

No app changes: `index.html` and `desktop/` were not touched, no version. Plan: `docs/native-mac-plan.md`. Pick page: https://claude.ai/artifact/17AwgeWUCCTaDX1Ct3mmcn . Test app: https://github.com/Audio-lab-DAW/Audio-Lab-Pro-Alpha/releases/tag/native-lab-1 .

### Written
- **`docs/native-mac-plan.md`**: what was read in the app with numbers (31 worklet processors, 42 devices of which 19 are worklets and 16 are built on the browser's own nodes, 32 instruments, 1,238 presets, about 17 windows, 124 settings); four routes with cost in tasks and sources (Swift rewrite 230 to 370; shared C++ engine with native interface 245 to 400; the same engine with the present interface kept, 95 to 150 for the engine; JUCE and its prices); a plug-in hosting chapter (VST3 SDK is MIT since 3.8, a host's duties, folders, Intel-only plug-ins, crash protection and its cost, the device strip, state in the project file, why exports with a plug-in cannot be promised bit-identical, Audio Units, CLAP, and VST2 with its legal limits); the recommendation (route C as the road, B as the destination, C++, no framework for now); five stages that each work on the Mac, with the test that proves the sound at each; what can and cannot be checked without a Mac; distribution without an Apple account.
- **Assumptions written down** (P1 to P7 on the pick page): instruments and effects; free well-known test plug-ins; own window plus an AudioLab strip; Audio Units then CLAP then VST2; plug-ins inside the engine first; VST2 last; Intel-only plug-ins later.

### Built (`native/`, `tools/native-lab/`, `.github/workflows/native-lab.yml`)
- **Test A**: `alnative`. The Bitcrusher ported from the worklet `al-crush` (`native/dsp/al_crush.h`), Core Audio device list and an output stream on the HAL (`native/common/coreaudio.cpp`).
- **Test B**: `alvst3`, a command line VST3 host on Steinberg's SDK 3.8.1 behind a format-neutral interface (`native/host/plugin_api.h`), with scanning in a child process.
- **Test C**: "AudioLab VST3 test", a small AppKit app (list, load, the plug-in's own window, keys or a test pattern, output device and buffer, a report file).
- The workflow runs only when started by hand and does not touch `desktop.yml`.

### Tested (workflow run 45933483595 on `macos-15`, a virtual Apple M1; an earlier run 45932930260 passed too)
- A: 144,000 of 144,000 samples the same bits as the web engine's render (three settings, also with dither and jitter), on Apple Silicon and on Linux x86-64. The Mac's own `pow` and `exp` gave the same numbers as the browser for this device. Building with fused multiply-add allowed made no difference for this device.
- A: devices listed; default output opened; buffer set to 64 frames; device switched to 96 kHz and back; exclusive use taken and given back. Also on the virtual device BlackHole.
- B: 6 bundles scanned in child processes, one made to crash on purpose, host alive; Surge XT, Surge XT Effects, mda JX10, Note Expression Synth, ADelay and AGain loaded, played, state saved and restored, export mode, 96 kHz and 32 frames, 0 failed blocks. Surge XT: 19 microseconds per 128-frame block on average. Two fresh copies of Surge XT differ by up to -7.6 dB sample against sample: the measured reason exports with plug-ins cannot be promised identical.
- C: the app started on the build machine, loaded Surge XT, opened its window (913 x 569), played five seconds with 0 failed blocks and 0 overloads, wrote its report. Screenshot in `docs/native-lab/`.
- Pick page: no console errors, no sideways scroll at 1280 and 400 px, the picks line fills.

### Not verified
- Nothing ran on real audio hardware or on an M4. The build machine's devices are virtual and say yes to everything; it reported 6 overloads in one second on its default device right after the buffer change.
- The test app was never clicked by a hand: the list, the keys, the Buffer menu, a device change while playing, controls in a plug-in's window. The last two layout fixes (plug-in window placement, key hint) were not seen in a picture.
- No paid plug-in, no plug-in with latency, no side-chain. Vital, Valhalla Supermassive and TAL-NoiseMaker were not put on the build machine (their terms do not allow an automatic download); they are for the user's Mac.
- The paragraph on rebuilding Chromium's own audio nodes (compressor, convolver) is from knowledge, not checked against Chromium's source.

### Found on the way
- **Fractals v10 is not in the repository.** The app carries "Fractals remake v6"; v10 exists only as build code in the project doc. "v10 as the only project on a fresh install" needs the file added first (question U5).
- The repository answers under a new name, `Audio-Lab-Pro-Alpha`; the old address forwards. The desktop app's update addresses still use the old name and work through the forward.
- The test app is published as a pre-release and not as "latest", so the desktop app's download page and the Windows updater keep pointing at 0.223.0 (checked after publishing).

### Next
Waiting for the picks line (`NM1 picks: ...`) and, more useful than any answer, the test app's report from the user's Mac. Project doc `claude/audiolab-native-mac-plan.md`.

### task NM1, later the same day: the picks and the first report from the Mac
- Picks: `NM1 picks: R1c R2b R3a R4a R5b R6a | P1a P2a P3a P4a P5a P6a P7a | U1c U2a U4abcdf U5b`, with U3 naming FabFilter, Ozone, Native Instruments, Kick 3, Kilohearts, Melodyne, Neutron, Arturia, Scaler, Serum 2, Steinberg, SubLabXL. Different from the stars: Windows after stage 3 (R5b), no Apple account for now (U1c), Fractals v10 with the first native build (U5b), a native look at 240 Hz added to what matters (U4f).
- The test app ran on the user's M4 (macOS 27.2): 253 bundles scanned without a crash, 222 plug-ins read, 31 bundles Intel only, 1 broken. The iFi Gryphon opened (rates to 768 kHz, 32-bit integer, a volume the computer can set). The Zoom AMS-24 was not in macOS's device list. No plug-in was loaded yet, so no figures per block.
